L374: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L375: In general, the FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment for the embryo-fetal developmental (EFD) toxicity studies. Due to an unusually high incidence of post-implantation loss of 16% observed in the concurrent vehicle control group in the pivotal study compared to 3% observed in the DRF control group and 6.4% in the historical control data (b) (4)
L376: we reviewed both the DRF and pivotal EFD studies in mice. The doses and schedule of administration were identical for the two studies. Based on the response to the information request sent to the Applicant, the high mean post-implantation loss in the control group of the pivotal study was due to the unusually high post-implantation loss rate of 100% observed in two animals, which skewed the overall mean value. The re-calculated mean post-implantation loss was 8.7% without the outliers.
L377: In the DRF EFD study in mice (Study No. 004-TOX-2008), dose levels of 4.7, 9.4, 14.1, or 28.2 mg/kg/dose (equivalent to 5, 10, 15, or 30 mg/kg/dose imetelstat sodium) were administered via a slow bolus IV injection on GD 6, 9 and 12. All animals survived to the scheduled euthanasia except for two animals, one in the control group and one in the low-dose group. There was a marked increase in the post-implantation loss at the HD level of 28.2 mg/kg (24% vs. 3% in the control). The effect was considered test-article related and was mainly due to an increase in early resorption of fetuses (2.7 per animal vs. 0.1 per animal in the control) in the HD group. No other imetelstat-related effects were observed in the uterine and ovarian examinations. These
L378: data demonstrated embryo-lethality toxicity at 28.2 mg/kg in mice with an AUC_{0-\infty} of 1374.32 \mu\text{g}\cdot\text{h}/\text{mL}, which is 2.5 times the human exposure at the recommended clinical dose (Refer to the FDA assessment in section 5.4 ADME/PK for the explanation on the AUC value and margin).

L384: ## 5.5.5. Other Toxicology Studies
L385: ## Impurity Qualification
L386: ## The Applicant's Position:
L387: Taking into consideration the recommendations of the ICH S9 guidance for anticancer products, no standalone studies with impurities were conducted. The qualified levels of specified impurities (ie, product related substances and impurities), and related safety margins in relation to the proposed specification limits, have been determined based on evaluation of the imetelstat lots used in the chronic toxicology studies.
L388: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L389: FDA considers body surface area (\text{mg}/\text{m}^2) the appropriate approach to compare the levels of the impurities in the nonclinical and clinical oligonucleotide batches. The proposed limits for several impurities in the drug substance specification exceed the qualification levels based on batches from the monkey toxicology studies. Two different lots (Lots #GAI-06-029-S4-B1-5.7 and #MM040520) were both used in the studies and the impurity doses were calculated using the highest percentage for a given impurity across the two batches to reflect the highest level of a given specified impurity.
L390: To qualify the impurities in imetelstat, the FDA considered multiple factors including the drug class (oligonucleotide), the nature of the impurities, the pharmacokinetics data, and the differences in dosing frequency between the animal studies and clinical trials. Based on the TK data from the 9-month toxicology study in monkeys showing an accumulation in the exposure (AUC) to imetelstat of 3-5-fold during the duration of the study, the impurities (being closely related to the API) are anticipated to also accumulate at the same or comparable levels. Given the accumulation together with the difference in the dosing frequency between animals and patients (higher frequency of dosing in animals), the FDA agrees that the impurities are qualified at the proposed specifications.
L391: ## **Immunotoxicity**
L392: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L393: A systematic assessment of the immunotoxicity potential of imetelstat in the 6-month mouse and 9-month monkey studies (003-TOX-2008 and 002-TOX-2008, respectively) indicated that there were no adverse effects, including no degenerative changes, on lymphatic organs such as the spleen, lymph nodes, and thymus suggestive of an immunotoxic response to imetelstat treatment. Additionally, there was no evidence of an immune suppression response in the chronic toxicity studies. Consistent with the expected ODN class effects (Levin AA and Henry SP, 2008), repeated dosing of imetelstat in general toxicity studies showed non-specific immune stimulation, including injection site inflammation correlated with systemic mononuclear cell infiltration in tissues, with mice showing greater sensitivity than monkeys.
L394: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L395: The FDA concurs.
L396: ## **Local Tolerance**
L397: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L398: Local tolerance to imetelstat administration at the injection site was evaluated in general toxicity studies in mice, rats, and monkeys. Injection site findings were largely limited to tail vein injection sites in mice and included vascular irritation, discoloration and microscopic observations of necrosis/inflammation and vascular/perivascular fibrosis. This injection site
L399: pathology was likely attributable to extravasation of imetelstat from the very small tail veins which commonly occurs with IV bolus injections in rodent studies and is not considered clinically relevant.
L400: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L401: The FDA concurs.
L402: ## **Phototoxicity**
L403: ## The Applicant's Position:
L404: An in vitro phototoxicity study of imetelstat in BALB/c 3T3 cells incubated for one hour at concentrations ranging from 68.1 to 1000 µg/mL, which utilized a neutral-red uptake endpoint that also reflects cytotoxicity, showed no effects, thereby confirming the absence of any potential for imetelstat to elicit phototoxicity (Study MB 10-19402.30).
L405: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L406: The FDA concurs.

L413: ## 6 Clinical Pharmacology
L414: ## 6.1. Executive Summary
L415: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L416: Imetelstat sodium (i.e., imetelstat) is a 13-mer N3'→P5' thio-phosphoramidate oligonucleotide with a palmitoyl lipid covalently attached by an aminoglycerol linker to the 5' thio-phosphate of the first nucleotide. It has a complementary sequence to the RNA component of human telomerase reverse transcriptase (hTERT) that lies in the active or catalytic site of hTERT, resulting in competitive inhibition of hTERT and prevention of telomerase binding. Rather than modulating gene expression products, the imetelstat mechanism of action resembles classic active site inhibition.
L417: The proposed indication for imetelstat is the treatment of transfusion-dependent anemia in adult patients with low- to intermediate-1 risk myelodysplastic syndromes (LR-MDS) who have failed to respond or have lost response to or are ineligible for erythropoiesis-stimulating agents (ESAs). The proposed dosing regimen is 7.1 mg/kg imetelstat (equivalent to 7.5 mg/kg imetelstat sodium via the salt rule) as 2-hour infusion every four weeks.
L418: The clinical pharmacology package includes data from nine clinical studies, pharmacometrics analyses, and an evaluation of immunogenicity. The pivotal trial is 63935937MDS3001 (MDS3001), a completed Phase 2/3 study in subjects with LR-MDS. The Phase 2 part was an open-label, single-arm trial and the Phase 3 (pivotal) part was a double-blind, randomized, placebo-controlled trial. The Applicant's pharmacometrics analyses include population PK (popPK) analyses and exposure-response (E-R) analyses for safety and efficacy using data from the monotherapy studies CP14A004, CP05-101, CP04-151, CP14B013, CP14B015, MYF2001, MDS3001. The immunogenicity analysis used data collected from two studies (Studies MYF2001 and MDS3001).
L419: The clinical pharmacology review focused on 1) the acceptability of the proposed dosing regimen; 2) the effects of anti-drug antibodies (ADAs) on pharmacokinetics (PK), safety, and efficacy of imetelstat; 3) the drug-drug interaction (DDI) potential of imetelstat as an inhibitor of transporters; and 4) the effect of mutations in MDS-related genes on response to imetelstat.
L420: **Recommendations:** The Office of Clinical Pharmacology has reviewed the information submitted in NDA 217779. This NDA is approvable from a clinical pharmacology perspective. However, an assessment on the risk/benefit profiles suggests the proposed dosing regimen
L421: Version date: June 2022 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)
L422: Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as "Data" and "The Applicant's Position" are completed by the Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.
L423: warrants further dose optimization. The key review issues with specific recommendations are summarized below in Table 10.
L424: **Table 10:** Key Clinical Pharmacology Review Issues
L425: Review Issue	Recommendations and Comments
Pivotal and Supportive evidence of effectiveness	
The primary evidence of effectiveness is the superiority of imetelstat vs placebo in the achievement of 8-week red blood cell (RBC) transfusion independence (TI) from the pivotal study MDS3001 (See Section 8.1).
Supportive evidence of effectiveness comes from the superiority in selected secondary endpoints including 24-week TI and the duration of TI, as well as the results of E-R analysis showing higher imetelstat average concentration (Cavg) associated with higher probability of achieving 8-week TI. However, the E-R analysis for efficacy has significant limitations because of the single dose level and the frequent dose modification in the study.

General dosing instructions	
The proposed recommended dosing regimen is 7.1 mg/kg imetelstat as a two-hour infusion every four weeks.
The proposed dosing regimen in the target LR-MDS patient population may not be optimized, and additional dose exploration will be required. Despite meeting the primary efficacy endpoint in Study MDS3001, only one dose level has been evaluated in the LR-MDS patient population, and data from the other submitted studies had issues that preclude confidence in an assessment of the dose-response relationships on the efficacy, safety, and pharmacodynamic endpoints. Additionally, higher rates of Grade 3/4 TEAEs, particularly thrombocytopenia and neutropenia, and higher rates of dose modifications, interruptions, and discontinuations were observed in the imetelstat treatment arm compared to the placebo arm. In addition, the E-R analysis for safety identified a positive relationship between the risks for Grade 3/4 thrombocytopenia and maximum plasma concentration (Cmax) in the first cycle, suggesting a potential to further optimize dosage selection in the proposed patient population.

Dosing in patient subgroups	No therapeutic individualization of imetelstat is needed based upon intrinsic factors such as age, sex, race (81% White, 4% Asian, 7% Black, 8% other/unknown), mild to moderate renal impairment (CrCL 30 to < 90 mL/min), mild hepatic impairment (total bilirubin ≤ ULN and AST > ULN, or total bilirubin > 1x to 1.5x ULN and any AST), or moderate hepatic impairment (total bilirubin > 1.5x to 3x ULN and any AST). The PK of imetelstat has not been adequately characterized in patients with severe renal impairment (CrCL 15 and < 30 mL/min), end-stage renal
L426: Review Issue	Recommendations and Comments
disease, or severe hepatic impairment (total bilirubin > 3x ULN and any AST).
No dose adjustment is needed based on the presence of ADAs. The median time to the formation of ADAs was longer than the median time to the onset of 8-week TI or adverse events.
No dose adjustment is needed based on SF3B1 mutational status, as this was considered exploratory.

Drug-drug interactions	Imetelstat is an inhibitor of OATP1 and OATP1B3 based upon in vitro study data. However, there is low risk for clinically meaningful drug-drug interactions (DDIs) due to the short half-life and long dosing interval. The risks for clinically meaningful drug-drug interactions due to CYP450 enzymes are low.
Labeling	The proposed labelling recommendations are acceptable upon the Applicant's agreement to the FDA revisions to the draft label.
L427: ## 6.2. Summary of Clinical Pharmacology Assessment
L428: The clinical pharmacology program for imetelstat consists of 9 completed clinical studies with imetelstat administered as a single agent or in combination with various systemic anticancer agents across a wide range of doses (0.4 to 11.0 mg/kg) and dose schedules (once weekly [qw], once every 2 weeks [q2w], one every 3 weeks [q3w], once every 4 weeks [q4w]). Population PK analysis using clinical PK data from 7 monotherapy studies with serial or sparse PK sampling were conducted to quantitatively describe the PK of imetelstat in subjects and to evaluate the effects of relevant covariates (e.g., demographics, laboratory baseline values, disease status) that may contribute to the variability in imetelstat exposure in individual subjects.
L429: ## 6.2.1. Pharmacology and Clinical Pharmacokinetics
L430: ## Data and Applicant's Position:
L431: Clinical Pharmacokinetics: The PK of imetelstat were evaluated in patients with solid tumors and hematologic malignancies following intravenous administration. Imetelstat exposure (AUC) increases in a more than dose proportional manner over the 0.4 to 11.0 mg/kg dose range. In subjects with MDS receiving an intravenous infusion of imetelstat of 7.1 mg/kg over 2 hours, the geometric mean (coefficient of variation [CV%]) C_{max} in plasma was 89.2 \mug/mL (27.2%) with peak concentrations observed at the end of the infusion. The AUC_{0-28} was 554 h \cdot \mug/mL (43.4%). Imetelstat does not accumulate between treatment cycles following every four-week dosing in patients with MDS. The geometric mean (CV%) apparent half-life for imetelstat in plasma is approximately 4.9 hours (45.4%) in patients with MDS following a 7.1 mg/kg dose.
L432: **Absorption:** The drug product is administered intravenously; no food effect is expected. In subjects with MDS receiving an intravenous infusion of imetelstat 7.1 mg/kg over 2 hours, maximum imetelstat concentrations were reached by the end of infusion.
L433: **Distribution:** The geometric mean (CV%) volume of distribution at steady state was 11.5 L (26.1%). Human plasma protein binding of imetelstat was greater than 94% in vitro at clinically relevant concentrations.
L434: **Elimination:** The geometric mean (CV%) apparent half-life for imetelstat in plasma is approximately 4.9 hours (45.4%) in patients with MDS following a 7.1 mg/kg dose. Nonclinical studies demonstrated that urine is the primary route of elimination, with 62% to 82% recovery of radiolabeled imetelstat in urine and 12 to 21% recovery in feces after 1 week. Unchanged imetelstat is not detected in the urine of rats that were administered radiolabeled imetelstat.
L435: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L436: The FDA generally agrees with the Applicant's description of the clinical pharmacology properties of imetelstat. It is noted that the reporting of PK exposures (e.g., Cmax, AUC) in this review is based on the mass concentration of the salt form, whereas the reporting of dosage is based on a conversion to the acid form. The reporting of PK exposures (based on updated popPK analysis; refer to Section 19.4.1) will be converted to molar concentration in the product label. This linear conversion does not impact the calculation of other PK parameters (e.g., Tmax, T1/2, etc.) or the exposure-response analyses.
L437: ## 6.2.2. General Dosing and Therapeutic Individualization
L438: ## 6.2.2.1. General Dosing
L439: ## **Data and Applicant's Position:**
L440: ## **General Dosing**
L441: The proposed imetelstat dosing regimen is 7.1 mg/kg q4w via 2 hour IV infusion for treatment of patients with MDS. This is the same dosage as studied in the MDS3001 trial. Selection of this proposed dosing regimen is based on the clinical efficacy and safety results from the pivotal Phase 3 study, MDS3001, and the supportive Phase 2 portion of MDS3001, along with results from exposure-response (E-R) analyses, clinical PK and target engagement, and supportive nonclinical data (Section 6.3.2.2).
L442: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L443: It is unclear whether the dosing regimen (7.1 mg/kg imetelstat as a two-hour infusion every four weeks) is optimized. Limited dose finding was conducted in the target population. In the

L447: ## 6.2.2.2. Therapeutic Individualization
L448: ## **Data:**
L449: Data regarding the impact of intrinsic and extrinsic factors on imetelstat PK are described in Sections 6.3.2.3 and 6.3.2.4.
L450: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L451: No therapeutic individualization is required for imetelstat based on intrinsic or extrinsic factors. No clinically relevant PK differences were seen for sex, age, race, mild to moderate hepatic impairment, or mild to moderate renal impairment. There is limited information on the effect of severe hepatic impairment, severe renal impairment, or end-stage renal disease on imetelstat PK (Section 6.3.2.3).
L452: Body weight with theory-based allometric exponents was included as a covariate in the popPK analysis. Using body-weight-based dosing, there is limited exposure variation expected for subjects with low body weight (52 kg) or high body weight (107 kg) compared to a reference subject of 70 kg, supporting the use of the body-weight-based dosing approach for imetelstat (Section 6.3.2.3).
L453: Dose adjustments for food-drug or drug-drug interactions extrinsic factors are not necessary for imetelstat (Section 6.3.2.4).
L454: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L455: The FDA agrees that no therapeutic individualization of imetelstat is needed based upon intrinsic factors from the popPK modeling results and the analysis on the mutational status of SF3B1. Refer to Section 6.2.2.3, Section 19.4.1, and Section 19.4.3.

L463: ## 6.3. Comprehensive Clinical Pharmacology Review
L464: ## 6.3.1. General Pharmacology and Pharmacokinetic Characteristics
L465: ## **Data and Sponsor's Position:**
L466: An overview of the pharmacology, ADME properties, clinical PK, and immunogenicity of imetelstat is provided below.
L467: Pharmacology
Mechanism of Action	Imetelstat is a specific inhibitor of human telomerase that binds with high affinity to the template region of the RNA component of human telomerase and acts as a direct competitive inhibitor of telomerase enzymatic activity, which prevents telomere binding. Telomerase activity and human telomerase reverse transcriptase (hTERT) RNA expression are known to be significantly increased in MDS and malignant stem and progenitor cells. Imetelstat treatment leads to selective loss of such neoplastic cells' ability to maintain telomere length, resulting in inhibition of malignant cell proliferation and induction of apoptosis/senescence leading to the observed reduction of malignant clones (Section 5.3).
Pharmacodynamics	At the recommended dosage of 7.1 mg/kg administered as a 2 hours IV infusion q4w in patients with MDS, imetelstat reduced TA by \ge 50\% from baseline in 60.0% (21/35) of participants compared to 37.5% (9/24) of participants in the placebo group during the first 2 cycles (Study MDS3001).
General Information and PK Properties
Bioanalysis	Bioanalytical assays were developed and fully validated in compliance with appropriate GLP and have been used to measure plasma concentrations of imetelstat and to detect anti-drug antibodies (ADA) to imetelstat. Imetelstat plasma concentrations were determined using a hybridization enzyme-linked immunosorbent assay (lower limit of quantification of 0.367
L468: \mu\text{g}/\text{mL} to 0.588 \mu\text{g}/\text{mL}). ADA against imetelstat were assayed in a three-tiered approach: 1) a screening assay which detected anti-imetelstat antibodies; 2) a confirmatory competition assay to assess the specificity of initial positive screening results; and 3) a titration assay for confirmed positive results to obtain semi-quantitative results for titers of detected ADA.

Exposure and Variability	
The geometric mean (CV%) C_{\text{max}} is 89.2 \mu\text{g}/\text{mL} (27.2%) and \text{AUC}_{0-28\text{d}} is 554 \text{ h}\cdot\mu\text{g}/\text{mL} (43.4%) for subjects with MDS receiving 7.1 \text{ mg}/\text{kg} imetelstat, based on derived PK parameters from the popPK model (Study MDS3001).

Dose Proportionality	
C_{\text{max}} and \text{AUC}_{0-24\text{h}} increased more than dose proportionally over the range of 0.4 to 11.0 \text{ mg}/\text{kg} in subjects with solid tumors and hematologic malignancies (Study CP05-101 and CP14B015) and 19 to 226 \text{ mg}/\text{m}^2 in subjects with CLD (Study CP04-151).

Accumulation	
No appreciable accumulation is observed in end of infusion (EOI) concentrations after multiple treatment cycles with q4w (Study MDS3001), q3w (MYF2001), or qw dosing frequency (Study CP14B015).

Absorption
No absorption process is necessary for imetelstat to reach systemic circulation due to administration as an IV infusion.

T_{\text{max}}	
Observed near EOI for 2 h infusions in subjects with MDS (Study MDS3001).

Distribution
Protein Binding	
Imetelstat is 94% bound to human plasma proteins (Study GP05-007; Section 5.4).

Organ Distribution	
Imetelstat rapidly distributes from plasma to tissue, with the highest concentrations in the liver and kidney followed by bone marrow and spleen (Study GS03-020, Study [b] (4) -493005; Section 5.4).

Volume of Distribution	
The geometric mean (CV%) V_{\text{ss}} is 11.5 \text{ L} (26.1%) for subjects with MDS receiving 7.1 \text{ mg}/\text{kg} imetelstat, based on derived PK parameters from the popPK model (Study MDS3001).

Metabolism
Primary pathway	
Imetelstat is likely metabolized into smaller fragments by nucleases in tissues with component fragments excreted in urine (Gaus, 1997; Griffey, 1997; Crooke, 2000; Geary, 2001; Yu, 2007; Geary, 2009). Hepatic CYP enzymes are not expected to metabolize imetelstat (Geary, 2009; FDA, 2022).
L469: Elimination and Excretion
Half-life	The geometric mean (%CV) apparent t_{1/2} is 4.9 h (45.5%) for subjects with MDS receiving 7.1 mg/kg, based on derived PK parameters from the popPK model (Study MDS3001).
Clearance	For subjects with MDS receiving 7.1 mg/kg imetelstat, clearance (CL) was estimated to be 0.97 L/h from the popPK analysis (Study MDS3001).
Excretion Pathways	Nonclinical studies demonstrated that urine is the primary route of elimination of radiolabeled imetelstat, with 62 to 82% recovery in urine and 12 to 21% recovery in feces by 168 h post-dose (Study [b] (4) -493005, Study GS03-020; Section 5.4). Unchanged imetelstat is not detected in urine of rats administered radiolabeled imetelstat (Study [b] (4) -493007; Section 5.4).
Dosing Considerations
Intrinsic Factors	Based on popPK analysis, there are no clinically meaningful impacts of age, sex, race, mild or moderate hepatic impairment, or mild or moderate renal impairment on imetelstat PK (Section 6.3.2.3). Therefore, no dose adjustments are warranted for these intrinsic factors. Body weight with theory-based allometric exponents was included as a covariate on imetelstat in the popPK analysis supporting the use of body weight-based dosing for imetelstat (Section 6.3.2.3).
Extrinsic Factors	Imetelstat is not expected to be impacted by food-drug or victim-based drug-drug interactions (Section 6.3.2.4). Imetelstat was evaluated as a perpetrator of drug-drug interactions in vitro. Imetelstat does not induce CYP enzymes, inhibit CYP enzymes, or inhibit P-glycoprotein (P-gp). Imetelstat was found to inhibit the uridine diphospho-glucuronate-glucuronosyltransferase (UGT) 1A1 enzyme and organic anion transporting polypeptide (OATP) 1B1 and OATP1B3 transporters in vitro. Based on an integrated assessment of clinical PK characteristics and safety data, imetelstat is unlikely to cause clinically meaningful drug-drug interactions (Section 6.3.2.4). Therefore, no dose adjustments are warranted for food-drug or drug-drug interactions.
Proarrhythmic Potential
hERG Inhibition	Imetelstat does not inhibit the hERG channel at concentrations up to 140x unbound C_{max} in subjects with MDS (Study PRE-14-002; Section 5.3.3).
Nonclinical QT Study	In a nonclinical safety pharmacology study in monkeys, there were no treatment-related clinical signs including effects on CNS, mean
L470: arterial pressure, heart rate, respiration rate, body temperature, electrocardiogram (ECG) activity, or blood gas parameters at imetelstat plasma concentrations >2.6 \times C_{max} in subjects with MDS (Study GS04-072; Section 5.3.3).
Clinical QT Study	A clinical ventricular repolarization/QTc substudy is ongoing in subjects with MDS under Study MDS3001. Analyses of ECG parameters and the relationship between imetelstat exposure and the QT/QTc interval will be conducted. Results will be provided upon completion of the study with an integrated risk assessment of nonclinical and clinical data to fully characterize the proarrhythmic potential of imetelstat.
Cardiac Safety	Based on review of clinical adverse events of ECG QT prolonged and associated cardiac events across imetelstat-monotherapy treated subjects with hematologic malignancies, no signal of cardiac toxicity with imetelstat treatment has been identified.
Immunogenicity
Incidence	ADA against imetelstat developed at a generally low frequency in subjects with MDS (16.9%) and MF (20.6%) (Study MDS3001, Study MYF2001). No subjects had pre-existing immunogenicity to imetelstat that was boosted by treatment, and all ADA positive subjects exhibited a treatment-induced ADA response. The median time to onset of ADA was late in treatment at approximately 25 weeks in MF and 38 weeks in MDS (after 8 treatment cycles in each study), and the median peak titer of ADA for subjects with ADA positivity was low in both studies (median titer values of 30 for MDS and 60 for MF).
Clinical Impact	There was no discernable effect of ADA on imetelstat PK (Applicant Figure 47), safety, or efficacy. ADA do not impact the overall benefit/risk profile of imetelstat.
L471: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L472: The FDA generally agrees with the Applicant on the clinical pharmacology properties of imetelstat with caveats.
L473: - No clinical ADME studies were conducted with imetelstat. The statement that "Imetelstat is likely metabolized into smaller fragments by nucleases" is supported by the available nonclinical data on imetelstat, no PK differences in the limited number of patients with severe renal impairment or hepatic impairment in the imetelstat clinical studies, and literature reports on drugs with similar molecular structures.
- The QT-study report is pending and remains subject to full review upon receipt.
L474: Version date: June 2022 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)
L475: Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as "Data" and "The Applicant's Position" are completed by the Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.
L476: - The use of telomerase activity (TA) as a biomarker for its effect on the treatment of transfusion dependent anemia may not be meaningful. The correlation between TA and TI have not been systemically evaluated throughout the development program. The Applicant claims that supportive evidence of effectiveness comes from \ge 50\% telomerase activity inhibition being observed in 60% (21/35) of patients in the randomized treatment arm of Study MDS3001. However, this was also observed in 37.5% (9/24) of patients in the randomized placebo arm and not maintained in the Part 1 portion of Study MDS3001 (43% or 9/21). In addition, the median reduction in telomerase activity in the placebo group was similar to those reported in the Part 1 portion of Study MDS3001 or at the higher dose levels in the early phase studies in solid tumors. The use of TA data is therefore considered as exploratory and may not a meaningful biomarker to support dose selection for TI.
- Imetelstat is an inhibitor of OATP1B1 and OATP1B3, but does not present a high risk of clinically meaningful DDIs with substrates of these transporters due to the short half-life of imetelstat in conjunction with the once monthly dosing interval (refer to Section 6.3.2.4).
L477: ## 6.3.2. Clinical Pharmacology Questions
L478: ## 6.3.2.1. Does the clinical pharmacology program provide supportive evidence of effectiveness?
L479: ## Data:
L480: Exposure-response analyses for efficacy explored the relationship between the primary efficacy endpoint, proportion of subjects achieving \ge 8-week TI, and the key secondary endpoint, proportion of subjects achieving \ge 24-week TI, and imetelstat exposure. The analyses utilized efficacy data from the target population [non-del(5q) karyotype and without prior treatment with either hypomethylating agents or lenalidomide] in Phase 2 and Phase 3 of Study MDS3001 alone (Section 19.4.2.2).
L481: In Study MDS3001, E-R analyses showed a statistically significant positive exposure-efficacy relationship between the proportion of subjects who met the primary efficacy endpoint of \ge 8-week TI and imetelstat average concentration up to the time of the event (C_{avg}) (Applicant Figure 50). In Study MDS3001, E-R analyses showed a statistically significant positive exposure-efficacy relationship between the proportion of subjects who met the key secondary efficacy endpoint of \ge 24-week TI and C_{avg} (Applicant Figure 51).
L482: ## The Applicant's Position:
L483: Based on totality of data, the Applicant believes the imetelstat clinical pharmacology program together with the efficacy results from Study MDS3001 provides sufficient evidence of effectiveness at the intended registration dosing regimen of 7.1 mg/kg q4w. The results from
L484: exposure-efficacy analyses suggest that imetelstat doses (and accordingly C_{avg}) lower than 7.1 mg/kg q4w may result in reduced clinical efficacy.
L485: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L486: The clinical pharmacology analyses provided supportive evidence of effectiveness, but the analyses are associated with notable limitations.
L487: - Despite meeting the primary efficacy endpoint in Study MDS3001, essentially only one dose level has been evaluated in the LR-MDS patient population. Limited dose finding in the target patient population from the pivotal study and frequent dose modifications introduces confounding factors that limited the interpretation of exposure-response analyses results on the efficacy endpoint. It is noted that, if the E-R relationship for efficacy could be further validated with additional clinical data, the Applicant could consider alternative dosages with lower doses (e.g., &lt;7.1 mg/kg) and an alternative dosing frequency (e.g., more frequent than Q4W) to explore the possibility of maintaining clinical efficacy while reducing toxicity.
- The early phase studies used overall response as the clinical endpoint, and TA inhibition was used to support dose selection in the early clinical development, using data from mainly patients with myelofibrosis. Overall, this data was used to demonstrate that imetelstat doses lower than 7.1 mg/kg would not achieve sufficient telomerase activity inhibition for response. Changing efficacy endpoints from overall response in early phase studies to 8-week RBC TI in the pivotal study resulted in interpretability issues with dose selection from the early phase studies as a correlation between overall response and RBC TI has not been established.
- Hematology labs were not consistently collected in the early phase studies that evaluated multiple dosages in solid tumors, myelofibrosis, and other diseases, and a lack of time course data in hematology labs hinders analysis of a dose-response relationship at alternative dosages that are pertinent to MDS.
L488: ## **6.3.2.2. Is the proposed dosing regimen appropriate for the general patient population for which the indication is being sought?**
L489: ## **Data:**
L490: Justification of the 7.1 mg/kg q4w dosing regimen is supported by the observed clinical efficacy and safety results from the pivotal, randomized placebo-controlled Phase 3 study MDS3001, and the supportive MDS3001 Phase 2 study, the cumulative available data on imetelstat clinical PK and target engagement, and results from comprehensive E-R analyses, along with supportive nonclinical studies. The key rationale for the imetelstat dosing regimen in patients with LR MDS is as follows:
L491: - Efficacy results from the Phase 3 of Study MDS3001 confirmed the treatment benefit of imetelstat demonstrating superiority of imetelstat 7.1 mg/kg q4w over placebo in the LR MDS population.Clinical dose intensity analysis demonstrated that &gt;75% of subjects received 7.1 mg/kg q4w at the time of onset of 8-week TI, and for subjects treated with imetelstat in the Phase 3 study, mean relative dose intensity was &gt;95% and &gt;93% in the period of time leading up to the achievement of 8-week TI and 24-week TI, respectively.
- Safety results from the Phase 3 of Study MDS3001 demonstrated a manageable safety profile that is consistent with Phase 2 and the imetelstat clinical program.Dose intensity analyses showed that &gt;90% mean relative dose intensity with respect to the 7.1 mg/kg q4w dosing regimen was achieved over the treatment period for all subjects, despite dose modifications which occurred as mandated per protocol.
- While neutropenia and thrombocytopenia are frequent with imetelstat treatment, these cytopenias were largely based on changes in laboratory values and were transient, with the majority of subjects recovering within the 4-week dosing schedule. Of clinical importance, severe infections or bleeding occur at low rates that are similar to those in the placebo arm.
- E-R analyses from the Phase 3 of Study MDS3001 support the imetelstat 7.1 mg/kg q4w dose and dosing regimen.Exposure-efficacy analysis demonstrated a statistically significant positive E-R relationship for 8-week TI rate and 24-week TI rate.
- Exposure-safety analysis demonstrated no significant E-R relationship for Grade 3/4 neutropenia and a positive E-R relationship for Grade 3/4 thrombocytopenia (statistically significant for C_{max} and approaching significance for AUC).Investigations of toxicity in identified subpopulations in MDS3001 Phase 2 and Phase 3 with potentially higher imetelstat exposure showed no clear trends between incidence of Grade 3/4 thrombocytopenia and increasing body weight quartiles or between sex. These analyses support that these subpopulations are not expected to be at increased risk of Grade 3/4 thrombocytopenia, and along with low rates of high-grade bleeding events in MDS patients in Study MDS3001, support the limited clinical impact of the characterized exposure-safety relationships.
- E-R analyses showed no significant E-R relationship between subjects recovering from Grade 3/4 neutropenia or thrombocytopenia and imetelstat exposure in MDS3001 Phase 2 and Phase 3 with more subjects recovering within 4 weeks compared to 2 weeks, supporting the 4-week schedule.
L492: - Finally, the popPK analysis incorporated body weight with theory-based allometric exponents as a covariate contributing to imetelstat PK variability, supporting the imetelstat body weight-based dosing approach.
L493: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L494: The intended registration dosing regimen of 7.1 mg/kg q4w for imetelstat is appropriate for patients with LR MDS. This is supported by the clinical efficacy, safety, PK, and E-R relationships along with the supportive nonclinical data demonstrating a favorable benefit/risk profile in the LR MDS population.
L495: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L496: Given the high rates of grade 3 AEs and dose interruptions and modifications, it is unclear whether a lower dose or an alternative regimen may provide similar efficacy with reduced rates of AEs or dose modifications. Consequently, further dose optimization is warranted.
L497: - There is limited dose exploration in patients with LR-MDS. While imetelstat doses of 0.4 mg/kg to 11.7 mg/kg, alone or in combination with other agents, and dosing schedules from weekly to every 4 weeks have been explored in a variety of indications throughout the drug development program, dosages lower than 7.1 mg/kg Q4W have not been explored in the context of MDS.
- There was a high rate of dose modifications with imetelstat compared to placebo in Study MDS3001, as shown in Table 11 and Figure 3 below. The reviewers noted that high rates of dose reduction and dose modification indicate safety and tolerability issues at the proposed dosing regimen, whereas dose intensity may not be a sensitive indicator for safety concerns (e.g., a treatment that results in rapid dose modification or a calculation using an early cutoff date can yield high dose intensity despite of high dose modification rate).

L503: - There are high rates of Grade 3/4 TEAEs, particularly thrombocytopenia and neutropenia, in the imetelstat arm in Study MDS3011. The E-R analysis indicates that higher imetelstat Cycle 1 Cmax is associated with higher incidence of Grade 3/4 thrombocytopenia in patients with LR-MDS patients (Figure 4). The positive E-R relationships for Grade 3/4 thrombocytopenia is also observed in the pooled analysis in pooled datasets including data from other studies. This relationship can also be verified through interventions, where 18% of patients receiving imetelstat required platelet transfusions on treatment (median: 1 transfusion, range: 1 to 10) compared to 3% of patients receiving placebo. This finding suggests that the starting dose and regimen of imetelstat are too high and may be further optimized.
L504: **Figure 4:** MDS3001-Phase 3: Positive exposure-response relationship between imetelstat exposure and Grade 3-4 thrombocytopenia
L505: Source: FDA analysis using ER_THR.csv dataset; Data cutoff 13 October 2022
L506: ## 6.3.2.3. Is an alternative dosing regimen or management strategy required for subpopulations based on intrinsic patient factors (e.g., race, ethnicity, age, performance status, genetic subpopulations, etc.)?
L507: ## Data:
L508: ## Age
L509: The popPK analysis included subjects ranging from 21 to 87 years (median 67 years). No effect of age was identified on imetelstat PK (Section 19.4.1).
L510: ## Sex
L511: PopPK analysis identified sex as a significant covariate on imetelstat CL with a limited effect on estimated C_{max} and AUC for females relative to males (increase of \le 30\%) (Section 19.4.1, Applicant Figure 48). Overall, the effect of sex on imetelstat exposure is not expected to be clinically relevant as the size of the effect is within the between-subject variability in imetelstat PK. Supportive of this, there is no evidence of increased toxicity (Grade 3/4 neutropenia or thrombocytopenia) in females in Study MDS3001 (Section 19.4.2.4).
L512: ## Race
L513: There was no effect of race on imetelstat PK identified in the popPK analysis, for which race was categorized as White/Caucasian versus all others (Black/African American, Asian, Other) based on the distribution of data (80.7% (342/424) of subjects were White; Section 19.4.1). The
L514: Version date: June 2022 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)
L515: Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “Data” and “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.
L516: observed distribution of race in the popPK dataset reflects the overall incidence of MDS, which is most prevalent in individuals who are white (Neukirchen, 2011; Ma, 2012; Visser, 2012).
L517: ## *Body Weight*
L518: Body weight with theory-based allometric exponents was included as a covariate on imetelstat V_c, CL, and transfer rate from the central to the peripheral compartment (K_{back}) in the popPK analysis, which included subjects ranging from 44 to 161 kg (median 75 kg). Using body-weight-based dosing, there is limited exposure variation expected for subjects with low body weight (52 kg) or high body weight (107 kg) compared to a reference subject of 70 kg (Section 19.4.1, Applicant Figure 48).
L519: ## *Hepatic Impairment*
L520: The effect of hepatic impairment on imetelstat PK was assessed in the popPK analysis using National Cancer Institute (NCI)- Organ Dysfunction Working Group (ODWG) hepatic function category, with categories assessed as normal function (N = 256), mild impairment (N = 119), combined moderate or severe impairment (N = 27, including N = 24 with moderate impairment and N = 3 with severe impairment), and missing (N = 22), based on the distribution of data. There was no evidence for differences in imetelstat CL across hepatic function categories (Section 19.4.1, Applicant Figure 45).
L521: ## *Renal Impairment*
L522: The effect of renal impairment on imetelstat PK was assessed in the popPK analysis using categorical renal function and continuous creatinine clearance (CrCL) as covariates. Renal function categories were assessed as normal function (N = 156), mild impairment (N = 147), and combined moderate or severe impairment (N = 121, including N = 115 moderate impairment and N = 6 severe impairment). There was no evidence for differences in imetelstat CL across renal function categories or across CrCL values (Section 19.4.1, Applicant Figure 46).
L523: ## The Applicant's Position:
L524: Given the totality of the data based on results from the imetelstat popPK analysis, together with subgroup analysis for the efficacy and safety, no dose adjustments are required for sex, age, race, mild to moderate hepatic impairment, or mild to moderate renal impairment. There is limited information on the effect of severe hepatic impairment, severe renal impairment, or end-stage renal disease on imetelstat PK. The use of the body-weight-based dosing approach for imetelstat is supported by available clinica data.
L525: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L526: The FDA agrees that no therapeutic individualization of imetelstat is needed based upon intrinsic factors from popPK modeling such as age, sex, race (81% White, 4% Asian, 7% Black, 8% other/unknown), mild to moderate renal impairment (CrCL 30 to &lt; 90 mL/min), mild hepatic
L527: impairment (total bilirubin \le ULN and AST &gt; ULN, or total bilirubin &gt; 1x to 1.5x ULN and any AST), or moderate hepatic impairment (total bilirubin &gt; 1.5x to 3x ULN and any AST). Dedicated PK studies have not been conducted in patients with severe renal impairment (CrCL &lt; 30 mL/min) or severe hepatic impairment (total bilirubin &gt; 3x ULN and any AST). However, 4 patients with severe hepatic impairment and 14 patients with severe renal impairment (creatinine clearance &gt;20 mL/min) were included in the population PK analysis and the analysis does not suggest meaningful differences in PK exposure. Refer to Section 19.4.1.
L528: In the patients with severe renal or hepatic impairment in Study MDS3001, the incidence rate of Grade 3/4 thrombocytopenia was not higher than those in the general population. Considering that imetelstat is not designed to target liver or kidney and is likely broken down into small fragments by nucleases, no additional PK studies in patients with severe renal or hepatic impairment are warranted.
L529: Additionally, no dose adjustment is needed based on SF3B1 mutational status. Refer to Section for 19.4.3 for details.
L530: ## 6.3.2.4. Are there clinically relevant food-drug or drug-drug interactions, and what is the appropriate management strategy?
L531: ## Data:
L532: ## *Food Effect*
L533: Imetelstat is not susceptible to food-drug interactions because it is administered via IV infusion.
L534: ## *Victim Drug-Drug Interactions*
L535: Imetelstat, like other ODNs, is likely metabolized into smaller fragments by nucleases in tissues and the component fragments are excreted in urine (Section 5.4, (Gaus, 1997; Griffey, 1997; Crooke, 2000; Geary, 2001; Yu, 2007; Geary, 2009)). Hepatic CYP enzymes are not expected to metabolize ODNs, and modulation of drug transporters is not expected to impact PK of ODNs (Geary, 2009; FDA, 2022).
L536: ## *Perpetrator Drug-Drug Interactions*
L537: CYP Induction: Imetelstat does not induce the activity of CYP1A2, CYP2A6, CYP2B6, CYP2C9, CYP2C19, CYP2D6, CYP2E1, or CYP3A4 in vitro (Study 303-1160).
L538: CYP Inhibition: Imetelstat causes little to no inhibition of CYP2C8, CYP1A2, CYP2B6, CYP2E1, CYP2C9, CYP2C19, CYP2D6, or CYP2C8 in hepatocytes (Study FK12087, Study FK11110).
L539: Imetelstat does not cause time-dependent inhibition of CYP enzymes (Kazmi, 2019).
L540: UGT Inhibition: Imetelstat inhibits UGT1A1 in human liver microsomes with a R value that exceeds the threshold for further evaluation (Study XT135035). However, this result is likely an artifact of the test system due to nonspecific effects in human liver microsomes (HLMs), as seen
L541: for imetelstat with CYP enzymes in HLMs and as reported for other ODNs for enzyme inhibition assays in this test system (Kazmi, 2018).
L542: **Transporter Inhibition:** Imetelstat does not inhibit P-gp in vitro (Study 18019). Imetelstat inhibits OATP1B1 and OATP1B3 with R values that exceed the threshold for further evaluation (Study XT138038). In vitro studies to assess whether imetelstat inhibits BCRP, BSEP, OAT1, OAT3, OCT1, OCT2, multidrug and toxin extrusion protein (MATE) 1, and MATE2-K are ongoing. **Clinical Evaluations:** Imetelstat is dosed infrequently (q4w) and rapidly cleared from plasma (t_{1/2} of 4.9 hours), there are no clinically relevant elevations in the OATP1B1/OAT1P1B3 and UGT1A1 endogenous substrate bilirubin in imetelstat-treated subjects compared to placebo in MDS, and there is no indication of altered safety profiles when imetelstat is administered with concomitant substrates of OATP1B1/OATP1B3 (statins) or UGT1A1 (deferasirox) in subjects with MDS.
L543: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L544: Consistent with other ODNs, imetelstat has limited risk of clinically relevant DDIs based on nonclinical data and an integrated assessment of PK characteristics and clinical data. Dose adjustments for food-drug, victim-based drug-drug interactions, or perpetrator-based drug-drug interactions are not necessary for imetelstat.
L545: The results of the ongoing in vitro study to assess potential for imetelstat to inhibit additional drug transporters will be provided within 60 days of submission of the NDA.
L546: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L547: The FDA generally agrees with the Applicant that there is a low risk of clinically meaningful DDIs. The study report for XT228134 that evaluated in vitro BCRP, BSEP, OATP1B1, OATP1B3, OAT1, OAT3, OCT1, OCT2, MATE1, and MATE2-K inhibition was submitted on 8/8/2023. The results indicate that imetelstat is an inhibitor of OATP1B1 and OATP1B3, with R values greater than the guidance recommended cutoff value of 1.1. However, the review team agrees that the short half-life of imetelstat in conjunction with the once monthly dosing interval would not produce meaningful DDIs with substrates of these transporters.

L555: ## 7.1. Table of Clinical Studies
L556: ## Data:
L557: The efficacy of imetelstat for patients with LR MDS that is relapsed after, refractory to or ineligible for ESA therapy, is supported by the Phase 2/3 study, MDS3001, using the cutoff date of 13 October 2022, which is one year from the last patient randomized (primary analysis). This application summarizes the key efficacy results from the randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled, Phase 3 Study MDS3001 (pivotal). Efficacy results from the Phase 2 open label portion of Study MDS3001, with a focus on the subset of subjects without the del(5q) cytogenetic abnormality and without prior treatment with either lenalidomide or HMAs (referred to as non-del(5q) and lenalidomide/HMA-naïve; N = 38), are also be provided as supportive evidence of efficacy where relevant.
L558: The overall evaluation of safety summarized in the Summary of Clinical Safety (SCS), presents the safety from the MDS3001 Phase 3 study (Group A), and the combined Phase 2 and Phase 3 LR MDS population (Group B). An additional safety group (Group C), includes pooled data from 5 clinical studies of imetelstat as monotherapy in hematological malignancies, including 4 completed Phase 2 studies and the pivotal MDS3001-Phase 3 study for a total of 450 subjects, of which 391 were treated with imetelstat monotherapy and 59 were treated with placebo as outlined in Applicant Table 41.

L578: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L579: The primary study supporting the evaluation of efficacy in this application is the MDS3001-Phase 3 study, which compared the efficacy of imetelstat at the 7.1 mg/kg q4w dose (equivalent to 7.5 mg/kg imetelstat-sodium) with placebo in terms of RBC TI and alleviation of anemia. The Phase 3 MDS3001 study is supported by the Phase 2 MDS3001 study and a high degree of consistency was observed for the efficacy and safety data across both studies. A detailed description of the results is provided in the sections below.
L580: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L581: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position that the primary study supporting the evaluation of efficacy in this application is the MDS3001-Phase 3 study. The MDS3001-Phase 2 study is considered supportive. Other studies that examine the use of imetelstat in conditions other than MDS and doses other than that used in MDS3001 are used as supportive data when assessing safety.
L582: ## 8 Statistical and Clinical Evaluation
L583: ## 8.1. Review of Relevant Individual Trials Used to Support Efficacy
L584: ## 8.1.1. **Pivotal Study in Support of Efficacy of Imetelstat – Study MDS3001**
L585: ## Trial Design
L586: ## **The Applicant's Description:**
L587: Study MDS3001 is a global 2-part, multicenter, Phase 2/3 study of imetelstat in transfusion-dependent subjects with International Prognostic Scoring System (IPSS) low or intermediate-1 risk MDS that is relapsed/refractory to or ineligible for ESA treatment. Part 2 (hereafter referred to as MDS3001-Phase 3 study) is a Phase 3 double-blind, randomized study to compare the efficacy of imetelstat with placebo (Applicant Figure 5) and provides the pivotal efficacy and safety data for this NDA submission. Part 1 (hereafter referred to as MDS3001-Phase 2 study) is a Phase 2, open-label, single-arm study to assess the efficacy and safety of imetelstat and provides strongly supportive data to Phase 3.
L588: ## **Applicant Figure 5: Schematic Overview of the Study**
L589: a. Supportive care, including transfusions or myeloid growth factors, is to be administered as needed per investigator discretion and according to local standard practices.
L590: b. Dose based on active moiety (free acid), which is equivalent to 7.5 mg/kg imetelstat-sodium.
L591: The primary objective of the Phase 3 study was to compare the efficacy, in terms of RBC TI, of imetelstat to placebo. Key secondary objectives included safety, time to and duration of RBC TI, \ge 24-week TI, and rate of hematologic improvement.
L592: The choice of placebo as a comparator, randomized study design and primary endpoint for Phase 3 was discussed and agreed upon with FDA as outlined in Section 3.
L593: ## **Choice of placebo control:**
L594: Blinded placebo-controlled study design provides the best opportunity for a rigorous demonstration of efficacy and safety, with the least potential for bias. At the time of study initiation there was no standard of care for transfusion-dependent LR-MDS patients who were relapsed or refractory to ESAs. The use of placebo control was also consistent with other ongoing Phase 3 studies at that time in subjects with LR MDS, including studies investigating the safety and efficacy of luspatercept, lenalidomide, and oral azacitidine. To ensure integrity of the blind, imetelstat and placebo were provided to the site in matching vials with blinded labels, had similar reconstitution characteristics, were administered IV in same way, and required premedication. Subjects in both treatment groups received supportive care in addition to study drug.
L595: ## **Randomization and stratification:**
L596: Randomization and stratification were used in the Phase 3 study to minimize bias in the assignment of subjects to treatment groups (per FDA guidance for studies where evidence of clinical benefit using symptom endpoints are assessed), to increase the likelihood that subject attributes (e.g., demographic and baseline characteristics) are evenly balanced across treatment groups, and to enhance the validity of statistical comparisons across treatment groups. The 2:1 randomization scheme was chosen to limit the number of subjects in the placebo group, to increase the likelihood of subjects receiving active treatment with imetelstat, and to study the safety of imetelstat in a larger cohort.
L597: ## **Choice of Efficacy Endpoints**
L598: The primary endpoint was achievement of \ge 8-week TI and achieving \ge 24-week TI was a key secondary endpoint. Transfusion independence is a relevant measure of efficacy in LR MDS as it targets the most common symptom of the disease (transfusion-dependent anemia) and as such indicates clinical benefit in this patient group. The use of TI as a primary endpoint was discussed and agreed with FDA during Type C meeting in 2017 (see Section 3) and in accordance with feedback from FDA, comprehensive transfusion history for patients was collected pre-study (up to 16 weeks) to establish baseline transfusion burden and actively assessed and documented in the electronic Case Report Form (eCRF) at every study visit. In addition, intense data monitoring at the clinical sites was implemented to ensure the rigor of the data, and the process for
L599: handling missing data was prespecified in the MDS3001 statistical analysis plan (SAP) which was submitted to FDA. Transfusion independence has also formed the basis of approval for other products in similar lower risk MDS disease settings.
L600: Achievement of hematologic improvement-erythroid (HI-E), including reduction in transfusion burden, and cytogenetic response are established endpoints in LR MDS per International Working Group (IWG) response criteria. Decrease of mutation burden and improvement in measures of fatigue by Functional Assessment of Chronic Illness Therapy (FACIT)-Fatigue questionnaire may be considered additional measures of efficacy in LR MDS.
L601: Safety assessments for the MDS3001 Phase 2 and Phase 3 studies include incidence, intensity, and type of adverse events (AEs), vital signs measurements, clinical laboratory values, ECG changes, and deaths, all of which are standard endpoints for evaluating the safety of new treatments.
L602: ## Trial Location
L603: Study MDS3001 was conducted globally in 17 countries in North America, European Union (EU) and Rest of World (ROW).
L604: ## Dose Selection
L605: In the clinical study reports (CSRs), study drug doses are expressed as milligrams of imetelstat sodium (the active substance), a salt of imetelstat. To convert a quantity of imetelstat salt to the equivalent quantity of imetelstat, a factor of 0.9416 based on relative molar mass of the free acid form (4610 g/mol) to the sodium salt (4896 g/mol; 4610/4896 = 0.9416) is applied (ie, 1 mg imetelstat sodium = 0.9416 mg imetelstat).
L606: Data from nonclinical, PK, pharmacodynamic, and clinical studies across the imetelstat clinical program were utilized to inform the MDS dosing schedule.
L607: In the Phase 2 pilot study of imetelstat in MF and other myeloid malignancies (Study CP14B019), subjects with MDS or MDS/ myeloproliferative neoplasms (MPN) overlap received a starting imetelstat dose of 7.1 mg/kg imetelstat (equivalent to 7.5 mg/kg imetelstat-sodium) every 4 weeks. Subsequent doses were adjusted based on investigator assessment of toxicity and clinical response, resulting in individualized regimens that included 5.6 mg/kg imetelstat (equivalent to (6.0 mg/kg imetelstat-sodium), 7.1 mg/kg and 8.9 mg/kg imetelstat (equivalent to (9.4 mg/kg imetelstat-sodium) every 4 weeks. Prolonged thrombocytopenia or neutropenia were not observed in the MDS cohort.
L608: In addition, the PK/pharmacodynamic relationship across the imetelstat clinical program suggested that a dose of 7.1 mg/kg provides optimal target engagement. In the Phase 2 study MYF2001 in MF, PK exposure was correlated with pharmacodynamic effect, so that a higher
L609: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 217779 imetelstat
## TRADENAME, imetelstat
L610: percentage of subjects in the highest exposure quartile for imetelstat achieved optimal pharmacodynamic effect (\ge 50\% reduction in TA or hTERT) compared to subjects in the lower exposure quartiles, indicating exposure-dependent on-target activity of imetelstat. In that study, \ge 50\% reduction in TA or hTERT by imetelstat correlated with clinical benefits, including OS (Mascarenhas, 2021).
L611: The estimated TA change at median C_{max} of 7.1 mg/kg in Study MDS3001 Phase 2 was similar to that obtained in the 8.9 mg/kg dose arm of Study MYF2001, indicating optimal target engagement.
L612: This was consistent with the observed clinical benefit/risk profile in study MYF2001 and the Phase 2 study MDS3001, where data showed that greatest benefit, including TI, was achieved during treatment with 7.1 mg/kg before any dose modifications or dose escalation. The safety profile was acceptable and manageable and the observed Grade 3/4 cytopenias were reversible to Grade 2 or less within 4 weeks.
L613: Based on the available data, the proposed starting dose of 7.1mg/kg imetelstat every 4 weeks in the Phase 3 MDS3001 study was discussed and agreed with FDA as part of the Type C meeting in June 2017 and subsequent interactions.
L614: ## Treatment Assignment
L615: Subjects were randomly assigned 2:1 to receive either imetelstat or placebo. Randomization was based on a computer-generated randomization schedule prepared before the study by or under the supervision of the Sponsor. Randomization was stratified by prior RBC transfusion burden (\le 6 or &gt; 6 units RBC) and by IPSS risk group (low versus intermediate-1). Prior RBC transfusion burden is defined as the maximum number of RBC units transfused over an 8-week period during the 16 weeks prior to randomization. Pre-transfusion Hb should have been \le 9.0 g/dL to count toward the 4 units total unless there was a clinical rationale for transfusing at a higher Hb level.
L616: ## Blinding
L617: Geron personnel with direct management of the study sites (with the exception of Geron Clinical Drug Supply Management and Geron Pharmacovigilance Team), the Geron and contract research organization (CRO) study teams, the Investigator, the study site personnel, and the subject remained blinded to each patient's treatment. The investigator was not provided with randomization codes. The codes were maintained within the Interactive Web Response System, which had the functionality to allow the investigator to break the blind for an individual subject. Data that had the potential to unblind the treatment assignment (ie, study drug plasma concentrations) were handled with special care to ensure that the integrity of the blind was maintained and the potential for bias minimized. To that end, imetelstat and placebo were
L618: ## TRADENAME, imetelstat
L619: provided to the site in matching vials with blinded labels, had similar reconstitution characteristics, were administered IV in same way and both required pre-medication.
L620: ## Dose Modifications
L621: Study drug dose delay or reduction for Grade 3 or Grade 4 toxicities observed at the time of the planned dose of the next cycle was instituted as needed, as described below. These instructions were not applicable when toxicities occurred mid-cycle and subsequently resolved to Grade &lt; 3 by the time of the next planned dose. Imetelstat could have been held for up to 28 days from the expected start date of the scheduled cycle; a hold &gt; 28 days had to be reviewed and approved by the Sponsor.
L622: The starting dose was 7.1mg/kg of imetelstat (equivalent to 7.5 mg/kg of imetelstat sodium) IV every 4 weeks. Subjects were allowed 2 dose reductions to 5.6mg/kg (equivalent to 6.0 mg/kg imetelstat sodium) q4w and 4.4mg/kg (equivalent to 4.7 mg/kg imetelstat sodium) q4w. All dose modifications were recorded in the Dosage Administration eCRF.
L623: ## Premedication
L624: All subjects who received an infusion of study drug had to be premedicated with an antihistamine (diphenhydramine 25 to 50 mg or equivalent) and a corticosteroid (hydrocortisone 100 to 200 mg or equivalent), either IV or orally. Premedications were administered per local standard of care approximately 1 hour prior to study drug infusion. If per investigator's discretion premedication(s) was/were contraindicated for a subject, modifications were to be reviewed and approved by the Sponsor's medical monitor. It was recommended that subjects be monitored for at least 1 hour after the infusion was completed.
L625: ## Concomitant Medications
L626: Subjects received supportive care as clinically indicated. This included blood product support (transfusions and use of myeloid growth factors [eg, granulocyte-colony stimulating factor], to be administered as needed per investigator discretion and according to local standard practices), iron chelation therapy, anti-diarrheals, anti-emetics, analgesics, antibiotic treatment, and treatment of other medical conditions.
L627: The following medications were prohibited during the study: ESA therapy, chemotherapy (anti-cancer/hypomethylating agents) including any anti-MDS therapy, immunomodulatory drugs (eg, lenalidomide), immunotherapy, experimental therapy, and radiotherapy. Systemic use of corticosteroids in excess of prednisone 20 mg/day or its equivalent for more than 10 days was prohibited unless reviewed and approved by the Sponsor's medical monitor. Long-term, chronic use of corticosteroids at any dose was also to be reviewed and approved by the Sponsor's medical monitor. Corticosteroids used as premedication were permitted.
L628: ## Procedures and Evaluations
L629: The Phase 3 study consisted of 3 phases: a Screening Phase of up to 28 days during which subject eligibility was reviewed and approved by the Sponsor prior to randomization; a
L630: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 217779 imetelstat
## TRADENAME, imetelstat
L631: Treatment Phase that extended from Cycle I 1 Day (D) 1 until disease progression or unacceptable toxicity, or withdrawal of consent; and a Post-treatment Follow-up Phase which continues until death, loss to follow-up, withdrawal of consent, or the End of the Study (whichever occurs first). The End of the Study is defined as 2 years after the randomization of the last subject in the main Phase 3 study or anytime the Sponsor terminates the study, whichever comes first. In addition, subjects are continuing in survival follow-up as part of an extension phase, until 5 years from the first dose of imetelstat or after 3 years of post-treatment follow-up (MDS3001 Protocol Amendment 8, Section 9.1.6).
L632: Efficacy was evaluated by the proportion of subjects achieving RBC TI lasting at least any consecutive 8-week and 24-week period, starting from Study Day 1 until subsequent anti-cancer therapy, if any. Study Day 1 for Phase 3 was defined as the day of randomization. Efficacy evaluation also included time to the 8-week or 24-week RBC TI, duration of RBC TI, rate of HI-E per modified IWG 2006 and revised IWG 2018, rates of CR, PR, or mCR per modified IWG 2006, OS, PFS, time to progression to AML, amount and relative change in units of RBC transfusions, rate of myeloid growth factors usage, and medical resource utilization. Exploratory efficacy variables included measurements of telomere length (TL), TA and hTERT, cytogenetic and mutation status, and PROs. Pharmacokinetic parameters and immunogenicity of imetelstat were also assessed.
L633: Safety was evaluated by analysis of the incidence, intensity, and type of treatment-emergent adverse events (TEAEs), vital signs measurements, clinical laboratory values, ECGs, and deaths.
L634: For the Phase 3 study an Independent Review Committee (IRC) was established to adjudicate investigator-assessed disease response (CR, PR, mCR, or cytogenetic response) and response (CR, PR, mCR, or cytogenetic response) for subjects with &gt; 5% baseline bone marrow blasts and/ or cytogenetic abnormalities at baseline based on the central pathology reviewer's assessment. The IRC assessed response based on the modified IWG Response Criteria 2006 for MDS (Cheson, 2006).
L635: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L636: To support the marketing application for imetelstat for the proposed indication, the Applicant submitted the results of Study 63935937MDS3001 (MDS3001, IMerge). The study was comprised of two parts. Part 1 was a single-arm, open-label, Phase 2 trial. Part 2 was a randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled, Phase 3 trial. The results of Phase 3 provide the primary basis for the Applicant's claim of efficacy for imetelstat, with the results of Phase 2 considered supportive. The study design is detailed by the Applicant above.
L637: In Phase 3, a total of 178 eligible subjects were randomized in a 2:1 ratio to receive either imetelstat or placebo. Randomization was stratified by prior RBC transfusion burden (\le 6 or &gt; 6
L638: units RBC over an 8-week period during the 16 weeks prior to randomization) and by IPSS risk group (low risk versus intermediate-1 risk).
L639: The primary endpoint was 8-week RBC transfusion independence (RBC-TI), defined as the proportion of subjects without any RBC transfusion during any consecutive 8 weeks starting from Study Day 1 until subsequent anti-cancer therapy, if any. If subsequent anti-cancer therapy was not reported, the end date of a transfusion-independent interval was determined by either an RBC transfusion or the last transfusion follow-up status date. The study was designed to have 88% power to detect a 22.5% difference in 8-week RBC-TI (30% versus 7.5%) between imetelstat and placebo with a 2-sided alpha of 0.05. Notably, there was no specific hemoglobin threshold for transfusion pre-specified in the protocol. Supportive care, including transfusions and myeloid growth factors, could be administered as needed per investigator discretion and according to local standard practices.
L640: Per statistical analysis plan (SAP), the secondary endpoints included in the hypothesis testing with overall Type I error rate 0.05 (2-sided): 24-week RBC TI, Rate of HI-E per modified IWG 2006, progression-free survival (PFS), overall survival (OS). Other secondary endpoints included duration of 8-week RBC-TI; rates of complete remission (CR), partial remission (PR), or marrow complete remission (mCR); time to AML progression; rate and duration of myeloid growth factor usage; and medical resource utilization. Exploratory endpoints included changes in mutational burden and patient reported outcomes, among others.
L641: ## *Clinical Reviewer comment:*
L642: *The choice of placebo control as a comparator, randomized study design, and primary endpoint for Phase 3 was discussed with the FDA prior to trial initiation as noted by the Applicant above. However, it should be noted that the FDA advised the Applicant that randomization to imetelstat versus physician's choice would be ideal, though a blinded placebo-controlled study offers the least potential for introducing bias. Additionally, the choice of 8-week RBC-TI as a primary endpoint was indeed discussed during a Type C meeting in 2016 (not 2017 as stated above). However, the FDA noted at the time that whether a positive result for this endpoint is supportive of registration will depend on the risk-benefit analysis at the time of review and that if the safety profile outweighs durable duration of TI, this would not be viewed favorably.*
L643: *Furthermore, it should be noted that the FDA does not consider HI-E, cytogenetic response, or decrease of mutation burden to be established measures of efficacy in lower-risk MDS at this time. There is also inadequate evidence of content validity for the FACIT-Fatigue questionnaire to support regulatory decision-making at this time.*
L644: ## Eligibility Criteria
L645: ## The Applicant's Description:
L646: ## Key inclusion criteria:
L647: - Myelodysplastic syndromes according to World Health Organization (WHO) 2008 criteria
- IPSS low- or intermediate-1 risk MDS
- Red blood cell transfusion dependent defined as requiring at least 4 RBC units transfused over an 8-week period during the 16 weeks prior to Cycle 1 Day 1 (Phase 2) or Randomization (Phase 3); pre-transfusion Hgb should be \le 9.0\text{g/dL} to count toward the 4 units total
- Relapsed/refractory to ESA treatment, as defined by meeting any one of the criteria below:Received at least 8 weeks of treatment with a minimum weekly dose of epoetin alfa 40,000 U, epoetin beta 30,000 U, or darbepoetin alfa 150 \mu\text{g} (or equivalent agent/dose), without having achieved a Hgb rise \ge 1.5\text{ g/dL} or decreased RBC transfusion requirement by at least 4 units over 8 weeks
- Transfusion dependence or reduction in Hgb by \ge 1.5\text{ g/dL} after hematologic improvement, in the absence of another explanation
- Endogenous serum erythropoietin (EPO) level &gt;500\text{ mU/mL}
- Hematology lab test values within the following limits:Absolute neutrophil count (ANC) \ge 1.5 \times 10^9/\text{L} independent of growth factor support
- Platelets \ge 75 \times 10^9/\text{L} independent of platelet transfusion
- Biochemical laboratory test values must be within the following limits:Aspartate aminotransferase (AST), alanine aminotransferase (ALT) and alkaline phosphatase (ALP) \le 2.5 times the upper limit of normal (ULN)
- Serum creatinine \le 2.0 \times \text{ULN}
- Total bilirubin \le 3 \times \text{ULN} and direct bilirubin \le 2 \times \text{ULN} (unless due to Gilbert's syndrome, ineffective erythropoiesis due to MDS, or hemolysis due to RBC transfusion)
L648: ## Key exclusion criteria:
L649: - Prior treatment with a hypomethylating agent (eg, azacitidine, decitabine), lenalidomide, thalidomide, or other thalidomide analogues (Phase 3 specific)
- Subject with del(5q) karyotype (Phase 3 specific)
- Subject with MDS/MPN overlap syndrome
- Subject had received an experimental or investigational drug or had used an invasive investigational medical device within 30 days prior to randomization or was currently enrolled in an investigational study
L650: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L651: Eligible patients were adults with the following key disease-related eligibility requirements, as noted by the Applicant above:
L652: - Diagnosis of MDS according to the World Health Organization (WHO) 2016 criteria
- Low or intermediate-1 risk by IPSS
- RBC transfusion dependent, defined as requiring at least 4 RBC units transfused over an 8-week period during the 16 weeks prior to randomization; pre-transfusion Hgb was required to be \le 9.0 g/dL to count towards the 4 units total
- Relapsed or refractory to ESA treatment, or erythropoietin level &gt; 500 mU/mL
- Absolute neutrophil count (ANC) \ge 1.5 \times 10^9/L independent of growth factor support
- Platelets \ge 75 \times 10^9/L independent of platelet transfusion
L653: During the course of the Phase 2 study, when 32 subjects had been enrolled, the Applicant observed better 8-week RBC-TI responses in the subgroup of subjects without del5q and without prior treatment with a hypomethylating agent (e.g., azacitidine or decitabine) or lenalidomide, when compared to all subjects. The following "target population" was thus defined, and eligibility requirements added accordingly, for the remainder of the Phase 2 study and the entirety of the Phase 3 study:
L654: - No del5q karyotype
- No prior treatment with a hypomethylating agent (HMA) or lenalidomide
