L673: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L674: FDA agrees with the description of the primary endpoint for Study MDS3001-Phase 3 listed by the Applicant above. Per SAP, the secondary endpoints included in the hypothesis testing with overall Type I error rate 0.05 (2-sided): 24-week RBC TI, Rate of HI-E per modified IWG 2006, PFS, OS. All other endpoints not included in the hypothesis testing with overall Type I error rate 0.05 (2-sided) are considered exploratory. Additionally, exploratory endpoints included not only patient-reported outcomes (PROs) per the FACIT-Fatigue questionnaire, but additional PRO measures using the QUALMS, FACT-An, EQ-5D-5L, and PGIC questionnaires.
L675: ## Statistical Analysis Plan and Amendments
L676: ## The Applicant's Description:
L677: The statistical analysis plan was agreed upon with FDA and version 2.0 was finalized before the conduct of the prespecified primary analysis of the pivotal MDS3001-Phase 3 study, at which time the Sponsor was unblinded to treatment randomization assignment.
L678: The primary hypothesis of this study is that the imetelstat will significantly improve the rate of RBC TI as compared to placebo in transfusion-dependent subjects with low or intermediate-1 risk MDS that is relapsed or refractory to ESA treatment. The hypothesis was tested using a stratified Cochran-Mantel-Haenszel (CMH) test adjusting for the stratification factors at a 2-sided significant level of 0.05. Efficacy data were compared between the imetelstat and placebo groups based on the Intent-to-Treat (ITT) population.
L679: ## Power and Sample Size
L680: On the basis of historical data, 8-week RBC TI rate was expected to be approximately 7.5% in patients with low or intermediate-1 risk MDS without any active treatment (Raza, 2008; Santini, 2014). The 8-week RBC TI rate with imetelstat treatment was expected to be approximately 30% based on preliminary data from a cohort of 9 subjects in Study CP14B019 (Tefferi, 2016). Approximately 170 subjects were initially planned to be randomized in a 2:1 ratio to receive either imetelstat or placebo. Using a 2:1 ratio randomization and a 2-group continuity corrected Chi square test with 0.05 (2 sided) significance level, 150 subjects were needed to achieve a power of approximately 88% to detect the difference between an RBC TI rate of 30% in the imetelstat group and an RBC TI rate of 7.5% in the placebo group. After correction for a 10% drop-out rate, a total of approximately 170 subjects (115 in imetelstat group and 55 in placebo group) were planned.
L681: ## Efficacy Analysis
L682: Statistical methods for the primary and the key secondary endpoints are outlined in Applicant Table 13.
L683: ## Safety Analysis
L684: All safety summaries and analyses were based on the Safety Analysis Set, which included all subjects who received at least 1 dose of study drug. Safety variables were tabulated by descriptive statistics. Adverse events that had missing causality (after data querying) were assumed to be related to study drug. Protocol-defined adverse events of interest (AEI) included liver function abnormalities based on laboratory data and hepatic AEs. All hepatic AEs based on Medical Dictionary of Regulatory Activities (MedDRA)-defined search criteria and liver function test (LFT) abnormalities were reviewed by an independent Hepatic Expert Committee (HEC) that consisted of 3 hepatologists, at least quarterly, and as needed. Bleeding events, infections, and infusion-related reaction (IRR) events were also analyzed as other selected AEIs. After the start of the Coronavirus Disease 2019 (COVID-19) pandemic in March 2020, events related to COVID-19 were also monitored as AEIs.
L685: ## Biomarker Analysis
L686: Statistical methods for biomarker data are outlined in Applicant Table 13.
L687: ## PRO Analysis
L688: Details of the PRO analyses are provided in the clinical study report (CSR) MDS3001-Phase 3 Section 9.7.1.6 and the PRO SAP (CSR MDS3001-Phase 3 Appendix 16.1.9) and were previously communicated to FDA in a complete response (submitted 18 February 2022) to an information request raised by the Agency (IND 147027, Serial No. 0059). The main PRO objective was to explore the hypothesis that, while they are on treatment, subjects treated with imetelstat are not more likely to experience a meaningful deterioration in fatigue, as measured by the FACIT-
L689: Fatigue score, than subjects treated with placebo, regardless of their transfusion-dependence status. The proportion of subjects who experienced an episode of meaningful deterioration in fatigue was calculated in the PRO population for each treatment group. Sensitivity analyses were conducted to investigate the impact of missing PRO assessments and robustness of the results on the main PRO objective. The proportion of subjects who experienced an meaningful improvement in fatigue was also explored. Sustained and meaningful deterioration/improvement in fatigue was defined as \ge 3-point decrease/increase on the FACIT-Fatigue Scale for \ge 2 consecutive non-missing treatment cycles.
L690: ## Missing or Partial Data
L691: No imputations for missing data were made for the primary and secondary efficacy endpoints. In order to be considered transfusion independent, a subject must have completed continuous transfusion assessments throughout the qualifying observation period where his/her TI was determined. An apparent transfusion-free period with 1 or more missing transfusion assessments was not considered a qualifying 8-week or 24-week TI period. Subjects with no qualifying observation periods were considered non-responders.
L692: In general, imputation of partial missing dates was made for AE onset date, AE resolution date, date of death, start and end dates of concomitant therapy, and date of initial diagnosis. Imputation rules for partial dates are described in Section 4.2.3 of the SAP
L693: ## Multiplicity Testing
L694: For Phase 3, a sequential gate-keeping procedure and Hochberg procedure (Hochberg, 1988) was implemented to ensure that the overall Type I error rate for the secondary endpoints was controlled. The \ge 24-week RBC TI and rate of HI-E per modified IWG 2006 (Cheson, 2006) were considered the most important secondary endpoints, and thus were used as gate keepers and tested at the \alpha level sequentially. Let \alpha be the critical type I error rate 0.05 (2-sided). The 24-week RBC TI was the first gate keeper and rate of HI-E per modified IWG 2006 (Cheson, 2006) was the second gate keeper. If the nominal P value was &lt; \alpha for the 24-week RBC TI, then the rate of HI-E was tested next at the \alpha level. If the nominal P value was &lt; \alpha for rate of HI-E as well, the remaining selected secondary hypotheses (PFS and OS) were tested using a Hochberg procedure.
L695: ## Interim and Final Analysis
L696: The primary efficacy analysis was planned at 12 months after the last subject was randomized in the main part of Phase 3 study, and the final analysis will be performed at the End of the Study defined as 24 months after randomization of the last subject in the main part of Phase 3 study or anytime the sponsor terminated the study, whichever came first. No interim analysis was planned.
L697: ## Planned Subgroup Analysis
L698: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 217779 imetelstat
## TRADENAME, imetelstat
L699: Subgroup analyses were performed to assess the consistency and robustness of the treatment benefit, mainly for the rate of RBC TI and the rate of HI-E. CMH tests between the 2 treatment groups within each subgroup were calculated for Phase 3. Results were displayed in forest plots. Subgroups investigated included demographic factors, and baseline characteristics. Subgroup analyses were also performed for selected safety parameters.

L704: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L705: FDA agrees with the Applicant's description of the statistical power, sample size, missing data, multiplicity, interim and final analysis, and SAP amendments. See *Efficacy Results – Secondary or exploratory COA (PRO) endpoints* section for comments on the secondary and exploratory endpoints and their analyses. No overall Type I error rate-controlled subgroup analyses were prespecified in SAP. Therefore, subgroup analyses conducted by FDA did not include hypothesis testing and did not include stratification factors due to small sample sizes and/or events in some subgroups.
L706: Regarding the change in PRO hypothesis testing, the following was provided by the Applicant in the information request response under IND 147027, Seq. 0059 (received on 18 February 2022):
L707: "Based on a systematic approach to update the PRO measurement strategy in the MDS3001 study, Geron plans to change the positioning of the PRO endpoint for the MDS3001 study by reclassifying it from a secondary to an exploratory endpoint.
L708: The stepwise approach to update the PRO measurement strategy for the MDS3001 study started with the identification of the core concepts of interest (COI) to measure in the specific context of use (COU) of the MDS3001 study. These COIs were established from the review of published qualitative literature and patient blogs describing the experience of LR MDS. A comprehensive conceptual model of the experience of patients with LR MDS was created from this research, covering symptoms (fatigue, gastrointestinal, systemic, cardio-respiratory, pain and neuropathy, and transfusion-specific), physical functioning (mobility, activities of daily living, work/school impact, and sleep/rest) and psychosocial functioning (emotional well-being, and social/leisure). Three expert MDS clinicians reviewed the conceptual model and endorsed fatigue, cardio-respiratory, and systemic symptoms as the most pertinent and relevant symptoms to patients, and acknowledged that symptom burden, especially fatigue, can lead to physical and psychosocial impacts. Subsequently, an item-to-concept mapping was performed considering all items of the PRO measures collected in the MDS3001 study. Tentative item sets from the Functional Assessment of Cancer Therapy-Anemia (FACT-An) and Quality of Life in Myelodysplasia Scale (QUALMS) were identified to assess each COI. The evaluation of the psychometric performances of these item sets using available data from a blinded interim data cut of the MDS3001 study provided supportive evidence for the use of the FACIT-Fatigue score to measure fatigue in this COU, but the psychometric performance of the item sets tested for the measurement of the other COIs (especially physical functioning and shortness of breath) were deemed not strong enough to warrant their use as secondary endpoints of the study.
L709: Geron considered that the benefit of imetelstat in terms of transfusion burden reduction and independence would not be at the cost of less controlled core symptoms of LR MDS. Based on the findings of the PRO measurement strategy described above, the assessment of fatigue using the FACIT-Fatigue score would be the COI measured for symptom control, however, the sample size of the study does not provide sufficient power to demonstrate the absence of deterioration of fatigue in imetelstat versus placebo treated patients in this COU.
L710: For these reasons, Geron does not plan to use the MDS3001 study to formally demonstrate the benefit of imetelstat compared with placebo in terms of PRO endpoint and the PRO data will not be used for the specification of secondary endpoints of the study. Instead, the PRO data will be included as exploratory endpoints, with descriptive analyses to document the change in PRO scores targeting the COIs over the course of the study."
L711: ## Protocol Amendments
L712: ## The Applicant's Description:
L713: The original protocol, dated 29 July 2015, was amended 8 times. Phase 3 of the study started enrolling under Amendment 3.
L714: Amendment 1 was completed on 31 May 2016 with the following important changes following consultation with Health Authorities:
L715: - The definitions of “prior RBC transfusion burden” and “transfusion dependent” were revised to include the 16 weeks (changed from 12 weeks) prior to study entry. RBC transfusion history collection period was increased to 16 weeks before study entry (changed from 12 weeks).
- Inclusion criterion no. 13 was added. Subjects with the del(5q) karyotype were eligible if they had failed treatment with lenalidomide.
- The requirement was added that total bilirubin must be \le 3 \times \text{ULN}. Requirement for direct bilirubin was changed from \le 1.5 \times \text{ULN} to \le 2 \times \text{ULN}. Subjects with elevated bilirubin due to Gilbert's syndrome could be eligible to participate in the study.
- The independent Data Monitoring Committee's (DMC) involvement in the study was extended to Phase 2.
L716: Amendment 2 was completed on 31 August 2017 following an analysis of Part 1, Week-24 data, with the following important changes:
L717: - Beginning with this amendment, enrolled subjects were to have non-del(5q) LR-MDS and no prior treatment with either a HMA or lenalidomide. Exclusion criteria were revised to exclude prior use of HMAs and lenalidomide, and to specify the minimum allowable interval following prior use of an ESA or any chemotherapy, immunomodulatory, or immunosuppressive therapy. Subjects with the del(5q) karyotype were excluded from the study.
- Individuals previously diagnosed with IPSS intermediate-2 or high risk MDS were excluded from the study. Subjects with MDS/MPN overlap syndrome were excluded.
- Enrollment in Part 1 was expanded to include approximately 22 additional subjects in the target population meeting the revised entry criteria. Total enrollment in Part 1 (initial cohort plus expanded cohort) was to increase from up to 30 subjects to approximately 55 subjects.
- Dose escalation to 9.4 mg/kg and dose re-escalation following dose reduction was no longer permitted. Clarification added that subjects whose dose was previously escalated (before Amendment 2) could continue at the 9.4 mg/kg dose.
L718: Amendment 3 was completed on 18 April 2019, with the following important changes:
L719: - Sponsor changed from Janssen Research &amp; Development to Geron Corporation.
L720: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 217779 imetelstat
## TRADENAME, imetelstat
L721: - The planned primary efficacy analysis was to be performed at 15 months (and no longer 12 months) after the last subject was randomized in the Phase 3 part (note that this was changed back to 12 months via Amendment 7).
L722: Amendment 4 was completed on 23 March 2020, with the following important changes in consideration of the COVID-19 pandemic:
L723: - Guidelines for the management of subjects who had confirmed COVID-19 infection, had clinical signs and symptoms consistent with COVID-19 infection, or had been exposed to a person with confirmed COVID-19 infection. A subsection was added to the protocol to include dose modification guidance regarding COVID-19 for subjects on treatment. Text was added to elucidate COVID-19 screening procedures and enrollment decisions as they pertained to COVID-19, and to elucidate monitoring, follow-up and reporting of clinical signs and symptoms of COVID-19 infection.
L724: Amendment 5 was completed on 29 June 2020, to clarify eligibility criteria/assessments and study procedures pertaining to the Phase 3 study. This amendment also further clarified considerations regarding the COVID-19 pandemic, including the provision for remote monitoring of study sites. The following important changes were made:
L725: - Added text to establish an IRC to adjudicate investigator-assessed disease response (CR, PR, mCR, or cytogenetic response) based on the modified IWG 2006 criteria.
L726: Responsibilities, authorities, and procedures were detailed in a separate charter
L727: Amendment 6 was completed on 01 April 2021, with the following important changes:
L728: - The dose modification criteria for LFT elevations and hepatic AEs were changed. Modifications were made to Table 9 that decreased the number of times the dose could be reduced due to Grade 3 or 4 ALT, AST, or bilirubin elevations, and Grade 3 or 4 hepatic AEs before discontinuing treatment. Modification and stopping criteria were added for Grade 2 ALT or AST with concomitant Grade 2 bilirubin elevations. Guidance was provided on investigations into possible causes of LFT elevations and hepatic AEs and subsequent monitoring.
- ALT or AST &gt; 3.0 \times ULN with Bilirubin &gt; 2.0 \times ULN was added as an AEI.
L729: Amendment 7 was completed on 02 September 2021, with the following important changes:
L730: - The planned primary efficacy and safety analysis from the main MDS3001 study was returned to 12 months (this was changed from 12 months to 15 months via Amendment 3) after the last subject is randomized in the Phase 3 study, as sufficient follow-up information would be collected by that time to determine the benefit/risk of imetelstat in low risk, transfusion dependent MDS subjects.
- Guidance regarding the disruptions due to the COVID-19 pandemic was modified to recommend that study sites follow local standard of care and local recommendations for quarantine, vaccination and/or potential dosing delays. Guidance was provided for beginning/resuming study treatment after a positive COVID-19 test or infection based on updated knowledge and guidance.
L731: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 217779 imetelstat
## TRADENAME, imetelstat
L732: - Modifications to the Ventricular Repolarization sub-study were made to include adjustments to eligibility criteria, time and events table, schedule of ECG and PK assessments, and unblinding activities.
L733: Amendment 8 was completed on 13 Dec 2022.
L734: The primary reason for this amendment was to add an Extension Phase to evaluate long-term safety, OS, and disease progression including progression to AML, until 5 years from the first dose of imetelstat or after 3 years of post-treatment follow-up. This amendment was in effect after the cutoff date for the primary analysis, and therefore does not apply to this CSR.
L735: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L736: FDA agrees that the protocol was amended as noted above.
L737: Additional clarification on IRC establishment is provided below.
L738: Per Amendment 5, "Added text to establish an Independent Review Committee (IRC) to adjudicate investigator-assessed disease response (CR, PR, mCR, or cytogenetic response) based on the modified IWG 2006 criteria." Per CSR, "In the Phase 3 study, bone marrow assessments were performed centrally at baseline and during disease evaluation visits. In addition, a baseline bone marrow blast of &gt; 5% based on modified IWG 2006 criteria was used as a cutoff for assessment of CR, PR, and mCR, and all potential responses were adjudicated by the Independent Review Committee (IRC) for Phase 3."
L739: ## 8.1.2. **Study Results**
L740: ## Compliance with Good Clinical Practices
L741: ## **Data:**
L742: MDS3001 study was conducted in compliance with the ICH Harmonised Tripartite Guidelines for Good Clinical Practice (GCP) regulations, ethical principles from the Declaration of Helsinki and applicable country and/or local statutes. The original protocol and protocol amendments were approved by an independent Institutional Review Board /Ethics Committee associated with each study center. Signed informed consent was obtained from all participants prior to enrollment in the study.
L743: ## **The Applicant's position:**
L744: The MDS3001 study was conducted in compliance with GCP regulations.
L745: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L746: FDA agrees that the study appears to have been conducted in compliance with GCP regulations.

L748: ## Financial Disclosure
L749: ## Data:
L750: Financial disclosure information was collected from all investigators participating in the pivotal MDS3001 study and adequately disclosed. Additional details are provided in Section 19.2 of this Assessment Aid.
L751: ## The Applicant's Position:
L752: Geron certifies that 1) the company has not entered into any financial arrangement whereby the value of compensation to the investigator could be affected by the outcome of the study; 2) no listed investigator disclosed a proprietary interest in the product or a significant equity holding in Geron; and 3) no listed investigator was the recipient of significant payments of other sorts. The integrity of the MDS3001 study data was not affected by financial interests of the investigators.
L753: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L754: Seventeen investigators on the study did not submit a financial disclosure form, however these were investigators who either did not assist on the study, did not have any patients in the study, or were from sites which never enrolled any patients. Financial disclosure information was submitted from all other investigators and there were no reportable financial disclosures per the Applicant.
L755: ## Patient Disposition
L756: ## Data:
L757: See below.
L758: ## The Applicant's Position:
L759: A total of 178 subjects were enrolled in the study at 77 sites across 17 countries. All 178 subjects were included in the ITT population, 118 of which were randomized to receive imetelstat and 60 subjects were randomized to receive placebo. In total, 177 subjects were treated (118 subjects received imetelstat and 59 subjects received placebo) and are included in the safety population. At the time of the clinical cutoff (13 October 2022), 63 (35.4%) subjects discontinued from the study; 42 (35.6%) subjects in the imetelstat group and 21 (35.0%) subjects in the placebo group.
L760: At the cutoff date, 27 (22.9%) subjects in the imetelstat group and 14 (23.7%) subjects in the placebo group were still receiving study treatment and 91 (77.1%) and 45 (76.3%) subjects, respectively, had discontinued treatment.
L761: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L762: FDA agrees with the patient disposition information summarized by the Applicant, aside from the percent of patients who discontinued treatment in the placebo group (45/60 = 75%). Some additional results are listed below.
L763: Table 15 shows treatment discontinuation summary. Figure 6 shows time on treatment.
L764: *Clinical Reviewer Comment: With a very effective and safe therapy, one would expect to observe a much longer duration of treatment compared to placebo. However, patients treated with imetelstat had a similar duration of treatment as patients treated with placebo, as shown in Figure 6, and the median duration of treatment was only 8 cycles for patients treated with imetelstat, the same as placebo.*

L771: ## Protocol Violations/Deviations
L772: ## Data:
L773: Overall, 43.3% of subjects had major protocol deviations (Table tsidv01_p2), including 55 (46.6%) subjects in the imetelstat group and 22 (36.7%) subjects in the placebo group. Most of these deviations were due to: continuing dosing despite toxicity that required a dose hold, not dose reducing due to toxicity, dosing before availability of all required laboratory results, and restarting treatment after interruption &gt; 28 days without Sponsor approval.
L774: Incorrect stratification accounted for major protocol deviations in 12.7% of subjects in the imetelstat group and 10.0% in the placebo group (Table tsidv01_p2). Higher than expected volume of stratification errors was largely related to the baseline transfusion burden and attributed to the detailed calculation required by the investigator to determine the maximum
L775: number of RBC units transfused over an 8-week period during the 16 weeks prior to randomization.
L776: To reduce these errors, the medical monitor started to review the stratification for each subject prior to randomization. This corrective action was implemented early during the study, when only 24 subjects had been randomized, and the number of stratification errors was subsequently minimized.
L777: ## The Applicant's Position:
L778: ## Assessment of major protocol violations on the study results
L779: Based on medical assessment, the major protocol deviations described above were concluded to have minimal impact on subject safety or data integrity in this study.
L780: Despite the stratification errors at randomization, the data showed 2:1 allocation ratio was maintained for imetelstat and placebo groups within each of the strata, i.e. prior RBC transfusion burden (\le 6 units, &gt; 6 units) and IPSS category (low, INT-1). To assess the impact of the major protocol deviations on the efficacy results, sensitivity analyses of primary and secondary endpoints were performed in the Per Protocol analysis set (summarized in Table tefti02b_p2 and Table tefti04b_p2, respectively). The results demonstrated that these major protocol deviations had minimal impact on the data integrity in this study.
L781: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L782: As noted by the Applicant above, 43.3% of patients had major protocol deviations. Notably, incorrect stratification accounted for major protocol deviations in 12.7% of patients in the imetelstat group and 10.0% of patients in the placebo group. Sensitivity analyses of the primary endpoint 8-week RBC-TI and key secondary endpoint 24-week RBC-TI were performed in the per-protocol analysis set by the Applicant. Per these sensitivity analyses, the major protocol deviations do not appear to substantially impact the efficacy results. For potential impact on other endpoints, see discussion of OS results in "Efficacy Results – Secondary and other relevant endpoints" section below for more information.
L783: Per CSR, "Overall, 43.3% of subjects had major protocol deviations. The proportion of subjects with major protocol deviations was higher in the imetelstat group (46.6%) compared to the placebo group (36.7%), which was mostly due to more frequent incorrect administration of study treatment in the imetelstat group (29.7%) versus the placebo group (13.3%). Among subjects in both treatment groups, major protocol deviations related to incorrect administration of study treatment included study treatment not held due to toxicity as per protocol (20 subjects in the imetelstat group, 3 subject in the placebo group), study treatment dose not reduced due to toxicity per protocol (13 subjects in the imetelstat group, 1 subject in the placebo group), treatment administered prior to availability of hematological or chemistry

## TRADENAME, imetelstat
L785: lab results (7 subjects in the imetelstat group, 3 subject in the placebo group), study treatment interruption lasting &gt; 28 days, and treatment restarted without Sponsor approval (5 subjects in the imetelstat group, 1 subject in the placebo group)...Other major protocol deviations included study drug not administered according to the protocol schedule (5 [4.2%] subjects in the imetelstat group and 4 [6.7%] subjects in the placebo group), hematology labs not done at scheduled visits (7 [5.9%] and 5 [8.3%] subjects, respectively), AE/SAE not reported as per protocol (7 [5.9%] and 2 [3.3%] subjects, respectively), and subjects not satisfying the entry criteria after study entry (3 [2.5%] and 2 [3.3%] subjects, respectively)."

L795: ## The Applicant's Position:
L796: Demographic characteristics were balanced between the imetelstat and placebo groups (Applicant Table 16). The median age for all subjects was 72 years (range: 39 to 87 years) with a higher proportion of males (62.4%), consistent with the epidemiology for this patient population. The imetelstat group was slightly younger compared to the placebo group with 77% versus 85% of subjects being ≥ 65 years old and 30% versus 38% being ≥ 75 years old.
L797: Study MDS3001 was designed as a global study; however, most of the subjects were from the EU and few were from North America. That said, there are no expected differences by region (US vs EU) given that MDS is predominantly observed in individuals who are white, male, and elderly, with the incidence increasing with age, all of which are consistent with the baseline demographics in MDS3001, with the majority being white, from Europe and North America, and ≥ 65 years. Furthermore, there are similar incidence and prevalence rates of MDS between the US and EU (see Section 2). Therefore, the MDS3001 study population can still be considered representative of this LR-MDS patient population as the epidemiology of MDS, clinical practice and methods of treatment are similar between the 2 regions.

## TRADENAME, imetelstat

L813: ## The Applicant's Position:
L814: Disease and other baseline characteristics were generally well balanced between the 2 treatment groups and were in line with those expected in patients with LR MDS with transfusion dependent anemia after ESA failure (Applicant Table 17). Median serum EPO level was lower in the imetelstat group (174.86 mU/mL) compared to the placebo group (277.00 mU/mL), with 22.0% and 36.7% of subjects, respectively, having serum EPO &gt; 500 mU/mL.
L815: Of importance, median prior RBC transfusion burden was 6.0 units per 8 weeks (range: 4 to 33 and 4 to 13 units per 8 weeks for the imetelstat and placebo groups, respectively). In the 16 weeks prior to study entry, the median total number of RBC transfusion units was 10.0 units (range: 4 to 54 and 4 to 24 units per 16 weeks, respectively) for the 2 treatment groups. Overall, a larger proportion of subjects in the imetelstat group (82.2%) were classified per IWG 2018 as high transfusion burden (HTB) (≥ 8 RBC units in the 16 weeks prior to study entry in at least 2 transfusion episodes) compared to the placebo group (70.0%).
L816: TRADENAME, imetelstat
L817: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L818: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position regarding disease and other baseline characteristics of subjects in Study MDS3001-Phase 3.
L819: ## **Treatment Compliance, Concomitant Medications, and Rescue Medication Use**
L820: ## **Data:**
L821: ## **Treatment compliance**
L822: Imetelstat was administered by qualified site staff, and the details of each administration were recorded in the eCRF. In both Phase 3 and Phase 2 of the MDS3001 study, mean relative dose intensity with respect to the planned dose was &gt; 99% (99.6 + 1.92 and 100.1 + 2.44, respectively; Table tex01b), indicating good treatment compliance.
L823: ## **Premedication**
L824: Per protocol, all subjects who received an infusion of study drug had to be premedicated with an antihistamine (diphenhydramine 25 to 50 mg or equivalent) and a corticosteroid (hydrocortisone 100 to 200 mg or equivalent), either IV or orally. Premedications were administered per local standard of care approximately 1 hour prior to study drug infusion.
L825: ## **Concomitant medications**
L826: The use of concomitant medication was reported for the majority of subjects in the imetelstat group (98.3%) and all subjects (100%) in the placebo group and were generally balanced between the treatment groups (Table tsicm05_p2) and consistent with expected for a LR MDS older patient population.
L827: The most common concomitant medications in the imetelstat and placebo groups, respectively, were iron chelating agents (63.6% and 62.7%).
L828: A larger proportion of subjects in the imetelstat group than in the placebo group required concomitant use of granulocyte colony stimulating factors (34.7% vs. 3.4%).
L829: A summary of concomitant medications started during treatment is provided in Table tsicm06_p2, and a summary of pre-study medications ongoing during treatment is provided in Table tsicm07_p2.
L830: ## **Rescue medication**
L831: Not relevant to the product and disease under study.
L832: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L833: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 217779 imetelstat
## TRADENAME, imetelstat
L834: Treatment compliance in the Phase 3 study was high with most subjects having received &gt; 99% of the planned dose. Premedication with steroid and antihistamine was administered before each cycle to minimize occurrence if infusion-related reactions (IRRs).
L835: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L836: The majority of patients (211) had prior ESA therapy (Table 18). Patients treated on the imetelstat arm of the Phase 3 portion of the study received prior ESA marginally more frequently than patients on the placebo arm or in the Phase 2 portion.

L849: Of note, patients in the Phase 2 study were more likely to have 5q deletions and were more likely to receive lenalidomide or HMAs compared to patients in the Phase 3 study. This can be attributed to the fact that the protocol was modified during the course of the Phase 2 portion of the study to exclude patients with prior exposure to lenalidomide or HMAs and patients with 5q- syndrome, including in the Phase 3 portion of the protocol. Among patients in the Phase 3 portion of the study, slightly more patients were treated with prior systemic therapy in the imetelstat arm, with relatively more receiving roxadustat. This agent is not approved in the US and the implications of prior use of this agent are unclear.
L850: Prior medications of any type were used by the majority of patients on both parts of the study and in patients treated with either imetelstat or placebo.

L896: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L897: The study met the primary endpoint with a highly statistically significant and clinically meaningful improvement in 8-week RBC TI rate in the imetelstat group (39.8% [47/118]) compared to the placebo group (15.0% [9/60]) (
L898: Applicant Table 23 and
L899: Applicant Figure 10). The difference in the rate of 8-week RBC TI between the 2 treatment groups was 24.8% (95% CI: 9.90%, 36.89%, P &lt; .001).
L900: In addition, subgroup analysis of 8-week TI based on demographics and the most relevant disease characteristics, showed an advantage for imetelstat over placebo in most subgroups tested. Statistically significant and clinically meaningful difference in 8-week RBC TI rates between the imetelstat and placebo groups was achieved irrespective of baseline RS status (26.3%, P = .016 [RS+]) and 23.1%, P = .038 [RS-]), baseline RBC transfusion burden (23.9%, P = .027 [\le 6 units] and 26.5%, P = .023 [&gt; 6 units]), or IPSS disease category (19.5%, P = .034 [low] and 34.7%, P = .004 [intermediate-1]) (Applicant Figure 8).
L901: ## **Sensitivity analyses**
L902: ## **Data**
L903: Sensitivity analyses were performed for the Phase 3 study to evaluate the robustness of the primary analysis and included:
L904: - CMH test of 8-week based on the modified intent-to-treat (mITT) analysis set in the case there were subjects who were dosed but did not receive any assigned treatment
- CMH test of 8-week based on per-protocol (PP) analysis set
- CMH estimate of the odds ratio of 8-week RBC TI with the stratification factors and the associated 95% CI
- Comparison of 8-week RBC TI based on CMH test adjusted by values of prior RBC transfusion burden and IPSS risk group recorded on the eCRF
- Comparison of 8-week RBC TI based on CMH test without adjusting for the baseline stratification factors
L905: Results of these analyses were supportive of the primary analysis (Table tefti02a_p2, Table tefti02b_p2, Table tefti02c_p2).
L906: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L907: FDA agrees that Study MDS3001-Part 2 (Phase 3) met its primary endpoint with a statistically significant improvement in 8-week RBC-TI rate in the imetelstat group compared to the placebo group (39.8% imetelstat versus 15.0% placebo; p = &lt;0.001), equating to a rate difference of +24.8% (95% CI 9.9, 36.9) in favor of imetelstat. However, it should be noted that the clinical
L908: meaningfulness of an 8-week RBC-TI period in the context of lower risk MDS is uncertain. In recent years, the general consensus among MDS experts has been that only a 16-week or longer period of transfusion independence is clinically meaningful (Platzbecker 2019). Therefore, the presumption in the use of the early outcome of 8-week RBC-TI is that the treatment effect seen early will persist for a more substantial period. Thus, the Applicant evaluated alternative definitions of RBC-TI reflecting greater durability, which are discussed in the “Efficacy Results – Secondary and other relevant endpoints” section below.
L909: Subgroup analyses of 8-week RBC-TI in the Phase 3 study based on demographics and most relevant disease characteristics per FDA analysis are shown in Figure 9 below. These analyses showed a numerical advantage for imetelstat over placebo in most subgroups. However, the 8-week RBC-TI rate was higher with placebo in the subgroup of patients with prior luspatercept use, with 0/7 (0%) subjects treated with imetelstat achieving an 8-week RBC-TI versus 1/4 (25.0%) subjects treated with placebo, equating to a rate difference of -25.0% (95% CI -78.1%, 24.9%) in favor of placebo. In comparison, the 8-week RBC-TI rate in the Phase 2 study was 3/5 (60%) in patients previously treated with luspatercept, with 2 subjects achieving at least a 1-year RBC-TI. Given the small sample sizes in both Phase 3 and Phase 2, it is difficult to make any conclusions regarding efficacy of imetelstat in the subgroup of patients with prior luspatercept treatment.
L910: Additionally, the treatment effect was marginal for subjects with lower transfusion burden at baseline (defined as 4-7 units RBCs in 16 weeks in at least 2 transfusion episodes), with a difference in 8-week RBC-TI rate of only +4.8% (95% CI -27.1, 34.7) between arms in favor of imetelstat.
L911: Figure 9: MDS3001-Phase 3: Subgroup analysis of rate of 8-week RBC-TI
L912: CI = Confidence Interval; NE = Not Evaluatable
L913: Source: FDA analysis based on ADSL and ADBDC datasets. Data cutoff: 13 Oct 2022.
L914: ## Data Quality and Integrity
L915: ## Data:
L916: ## Consistency of Transfusion Practices
L917: Additional analysis was performed in order to assess for any potential bias of study investigators for when RBC transfusions were prescribed. Per protocol, transfusions were administered as needed per investigator discretion and according to local standard practices. The protocol did not outline transfusion parameters, such as Hgb level threshold. Results of this analysis are summarized in Applicant Table 24. Analysis performed evaluating the Hgb levels
L918: prior to receiving RBC transfusions on study demonstrated a median Hgb of 7.4 g/dL on imetelstat and 7.5 g/dL on placebo. Pre-study baseline Hgb levels leading to transfusions were also compared to on-study Hgb levels resulting in transfusions. This demonstrated a median change (pre-study vs. on-study) of -0.45 g/dL on imetelstat and -0.30 g/dL on placebo. Based on these data, while on treatment, there is no notable difference in investigators transfusion parameters between treatment groups, or within each group prior to enrollment compared to while on study.

L924: ## The Applicant's Position:
L925: ## Overall Quality
L926: Geron was the sponsor for the Phase 3 study. The monitoring was outsourced to a CRO and performed in accordance with the CRO's SOPs. The data submitted in this NDA are of adequate quality to perform the safety and efficacy review. Overall, there are no concerns regarding the integrity of data in this submission.
L927: This study was conducted in accordance with the FDA and Health Canada regulations, the ICH E6 Guidelines for GCP, and applicable local, state, national laws. For EU member states, this includes Directive 201/20/EC, Directive 205/28/EC, and other directives as applicable as well as applicable local and national laws.
L928: The Sponsor performed oversight of the data management of this trial, including approval of the CRO's data management and data quality plans.
L929: An electronic data capture (EDC) vendor, [REDACTED](b) (4), developed and provided eCRFs for this study. The CRO, was responsible for data management of this trial, including quality checking of the data. The data were collected via EDC using eCRFs. A data quality plan was produced by the CRO that describes the quality checking to be performed on the data. The site was responsible for data entry into the EDC system. In the event of discrepant data, the CRO requested data clarification from the sites, which the sites resolved electronically in the EDC system. eCRFs and query resolution documentation was maintained in the EDC system's audit trail. System backups for data stored at the CRO was performed in accordance with the CRO's standard operating procedures.
L930: Study sites were monitored by the CRO at regular intervals following the principles of GCP, regulatory, and ICH guidelines. Monitoring visits included review and inspection of the facilities and records relevant to the study, which included but were not limited to subject signed ICFs, source documentation, regulatory and essential documents, eCRFs, and drug accountability records. Between site visits, the sponsor maintained appropriate communications with the investigator site personnel.
L931: During this study, 7 investigator site audits were conducted.
L932: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L933: Per protocol, transfusions were administered as needed per investigator discretion and according to local standard practice. Although there was no major difference in the mean and median pre-transfusion Hgb between the imetelstat and placebo groups as noted by the Applicant above, there were differences in 8-week RBC-TI rates by region of enrollment. The 8-week RBC-TI rate with imetelstat was much lower in the subgroup of patients enrolled at sites within the US compared to non-US sites, with a difference in response rate of only 12.5% for US subjects compared to 25.5% for non-US subjects (Table 38). Although no definitive conclusions can be drawn due to the small sample size of the subgroups and the lack of prespecified subgroup hypothesis testing, potential impact of region-specific patient or treatment-related factors cannot be ruled out.
L934: Additionally, the Agency sent an information request to the Applicant requesting clarification on the time period during which RBC and platelet transfusions were required to be reported. Based on the Applicant's response to the Agency's information request, as of Protocol Amendment 7 (dated 2 Sept 2021), investigators were not reported to report RBC transfusions that occurred after the first RBC transfusion in the post-treatment follow-up period. In addition, the protocol did not mandate the collection of platelet transfusion data after the end of treatment as part of post-treatment follow-up; however, if subjects had ongoing Grade 3+ thrombocytopenia at the time of post-treatment follow-up and platelet transfusions were given as part of treatment for the AE of thrombocytopenia, investigators were to report the
L935: transfusion in association with the thrombocytopenia AE. Given this, there may have been under-reporting of RBC and platelet transfusions that occurred after the end of treatment.

L976: ## Change in Hemoglobin Levels
L977: Imetelstat treatment led to a significant and sustained increase in Hgb levels (based on central laboratory results) compared to placebo (least squares [LS] mean difference in Hgb increase was 1.18 g/dL, 95% CI: 0.693, 1.665, P &lt; .001; Table tefhgb01a_p2). Mean change from baseline in Hgb values over time, shown in Applicant Figure 13, was significantly higher with imetelstat
L978: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 217779 imetelstat
## TRADENAME, imetelstat
L979: treatment than placebo, indicating clinical benefit with imetelstat even in subjects who did not achieve RBC TI.
L980: Median Hgb rise in the longest TI period from pretreatment levels for 8-week RBC TI responders was 3.6 g/dL for the imetelstat group compared to 0.8 g/dL in the placebo group (Applicant Table 27). Median Hgb peak in the longest RBC TI interval was 11.3 g/dL in the imetelstat group compared to 8.9 g/dL in the placebo group.

L991: Hb = hemoglobin; RBC = red blood cell; SD = standard deviation; TI = transfusion independence.
L992: a The Hb rise = the maximum Hb value in the longest transfusion free interval excluding the first 2 weeks – the pretreatment Hb level. Pretreatment Hb level was defined as the average of all Hb values in the 8 weeks prior to the first dose date, including the value on the first dose date and excluding values that were within 14 days after transfusion (thus, considered to be influenced by transfusion) for all treated subjects. If there were no Hb values that met this definition of not being influenced by transfusions, then the baseline value was used. For subjects who have been randomized but not treated with any study drug, the last non-missing Hb, or the average if multiple values from the same date, from local or central lab collected on or before the randomization date was used for pretreatment Hb.
L993: ## Change in Transfusion Burden
L994: Mean absolute change from baseline in RBC transfusion burden during the best 8-week interval (Applicant Figure 14) for the imetelstat group was -4.3 units (range: -24 to 15) and for the placebo group -3.6 units (range: -11 to +2). Least squares mean absolute change in RBC units from prior transfusion burden for all subjects was significantly lower in the imetelstat group versus the placebo group, with the LS mean difference of -0.97 units (P = .042) (Table teftran12_p2).

L1001: ## Rate of HI-E Per Modified IWG 2018
L1002: Analysis of HI-E was performed using the revised 2018 IWG criteria, which is considered more clinically relevant in MDS than the previous IWG 2006 criteria (Platzbecker, 2019), as it emphasizes durability of response over a 16-week interval versus an 8-week interval used by the 2006 IWG criteria. HI-E results for both IWG 2006 and IWG 2018 are shown in Applicant Table 28. HI-E per IWG 2018 criteria was 42.2% in subjects treated with imetelstat vs. 13.3% with placebo (P &lt; .001), reflecting the increased durability of the RBC-TI response with imetelstat (see also
L1003: Applicant Figure 10 for 16-week RBC TI rates). Among low transfusion burden (LTB) subjects (who received 3 to 7 RBC units in the 16 weeks prior to study entry), the HI-E response rate was 33.3% and 22.2% in imetelstat and placebo subjects, respectively (P = .562). Among HTB
L1004: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 217779 imetelstat
## TRADENAME, imetelstat
L1005: subjects (who received \ge 8 RBC units in the 16 weeks prior to study entry), 30.9% of imetelstat subjects and none in the placebo group had a major HI-E response (P &lt; .01) and 44.3% of the imetelstat-treated subjects compared to 9.5% of the placebo-treated subjects had a minor HI-E response (P &lt; .001).
L1006: The imetelstat group also demonstrated statistically significant differences compared to the placebo group in hematologic improvement per revised IWG 2018 criteria group across key subgroups, including RS status (27.7%, P = .01 [RS+] and 32.0%, P = .007 [RS-]), baseline RBC transfusion burden (23.8%, P = .021 [\le 6 units] and 35.4%, P = .003 [&gt; 6 units]), or IPSS disease category (29.6%, P = .001 [low] and 27.3%, P = .026 [intermediate-1]) (Table tefphi05_p2).

L1018: ## Additional Measures of Clinical Benefit Associated with Achieving TI
L1019: In addition to high rates of TI across various LR MDS subgroups with accompanying improvement in Hgb and reduction in transfusion burden among TI responders, subjects treated with imetelstat experienced other benefits. These include clinical benefits such as improvement in patient-reported symptoms of fatigue and lack of deterioration of dyspnea, as well as improvements in measures of LR MDS disease burden indicators such as cytogenetic responses, reduction of variant allele frequency (VAF) for mutations characteristic of MDS, and reduction in BM features of MDS such as RS+ cells.
L1020: Importantly, all the above measures of clinical benefit and evidence of disease modification were more likely to occur in subjects who experienced both \ge 8- and \ge 24-week TI (Applicant Figure 15). This association reinforces the conclusion that TI is an important outcome reflecting improvement in the signs and symptoms of LR MDS and that achieving TI results in additive clinical benefits across multiple efficacy endpoints.
L1021: Applicant Figure 15: 8- and 24-week TI in Imetelstat Treated Subjects by Associated Outcome Measures in Study MDS3001-Phase 3
L1022: BM = bone marrow; CR = complete response; IRC = Independent Review Committee; PR = partial response; RS = ring sideroblasts; TI = transfusion independence
L1023: P-value is calculated using Fisher exact test between yes vs no in each outcome
L1024: Source: Figure fefti04_p2 and Figure fefti05_p2
L1025: ## Cytogenetic Responses
L1026: A key characteristic of LR MDS is the absence of increased bone marrow blasts (IPSS low risk: &lt; 5%; intermediate 1 risk: 5% to 10%; (Greenberg, 1997)). In the MDS3001-Phase 3 study, 97.2% of subjects had ≤ 5% BM (Applicant Table 17), thus the assessment of CR, PR or mCR by IWG 2006 criteria (which is generally applied only to patients with BM blasts &gt; 5%), has limited applicability to this patient population.
L1027: Twenty-two percent (26/118) of subjects in the imetelstat group and 21.7% (13/60) in the placebo group had a baseline cytogenetic abnormality and were considered for cytogenetic response by IRC adjudication. Based on IRC adjudication, the cytogenetic response rate (cytogenetic CR + PR) was 9/26 (34.6%, 95% CI: 17.21%, 55.67%) in the imetelstat group and 2/13 (15.4%, 95% CI: 1.92%, 45.45%) in the placebo group (% difference 19.2%; 95% CI: -15.93%, 43.78%; P = .216) (Table tefbrO2_p2). Despite not reaching statistical significance due to small sample size, objective cytogenic responses favored the imetelstat group.
L1028: ## Association of Cytogenetic Responses with TI
L1029: In the imetelstat group, 88.9% (8/9) of the cytogenetic responders also achieved 8-week TI response compared to 50.0% (1/2) of cytogenetic responders in the placebo group (Applicant Figure 15A and Listing lefrsO2_p2 and Listing lefresO1_p2). Similar results were observed in subjects who achieved 24-week TI (Applicant Figure 15B).
L1030: ## Change in Mutation Burden
L1031: *SF3B1* is one of the most frequent mutations found in MDS and is associated with the RS subtype (Malcovati, 2015; Tang, 2019; Jafari, 2020). It is also significantly associated with lower Hgb values, consistent with a high degree of ineffective erythropoiesis, higher neutrophil and platelet counts, and lower BM blasts in LR-MDS patients (Malcovati L, 2020). Mutations in *ASXL1*, *RUNX1*, *TP53*, *EZH2*, *ETV6* genes are also highly prevalent in MDS patients, and are associated with poorer outcomes (Bejar, 2011; Thol, 2011; Liang, 2019).
L1032: In the Phase 3 study, blood samples at baseline and every 12-week during treatment were collected for mutation analysis. One-hundred-sixteen subjects had the *SF3B1* mutation detected at baseline and at least 1 post-treatment mutation assessment, including 78 and 38 subjects in imetelstat and placebo group, respectively (SCE Section 3.2.4.3). Subjects in the imetelstat group had sustained reduction of *SF3B1* VAF over treatment duration compared to the placebo group, with 29.5% of imetelstat-treated subjects versus 2.6% of placebo-treated subjects achieving ≥ 50% reduction in *SF3B1* VAF. In addition, compared to placebo, a higher percentage of subjects in the imetelstat group achieved ≥ 50% VAF reduction in the mutation
L1033: NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 217779 imetelstat
L1034: TRADENAME, imetelstat
L1035: biomarkers *TET2* (34.3% versus 8.3%), *DNMT3A* (11.1% versus 0%), and *ASXL1* (40.0% versus 16.7%) genes (Figure fefmsrdc01_p2).
L1036: Because *SF3B1* mutant MDS-initiating cells may arise from the hematopoietic stem and progenitor cells (Mian, 2015; Mortera-Blanco, 2017), the decrease in *SF3B1* mutation VAF suggests a reduction in the clonal burden of malignant stem and progenitor cells in the BM.
L1037: ## Correlation of Change in Mutation Burden with Clinical Benefit
L1038: In the imetelstat group, the proportion of RBC TI responders was significantly higher among subjects achieving \ge 50\% *SF3B1* VAF reduction compared to those who did not (Applicant Figure 16A); the rate of 8-week RBC TI was 82.6% (19/23) versus 38.2% (21/55) (Applicant Figure 15A), 24-week TI was 69.6% (16/23) versus 23.6% (13/55) (Applicant Figure 15B), and 1-year TI was 47.8% (11/23) versus 5.5% (3/55), respectively (P &lt; 0.001 in all comparisons; SCE Section 3.2.4.3). Similar results were also seen for *TET2*, *DNMT3A* and *ASXL1* VAF reduction with association to durable TI responses in imetelstat-treated subjects (SCE Section 3.2.4.3). Linear regression analysis showed a statistically significant correlation between RBC TI duration and *SF3B1* VAF reduction; the greater the maximum reduction of *SF3B1* VAF observed, the longer the TI duration achieved (r = -0.549, P &lt; 0.001) (Applicant Figure 16B). The correlation between RBC TI duration and *TET2* (r = -0.577, P &lt; 0.001), *DNMT3A* (r = -0.545, P &lt; .019) and *ASXL1* (r = -0.518, P = 0.125) VAF reduction was also observed (SCE Section 3.2.4.3). Importantly, there was a statistically significant negative correlation between *SF3B1* VAF reduction and level of Hgb in the imetelstat group where the greater the reduction in *SF3B1* VAF, the higher the increase in Hgb level (r = -0.626, P &lt; 0.001).

L1046: ## Improvement in Bone Marrow Morphology
L1047: The imetelstat group had a greater maximum reduction in BM RS relative to baseline (-30.5%, range: -98%, 62%) compared to the placebo group (-4.0%, range: -94%, 44%). Overall, 29/71 (40.8%) subjects in the imetelstat group and 3/31 (9.7%) in the placebo group achieved \ge 50\% reduction in central BM RS relative to baseline (Table tefbm04_p2).
L1048: ## Association of Improvement in BM Morphology with TI
L1049: In the imetelstat group, 75.9% (22/29) of the subjects who achieved \ge 50\% reduction in central BM RS relative to baseline also achieved 8-week TI response, and 62.1% (18/29) achieved 24-week TI response (Table tefbm06_p2 and Applicant Figure 15).
L1050: ## Long-term Outcomes
L1051: At the time of the clinical cut off for the primary analysis of MDS3001 (13 Oct 2022), the median follow-up was 19.48 and 17.51 months in the imetelstat and placebo groups, respectively. At this time, the OS, PFS and time to AML progression data are considered preliminary, and subjects are still in survival follow-up. Results from the primary analysis demonstrated no detrimental impact of imetelstat treatment on PFS or OS (MDS3001 CSR Section 11.2.3.4 and MDS3001 CSR Section 11.2.3.6). Subjects are continuing in survival follow-up as part of an extension phase, until 5 years from the first dose of imetelstat or after 3 years of post-treatment follow-up (MDS3001 Protocol Amendment 8, Section 9.1.6).
L1052: ## Overall Survival
L1053: MDS3001 was not designed or powered to demonstrate an OS benefit, given that TI was the pre-specified primary endpoint. As of the cutoff date for the primary analysis, the proportion of subjects who died was similar between the two treatment groups (16.1% [19 subjects] in the imetelstat group and 13.3% [8 subjects] in the placebo group). Median OS has not been reached in either treatment group (HR = 1.07; 95% CI: 0.459, 2.476) (Table tefsur01_p2). Based on Kaplan-Meier estimates, the 1-year survival rate was 92.5% (95% CI: 85.49, 96.17) for the imetelstat group and 94.7% (95% CI: 84.49, 98.27) for the placebo group, and the 18-month survival rate was 88.7% (80.36, 93.66) and 84.8% (70.22, 92.61), respectively (Table tefsur01_p2). The Applicant is continuing ongoing data cleaning with the aim of providing additional OS data per the protocol-specified analysis that will occur in October 2023, which corresponds to 2 years after the last subject was randomized in the MDS3001-Phase 3 study.
L1054: ## Progression-free Survival
L1055: As of the data cutoff date for the primary analysis, based on Kaplan-Meier estimates, the 1-year PFS rate was 83.6% (95% CI: 74.06%, 89.81%) for the imetelstat group and 82.6% (95% CI: 66.06%, 91.60%) for the placebo group (Table tefpfs01a_p2).
L1056: ## Disease Progression and Time to Progression to AML
L1057: As of the data cutoff date for the primary analysis, ~10% of subjects in either treatment group had disease progression (12/118 in the imetelstat group and 6/60 in the placebo group). This included 2 (1.7%) subjects in the imetelstat group and 1 (1.7%) subject in the placebo group who had transformation to AML during follow-up (Table tefpd01_p2 and Table tefamlfs01_p2). Three additional subjects (2 in the imetelstat group and 1 in the placebo group) progressed to AML but were censored from the analysis of progression to AML because they received subsequent anti-cancer therapy before progression to AML (SCE Section 3.2.3.6). The Kaplan-Meier estimate of the proportion of subjects without progression to AML was 97.9% (95% CI: 91.71%, 99.47%) at 6, 12 and 24 months for both treatment groups. Currently, the AML data are preliminary, and subjects are still being followed up (Table tefamlfs01_p2).
L1058: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1059: The MDS3001 Phase 3 study demonstrated a clear advantage for imetelstat compared to placebo in all of the key secondary endpoints, which further support the primary endpoint. Overall, the totality of the data from this Phase 3 study provides statistically persuasive and consistent efficacy findings, including the high and durable rates of TI together with additional clinical benefits for LR MDS patients. The durability of the transfusion independence response, accompanied by a significant increase in Hgb levels and reduction in transfusion burden are unprecedented and not observed with approved products in the MDS setting and as such are indicative of clinically relevant benefits that address the signs and symptoms of the disease in this heavily transfused patient population. These results were obtained in most key MDS subgroups including baseline RS status, baseline transfusion burden and IPSS risk category, demonstrating the breadth of activity seen with imetelstat.
L1060: Furthermore, achieving TI in response to imetelstat treatment was associated with a meaningful, sustained improvement in patient-reported fatigue compared with placebo. A significant association was observed between achieving RBC TI in response to imetelstat treatment and improvement in fatigue, which was not observed with placebo. This improvement in fatigue has not been observed in other studies of treatment for LR MDS.
L1061: Additionally, cytogenetic responses, decreases in mutation burden, decrease in BM RS+ cells and a correlation of these decreases with TI, further demonstrate the clinical benefit of
L1062: achieving TI and the possibility for imetelstat treatment to change longer term disease-based outcomes.
L1063: Though data for OS, PFS, and time to AML transformation in the Phase 3 study are preliminary, longer-term Phase 2 OS data indicates a possible improvement in survival compared with that reported in the literature. Given the evidence that indicates an association between reduction in transfusion burden and achieving TI with the improvement in longer-term clinical outcomes such as survival (De Witte, 2020; Santini, 2022, it is reasonable to anticipate that treatment with imetelstat would result in similar longer-term clinical benefits.
L1064: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1065: ## 24-week RBC TI
L1066: Although Study MDS3001 met its primary endpoint of 8-week RBC-TI as noted above, the presumption in the use of the early outcome of 8-week RBC-TI is that the treatment effect seen early will persist for a more substantial period. Thus, the Applicant evaluated alternative definitions of RBC-TI reflecting greater durability. In addition to the primary endpoint of 8-week RBC-TI, the Applicant evaluated the rates of RBC-TI lasting at least 24 weeks (24-week RBC-TI). The point estimate of the response rate decreased as the target duration of RBC-TI increased, with 28.0% (95% CI 20.1, 37.0) of imetelstat-treated subjects achieving 24-week RBC-TI vs. 3.3% (95% CI 0.4, 11.5) of placebo-treated subjects. However, the rate difference between the imetelstat and placebo groups was maintained at +24.6% (95% CI 12.6, 34.2) for 24-week RBC-TI. This is reassuring and suggests that imetelstat can lead to a clinically meaningful duration of RBC-TI in a subset of patients.
L1067: Subgroup analyses of 24-week RBC-TI in the Phase 3 study based on demographics and most relevant disease characteristics per FDA analysis are shown in Figure 17 below. These analyses showed a numerical advantage for imetelstat over placebo in most subgroups.
L1068: Figure 17: MDS3001-Phase 3: Subgroup analysis of rate of 24-week RBC-TI
L1069: CI = Confidence Interval; NE = Not Evaluable
L1070: Source: FDA analysis based on ADSL and ADBDC datasets. Data cutoff: 13 Oct 2022.
L1071: ## Duration of RBC-TI
L1072: The Applicant reports that the median duration of the longest RBC-TI interval was 51.6 weeks (95% CI 26.9, 83.9) for imetelstat versus 13.3 weeks (95% CI 8.0, 24.9) for placebo.
L1073: ## Clinical Reviewer comments:
L1074: The duration of RBC-TI was confirmed by the FDA; however, it should be noted that this metric applies only to the subgroup of patients who achieved an 8-week RBC-TI response, rather than the entire study population. As shown in Table 29, in the entire study population, the median duration of the longest RBC-TI interval per patient was substantially shorter: 5.0 weeks (95% CI 4.0, 7.7) with imetelstat versus 3.9 weeks (95% CI 3.6, 4.0) with placebo, which was only a 1.1 week difference. This minimal difference in the median duration of the longest RBC-TI interval
L1075: between arms can be attributed to the fact that the majority of subjects in the imetelstat arm did not have an RBC-TI interval of 8 weeks or more.

L1081: ## HI-E response
L1082: Hematologic improvement in the erythroid lineage (HI-E) per IWG 2006 criteria was a key secondary endpoint in the MDS3001-Phase 3 study. The definition of an HI-E response per IWG 2006 criteria is a hemoglobin increase by \ge 1.5 g/dL with concurrent relevant reduction of units of RBC transfusions by an absolute number of at least 4 RBC transfusions over 8 weeks compared with the pre-treatment transfusion number in the previous 8 weeks. Only RBC transfusions given for a hemoglobin of \le 9.0 g/dL pre-transfusion count in the RBC transfusion response evaluation (Cheson 2006).
L1083: These results were not adjudicated by the FDA. However, based on the Applicant's analysis, there was no significant difference in the proportion of subjects who achieved an HI-E response per IWG 2006 criteria in the Phase 3 trial, with an HI-E rate of 63.6% (75/118) for imetelstat versus 51.7% (31/60) for placebo, p=0.112. Additionally, transfusion reduction by \ge 4 units/8 weeks (a component of the HI-E response) was achieved by 60.2% (71/118) of subjects receiving imetelstat versus 50% (30/60) of subjects receiving placebo.
L1084: ## *Clinical Reviewer comment:*
L1085: *The high proportion of subjects who achieved an HI-E response and transfusion reduction in the placebo group is notable; these results suggest that a portion of hemoglobin rises and corresponding periods of transfusion reduction are due in part to natural fluctuations of the underlying disease, rather than a direct treatment effect.*
L1086: *The Applicant also evaluated HI-E per the updated IWG 2018 criteria (Platzbecker 2019). Based on these updated criteria, the rate of HI-E was 42.2% for the imetelstat group versus 13.3% with*
L1087: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 217779 imetelstat
## TRADENAME, imetelstat
L1088: placebo, which appears favorable for imetelstat. However, when evaluating the HI-E rate per IWG 2018 criteria according to baseline transfusion, the HI-E benefit for patients with low transfusion at baseline was marginal, as shown in Table 30. It should also be noted that evaluation of HI-E per IWG 2018 criteria was an ad hoc analysis without alpha allocation or multiplicity adjustment, therefore the results should be interpreted with caution.

L1095: ## Patient-reported outcomes
L1096: See discussion of PROs in “Efficacy Results – Secondary or exploratory COA (PRO) endpoints” section below.
L1097: ## Complete remission (CR) and partial remission (PR)
L1098: CR and PR per IWG 2006 criteria were secondary endpoints in Study MDS3001-Phase 3. According to the IWG 2006 criteria, CR is defined as bone marrow \le 5\% myeloblasts with normal maturation of all cell lines (persistent dysplasia will be noted) with peripheral blood counts as follows: hemoglobin \ge 11 g/dL, platelets \ge 100 \times 10^9/L, neutrophils \ge 1.0 \times 10^9/L, and blasts 0%. PR is defined as all CR criteria if abnormal before treatment except bone marrow blasts decreased by \ge 50\% over pretreatment but still &gt; 5\%. In both cases, response must last at least 4 weeks (Cheson 2006).
L1099: For the Phase 3 study, an independent review committee (IRC) was established to assess blinded data and adjudicate CR, PR, and marrow CR (mCR) responses per the IWG 2006 criteria for subjects with &gt; 5\% baseline blasts per central pathology reviewer assessment and a CR, PR, mCR, or cytogenetic response per investigator assessment. However, the Applicant reported that only 2 of the total 178 randomized subjects (1 subject in each treatment group) had &gt; 5\% baseline marrow blasts and were therefore eligible for IRC adjudication, and neither of these 2 subjects achieved a CR, PR, or mCR per the IRC at the time of the primary analysis.
L1100: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 217779 imetelstat
## TRADENAME, imetelstat
L1101: However, even when looking at CR and PR rates per investigator assessment, no subjects achieved a CR or PR response with imetelstat. It should be noted that 24% (28/118) subjects in the imetelstat arm were deemed not evaluable due to absent post-baseline bone marrow assessments.
L1102: ## **Clinical reviewer comment:**
L1103: *In December 2023, the Applicant updated the IRC charter to allow subjects with baseline marrow blasts \le 5% to be eligible for CR response, if they had at least one post-baseline bone marrow sample with central reviewer assessment. Based on this updated IRC charter, 83 subjects treated with imetelstat and 39 subjects treated with placebo were now eligible for CR response evaluation. Based on FDA adjudication of responses, at this "final analysis," the CR rate was at best 7/118 (6%) for the imetelstat arm. However, significant limitations were noted during the adjudication process, such as excess time allowed between the marrow and CBC confirming CR and missing data. The Applicant reported a CR rate of 0 for the placebo arm.*
L1104: *Notably, duration of CR was not evaluable, because the IRC only assessed best response and no data was collected on date of relapse from CR. Because of this, and the limitations previously noted, it's unclear if these CRs were clinically meaningful.*
L1105: ## **Cytogenetic response**
L1106: Cytogenetic complete response (CyCR) per IWG 2006 and updated IWG 2023 criteria is defined as the disappearance of baseline chromosomal abnormalities without the appearance of new ones. Cytogenetic partial response (CyPR) per IWG 2006 and updated IWG 2006 criteria is defined as a \ge 50% reduction in a baseline chromosomal abnormality (Cheson 2006; Zeidan 2023).
L1107: ## **Clinical Reviewer comment:**
L1108: *Only a small number of subjects in Study MDS3001-Phase 3 had any baseline cytogenetic abnormalities: 26 subjects in the imetelstat arm, and 13 in the placebo arm. Of these, 6 subjects achieved a cytogenetic CR in the imetelstat arm and 1 achieved a cytogenetic CR in the placebo arm. However, nearly half of subjects in each treatment arm were not evaluable for cytogenetic response analysis, all but one due to lack of a post-baseline sample (Table 31). Furthermore, it should be noted that cytogenetic response was merely an exploratory endpoint in this study, and this is not an endpoint that reflects direct clinical benefit. Due to these significant limitations, no definitive conclusions can be drawn regarding cytogenetic response in this study.*

L1115: ## Change in Mutation Burden
L1116: The Applicant reports that more subjects receiving imetelstat achieved a 50% or greater variant allele frequency (VAF) reduction in SF3B1, one of the most frequent mutations found in MDS associated with the ringed sideroblasts subtype, with a trend toward VAF reduction in other mutations such as TET2, DNMT3A, and ASXL1, as shown in Figure 18.
L1117: **Figure 18.** MDS3001-Phase 3: Subjects Achieving \ge 50\% Reduction from Baseline in SF3B1, TET2, DNMT3A and ASXL1 VAF (Mutation Biomarker Analysis Set)
L1118: Source: Study MDS3001 Phase 3 Clinical Study Report
L1119: Data cutoff 13 October 2022
L1120: ## Clinical Reviewer comment:
L1121: Despite the above results, reduction in mutation burden was an exploratory endpoint in Study MDS3001; the study was not designed to examine the impact of treatment on mutational burden. Furthermore, there are issues with the methodology of data collection. The next generation sequencing assay used for mutation analyses in the study has not been designed for VAF tracking. In addition, only a subset of patients had each relevant mutation at baseline and at least 1 post-baseline assessment making them evaluable for assessment. Samples for mutation analyses were collected from peripheral blood only, and at relatively sparse timepoints
L1122: (every 12 weeks and at the time of suspected response or progression). This analysis is also based on maximal VAF reductions from baseline, which may have been achieved at any timepoint. Without serial data at distinct pre-specified intervals for all patients, it is difficult to examine associations between VAF changes and the dynamics of RBC transfusion independence. Additionally, it is unclear whether a \ge 50\% VAF reduction in mutation burden is clinically significant, as there was no a priori well-justified rationale for use of this cutoff. Given the lack of evidence of a corresponding survival benefit, any impact on mutation burden does not appear to be of sufficient magnitude to result in a direct clinical benefit to the patient.
L1123: ## Overall Survival
L1124: Overall survival (OS) is considered both an efficacy and a safety endpoint. FDA requires collection and submission of OS data in oncology clinical trials to assess clinical benefit. Furthermore, one argument for therapeutically targeting transfusion-dependent anemia in subjects with lower-risk MDS is that higher RBC transfusion density has been reported to correlate with decreased overall survival (OS) (Santini et al. 2016). However, the association between a treatment-induced increased rate of RBC-transfusion independence and an improvement in OS has not been demonstrated in prospective studies.
L1125: In Study MDS3001-Phase 3, OS was included as a secondary endpoint, but no formal hypothesis testing was planned. Of note, no other trial has demonstrated an OS advantage in lower-risk MDS with transfusion-dependent anemia. The OS results at the time of the primary efficacy analysis and the most recent planned analysis are summarized in Table 32: below. At the time of the most recent analysis (data cutoff 5 January 2024), the median follow-up times were 32 and 28 months for the imetelstat and placebo groups, respectively. The stratified hazard ratio between the imetelstat and placebo arms was 0.98 (95% CI: 0.53, 1.82) (Table 32: and Figure 19). Median overall survival was 40 months (95% CI: 37, NE) for the imetelstat group and was not estimable for the placebo group. Numerically, a higher percentage of deaths was observed in the imetelstat arm both at the time of primary analysis (data cutoff 13 October 2022) and updated analysis (data cutoff 5 January 2024).
L1126: ## *Statistical Reviewer Comment:*
L1127: *The upper bound of the stratified OS hazard ratio 95% confidence interval of 1.82 indicates that potential harm cannot be ruled out. The numerically higher percentage of deaths in the imetelstat arm is also concerning. Furthermore, it's important to note that incorrect stratification accounted for major protocol deviations in 12.7% of subjects in the imetelstat group and 10.0% of subjects in the placebo group, as noted in the "Protocol Violations/Deviations" section above. Therefore, the stratified hazard ratio should be interpreted with caution. Notably, the unstratified OS hazard ratio (data cutoff 5 January 2024) is 1.11 (95% CI 0.61, 2.03), again illustrating that potential harm cannot be ruled out.*

L1144: ## Progression-free survival, disease progression, and progression to AML
L1145: The Applicant submitted a long-term efficacy update with data cutoff of 5 January 2024, including updated progression-free survival (PFS), disease progression, and progression to AML results. As of the most recent efficacy update (data cutoff 5 January 2024) with median follow-up of 32.2 months and 28.4 months in the imetelstat and placebo groups, respectively, the stratified PFS hazard ratio for imetelstat versus placebo was 0.85 (95% CI 0.44, 1.64), which is lower than the PFS hazard ratio at the time of primary analysis, which was 0.96 (95% CI 0.46, 2.04).
L1146: ## *Clinical Reviewer comment:*
L1147: *It is important to note that the definition of PFS used for this analysis was per IWG 2006 criteria (Cheson 2006), which is “disease progression or death from MDS.” Disease progression per the IWG 2006 definition includes not only progression to AML, but also an increase in blasts, decrease in granulocyte or platelet count, reduction in hemoglobin by at least 2 g/dL, or transfusion dependence. This definition of disease progression and PFS has not been validated for MDS, therefore no meaningful conclusions can be drawn from the PFS analysis.*
L1148: *With regard to progression to AML, as of the most updated analysis (data cutoff 5 January 2024), there were only 2 progression to AML events in each treatment arm. Additionally, the AML data are preliminary as noted by the Applicant above. Therefore, no meaningful conclusions can be drawn at this time regarding progression to AML in Study MDS3001.*
L1149: ## Dose/Dose Response
L1150: ## Data:
L1151: In the CSRs, study drug doses are expressed as milligrams of imetelstat sodium (the active substance), a salt of imetelstat. To convert a quantity of imetelstat salt to the equivalent quantity of imetelstat free acid, a factor of 0.9416 based on relative molar mass of the free acid form (4610 g/mol) to the sodium salt (4896 g/mol; 4610/4896 = 0.9416) is applied (ie, 1 mg imetelstat sodium = 0.9416 mg imetelstat).
L1152: The use of the 7.1 mg/kg imetelstat (free acid, equivalent to 7.5 mg/kg imetelstat-sodium) dose q4w is supported by exposure-efficacy analyses using population PK (Section 6.3.2.2 and 6.3.2.3) as well as analysis of the primary endpoint of RBC TI in the Phase 2 and Phase 3 MDS3001 studies.
L1153: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 217779 imetelstat
## TRADENAME, imetelstat
L1154: - In both the Phase 2 and Phase 3 studies, the majority of imetelstat-treated subjects achieved their first 8-week RBC TI interval while on the 7.1 mg/kg dose (81.0% and 74.5%, respectively; Applicant Table 33). Similar results were observed for the 24-week TI. Combined data from Phase 2 and Phase 3 indicate that 76.5% and 78.7% of imetelstat-treated subjects were on the 7.1 mg/kg dose at the onset of 8-week RBC TI and 24-week TI, respectively.

L1162: - The mean (SD) relative dose intensity (calculated as the average of actual cumulative dose level per cycle and takes into consideration dose reductions) with respect to the 7.1 mg/kg dose of imetelstat (active moiety, equivalent to 7.5 mg/kg imetelstat-sodium) was 90.5 (\pm 11.8) in the Phase 3 study and 89.92 (\pm 14.15) in the target population of the Phase 2 study (Table tsiex01_p2 and Table tsiex01_p1). Because dose reductions were to decrease the dose to approximately 60% to 80% of the intended 7.1 mg/kg dose, a mean relative dose intensity &gt;80% indicates subjects received 7.1 mg/kg for a greater proportion of the treatment course than for which they received a lower dose.
- The mean relative dose intensity with respect to the 7.1 mg/kg dose from the first dose until the onset of 8-week TI in both phases of the study was &gt; 95% (96.8% and 95.2% in Phase 2 and Phase 3, respectively) (SCE Table 20) indicating that most subjects were receiving the 7.1 mg/kg dose in the 4 weeks leading to the start of TI. Additionally, the
L1163: mean relative dose intensity was 93.16% and 89.16% when 24-week and 1-year TI were achieved (Table tsix21_p2 and Table tsix22_p2).
L1164: - The mean dose intensity from the first dose until the start of the TI and achievement of the TI provides an indication of the dose intensity of imetelstat needed to elicit and maintain clinical benefit of 8-week RBC TI, 24-week RBC TI, or 1-year TI after initiating treatment.
- In the imetelstat group of the Phase 3 study, mean relative dose intensity with respect to the 7.1 mg/kg dose remained steady around 90% throughout treatment in 8-week TI responders while declining below 80% in non-responders (SCE Figure 25); therefore, subjects who achieved an 8-week TI response required less fewer dose reductions than non-responders. Additionally, it can be surmised that dose reduction prior to achieving TI may have diminished the likelihood of attaining a TI response.
- In Phase 3, 35 (74.5%) of the 47 imetelstat-treated 8-week RBC TI responders did not have any dose reductions during the TI period. Among the 12 (25.5%) 8-week RBC TI responders with at least 1 dose reduction to 5.6 mg/kg (equivalent to 6.0 mg/kg imetelstat-sodium), 6 (12.8%) responders had a second dose reduction to 4.4 mg/kg (equivalent to 4.7 mg/kg imetelstat-sodium). The median time from the first and second dose reduction to the end of TI (ie, first transfusion after TI period) was 46.79 weeks (range: 3.9 to 119.9) and 44.50 weeks (range: 8.3 to 114.9) respectively (SCE Table 21), indicating that dose reductions did not lead to immediate loss of response, and response was maintained for a median of &gt; 10 months despite dose reductions.
L1165: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1166: While dose modifications occurred as mandated per protocol in both the Phase 2 and Phase 3 study, ≥ 90% overall dose intensity of the 7.1 mg/kg dose was achieved over the treatment period for all subjects. In both MDS3001 Phase 2 and Phase 3, &gt; 75% of subjects received 7.1 mg/kg at the time of onset of 8-week RBC TI, and &gt; 95% mean relative dose intensity was demonstrated from the first dose until the achievement of 8-week RBC TI. Overall relative dose intensity throughout treatment was higher for 8-week RBC TI responders than non-responders, indicative of a greater need for dose reductions in non-responders that is supported by the dose reduction data. Furthermore, dose reduction did not lead to loss of TI response in either 8-week RBC TI or 24-week RBC TI responders.
L1167: In summary, the 7.1 mg/kg dose demonstrated favorable efficacy and was well tolerated by those who achieved at least 8- or 24-week RBC TI. When indicated, subsequent dose reductions in responders allow for continued dosing and maintenance of durable transfusion-free periods. In addition, the results from exposure-efficacy analyses suggest that imetelstat doses (and accordingly C_{avg}) lower than 7.1 mg/kg q4w may result in reduced clinical efficacy.
