Reasons for dose reduction among patients receiving placebo included neutropenia (1 patient), transaminase increased (2 patients), and thrombocytopenia (1 patient)
L1182: ## Dose exploration across the drug development program
L1183: Imetelstat doses of 0.4 mg/kg to 11 mg/kg, alone or in combination with other agents, and dosing schedules from weekly to every 4 weeks were explored in a variety of indications throughout the drug development program. However, the evaluation of imetelstat in MDS has been limited to two studies. The first study evaluated imetelstat as a single agent given to patients with MDS or MDS/MPN at a dose of 7.1 mg/kg weekly with the possibility to dose escalate to 8.9 mg/kg weekly. The second study, MDS3001, evaluated the effect of imetelstat at 7.1 mg/kg every 4 weeks in patients with lower risk MDS.
L1184: ## Exposure-response relationship between imetelstat and Grade 3-4 thrombocytopenia
L1185: In Study MDS3001-Phase 3, higher maximum plasma concentrations of imetelstat (C_{max}) correlated with a higher probability of Grade 3-4 thrombocytopenia, as shown in Figure 22, which supports the observation of higher rates of thrombocytopenia with imetelstat (Section 8.2.4). This finding suggests that the starting dose and regimen of imetelstat may be further optimized.
L1186: **Figure 22:** MDS3001-Phase 3: Positive exposure-response relationship between imetelstat exposure and Grade 3-4 thrombocytopenia
L1187: Source: FDA analysis using ER_THR.csv dataset
Data cutoff 13 October 2022
L1188: ## Exposure-response relationship between imetelstat and 8-week RBC-TI response
L1189: Although there was also a positive exposure-response relationship between the average plasma concentration of imetelstat (C_{avg}) and 8-week RBC-TI response, this analysis was significantly limited by the single dose and narrow exposure range studied in patients with MDS and is limited by confounding factors, such as frequent dose modifications and subjects dropping off treatment.
L1190: In summary, although other dose levels of imetelstat have been explored in myelofibrosis and solid tumors, dose exploration in MDS has been limited to a single dose level. Therefore, it is unclear whether the proposed starting dose is the optimal dose for the proposed indication, particularly in light of the high rate of dose modifications, toxicities, and low dose intensity with imetelstat seen in Study MDS3001. A post-marketing study of additional dose levels of imetelstat may be warranted.
L1191: ## Durability of Response
L1192: ## Data:
L1193: Median duration RBC TI was 51.6 weeks (95% CI: 26.86, 83.86; range: 8.0 to 136.9 weeks) in the imetelstat group versus 13.3 weeks (95% CI: 8.00, 24.86; range: 8.0 to 111.3 weeks) in the placebo group based on Kaplan-Meier estimates (Applicant Table 26 and Applicant Figure 12). The hazard ratio between the treatment groups was 0.23 (95% CI: 0.091, 0.571, P &lt; 0.001).
L1194: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 217779 imetelstat
## TRADENAME, imetelstat
L1195: Approximately 83% of 8-week RBC TI responders had a single continuous TI period, further demonstrating the durability of the response (Applicant Figure 7).
L1196: Additional evidence of long continuous TI achieved with imetelstat can be seen among the subjects who achieved 24- week RBC-TI. Median duration of RBC TI for these subjects was 80.0 weeks (95% CI: 51.57, non-estimable [NE]; range: 24.9 to 136.9) and NE (95% CI: 24.86, NE; range: 24.9, 111.3 weeks) in the placebo group (Applicant Table 26). The hazard ratio between the imetelstat and placebo treatment groups was 0.59 (95% CI: 0.069, 5.103, P= 0.655).
L1197: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1198: Transfusion dependence and high transfusion burden have been linked to reduced OS and PFS and increased risk of transformation to AML and subsequent achievement of TI with therapy has been shown to contribute to mitigation of those reductions. Therefore, achieving durable TI and thus alleviating the signs and symptoms associated with it, is an important clinical outcome in LR MDS. The durability of continuous RBC TI seen with imetelstat across LR MDS subgroups is unprecedented and has the potential to translate to positive long-term clinical outcomes such as OS.
L1199: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1200: See FDA Assessment in "Efficacy Results – Secondary and other relevant endpoints" for comment on the duration of the longest RBC TI interval.
L1201: ## **Persistence of Effect**
L1202: ## Data:
L1203: As of the 13 Oct 2022 clinical cutoff date, the median follow-up time in Phase 3 was 19.48 and 17.51 months in the imetelstat and placebo groups, respectively (Table tsids07_p2).
L1204: The 1-year RBC TI rate (
L1205: Applicant Figure 10) and the duration of TI (Applicant Figure 12) were included to assess the durability of the effect of imetelstat on TI.
L1206: In Phase 3, 1-year RBC TI was achieved by 13.6% of imetelstat-treated subjects versus 1.7% of placebo-treated subjects, with the difference between the 2 treatment groups of 11.9% (95% CI: 1.86%, 19.87%, P = 0.012) (Table tefti07_p2 and (
L1207: Applicant Figure 10). While statistically significant compared with placebo, the imetelstat data are expected to mature further, with longer-term follow-up still ongoing in the study.
L1208: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1209: Transfusion dependence and high transfusion burden have been linked to reduced OS and PFS and increased risk of transformation to AML and subsequent achievement of TI with therapy has been shown to contribute to mitigation of those reductions. Therefore, achieving durable TI and thus alleviating the signs and symptoms associated with it, is an important clinical outcome in LR MDS. The durability of continuous RBC TI seen with imetelstat across LR MDS subgroups is unprecedented and has the potential to translate to positive long-term clinical outcomes such as OS.
L1210: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1211: The Applicant provided an updated analysis of 1-year RBC-TI in the Phase 3 study with data cutoff of 13 October 2023, with a median time on study of 32.2 months for imetelstat-treated subjects and 28.4 months for placebo-treated subjects. Based on this updated data cutoff, the rate of 1-year RBC-TI is 17.8% (95% CI 11.4, 25.9) for imetelstat-treated subjects versus 1.8% (95% CI 0, 8.9) for placebo-treated subjects, with the difference between the 2 groups of 16.1% (95% CI 5.6, 24.6). This suggests that a small subset of patients may achieve a prolonged period of RBC-TI with imetelstat treatment. However, it should be noted that 1-year RBC-TI was not a prespecified endpoint in the study.
L1212: ## **Efficacy Results – Secondary or exploratory COA (PRO) endpoints**
L1213: ## **Data:**
L1214: ## **Fatigue Measured by FACIT-Fatigue Score**
L1215: The FACIT-Fatigue Scale is a QoL instrument that was included in the study to measure fatigue from the patient perspective. A higher score indicates improvement (i.e., less fatigue on the FACIT-Fatigue Scale).
L1216: The results demonstrate that imetelstat treatment did not cause a clinically meaningful deterioration in fatigue compared to placebo; rather, subjects treated with imetelstat demonstrated favorable, sustained, meaningful improvement in fatigue (defined by a \ge 3-point increase on the FACIT Fatigue Scale for \ge 2 consecutive non-missing treatment cycles) compared with placebo (50.0% in the imetelstat group compared with 40.4% in the placebo group) (SCE Section 3.2.4.1.2). Furthermore, imetelstat-treated subjects experienced a shorter median time to reported first sustained meaningful improvement in fatigue versus placebo; 28.3 vs 65.0 weeks, HR = 1.34 (95% CI, 0.82–2.20). After 12 weeks, more subjects in the imetelstat group reported improvement in the FACIT Fatigue Scale than placebo (SCE Section 3.2.4.1.3).
L1217: Additional analysis demonstrated that subjects who reported TEAE of Grade 3 or 4 neutropenia or thrombocytopenia had the same rates of sustained meaningful improvement in fatigue (42/80; 52.5% and 39/73; 53.4% respectively) as the total imetelstat population (50%) (SCE Section 3.2.4.1.2).
L1218: ## Association of Improvement in Fatigue with TI
L1219: Further analysis demonstrated that in the imetelstat group, a significantly higher proportion of 8-week and 24-week TI responders experienced sustained meaningful improvement in fatigue scores than non-responders (Applicant Figure 15). This association was not observed in the placebo group (Table tspqs71_p2 and Figure fspqs38_p2). The majority of the 8-week TI responders in the imetelstat group consistently had sustained meaningful improvement in FACIT Fatigue scores through the durable TI intervals, as indicated by the yellow stars in Applicant Figure 23.
L1220: **Applicant Figure 23:** TI Periods are Associated with Meaningful Improvement in FACIT-Fatigue Score in MDS3001 Phase 3
L1221: RBC = red blood cell; TI = transfusion independence
L1222: *Timepoint of meaningful improvement in fatigue when FACIT-Fatigue questionnaire completed

L1224: ## Other PRO Measures
L1225: Exploratory analyses were performed to investigate the impact of imetelstat on other PRO concepts of interest (COIs) that were identified as relevant for patients with LR MDS: physical function, dyspnea, pain, bruising, and systemic symptoms. The analyses suggested that subjects in the imetelstat group had better outcomes in terms of dyspnea than those in the placebo group. Although no improvement from baseline was observed for other pre-identified COIs, subjects in the imetelstat group showed no more deterioration in any of the pre-identified COIs than those in the placebo group (SCE Section 3.2.4.1.5).
L1226: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1227: Level of fatigue is an important patient-centered consideration for treatment of LR MDS as patients with chronic transfusion-dependent anemia suffer from exhaustive fatigue (Platzbecker, 2021). Fatigue was selected as the main PRO COI for study MDS3001 as it is a central concern to patients and treatment providers of LR MDS. Maintaining the same levels or avoiding deterioration in fatigue with treatment is an important therapeutic goal. The improvement in fatigue observed in subjects who achieved TI, indicates that imetelstat is targeting multiple core symptoms of LR MDS simultaneously, bringing greater clinical benefit to patients than current treatment options.
L1228: Existing Functional Assessment of Cancer Therapy – Anemia-Related Effects (FACT-An) and Quality of Life in Myelodysplasia Scale (QUALMS) composite scores (FACT-An total score, FACT-An Trial Outcome Index score, QUALMS physical burden score, and QUALMS total score) suggest better general health outcomes for subjects treated with imetelstat than those with placebo (SCE Section 3.2.4.1.5).
L1229: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1230: Patient-generated disease symptom information was collected in MDS3001 using the FACT-Anemia and QUALMS measures, among other PRO measures. The assessment frequency was day 1 of each cycle until end of treatment and then every 12-16 weeks during follow up. Patient-reported outcomes (PRO) were included as non-multiplicity controlled exploratory endpoints.
L1231: The proportion of patients providing a response out of those expected to respond was &gt;85% in both arms during the first year of the trial. FDA examined the patient-reported outcomes data for supportive evidence that treatment with imetelstat provided improvement in anemia related symptoms, however there was no clear evidence that patients treated with imetelstat experienced sustained improvement in fatigue or other anemia related symptoms. For example, the Applicant's PRO endpoint of interest was the proportion of patients who experienced deterioration in fatigue, with similar proportion in each arm observed (43% imetelstat versus 46% placebo). FDA examined categorical responses for key PRO items such as
L1232: the FACT-Anemia question GP1 “I have lack of energy” and noted similar results between arms during the first 6 months (Figure 24).
L1233: **Figure 24:** Study MDS3001-Phase 3: Bar plot of Distribution of Categorical Responses for FACT-An Item GP1 (I Have Lack of Energy) at Each Cycle (ITT analysis set)
L1234: Source: Applicant's Response to FDA Information Request dated 10 January 2024
L1235: Of note, there is no clear evidence that patients treated with imetelstat experienced sustained improvement in fatigue or other anemia related symptoms. Additionally, it should be noted that although patient-reported deterioration in fatigue results were similar between arms, more patients in the imetelstat arm experienced all grade fatigue per investigator AE reporting. More frequent assessment of patient-reported fatigue would have provided more comprehensive information, particularly the experience within cycles (and not just the seven days preceding Day 1 of a new cycle as recorded per the FACIT-Fatigue questionnaire). FDA notes the median duration of the longest episode of fatigue per investigator AE reporting was also much longer for patients treated with imetelstat, as shown in Table 35. Given the discrepancies between patient-reported and investigator-reported fatigue, as well as the fact that the PRO findings were purely exploratory in this study, no clear conclusions can be drawn regarding the impact of imetelstat on fatigue based on Study MDS3001.

L1241: Data cutoff 13 October 2022
L1242: In order to support a marketing application, transfusion independence data should ideally be supported by evidence of direct clinical benefit to the patient (e.g., survival benefit, CR/PR benefit, or improvement in quality of life). Therefore, in the absence of a survival benefit or CR/PR benefit, the question of whether there is an improvement in patient experience becomes paramount. Although these PRO findings were purely exploratory and descriptive, FDA found no compelling evidence to support improvement in fatigue or other anemia related symptoms.
L1243: ## Additional Analyses Conducted on the Individual Trial
L1244: ## Data:
L1245: ## **TA and hTERT Reduction and Association with TI Response**
L1246: In both the pivotal Phase 3 study and the supportive Phase 2 study, imetelstat treatment induced a \ge 50\% reduction from baseline in TA and hTERT mRNA levels (optimal pharmacodynamic effect). A \ge 50\% reduction in TA was observed in 60% of subjects in the imetelstat group compared with 37.5% of subjects in the placebo group in the Phase 3 study and in 42.9% of subjects overall (N = 57) and in 23.1% of subjects in the target population (N = 38) of the Phase 2 study.
L1247: In the Phase 3 study, \ge 50\% reduction from baseline in hTERT expression was similar among the imetelstat-treated (43.2%) and placebo-treated subjects (54.0%). In the Phase 2 study, a reduction of \ge 50\% in hTERT expression was observed in 54.3% and 58.8% of subjects in the target population and overall population, respectively.
L1248: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 217779 imetelstat
## TRADENAME, imetelstat
L1249: Optimal pharmacodynamic effect was associated with clinical response (8-week, 24-week, or 1-year RBC TI) (Applicant Figure 25). In the Phase 3 study, the rate of clinical response in the imetelstat group was higher in subjects who achieved the optimal pharmacodynamic effect compared with those who did not, but not statistically significant: for 8-week RBC TI (50% versus 39.5%; P = .408), 24-week RBC TI (36.5% versus 30.2%; P = .663), and 1-year RBC TI (17.3% versus 14.0%; P = .780). In contrast, in the placebo group, RBC TI rates were higher in subjects who did not have \ge 50\% decrease from baseline in TA or hTERT. Similar results were observed in the target population the Phase 2 study: for 8-week TI (63.2% versus 18.8%; P = 0.016), 24-week TI (57.9% versus 6.3%; P = 0.002), and 1 year TI (52.6% versus 6.3%; P = 0.004) (MDS3001 CSR Phase 2 Section 11.2.4.1).
L1250: In the Phase 2 study, median duration of RBC TI was 68.4 weeks in subjects who achieved optimal pharmacodynamic effect compared to 5.5 weeks in subjects who did not (HR = 0.326), log-rank P = 0.003). Similar results were observed in the overall population (MDS3001 CSR Phase 2 Section 11.2.4.1).
L1251: **Applicant Figure 25: Transfusion Independence by Optimal Pharmacodynamic Effect (Phase 3, Biomarker Analysis Set)**

L1255: Source: MDS3001CSR Phase 3 Figure 10
L1256: ## Association with TI
L1257: Additional analysis demonstrated that effect of imetelstat on TI response was associated with baseline TA levels. Subjects who had baseline TA level &gt; median value had significantly higher rates of 8-week and 24-week RBC TI with imetelstat compared to Placebo treatment, with 8-week RBC TI rate of 62.5% (15/24, P = .004) and 24-week TI rate of 50% (12/24, P = .018) in imetelstat group vs. 0% in placebo group (0/9). In the subgroup with baseline TA level ≤ median value, compared to placebo, imetelstat also had higher rates of 8 week (41.2% vs. 29.4%; P = .621) and 24-week RBC TI (29.4% vs. 0%; P = .074), although these differences were not statistically significant (Applicant Figure 26). This suggests that patients with higher baseline TA level may be more susceptible to imetelstat treatment, which is consistent with mechanism of action of imetelstat.
L1258: Analyses based on baseline hTERT level yielded demonstrated that imetelstat had significantly superior RBC TI rates compared to placebo treatment regardless of baseline hTERT level: in the subgroup with higher hTERT level (&gt; median value), 8-week RBC TI was 40.8% versus 11.5% (P = .008); 24-week RBC TI was 34.7% versus 0% (P &lt; .001); in the subgroup with lower hTERT level (≤ median value), 8-week RBC TI was 45.1% versus 20% (P = .046); 24-week RBC TI was 29.4% versus 8% (P = .042). Within each treatment group, these was no significant difference in RBC TI responses between the hTERT subgroups (Applicant Figure 26).
L1259: In the imetelstat group, there was no significant difference in RBC TI responses between subjects with TL ≤ median value and subjects with TL &gt; median value. However, in the subgroup with shorter TL (≤ median value), imetelstat treatment had significantly higher RBC TI response rates compared with placebo treatment: 8-week RBC TI was 35% versus 12.5% (P = .020); 24-week RBC TI was 32.5% versus 3.1% (P = .001) (Applicant Figure 26).
L1260: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 217779 imetelstat
## TRADENAME, imetelstat

L1268: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1269: On-target activity of imetelstat was demonstrated by reductions in both TA and hTERT expression. An association between hTERT reduction from baseline and TI response was evident, with patients who achieved 8- or 24-week TI having significantly higher proportions of &gt; 50\% reduction in hTERT expression. Such an association was not observed with placebo.
L1270: Together, these results demonstrate that on-target activity of imetelstat results in clinical outcomes.
L1271: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1272: FDA did not adjudicate reduction in telomerase activity (TA) and human telomerase reverse transcriptase (hTERT) expression, which was an exploratory endpoint in this study. Based on the Applicant's analyses reported above, FDA's interpretation is that \ge 50\% reduction in TA was observed in both the imetelstat and placebo groups, though the rate was higher for the imetelstat group (50% vs 37.5%). However, \ge 50\% reduction from baseline in hTERT expression was higher in placebo-treated subjects compared to imetelstat-treated subjects (54.0% vs 43.2%). Therefore, the mechanism of action of imetelstat cannot be determined with currently available data.
L1273: ## 8.1.3. **Integrated Review of Effectiveness**
L1274: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1275: The Applicant is seeking approval of imetelstat for the treatment of transfusion-dependent anemia in adult patients with low- to intermediate-1 risk MDS who have not responded to, or have lost response to, or are ineligible for ESA. This application is based primarily on the results of Study MDS3001-Phase 3. Study MDS3001-Phase 3 met the statistical goals for the primary endpoint of 8-week RBC-TI (39.8% imetelstat versus 15.0% placebo; p &lt; 0.001) and key secondary endpoint 24-week RBC-TI (28.0% imetelstat versus 3.3% placebo; p &lt; 0.001). Although the median duration of RBC-TI was only 5.0 weeks (95% CI 4.0, 7.7) for imetelstat versus 3.9 weeks (95% CI 3.6, 4.0) for placebo in all subjects, the median duration of RBC-TI in 8-week RBC-TI responders was 51.6 weeks (95% CI 26.9, 83.9) for imetelstat versus 13.3 weeks (95% 8.0, 24.9) for placebo, indicating that a subset of patients has a clinically meaningful duration of benefit.
L1276: While MDS3001-Phase 3 met its primary and key secondary endpoints, questions remain regarding the nature of these effects. Imetelstat is purported to have a direct effect on the underlying MDS through telomerase inhibition resulting in cell-cycle arrest, apoptosis, or senescence of malignant cells. However, a statistically significant treatment effect was not observed on the key secondary endpoint of HI-E per IWG 2006 criteria (63.6% vs. 51.7%) or other secondary endpoints reflective of a disease-modifying effect such as CR or PR (0% vs. 0% at the time of primary analysis; 5.9% at best vs. 0% at the time of updated analysis) and OS (HR=0.98 [95% CI: 0.53, 1.82] at most recent data cut). In addition, the patient-reported outcomes collected in Study MDS3001-Phase 3 did not reflect an improvement in fatigue or other anemia-related symptoms. There are also residual uncertainties regarding the impact of imetelstat on overall medical resource utilization (see Section 8.1.4 under Additional Efficacy Considerations) and the applicability of the trial results to the US population, given the limitations of the medical resource utilization analyses and lower RBC-TI rates in the US population compared to the non-US population. Additionally, very few patients in Study
L1277: MDS3001 had prior treatment with luspatercept, which is now approved for frontline treatment of transfusion-dependent anemia in patients with lower-risk MDS.
L1278: In conclusion, while a subset of patients appears to have a clinically meaningful benefit with imetelstat, it is important to weigh the potential benefits of imetelstat against the potential risks, which are further discussed in the safety review below.
L1279: ## 8.1.4. Assessment of Efficacy Across Trials
L1280: ## Study MDS3001-Phase 2
L1281: ## Study Design
L1282: Part 1 of the MDS3001 study was designed as a single arm Phase 2 study. Initially ~30 subjects were planned to be enrolled. Per Amendment 2, enrollment was expanded to include ~22 additional subjects for a total planned enrollment of ~55 subjects. The open-label, single-arm design was considered appropriate to provide supportive safety data and demonstrate preliminary clinical benefit of treatment with imetelstat in MDS at 7.1 mg/kg (active moiety, equivalent to 7.5 mg/kg imetelstat-sodium) every 4 weeks in the Phase 2 setting and to support the initiation of the randomized, placebo-controlled Phase 3 part of the study (Part 2).
L1283: The primary objective of the Phase 2 study was to evaluate the efficacy and safety of imetelstat in transfusion-dependent subjects with low- or intermediate-1 risk MDS that is relapsed/refractory to ESA treatment.
L1284: The primary efficacy endpoint of the Phase 2 study was the rate of RBC TI lasting \ge 8 weeks and was calculated as described for the pivotal Phase 3 study.
L1285: Key secondary endpoints of the Phase 2 study were:
L1286: - Safety of imetelstat in subjects with MDS (e.g., incidence, intensity, and type of AEs, vital signs measurements, clinical laboratory values, ECGs, and deaths)
- 24 week RBC TI rate, defined as the proportion of subjects without any RBC transfusion during at least any consecutive 24 weeks (168 days) starting from Study Day 1
- Time to the 8 week (or 24-week) RBC TI, defined as the interval from Study Day 1 to the first day of the first 8 week (or 24-week) RBC TI period
- Duration of RBC TI, defined as the first day of the longest RBC TI period to the date of the first RBC transfusion after the TI period started
- Rate of hematologic improvement, including HI-E, per modified IWG 2006
- Rates of CR, PR, or mCR per modified IWG 2006
L1287: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 217779 imetelstat
## TRADENAME, imetelstat
L1288: - OS, defined as the interval from Study Day 1 to death from any cause. Survival time of living subjects was censored on the last date a subject was known to be alive or lost to follow-up.
- PFS, defined as the time interval from Study Day 1 to the first date of disease progression or death from any cause, whichever occurred first
- Time to progression to AML, defined as the interval from Study Day 1 to the date of AML diagnosis
- Amount and relative change in units of RBC transfusions
- Rate of myeloid growth factors usage, defined as the proportion of subjects who received any myeloid growth factors starting from Study Day 1; duration of myeloid growth factor administered starting from Study Day 1
- PK parameters (e.g., C_{max}, AUC0-t), and immunogenicity of imetelstat (e.g., antibodies to imetelstat)
L1289: The exploratory endpoints of the Phase 2 study were:
L1290: - TA and hTERT at baseline and the change from baseline, TL at baseline only
- Cytogenetic status at baseline and change over time for cytogenetic response
- Mutation status at baseline and change over time including at the time of suspected response (CR, PR, HI-E) or progressive disease

L1311: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1312: The Phase 2 study demonstrated a high rate of RBC TI lasting at least 8 weeks. Results obtained in the target population which is the same as the LR MDS population of the pivotal Phase 3 study, were consistent and supportive of the Phase 3 study. High rates of 8-week TI were observed in most key LR MDS subgroups including including RS status, baseline transfusion burden, and baseline serum EPO levels.
L1313: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1314: ## Phase 2 study design
L1315: Study MDS3001-Part 1 (Phase 2) was a multicenter, single-arm, open-label trial evaluating imetelstat in patients with transfusion dependent anemia due to IPSS low- or intermediate-1 risk MDS who are relapsed/refractory to or ineligible for ESA.
L1316: The study initially planned to enroll about 30 subjects. However, upon review of the efficacy and safety data among 32 subjects, a higher RBC-TI rate was noted in a subset of 13 subjects with non-del(5q) karyotype and without prior treatment with either HMA or lenalidomide. Thus, the Phase 2 study was expanded with Amendment 2 to enroll an additional 25 subjects who met these criteria. Thus, there were a total of 57 subjects enrolled in the Phase 2 study, 38 of whom met these criteria (referred to as the "target population").
L1317: The starting dose was imetelstat sodium 7.5 mg/kg (equivalent to imetelstat 7.1 mg/kg) IV every 4 weeks. The study protocol initially allowed for dose escalation to imetelstat sodium 9.4 mg/kg, however it was noted that the higher dose was not associated with improved TI; therefore, as of Amendment 2, dose escalation was no longer allowed.
L1318: The primary efficacy endpoint was 8-week RBC-TI, defined as the proportion of subjects without any RBC transfusion during at least any consecutive 8 weeks (56 days) starting from Study Day 1 until subsequent anti-cancer therapy, if any. Study Day 1 was defined as the day of the first dose. Secondary and exploratory endpoints were as noted above by the Applicant.
L1319: ## Primary endpoint (8-week RBC-TI) results
L1320: The rate of 8-week RBC-TI was 42.1% (95% CI 26.3, 59.2) for the target population and 36.8% (95% CI 24.5, 50.7) for all subjects in the Phase 2 study, which is generally consistent with the 8-week RBC-TI rate observed in the Phase 3 study. Subgroup analyses are discussed in the "Subpopulations" section below.

L1329: ## Key Secondary Efficacy Variables
L1330: - The rate of 24-week RBC TI in the target population was 31.6%. Among all subjects overall, the 24-week RBC TI rate was 24.6% (Applicant Table 37).
- Imetelstat demonstrated clinical benefit across all subgroups in the target population, including RS status (37.0% [RS+] and 18.2% [RS-]), baseline transfusion burden (35.3% [\le 6 units] and 28.6% [&gt; 6 units]), IPSS risk category (20.8% [low] and 50.0% [intermediate-1]),
L1331: and baseline serum EPO levels (40.0% [\le 500 mU/mL] and 16.7% [&gt; 500 mU/mL]) (shown for the Target population in Applicant Figure 28). In both the target population and overall, over 91% of the subjects who achieved at least 24-week TI went on to achieve at least 1-year TI.
L1332: - Duration of RBC TIMedian duration of RBC TI among the 16 subjects in the target population who achieved 8-week RBC TI was 74.5 weeks. Among the 21 subjects in the overall subject population who achieved 8-week RBC TI, median duration of RBC TI was 69.6 weeks. Median duration of RBC TI among both the 12 subjects in the target population and the 14 subjects in the overall subject population who achieved 24-week RBC TI was 85.9 weeks.
- Hematologic improvement:Imetelstat treatment led to a sustained increase in Hb levels. The median Hb rise from pretreatment in the longest RBC TI period for 8-week RBC TI responders was 3.96 g/dL in the target population and 3.75 g/dL in the overall subject population. Median Hb peak in the longest RBC TI interval was 11.6 g/dL in the target population and 11.4 g/dL in the overall subject population.
- Median relative change from baseline in RBC transfusion burden during the best 8-week interval was -77.8% in the target population and -66.7% in the overall subject population.
- The rate of HI-E per modified IWG 2006 criteria in the target population was 65.8%, which included 13 (34.2%) subjects with Hb increase \ge 1.5 g/dL above pretreatment and lasting \ge 8 weeks. Among all subjects overall, the rate of HI-E was 61.4%, which included 16 (28.1%) subjects with Hb increase \ge 1.5 g/dL above pretreatment and lasting \ge 8 weeks. One subject (2.6%) in the target population had HI-P. The HI-P in the overall subject population was 1.8%.
- The rate of hematologic improvement per revised IWG 2018 criteria was also analyzed. Among subjects in the target population (LTB and HTB), the HI-E response rate was 50.0%. Among LTB subjects (n = 4), the HI-E response rate was 50.0%. Among HTB subjects (n = 34), 32.4% of subjects had a major HI-E response, and 50% of subjects had a minor HI-E response. HI-E rates were similar in the overall subject population.
L1333: ## Other Secondary Efficacy Variables
L1334: - Median time to 8-week RBC TI among the 16 subjects in the target population and 21 subjects in the overall subject population who achieved 8-week RBC TI was 8.3 weeks.
- Median time to 24-week RBC TI among the 12 subjects in the target population and 14 subjects in the overall subject population who achieved 24-week RBC TI was 8.8 weeks.
- Per investigator assessment, in the target population, 4 (10.5%) subjects had CR, 5 (13.2%) subjects had mCR, and no subjects had PR, for an overall response rate (CR + PR + mCR) of
L1335: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 217779 imetelstat
## TRADENAME, imetelstat
L1336: 23.7%. In the overall subject population, 5 (8.8%) subjects had CR, 7 (12.3%) subjects had mCR, and there were no subjects with PR, for an overall response rate of 21.1%. Of note, 91.2% of subjects in the overall subject population had \le 5% bone marrow blasts at baseline but were still evaluated for CR/PR/mCR per investigator.
L1337: - Median OS was 55.2 months in the target population and 55.3 months in the overall subject population.
- Median estimated PFS was 34.2 months in both the target population and the overall subject population. Eleven (28.9%) subjects in the target population and 13 (22.8%) subjects in the overall subject population had disease progression.
- With post-treatment follow-up, 4 subjects, all in the target population, had reported progression to AML as of the cutoff date. Median estimated time to progression to AML was 44.2 months in the target population. In the overall subject population, the median estimated time to progression to AML was not reached.
- Thirteen (34%) subjects in the target population and 19 (33%) subjects in the overall subject population had at least 1 dose of myeloid growth factors as of the cutoff date.
L1338: ## Exploratory Efficacy Variables
L1339: - A reduction of \ge 50% in TA and hTERT (optimal pharmacodynamic effect) was observed in 23.1% and 54.3% of subjects, respectively, in the target population. In the overall population, 42.9% and 58.8% of subjects achieved TA and hTERT \ge 50% reduction from baseline, respectively. In the target population, a higher response rate was observed in subjects who achieved the optimal pharmacodynamic effect compared with those who did not, for 8-week RBC TI (63.2% versus 18.8%; P = 0.016), 24-week RBC TI (57.9% versus 6.3%; P = 0.002), and 1-year RBC TI (52.6% versus 6.3%; P = 0.004). Median duration of RBC TI was 68.4 weeks in subjects who achieved optimal pharmacodynamic effect compared to 5.5 weeks in subjects who did not (HR = 0.326), log-rank P = 0.003). Similar results were observed in the overall population.
- In the target population, among 31 subjects with baseline mutation data available, 27 had SF3B1 mutations, of whom 12 subjects also had post-treatment mutation data. Eleven subjects had a reduction (range: -9.8% to -93%) in SF3B1 VAF from baseline. The reduction of SF3B1 VAF was significantly correlated with TI duration; with a longer TI duration associated with a greater reduction in SF3B1 VAF (Pearson correlation coefficient r = -0.71; P = 0.010).
- There was no significant difference in 8-week, 24-week and 1-year TI rate in subjects with or without baseline mutations. There was no significant difference in durable TI rates for mutation defined subgroups based on genes involved in different biological functions, including splicing process, epigenetic regulation, or receptors/kinases.
L1340: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1341: In the Phase 2 study, imetelstat induced clinically meaningful and durable TI and a high HI-E rate in a population of heavily RBC transfusion-dependent, ESA-relapsed/refractory lower risk MDS, irrespective of the presence of RS. Correlations between on-target effects and decreased mutation and cytogenetic burden in responders suggest the potential for disease modification with imetelstat treatment. Imetelstat demonstrated an acceptable safety profile that was manageable and generally reversible, with no new safety signals or evidence of cumulative toxicity identified.
L1342: The results in the target population of this study are highly consistent and supportive of the pivotal Phase 3 study in the same LR MDS patient population.
L1343: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1344: The rate of 24-week RBC-TI was 31.6% (95% CI 17.5, 48.7) for the target population and 24.6% (95% CI 14.1, 7.8) for all subjects in the Phase 2 study, which is generally consistent with the 24-week RBC-TI rate observed in the Phase 3 study. Subgroup analyses are discussed in the "subpopulations" section below.
L1345: The other secondary and exploratory endpoints in the Phase 2 study were not adjudicated by the FDA. However, based on the Applicant's analyses which are summarized above, the results were generally consistent with the results of the Phase 3 study. For example, the HI-E rate per IWG 2006 criteria was 65.8% in the target population and 61.4% in the overall population in Phase 2, which are similar to the HI-E rate of 63.6% observed for the imetelstat arm of the Phase 3 study.
L1346: However, it should be noted that the CR rate per investigator assessment was 10.5% for the target population and 8.8% for the overall population in the Phase 2 study, which is higher than the CR rate of 0% at the time of primary analysis in the Phase 3 study.
L1347: Lastly, it should be noted that time-to-event endpoints cannot be adequately assessed in a single-arm trial due to the lack of a placebo comparator; therefore, no definitive conclusions can be drawn based on the median PFS or median OS reported by the Applicant for the Phase 2 study above.
L1348: ## Subpopulations
L1349: ## Data:
L1350: Subgroup analyses were performed to assess the consistency and robustness of the treatment benefit mainly for the rate of RBC TI and the rate of HI-E. Subgroup analysis could be performed
L1351: for other selected efficacy and safety endpoints. The following subgroup variables and the cutoff values were subject to change if warranted to better represent the data.
L1352: - Sex (male versus female)
- Age (&lt; 65 versus \ge 65 years old; &lt; 75 versus \ge 75 years old)
- Time since initial diagnosis (&lt; 2 versus \ge 2 years)
- WHO Classification (RS+ versus RS-)
- Prior RBC transfusion burden (\le 6 versus &gt; 6 units RBC; &lt; 8, 8 to 12, or 12 units RBC)
- Transfusion burden per IWG 2018 (HTB versus LTB)
- Baseline bone marrow blasts per central lab (\le 5% versus &gt; 5%)
- Baseline platelets (&lt; 150 \times 10^9/L versus \ge 150 \times 10^9/L)
- Baseline neutrophils (&lt; 1.5 \times 10^9/L versus \ge 1.5 \times 10^9/L versus)
- IPSS risk group (low risk versus intermediate-1 risk)
- Number of cytopenia (0 to 1 versus 2 to 3)
- Prior ESA use (yes versus no)
- Baseline serum EPO levels (\le 500 versus &gt; 500 mU/mL)
- Eastern Cooperative Oncology Group (ECOG) score (0 versus 1 to 2)
- IPSS-R cytogenetic risk groups (very good/good, intermediate, or poor/very poor) (Greenberg, 2012)
- IPSS-R Prognostic risk groups (very low, low, intermediate, high, or very high) (Greenberg, 2012)
L1353: ## Subgroup Analysis in Study MDS3001-Phase 3
L1354: Subgroup analysis is discussed in Efficacy Analysis of the Phase 3 study (Section 8.1.2). Subgroup analysis of 8-week and 24-week TI (primary and key secondary endpoints) based on demographics and the most relevant disease characteristics, is shown in Applicant Figure 8 and Applicant Figure 11, respectively. Subgroup analyses for duration of response and HI-E are summarized in the sections describing the results for these endpoints (Section 8.1.2).
L1355: ## Subgroup Analysis in Study MDS3001-Phase 2
L1356: ## &gt; 8-week and &gt; 24-week RBC-TI
L1357: Imetelstat demonstrated comparable clinical benefit of &gt; 8-week TI across most subgroups in the target population (N = 38), including RS status (44.4% [RS+] and 36.4% [RS-]), baseline transfusion burden (47.1% [\le 6 units] and 38.1% [&gt; 6 units]), and baseline serum EPO levels (48.0% [\le 500 mU/mL] and 33.3% [&gt; 500 mU/mL]).
L1358: Subgroup analysis by baseline characteristics for the target population is shown in Applicant Figure 28. Subgroup analyses for the overall subject population is shown in Figure fefti01a_p1.
L1359: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 217779 imetelstat
## TRADENAME, imetelstat
L1360: Similarly, clinical benefit in terms of &gt; 24-week TI was observed across all subgroups in the target population, including RS status (37.0% [RS+] and 18.2% [RS-]), baseline transfusion burden (35.3% [\le 6 units] and 28.6% [&gt; 6 units]), IPSS risk category (20.8% [low] and 50.0% [intermediate-1]), and baseline serum EPO levels (40.0% [\le 500 mU/mL] and 16.7% [&gt; 500 mU/mL]) (Table tefti06_p1 and Figure fefti02_p1).
L1361: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1362: In the Phase 3 MDS3001 pivotal study, imetelstat demonstrated superiority compared to placebo in most key LR-MDS subgroups, including those based on RS status, baseline transfusion burden and baseline EPO levels, across several efficacy endpoints. The subgroup analyses for RBC-TI in the Phase 2 MDS3001 study were strongly supportive of those from the pivotal study and demonstrate the broad applicability of imetelstat as a therapeutic option in LR MDS.
L1363: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1364: The vast majority of subjects in Study MDS3001-Phase 3 (93%) were enrolled at non-US sites. The Agency sent an information request to the Applicant requesting information on efficacy by region. Based on the Applicant's response to the Agency's information request, the primary efficacy result varied greatly between the US and non-US sites. As shown in Table 38, the 8-week RBC-TI rate with imetelstat was much lower in the subgroup of patients enrolled at sites within the US compared to non-US sites, with a difference in response rate of only 12.5% for US subjects compared to 25.5% for non-US subjects.

L1368: Regarding the region-specific population and treatment-related factors, the Applicant noted the following in the response to the information request dated 2 October 2023:
L1369: - **MDS diagnosis and Prognostic Factors.** "All enrolled subjects had their MDS diagnosis confirmed by a central pathology reviewer. There were no notable differences in disease
L1370: characteristics and prognostic factors between regions when taking into account the smaller numbers in NA and ROW [Rest of the World] compared with EU.”
L1371: - ***Concomitant medications.*** “Regarding the potential impact of concomitant medication on regional TI rate differences, the use of iron chelators (IC) among imetelstat-treated subjects was higher in the EU (70%) and ROW (68%), compared with NA (15%), although there was no difference in use of IC between the imetelstat and placebo treated subjects, and no impact of IC use on the rates of 8-week TI. Growth factor usage for imetelstat-treated subjects was lower in NA compared to EU and ROW; however, this is likely due to the shorter duration of treatment of NA subjects (described in detail below). No other apparent trends of concomitant medications which could impact the efficacy across regions were identified.”
- ***Transfusion Practices on study by region.*** Analysis of baseline pretreatment Hgb levels by region showed no notable differences between regions in the study population (median Hgb 7.7 g/dL NA, 8.0 g/dL EU, 7.5 g/dL ROW) (Table 7). The on-study pre-transfusion Hgb was also similar between regions (median Hgb 7.6 g/dL NA, 7.4 g/dL EU, 7.2 g/dL ROW) (Table 7). These results are in line with guidelines and medical practice, as noted above. In addition, when baseline pretreatment Hgb levels leading to transfusions were also compared to on-study Hgb levels resulting in transfusions (pre-study versus on-study) the median change was comparable between regions and between imetelstat and placebo. Small differences can be attributed to the small sample sizes in NA and ROW and are not clinically meaningful. Based on these data, there are no notable regional differences in MDS3001 Phase 3 investigators' transfusion practices between treatment groups on study or a change in Hgb levels leading to RBC transfusions while on study vs. pre-study.”
- ***Treatment Duration.*** “Overall, in the Phase 3 study, subjects in the NA region tended to stay on treatment for a shorter duration than those in other regions. Thirteen (11%) of 118 imetelstat-treated subjects were enrolled in NA, 8 of whom were enrolled in the US. The median treatment duration for these NA subjects was shorter compared with those in the EU and ROW (18 weeks, 53 weeks, and 26 weeks, respectively). Among the imetelstat-treated subjects in the US (n=8), it is notable that except for the 1 subject who was a long TI responder (treatment duration of 105.1 weeks at primary analysis), all other US subjects were treated for 6 cycles or less. Of these 7 subjects, 2 subjects received only 1 cycle, and 1 subject received 2 cycles. In NA, the availability of alternative treatment options such as hypomethylating agents and luspatercept, possibly impacted investigator and subject decision making to discontinue treatment earlier before achieving TI. Due to the small number of subjects and short treatment duration in the US, it is difficult to assess the efficacy of imetelstat in US subjects in isolation...”
L1372: Ultimately, no definitive conclusions can be drawn due to the small sample size of the subgroups and the lack of the prespecified subgroup hypothesis testing. However, potential
L1373: impact of region-specific patient or treatment-related factors cannot be ruled out. Residual uncertainty regarding applicability of the efficacy results to the US population remains.
L1374: Subgroup analyses for 8-week RBC-TI in the target population and all subjects in the Phase 2 study are shown in Figure 29 and Figure 30, respectively. The rate of 8-week RBC-TI was similar across most subgroups, with a few notable exceptions. Subjects with IPSS low-risk MDS had a lower 8-week RBC-TI rate than subjects with IPSS intermediate-1 risk MDS. Additionally, subjects treated with prior hypomethylating agent (HMA) and/or lenalidomide had a substantially lower 8-week RBC-TI rate than other subgroups, with 0/8 (0%) of subjects with prior HMA achieving an 8-week RBC-TI and only 1/12 (8.3%) of subjects with prior lenalidomide achieving an 8-week RBC-TI.

Subjects treated with prior HMA and/or lenalidomide had a substantially lower 24-week RBC-TI rate than other subgroups, with 0/8 (0%) of subjects with prior HMA achieving a 24-week RBC-TI and only 1/12 (8.3%) of subjects with prior lenalidomide achieving a 24-week RBC-TI.

L1384: ## **Clinical Reviewer comments:**
L1385: These subgroup analyses from the Phase 2 study suggest that patients with IPSS low risk disease benefit less from imetelstat treatment than patients with IPSS intermediate-1 risk disease. Additionally, the data suggest little to no efficacy of imetelstat in patients with prior HMA or lenalidomide, though the sample sizes are too small to make definitive conclusions. Of note, subjects with prior HMA or lenalidomide were excluded from the Phase 3 study, as previously noted.
L1386: ## Additional Efficacy Considerations
L1387: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1388: ## Missing Data
L1389: The primary efficacy endpoint in Study MDS3001 was 8-week RBC TI rate, defined as the proportion of patients without any RBC transfusion during any consecutive 8 weeks (56 days) starting from the day of randomization. Reliable assessment of the patient transfusion status is essential for validity of such an endpoint. Potential missing data issues were assessed, and key aspects are summarized below.
L1390: ## *Scheduled and Unscheduled Visits*
L1391: Per Applicant's information request response, "According to the Time and Events schedule for MDS3001 Phase 3, cycle visits occurred every 4 weeks for study drug administration (i.e., Cycle X, Day 1; CXD1), and weekly for the first two cycles (i.e., additional visits on Day 8, Day 15 and Day 22 for Cycle 1 and Cycle 2). Therefore, the total number of scheduled visits per protocol for each subject is the total number of cycles (or doses) a subject received plus (+) an additional 6 Cycle 1 and Cycle 2 visits plus (+) the end of treatment visits plus (+) the visits in post treatment follow up until the first transfusion. A window of +/-3 days was allowed for visits. If a cycle delay occurred, it was not considered a missed dose or missed visit and the length of a cycle was extended, with Day 1 of the next cycle occurring on the day of study drug administration.
L1392: In addition to the total expected scheduled visits mentioned above, subjects could have unscheduled visits at any time for various reasons, for example, for monitoring of laboratory values. In the event laboratory values did not meet criteria for dosing on a scheduled visit, that visit became an unscheduled visit. If a subject had an unscheduled visit, the Transfusion Status Form was required to be completed."
L1393: ## *Dataset Discrepancy Issues*
L1394: Discrepancies between records in the CE (Clinical Events) and PR (procedures) datasets were identified. For the participants with the recorded response "Yes" to the question "Have there been any transfusions since the last visit?", the corresponding data appeared to be missing from the PR dataset. For example, for some patients there were multiple subsequent dates with the recorded "Yes" response to the question "Have there been any transfusions since the last visit?" without any corresponding transfusion data entries in the PR dataset.
L1395: Key Applicant's clarifications from a response to the information request: "As of Protocol Amendment 7 (dated 02 Sep 2021), investigators were not required to report transfusions that occurred after the first RBC transfusion in the PTFU period. All instances with CE data "Yes" and no concurrent PR data occurred after the first RBC transfusion in the follow-up phase or after the subsequent anti-cancer therapy, i.e., after the end date of the RBC Transfusion monitoring
L1396: period and did not affect the calculation of RBC TI interval, the primary efficacy endpoint or any TI based endpoint... Reconciliations between the Transfusion Status Form (CE dataset) and Transfusion Form (PR dataset) were performed and reviewed rigorously on a regular basis before the database lock. For the subjects with the recorded response “Yes” to the question “Have there been any transfusions since the last visit?” in the CE dataset, it was required to record all the transfusions in the PR dataset (e.g., the type of transfusion, the date and number of units transfused) between two consecutive visit dates (the current visit date and the last visit date in CE dataset). However, only the CE and PR data reported until the first transfusion in PTFU were relevant for assessments of TI rate and duration. Specifically, the reconciliation and missing transfusion data reviews focused on transfusion assessments from Study Day 1 until the first RBC transfusion was recorded in the follow-up phase (or the end of the TI duration)...Based on MDS3001 Phase 3 Study primary analysis data cut off (13 October 2022), of 5802 records from all subjects (imetelstat or placebo), the reconciliation listing identified only two discrepancies (0.03%) that may indicate missing transfusion records...Neither of these subjects were ≥8- week TI responders.”
L1397: *Statistical Reviewer Comment: As of Protocol Amendment 7 (dated 02 Sep 2021), investigators were not required to report transfusions that occurred after the first RBC transfusion in the PTFU period. Lack of adequate data precludes any assessment of an outcome related to the RBC transfusions after the first RBC transfusion in the PTFU period.*
L1398: ## *Data Collection*
L1399: Per Applicant's information request response, “Per protocol, transfusion data were required and collected during the entire treatment period, including at end of treatment (EOT), and in post-treatment follow up until the first transfusion in the follow-up phase (i.e., after the EOT visit). Transfusion status was actively assessed by completion of the Transfusion Status Form and detailed transfusion data was recorded at each visit (scheduled or unscheduled) on the Transfusions Form. During the post-treatment follow-up (PTFU) phase, transfusion data continued to be actively assessed and recorded in the same manner every 4 to 6 weeks until at least the first transfusion in follow-up.
L1400: The Transfusion Status Form contained the question “Have there been any transfusions since the last visit (including today)?”. This transfusion status question covered the entire period since the previously completed study visit (scheduled or unscheduled) up to and including the current visit. If the transfusion status question was answered as “Yes” then the site staff was required to record the pertinent transfusion data (e.g., the type of transfusion, the date and number of units transfused) since the last study visit on the Transfusions Form. The response to the transfusion status question was a required field in the Electronic Data Capture (EDC) system and it was not allowed to be left blank. Therefore, the question was asked, and responses were provided for all subjects (imetelstat or placebo) at almost all nonmissing visits (≥98.6%). Only 1.1% and 1.4% of transfusion status data was missing in each arm respectively and these were
L1401: due to missed visits, all of which were during the additional weekly monitoring visits within the first 2 cycles where study drug was not administered.
L1402: Note, the transfusion status question covered the entire period between two consecutive study visits that actually occurred even if there was a missed visit or a delay between cycles. For example, if a subject missed Cycle 1 Day 8, then the transfusion status question at Cycle 1 Day 15 would cover the 14-day period from the date of Cycle 1 Day 1 up to and including the date of the Cycle 1 Day 15 visit. Therefore, the frequency at which the transfusion status question was asked and answered was at least once a cycle. Additionally, the transfusion status question was not asked in only 4 visits (0.2% of transfusion status data in imetelstat treated only), all either at EOT or at the first post-treatment follow-up visit."
L1403: ## *Quality Control for the Completeness of Transfusion Data*
L1404: In addition to the active assessment of transfusion status in the EDC, several checks were in place to ensure completeness of transfusion data. An edit check was in place to query the site to record corresponding transfusions when the site answered affirmatively on the transfusion status form, but no corresponding transfusions were entered on the Transfusions Form during the intervening time period between the visits (scheduled or unscheduled). Additionally, the sponsor and the Contract Research Organization (CRO) medical monitors regularly reviewed patient hemoglobin levels compared to transfusion data and questioned any unexplained hemoglobin increases that did not accompany a reported transfusion, and vice versa, any transfusions reported without a preceding hemoglobin level. Queries were issued to record potentially missing transfusions or pre-transfusion hemoglobin levels, including in the event where a site may have inadvertently answered "No" to the initial question of "Have there been any transfusions since the last visit (including today)?" despite an observed increase in hemoglobin.
L1405: ## *Summary*
L1406: Per Applicant's information request response,
L1407: - "The missing transfusion status data due to missed visits (1.1% and 1.4% in imetelstat and placebo arm respectively), all occurred during the additional weekly monitoring visits within the first 2 cycles (i.e., Cycle 1 and Cycle 2 Day 8, 15, 22) and were &lt; 4 weeks from a previous visit.
- The four instances where transfusion status question was not asked occurred at EOT/Follow up 1, which would not have been considered in the calculation of TI. Additionally, the instances all occurred in subjects who did not achieve \ge 8-week TI, and, therefore, would not have impacted the accurate evaluation of transfusion independence rates or durations.
L1408: - The two instances of missing reported transfusion data occurred in subjects who did not achieve \ge 8-week TI, and, therefore, would not have impacted the accurate evaluation of transfusion independence rates or durations."
L1409: *Statistical Reviewer Comment: Missing data likely had minimal impact on the primary efficacy endpoint.*
L1410: ## Medical resource utilization
L1411: For Study MDS3001-Phase 3, the Applicant reported that the total number of subjects who had at least 1 medical encounter (including both inpatient and outpatient encounters) was slightly higher in the imetelstat arm (56% versus 52%). This can be attributed to a higher number of subjects in the imetelstat arm requiring at least 1 hospitalization, as shown in Table 39, though the total number of medical encounters per patient was comparable between arms.
L1412: **Table 39:** MDS3001-Phase 3: Medical resource utilization
L1413: Imetelstat
(N=118)	Placebo

L1417: However, there are significant limitations with this analysis based on the methodological approach and potential for confounding resulting in limited interpretability of this data. Accurate assessment of transfusion and infusion visits is one of the key benefit-risk considerations in this clinical setting. Exclusion of protocol-mandated procedures, tests, and encounters limits the relevance of the medical resource utilization analysis due to the fact that all transfusions were excluded as protocol mandated (inclusive of RBC and platelet transfusion visits). Based on the limited data provided, there appears to be no significant reduction in medical resource utilization with imetelstat and further study would be required for validation.
L1418: ## 8.1.5. **Integrated Assessment of Effectiveness**
L1419: ## Data:
L1420: Not applicable.
L1421: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1422: Due to the differences in study design (open-label versus randomized and stratified, respectively), results from the Phase 2 and Phase 3 of Study MDS3001 were not pooled and integration of efficacy results was not performed.
L1423: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1424: FDA concurs with this approach.
L1425: ## 8.2. Review of Safety
L1426: ## Data:
L1427: The safety evaluation of imetelstat monotherapy in the treatment of hematologic malignancies is based on 5 clinical trials:
L1428: - The primary safety analysis is based on 1 placebo-controlled Phase 3 study in subjects with IPSS low or intermediate-1 risk MDS that is relapsed/refractory to or ineligible for ESA treatment (e.g., "lower-risk MDS" or LR MDS): MDS3001 (Phase 3 part)
- Phase 2 studies in subjects with hematologic malignancies (including LR MDS), MDS3001 (Phase 2 part), CP14B013, CP14B015, CP14B019, and MYF2001
L1429: In these 5 studies, a total of 391 subjects received treatment with imetelstat monotherapy. The following groupings are evaluated:
L1430: - MDS3001 Phase 3 imetelstat group (N = 118) versus placebo group (N = 59)
The **primary safety analysis** that will inform the label. All subjects treated with a starting dose of 7.1mg/kg imetelstat (equivalent to 7.5 mg/kg imetelstat-sodium) q4w (referred to as "Group A" in the SCS).
- MDS3001 Phase 2 imetelstat group (N = 57)
Data from the Phase 2 part of the MDS3001 study is confirmatory of the results observed in Phase 3. Data pooled from imetelstat-treated subjects in MDS3001 Phase 2 and Phase 3 provide a safety grouping of all subjects with LR MDS treated at 7.1 mg/kg imetelstat q4w (referred to as "Group B" in the SCS).
- Hematologic malignancy monotherapy grouping (N = 391)
Pooled data from all 5 studies (MDS3001, CP14B013, CP14B015, CP14B019, and MYF2001) and provides a safety grouping of subjects with all hematologic malignancies treated with imetelstat monotherapy (ie, monotherapy pool). Starting dose levels varied from 4.4mg/kg
L1431: to 8.9mg/kg imetelstat (equivalent to 4.7 mg/kg to 9.4 mg/kg imetelstat-sodium). MF subjects comprise approximately 40% of the pool. While differences in safety outcomes from the LR MDS pool are expected due to differences in underlying disease, these data increase the chances of detecting infrequent safety events that may not be observable in a smaller safety population (referred to as “Group C” in the SCS).
L1432: Total overall cumulative exposure to imetelstat monotherapy is 349.2 patient-years (PY) in the monotherapy pool (N = 391) and 105.7 PY in the MDS3001 Phase 3 imetelstat group (N = 118).
L1433: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1434: Overall, the number of subjects treated and the total cumulative exposure to imetelstat as of the initial NDA submission provides an adequate database to assess the safety of imetelstat in patients with LR MDS.
L1435: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1436: The total number of patients in the integrated safety database is 795, of whom 243 were treated for MDS or MDS/MPN, 198 for myelofibrosis or other MPN, 57 for other hematologic neoplasms, and 284 for solid tumors. Within the general category of MPN, 169 patients had myelofibrosis, 9 blast phase MF/AML, 18 essential thrombocytopenia, and two polycythemia vera. Other hematologic malignancies included chronic lymphoproliferative disease (28) and multiple myeloma (42). Solid tumors included advanced or metastatic non-small cell lung cancer (105), locally recurrent or metastatic breast cancer (106), and relapsed or refractory solid tumors (75).
L1437: Treatment was highly variable, with patients receiving a variety of doses and dosing schedules of imetelstat and some patients receiving treatment in combination with other agents, including bevacizumab and paclitaxel. The dose of imetelstat used ranged from 0.4 mg/kg to 11.7 mg/kg. In addition, some patients were dosed based on body surface area at doses ranging from 20 mg/m2 to 375 mg/m2. Dosing schedules ranged from twice weekly to every 4 weeks. Details of each study and the dose used are described below. Note that doses in this section only refer to imetelstat sodium; the dose of 7.5 mg/kg of imetelstat sodium is equivalent to 7.1 mg/kg of imetelstat:
L1438: - 63935937MDS3001: Monotherapy with initial imetelstat dose of 7.5 mg/kg every 4 weeks or placebo in patients with MDS.
- 63935937MYF2001: Monotherapy with initial imetelstat dose of 9.4 mg/kg every 3 weeks or 4.7 mg/kg every 3 weeks in patients with MF; 48 patients dosed at 4.7 mg/kg and 59 at 9.4 mg/kg
- CP05-101_ST: 0.4-4.8 mg/kg weekly, 4.8-11.7 mg/kg days 1 and 8 of 21-day cycle, 9.4-11.7 mg/kg on day 1 of 21-day cycle in patients with advanced solid tumors.
L1439: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 217779 imetelstat
## TRADENAME, imetelstat
L1440: - **CP14A004:** 3.2-14.0 mg/kg days 1, 8, and 15 of 21-day cycle initially planned, later modified to 3.2-7.2 mg/kg weekly and 6.0 mg/kg days 1 and 8 of 21-day cycle in patients with relapsed or refractory multiple myeloma.
- **CP14A005:** 3.2 mg/kg on day 1 of 21-day cycle, 3.2-7.5 mg/kg on days 1 and 8 of 21-day cycle in patients with non-small cell lung cancer.
- **CP04-151 CLD:** 20-240 mg/m2 weekly in a 28-day cycle, 160-200 mg/m2 with a shorter infusion time weekly in a 28-day cycle, 200 mg/m2 days 1 and 8 of 3-week cycle in patients with chronic lymphoproliferative disease.
- **CP14A010:** 160-320 mg/m2 on days 1, 8, and 15, 300 mg/m2 on day 8, 300 mg on days 1 and 15, 375 mg/m2 on days 1,8, and 15, 375 mg/m2 on day 8 of 28-day cycles with bevacizumab or paclitaxel in patients with breast cancer.
- **CP14A011:** Combination therapy with bortezomib and dexamethasone in patients with relapsed or refractory multiple myeloma. Imetelstat was dosed at 160 or 200 mg/m2.
- **CP14B014:** 300 mg/m2 on day 1 of 21-day cycle with paclitaxel or bevacizumab in patients with breast cancer.
L1441: *Clinical Reviewer comment: Patients who received imetelstat in doses determined by body surface area were excluded from dose specific investigations, as the dose in mg/m2 could not be readily converted to a dose in mg/kg.*
L1442: - **CP14B012:** 9.4 mg/kg on days 1 and 8 of 21-day cycle, with or without bevacizumab, on day 1 of a 21-day cycle in patients with non-small cell lung cancer
- **CP14B013:** Imetelstat dosed at 7.5 mg/kg on days 1 and 8 of 28-day cycle in patients with multiple myeloma; if lenalidomide maintenance was being used, it was continued.
L1443: *Clinical Reviewer comments: This study is considered as monotherapy as lenalidomide was being given as ongoing therapy. Four of 9 patients received at least one dose of lenalidomide on study. These patients are not identified separately in the dataset.*
L1444: - **CP14B015:** Monotherapy with imetelstat at a dose of 7.5 mg/kg or 9.4 mg/kg weekly in patients with essential thrombocytopenia or polycythemia vera.
- **CP14B019:** Arms A-D: 9.4 mg/kg on various schedules, arms E and G: 7.5 mg/kg every 28 days, arm F: 9.5 mg/kg cycles 1 and 2 followed by 7.5 mg/kg or 9.4 mg/kg depending on response; 18 patients treated on arms E and G, 62 on other arms. Of note, dose could be increased to 9.4 mg/kg if response criteria not met. Conditions under study included MF (arms A-C, E, F), MDS or MDS/MPN (arm G), and blast phase MF/AML (arm D).
L1445: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 217779 imetelstat

L1455: Equivalent doses across different dosing schedules may be hard to determine as the peak concentration will vary more with dose but trough more with frequency of administration. In addition, a direct comparison between dosing by weight and by body surface area is difficult.
L1456: For the purposes of this review, dose (i.e., higher or lower than 7.5 mg/kg every 4 weeks) will be based on total dose per kilogram per 4 weeks. Thus, doses that are lower than 7.5 mg/kg may be higher if they are given more frequently. In addition, when dose escalation was allowed, the start dose is used to determine whether the dose is higher, lower, or equal to the proposed dose.
L1457: Four studies include at least one arm in which imetelstat was combined with other chemotherapeutic medications, including bevacizumab, bortezomib, and paclitaxel.
L1458: Overall, the safety database appears adequate to characterize the safety of imetelstat at a dose of 7.1 mg/kg every 4 weeks in patients with MDS. However, safety may not be fully characterizable in subpopulations. The safety of imetelstat in other conditions and at other doses and dosing schedules is outside of the scope of this NDA. However, the availability of data from other studies may be used to evaluate potential risks identified in the safety population of MDS3001 and assess the probability that these risks are associated with the drug versus the underlying condition. In particular, hematologic AEs are expected in patients with MDS and often with other hematologic malignancies but would not be expected to be seen due to the underlying condition in most patients with solid tumors. In addition, evaluation of AE in
L1459: different doses and dosing schedules may be helpful in defining whether a dose-response can be observed.
L1460: ## 8.2.1. Safety Review Approach
L1461: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1462: The main focus of the safety evaluation will be MDS3001 study results and the Phase 3 data in particular. The monotherapy pool may be summarized to show consistency across indications or to examine rare events.
L1463: The clinical review of safety for this NDA is based on the following:
L1464: - Clinical study reports for the 4 clinical trials listed above
- Summary of Clinical Safety and pooled datasets
- Case Report Forms for and Safety Narratives for deaths, SAEs, adverse events of special interest (AESIs), and other TEAEs of clinical concern
- A planned 120-Day Safety Update that will include data from the remaining MDS3001 Phase 3 subjects who completed treatment since the clinical data cutoff date of the initial NDA filing.
L1465: Safety was evaluated by analysis of the incidence, intensity, and type of TEAEs, vital signs measurements, clinical laboratory values, ECGs, and deaths. All summaries and analyses were based on the Treated population, defined as all subjects who received at least one dose of study drug. All subjects were analyzed according to the treatment actually received. For analysis purposes, the following PTs were combined thrombocytopenia (thrombocytopenia + platelet count decreased), neutropenia (neutropenia + neutrophil count decreased), and leukopenia (leukopenia + white blood cell count decreased).
L1466: Across all studies, the primary risks associated with imetelstat treatment are neutropenia and thrombocytopenia, consistent with the on-target effects of imetelstat. These cytopenias have been well characterized and are manageable through appropriate dose modifications (including dose delays and reductions) that will be clearly outlined in product labeling, and monitored as per routine pharmacovigilance.
L1467: Furthermore, the risk of cytopenias is commonly seen with other therapies used for treatment of hematologic malignancies and MDS specifically and is to be expected as these malignancies originate in the BM. Peripheral blood cytopenias are a consequence of reducing disease burden by eliminating the malignant clone and temporarily reducing the amount of circulating hematopoietic cells until BM recovery. Therefore, hematologists and health care professionals
L1468: who care for patients with MDS are familiar with and skilled at the management of neutropenia and thrombocytopenia and their potential clinical complications of infections and bleedings.
L1469: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1470: The data presented is a comprehensive analysis of safety data that supports assessment of the safe and manageable use of imetelstat monotherapy 7.1 mg/kg q4w in the treatment of patients with LR MDS.
L1471: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1472: FDA agrees that the primary safety analysis will be based on data from the MDS3001 study, including both part 1 (phase 2 portion) and part 2 (randomized, phase 3 portion). Data from other studies will be used to support the analysis of safety, including determining whether a correlation with dose is observed, whether AEs are observed consistently in different diseases and different populations, and screening for potential rare signals.
L1473: FDA agrees that cytopenias (thrombocytopenia, neutropenia, leukopenia) are much more commonly observed with use of imetelstat. However, we have concerns that the risks of neutropenia and thrombocytopenia are underappreciated by the Applicant. Details regarding the extent of cytopenias will be presented in the review. In addition, the statement that the cytopenias are the result of clearing of the malignant clone is not well supported (see below for further details). This conclusion cannot be reached without evaluation of the frequency of cytopenias in patients who received imetelstat for solid tumors, the correlation between cytopenias and resolution of cytogenetic abnormalities or histologic bone marrow or peripheral blood changes (i.e., dysplasia), and the persistence of cytopenias or other evidence of the ability of the bone marrow to recover after use of imetelstat. To summarize data that will be presented below, there is strong evidence that neutropenia and thrombocytopenia in particular are due to drug toxicity and not to clearing of the malignant clone, which, as noted in the efficacy section, rarely occurs.
L1474: ## 8.2.2. **Review of the Safety Database**
L1475: ## Overall Exposure
L1476: ## Data:
L1477: In the MDS3001 Phase 3 study, the median duration of treatment for the imetelstat group (N = 118) was 33.93 weeks (range: 0.1 to 141.1 weeks) and for the placebo group (N = 59) was 28.29 weeks (range: 0.1 to 116.1 weeks) (Applicant Table 42). Subjects in the imetelstat and placebo groups received a median of 8 cycles of treatment (range: 1 to 34 and 1 to 30 cycles, respectively), and 40.7% of subjects in the imetelstat group and 28.8% of subjects in the
L1478: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 217779 imetelstat
## TRADENAME, imetelstat
L1479: placebo group received more than 13 cycles of treatment. In the imetelstat and placebo groups, 68.6% and 69.5% of subjects, respectively, completed 24 weeks of treatment, and 44.9% and 28.8% of subjects, respectively, completed 48 weeks of treatment. Similar rates of treatment for imetelstat and placebo at 24 weeks are expected as subjects in a study tend to stay on for 24 weeks (approximately 6 months) and then, as expected, placebo subjects begin to drop off at a higher rate after 24 weeks due to lack of efficacy.
L1480: In the Phase 2 imetelstat group (N = 57), median duration of treatment was 32.29 weeks (range: 0.1 to 260.1 weeks) (Applicant Table 42). Subjects received a median of 8 cycles of treatment and 36.8% of subjects received more than 13 cycles. In Phase 2, 61.4% of subjects completed 24 weeks of treatment, and 38.6% of subjects, respectively, completed 48 weeks of treatment. Total cumulative exposure was 105.72 PY in the imetelstat group and 44.77 PY in the placebo group.
L1481: In the hematologic malignancies monotherapy grouping (N = 391), subjects were treated with imetelstat monotherapy for a cumulative total of 349.2 PY, including 118 subjects with LR MDS treated for 105.7 PY in Phase 3 of the main MDS3001 study, 57 subjects with LR MDS treated for 65.3 PY in Phase 2 of MDS3001, and 175 pooled subjects with LR MDS treated for 171.0 PY in Phase 2 and Phase 3 of the main MDS3001 study. Doses and dosing schedules of imetelstat used in the studies included in the safety analysis are listed in Applicant Table 43. Median duration of monotherapy treatment was 30.14 weeks (range: 0.1 to 263.4 weeks) (Table tex01c). Similar to the LR MDS pool, subjects in the Group C pool received a median of 8 cycles of treatment, and 37.3% of subjects in the overall population received more than 13 cycles. In the overall subject population, 60.6% of subjects completed 24 weeks of treatment, and 38.6% of subjects completed 48 weeks of treatment. Total cumulative PY of exposure was 349.22 PY.
L1482: Dose modifications other than dose reductions (e.g., infusion interruptions, dose delays) were not uniformly collected across studies in the Group C pool. In the overall pooled Group C population, 33.2% of subjects had a dose reduction, and most (30.2%) were due to AEs (Table tex03c).

Doses in this table (including the footnotes) are shown for the active moiety (free acid). To convert a quantity of imetelstat salt to the equivalent quantity of imetelstat, a factor of 0.9416 based on relative molar mass of the free acid form (4610 g/mol) to the sodium salt (4896 g/mol; 4610/4896 = 0.9416) is applied (i.e., 1 mg imetelstat sodium = 0.9416 mg imetelstat).
L1502: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1503: In the MDS3001 Phase 3 study, the median duration of treatment was higher in the imetelstat group compared to the placebo group. Similar proportions of subjects in the imetelstat and placebo groups completed 24 weeks of treatment; however, more than a third of subjects in the imetelstat group completed 48 weeks of treatment compared to placebo. Rates for treatment discontinuations were similar for both groups however placebo was twice as likely to discontinue due to lack of efficacy while imetelstat subjects had a higher rate of discontinuation due to AEs, the majority of which were due to neutropenia and thrombocytopenia. Additionally, imetelstat had twice the rate of dose modifications compared to placebo including dose reductions which suggests that the dose modifications allowed subjects on imetelstat to stay on treatment longer.
L1504: Imetelstat exposure was similar in Phase 2 and Phase 3 in LR MDS subjects. Study duration and follow-up was longer in Phase 2.
L1505: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1506: Assessment of the exposure to treatment for the imetelstat versus placebo arms is complicated by the fact that dose delays were more common in the imetelstat arm versus the placebo arm. Notably, the median number of cycles on each arm was similar, suggesting that the reason that there was a longer duration of treatment in the imetelstat arm was due to dose delays slowing the administration of treatment, leading to longer times but similar number of cycles.
L1507: *Clinical Reviewer comments: Patients receiving imetelstat received the same median number of cycles but over a longer period of time. This suggests that the benefit-risk ratio may be problematic since one would expect a highly effective and tolerable treatment to result in a longer duration of treatment as measured by number of cycles received. A toxic treatment resulting in multiple delays would be expected to result in longer cycles due to treatment delays.*
L1508: ## **Relevant characteristics of the safety population:**
L1509: ## **Data:**
L1510: Demographics were well-balanced between imetelstat and placebo groups and were consistent with the characteristics of patients in the proposed target indication. The median subject age of the imetelstat group was 71.5 years (range: 44 to 87 years) and in the placebo group was 73 years (range: 39 to 85 years), as anticipated for this subject population (Applicant Table 44). Most subjects in the imetelstat and placebo groups, respectively, were male (60.2% and 66.1%), White (80.5% and 79.7%) and not Hispanic or Latino (84.7% and 79.7%), reflecting the expected demographics of patients with MDS. Most subjects were enrolled at sites in the EU (67.8% and 64.4%), followed by the ROW (21.2% and 16.9%), and North America (11.0% and 18.6%). Median age at diagnosis in the imetelstat group was 66.71 years (range: 38.1 to 86.9 years) and in the placebo group was 69.29 years (range: 32.8 to 84.5 years) (Applicant Table 45). In the imetelstat and placebo groups, 47.5% and 45.8% of subjects, respectively, had a transfusion burden &gt; 6 units per 8 weeks. The median baseline value for ANC was 2.63 \times 10^9/L and 2.80 \times 10^9/L, respectively, and platelet count was 230 \times 10^9/L and 231 \times 10^9/L, respectively. There was a higher proportion of subjects in the IPSS low risk (67.8% and 66.1%, respectively) vs Int-1 (33.2% and 33.9%, respectively). As required per the study entry criteria, all subjects had a baseline ECOG score of \le 2, and most subjects in both groups (\ge 94.9\%) had a baseline score of \le 1.
L1511: Demographics and baseline disease characteristics in the supportive MDS3001 Phase 2 study were highly consistent with Phase 3, with no major differences in any category. In the MDS Phase 2 imetelstat group (N = 57), median age was 71.0 years (range: 46 to 83 years); 75.4%
L1512: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 217779 imetelstat
## TRADENAME, imetelstat
L1513: were \ge 65 years old, and 31.6% were \ge 75 years old (Applicant Table 44). Most subjects were White (82.1%) and not Hispanic or Latino (85.7%) and 56.1% were male. Most subjects were treated at sites in the EU (64.9%), followed by North America (21.1%) and ROW (14.0%). Median age at diagnosis was 66 years (range: 43.8 to 82.3 years) (Applicant Table 45). Overall, 50.9% subjects had a transfusion burden &gt; 6 units per 8 weeks. The median baseline value for ANC was 2.90 \times 10^9/L (range: 1.5, 20.5) and platelet count was 297 \times 10^9/L (range: 89.3 to 800). A higher proportion of subjects were IPSS low risk (63.2%) versus Int-1 (36.8%). As required per the study entry criteria, all subjects had a baseline ECOG score of \le 2, and most subjects (91.2%) had a baseline score of \le 1.
L1514: In the hematologic malignancy monotherapy grouping (N = 391), demographics were similar to the pooled LR MDS subjects, except for a higher proportion of North America subjects (due to the MF studies), and a lower proportion from Europe (SCS Table 15 and Table tdm01c). Median age was 68.0 years (range: 21 to 93 years), 60.6% of subjects were male, 83.6% were White, and 81.0% were not Hispanic or Latino. Treatment was administered to 171 (43.7%) subjects at study sites in North America, 176 (45.0%) subjects in Europe, and 44 (11.3%) subjects in ROW. Subjects were distributed across renal function categories of normal (31.5%; 98 subjects), mildly impaired (36.7%; 114 subjects), moderately impaired (30.2%; 94 subjects), or severe (1.6%; 5 subjects), while most subjects had normal (61.1%; 239 subjects) or mildly impaired (30.7%; 120 subjects) hepatic function. Baseline characteristics for Group C differ based on the subjects' underlying disease (Table tdm02c).
