L1527: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 217779 imetelstat
L1528: ## TRADENAME, imetelstat
L1529: ECOG = Eastern Cooperative Oncology Group; IPSS = International Prognostic Scoring System; MDS = myelodysplastic syndromes; n = the number of subjects in each group with non-missing parameter value.
L1530: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1531: In the pivotal MDS3001 Phase 3 study, demographics and baseline characteristics were well balanced between the imetelstat and placebo groups and were similar to the safety population of the supportive Phase 2 study. The baseline characteristics of the MDS3001 subject population are consistent with that of the LR MDS patient population for which imetelstat is being proposed as treatment. The population of subjects with hematological malignancies in the monotherapy pool represents the mix of different indications studied and is provided mainly for signal detection in patients treated with imetelstat monotherapy.
L1532: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1533: FDA has confirmed the numbers provided by the Applicant in Applicant Table 44 with the following clarifications:
L1534: - One patient in the open label imetelstat cohort had no value provided for race and ethnicity. It is noted that this patient was treated in France and that race and ethnicity are often not reported in France for legal reasons. Thus, the reviewer feels confident that this patient could be considered "not reported" for both values.
- Race and ethnicity are typically not reported in Germany for similar reasons. It is unclear how data regarding race and ethnicity were determined for patients in Germany (all of whom are listed as non-Hispanic white.) It is not clear whether the Applicant obtained the actual race and ethnicity for patients or if they made assumptions based on their expectations.
- The method used to derive renal and hepatic function are unclear and cannot be confirmed without further information.
L1535: Regarding the comparison between the imetelstat phase 2, imetelstat phase 3, and placebo safety populations, we have the following comments:
L1536: - Patients on the placebo arm are generally older, with a difference of about 1.5 years between the median ages of patients in the imetelstat versus placebo arms of the phase 3 portion of the study and fewer patients age &lt;65 in the placebo arm compared to either imetelstat arm. This difference may be of importance, given that the overall survival analysis was not stratified by age.
- More patients in the placebo arm were male.
- The majority of patients in any arm were white and non-Hispanic, limiting evaluation of the effect of race on the safety profile of imetelstat.
- More patients in the placebo arm were treated in North America and fewer in rest of the world (other than the EU or North America) compared to imetelstat in the phase 3
L1537: portion of the study. This may have implications for both the efficacy and the safety evaluations.
L1538: In addition to the baseline disease data provided by the Applicant, we would like to add the information described in Table 46 below.

L1548: ## Adequacy of the safety database:
L1549: ## Data:
L1550: The size of the database for the MDS3001 Phase 3 safety population is adequate to provide an estimate of adverse reactions that may be associated with imetelstat use in the LR MDS population. The database from the total pooled population (N = 391) includes subjects with other hematologic malignancies (MF, MDS/MPN overlap, MM, ET, and polycythemia vera [PV]) and provides supportive safety. Demographic and baseline characteristics of subjects treated with imetelstat at the 7.1 mg/kg dose (equivalent to 7.5 mg/kg imetelstat-sodium) were generally representative of the LR MDS population. The duration of treatment in the overall LR MDS population is adequate to provide assessment of adverse reactions.
L1551: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1552: The imetelstat safety database is considered to be adequate to assess the safety of the 7.1 mg/kg q4w dose and schedule.
L1553: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1554: The safety database, including the data from MDS3001 and supportive data from other studies, is generally adequate to evaluate the safety of imetelstat at 7.1 mg/kg every 4 weeks in patients with lower risk MDS. However, the database does not contain adequate numbers of patients from underrepresented racial and ethnic populations and younger patients to characterize the risk in these subpopulations. In addition, comparison of the risk between other subpopulations (i.e., sex, transfusion burden) is limited. Finally, no conclusions can be drawn regarding the use of imetelstat in patients with an absolute neutrophil count of &lt;500 or platelet count of &lt;75,000, patients with moderate or severe organ impairment, or higher risk (IPSS-R intermediate, high, or very high risk) MDS and the findings of the safety review cannot be extrapolated to these populations.
L1555: ## 8.2.3. **Adequacy of Applicant's Clinical Safety Assessments**
L1556: ## Issues Regarding Data Integrity and Submission Quality
L1557: ## **Data:**
L1558: In the pivotal MDS3001, an independent DMC was established for the study to monitor safety data. In the Phase 3 study, the DMC periodically reviewed unblinded safety and efficacy data and made recommendations regarding the conduct of the study.
L1559: A HEC consisting of 3 hepatologists reviewed all investigator-reported AEs as well as all hepatic AEs and LFT laboratory abnormalities at least quarterly, or more frequently as needed. In general, the DMC and HEC recommendations were to continue study conduct without modifications.
L1560: The Sponsor performed oversight of the data management of this trial, including approval of the CRO's data management and data quality plans. The sites were responsible for data entry into the EDC system. In the event of discrepant data, the CRO requested data clarification from the sites, which the sites resolved electronically in the EDC system. eCRFs and query resolution documentation was maintained in the EDC system's audit trail. System backups for data stored at the CRO was performed in accordance with the CRO's standard operating procedures.
L1561: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1562: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 217779 imetelstat
## TRADENAME, imetelstat
L1563: No meaningful concerns are anticipated in the quality and integrity of the submitted data; these were sufficiently complete to allow for a thorough review of safety. Furthermore, no data integrity concerns were reported following completion of site inspections; data in the CRFs and AE databases were consistent.
L1564: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1565: In general, we agree that the data quality of the submission is adequate. However, several points required clarification:
L1566: We noted on analysis of the initial ADAE dataset that among 275 ADAE described for which the treatment emergent flag was not checked, the action taken with study treatment was described as drug withdrawn for 5 cases, drug interrupted for 7, unknown for one, and dose not changed for 89, suggesting that a total of 102 cases occurred while the patient was receiving medication. Among these 102 events, 43 occurred in 5 patients in the phase 2 imetelstat cohort, 52 in 15 patients in the imetelstat arm of the phase 3 portion of the study, and seven in four patients on the placebo arm. An IR was sent to the Sponsor to clarify this apparent discrepancy.
L1567: Of note, some patients had multiple reasons for treatment modification and may have events both described as treatment emergent and non-treatment emergent. When total number of patients affected was considered, no patient treated with imetelstat on the phase 3 arm had only non-TEAE as reason for treatment delay or dose modification and three out of 21 had only treatment non-emergent events as the reason for discontinuing treatment (that is, the rate of all events leading to discontinuation was 18% (21 patients), the rate of treatment emergent events only was 15% (18 patients), with one patient having both treatment emergent and treatment non-emergent events flagged as leading to treatment discontinuation.
L1568: The Applicant clarified that for situations in which the AE is described as not treatment emergent but an action was taken regarding the study medication, the AE occurred while treatment was on hold and further actions may have been required due to the AE, although it occurred in the absence of active treatment and therefore was not considered treatment emergent. For example, a patient whose treatment was held due to a treatment emergent AE may experience a further AE while medication was being held that led to treatment discontinuation.
L1569: *Clinical Reviewer comment: The Applicant's rationale for not considering the events described above as treatment emergent is not entirely consistent with the definition of TEAE as described in the protocol. However, the effect on the total safety profile is relatively small. Also see section on treatment discontinuation for further information.*
L1570: Another issue was a potential under-reporting of events. Specifically, patient (b) (6) a patient on the phase 2 portion of the study, experienced transaminase and bilirubin elevations consistent with a Hy's law case. The Applicant stated that this was not a Hy's law case as the abnormalities were related to sepsis. However, the patient had not had sepsis documented in the ADAE database. The Applicant's response follows:
L1571: "For patient (b) (6) the attribution for the events of transaminase and bilirubin elevations on days 231-235 due to sepsis was derived from the investigator's assessment in a follow up questionnaire. The investigator responded that the only SAE for this patient was the grade 3 Escherichia UTI which was responsible for the sepsis and transaminase elevations. The sepsis was not captured as an SAE in the safety database based on the investigator's response to the safety follow up report. An SAE of grade 3 Escherichia UTI was also reported in the clinical database, however, the event of sepsis was not reported in the clinical database and, therefore, does not appear in the ADAE dataset."
L1572: *Clinical Reviewer comments: This response raises concerns about under-reporting of safety events. However, the OSI inspections did not reveal a systematic issue and therefore we consider that this is likely an isolated incident.*
L1573: *In summary, the data quality appears generally adequate, but there are minor issues that should be taken into account, particularly given the unexpectedly low number of events of infection and bleeding given the very severe and frequent cytopenias observed.*
L1574: ## Categorization of Adverse Event
L1575: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1576: TEAEs, as reported in each study, were used for the safety analysis. The definition of TEAEs in each study were:
L1577: - MDS3001: 1) AEs that occurred or worsened after the first dose of study drug through the treatment phase and for 30 days following the last dose of study drug or until subsequent anti-cancer therapy if earlier; 2) any event that was considered study drug-related regardless of the start date of the event; or 3) any event that was presented at baseline but worsened in severity or was subsequently considered by the investigator to be study drug-related.
- MYF2001 and CP14B019: AEs that occurred or worsened after the first dose of study drug and within 30 days following the last dose of study drug.
- CP14B015: AEs that occurred or worsened after the first dose of study drug until 30 days after the last administration of study treatment or study discontinuation/termination, whichever occurred first.
L1578: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 217779 imetelstat
## TRADENAME, imetelstat
L1579: - CP14B013: AEs that occurred or worsened after the first dose of study drug until 30 days after their last infusion of imetelstat or until initiation of alternate therapy for their MM, whichever occurred first.
L1580: All AEs were coded with system organ class (SOC) and PT according to MedDRA version 25.0. For analysis purposes, the following PTs were combined thrombocytopenia (thrombocytopenia + platelet count decreased), neutropenia (neutropenia + neutrophil count decreased), and leukopenia (leukopenia + white blood cell count decreased). The severity of AEs was graded 1 to 5 according to National Cancer Institute (NCI)-Common Terminology Criteria for Adverse Events (CTCAE) as assigned in the original studies. For each subject and each TEAE, the worst severity recorded was attributed and used in the by-severity summaries.
L1581: Adverse events reflective of laboratory measurements were considered based on worsening of laboratory values, given the high prevalence of laboratory abnormalities in this subject population and higher degree of accuracy in the laboratory values compared to AE reporting, in order to provide the most conservative frequencies.
L1582: Adverse events of interest as defined in the MDS3001 protocol include liver function abnormalities based on laboratory data, and hepatic TEAEs (based on high level terms and hepatic PTs). In addition, other selected AEs of interest (thrombocytopenia and bleeding events, neutropenia and infections, IRRs), which were not specifically defined in the MDS3001 protocol, were identified based on the mechanism of action of imetelstat and the safety profile observed to date and have been monitored throughout the study. After the start of the COVID-19 pandemic in March 2020, events related to COVID-19 were also monitored as events of interest in Study MDS3001.
L1583: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1584: In general, the Applicant's definition of treatment emergent AE is reasonable. We note that in addition to the preferred terms, some AE will be examined using grouped terms as noted in the appendix. In addition, as noted later in the safety section, some events that occur more than 30 days after the end of treatment are considered potentially related for the following reasons:
L1585: - A related AE occurred on treatment or within 30 days of treatment and there is evidence that the later, non-treatment related AE is related to the earlier TEAE. An example might include infections occurring &gt;30 days after last dose but with neutropenia that began within 30 days of last dose and had not resolved at the time of the infection.
- The duration of cytopenias after end of treatment with imetelstat is unclear and events that may be related to cytopenias (infection, hemorrhage) may be related to treatment, despite occurring &gt;30 days after end of treatment. This issue will be explored further below.
L1586: ## **Routine Clinical Tests**
L1587: TRADENAME, imetelstat
L1588: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1589: In the pivotal MDS3001 Phase 3 study, clinical assessments including physical examinations and vital signs were taken at screening and pre-dose on Day 1 of every 28-day cycle. 12-lead ECG was assessed at Screening and Day 1 of Cycle 2. Samples for clinical laboratory assessments were collected at Screening, pre-dose on Cycle 1 Day 1, weekly during Cycle 1, and Cycle 2 and then pre-dose on Day 1 of every 28-day cycle until end of treatment. Local laboratories were used for routine safety laboratory parameters, and reference ranges for each laboratory were collected and used in the analysis. Clinical significance of laboratory values were determined by the investigator; clinically significant abnormalities were reported as AEs. The following laboratory parameters were assessed for safety: hematology, coagulation, and serum chemistry, including liver function tests.
L1590: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1591: The Agency acknowledges the Applicant's description of the physical examination, vital signs, laboratory tests, and ECG evaluations performed as part of MDS3001. We note the following regarding their adequacy:
L1592: - The ECG assessments were inadequate to definitively assess the risk of QT prolongation with use of imetelstat. A further ventricular repolarization study to address this issue is underway. In addition, very little information is provided regarding the ECGs that were documented. With few exceptions, the only data provided was whether the ECG was read as normal or abnormal with or without clinical significance to the abnormality. The ventricular repolarization study should provide further information regarding cardiac issues that can be observed on ECG (rhythm, signs of stress) as well as QT interval information.
- Laboratory tests, including complete blood count (CBC) were performed less frequently after cycle 2. Therefore, the rate of cytopenias observed in each cycle may be biased by lack of evaluation in later cycles. Similarly, monitoring of hepatic function and other potential laboratory abnormalities was decreased after the first two cycles and may lead to missed findings of asymptomatic transaminase increases.
L1593: ## 8.2.4. Safety Results
L1594: ## Deaths
L1595: ## Data:
L1596: In the MDS3001 Phase 3 study, the rate and causes of death (including during treatment and in the post-treatment follow-up period) were similar between the imetelstat group (16.1%; N = 118) and the placebo group (13.6%; N = 59) and most deaths occurred during follow-up (Applicant Table 47). One subject in each arm died during the treatment period and the events
L1597: were considered Grade 5 TEAEs (sepsis in the imetelstat-treated subject and aortic stenosis in the placebo-treated subject) (Table tae05a). Both events were considered unrelated to study treatment (Table tae05d).
L1598: In the MDS3001 Phase 3 post-treatment follow-up, 15.3% of subjects in the imetelstat group and 11.9% of subjects in the placebo group died (Applicant Table 47). The reported cause of death in 6 (5.1%) subjects in the imetelstat group and 2 (3.4%) subjects in the placebo group were non-TEAEs, all assessed as not related to study treatment and all occurred &gt; 30 days after last dose of study treatment. Progressive disease was the reported cause of death in 1 (0.8%) and 3 (5.1%) subjects in the imetelstat and placebo groups, respectively. The cause of death was reported as “Other” for 11 (9.3%) subjects in imetelstat and 2 (3.4%) subjects in placebo and all of these deaths occurred \ge 90 days from the last treatment dose. These deaths included events associated with the underlying disease or subsequent therapies, such as infections and clinical deterioration (7 subjects), unknown causes of death (3 subjects), and death from complications of hematopoietic stem cell transplant (2 subjects). These deaths all occurred \ge 90 days from the last treatment dose.
L1599: There was a greater proportion of deaths overall in imetelstat-treated subjects in MDS3001 Phase 2 compared to MDS3001 Phase 3 imetelstat groups (47.4% vs 16.1%, respectively; Applicant Table 47), which is expected due to the approximately 3 years of longer median duration of time on study in Phase 2 (57.3 months vs 19.5 months, respectively). More subjects died during the treatment period with Grade 5 TEAEs in Phase 2 (5 subjects with one of the following events: cerebrovascular accident, COVID-19 pneumonia, femur fracture, neurodegenerative disorder, and thrombosis mesenteric vessel) compared to Phase 3 (1 subject with sepsis; Table tae05b). All of the events were considered unrelated to study treatment (Table tae05e). The remaining deaths occurred during post-treatment follow-up. In the Phase 2 post-treatment follow-up, 22 (38.6%) subjects died with reported cause of death of non-TEAE in 1 (1.8%) subject, progressive disease in 8 (14.0%) subjects, and “Other” in 13 (22.8%) subjects (Applicant Table 47). These deaths included events associated with the underlying disease or subsequent therapies, such as infections and clinical deterioration (10 subjects), unknown causes of death (2 subjects), and death from complications of hematopoietic stem cell transplant (1 subject).
L1600: After review of death from across all monotherapy studies in hematologic malignancies (N = 391), no patterns of fatal events or infrequent deaths of concern were apparent. There were higher proportions of deaths overall in the &lt; 7.1 mg/kg and &gt; 7.1 mg/kg groups (72.9% and 59.0%, respectively) compared to the 7.1 mg/kg group (29.9%) (Applicant Table 48), which corresponds with longer median times on study (41.6 months and 42.4 months vs 24.2 months, respectively). The following TEAEs that led to death (including death &gt; 30 days after last dose) were reported in 1 subject each in the following dose groups (Table tae05c):
L1601: - &lt; 7.1 mg/kg group: sepsis, head injury, and splenic infarction
L1602: - 7.1 mg/kg group: sepsis (MDS3001 Phase 3); cerebrovascular accident, COVID-19 pneumonia, femur fracture, neurodegenerative disorder, and thrombosis mesenteric vessel (MDS3001 Phase 2); and oesophageal varices haemorrhage and pneumonia
- &gt; 7.1 mg/kg: acute respiratory failure, cardiac failure, haemorrhage, and respiratory failure
L1603: All of the events were considered unrelated to study treatment except for the haemorrhage in the &gt; 7.1 mg/kg group, for which a brief narrative is provided below (Table tae05f). There were no fatal TEAEs in Study CP14B013 (Listing lae02a).

L1621: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1622: A review of the overall causes of death in the MDS3001 Phase 3 study indicates no increased mortality from imetelstat treatment as compared to placebo. Deaths during treatment occurred infrequently with only 1 death in each group, both of which were not related to study treatment. The majority of deaths occurred in post-treatment follow up ≥ 90 days from the last treatment dose and were generally balanced between both groups. All deaths in the post-treatment follow up period were considered unrelated to study treatment, were due to
L1623: underlying MDS and/or disease progression or complications from comorbidities and subsequent therapies. No early deaths were reported.
L1624: Results in the MDS3001 Phase 2 imetelstat group were consistent with MDS3001 Phase 3. A review of the overall causes of death shows that deaths occurred infrequently during imetelstat treatment (5 subjects experiencing Grade 5 TEAEs that were all not related to imetelstat treatment), with the majority of deaths occurring in post-treatment follow-up. All deaths in the post-treatment follow-up period were considered unrelated to study treatment and were due to underlying MDS and/or disease progression or complications from comorbidities and subsequent therapies.
L1625: A review of the deaths in the pooled monotherapy grouping does not suggest a causal relationship to imetelstat as these deaths are confounded by underlying disease and comorbidities.
L1626: The Sponsor continues to actively follow all subjects to monitor for death on treatment and in post-treatment follow-up.
L1627: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1628: **Overview:** Updated information on overall survival was provided twice after the initial submission. First, in the 120-day safety update (data cut-off 10 May 2023) and then in an ad hoc overall survival update with data cut-off date of 5 Jan 2024. An overview of deaths documented at each time point is provided in Table 49. Of note, the number of patients who died was higher in the imetelstat arm of the phase 3 portion at each time point provided. In the phase 2 portion of the study, nearly half of patients have died. No further fatal events were reported in the imetelstat phase 2 arm between the 120-day safety update and the overall survival update. However, all patients on this portion of the study have completed or discontinued the study and the most recent end of study date was 2 October 2023, suggesting that the lack of further recorded deaths is related to lack of further follow up rather than to no further deaths occurring. It should be noted that due to differences in patient characteristics and follow up time, deaths in the phase 2 portion cannot be directly compared to those in the phase 3 portion. Deaths are described based on the time of submission, with the data from the initial submission provided first followed by the data in the 120-day safety update and the OS update.

L1632: ## Data from initial submission:
L1633: As noted by the Applicant, a higher percentage of patients in the imetelstat arm died on study compared to patients in the placebo arm, with death by the time of data cut-off occurring in 19 (16%) of patients on the imetelstat arm and 8 (14%) of patients on the placebo arm. Of these, two (one in each arm) occurred on treatment or within 30 days of last dose of imetelstat/placebo. However, it is noted that some fatal events began within 30 days of last dose of the investigational product, although death was delayed to &gt;30 days, suggesting a potential relationship to the study drug. Brief narratives of the events and table with the FDA's adjudication of the events are below.

L1639: Narratives for events in the imetelstat arm described in the table above follow:
L1640: (b) (6) was a 72 y/o M with IPSS low risk MDS with baseline RBC transfusion requirement of 8 units/8 weeks and prior use of ESA. He was randomized to imetelstat and received 23 cycles of imetelstat total. On day 619 (C23 D1) he developed grade 3 neutropenia, anemia, and thrombocytopenia which worsened to grade 4 by day 633. Imetelstat was given despite low counts, which was a major protocol deviation. On study day 644, he developed grade 4 sepsis. He died on day 649 of acute respiratory failure, septic shock, pneumonia, and cardiac ischemia.
L1641: *Clinical Reviewer comments: Neutropenia is an AE associated with use of imetelstat and fatal sepsis in the context of neutropenia in a patient on active treatment with imetelstat is considered related to the drug.*
L1642: (b) (6) 78 y/o M with IPSS intermediate-1 MDS with baseline transfusion burden of 6 units/8 weeks, prior treatment with luspatercept randomized to imetelstat arm. The patient had grade 3 decreased leukocytes and grade 2 neutropenia on study day 15. On study day 16, a grade 1 upper respiratory tract infection was reported. On study day 20, bacterial pneumonia and congestive heart failure were reported. On study day 29, leukocyte count was grade 1 and neutropenia had resolved. COVID-19 pneumonia was documented on day 35 and was fatal on day 42.
L1643: *Clinical Reviewer comments: The event of COVID-19 pneumonia occurred more than 30 days after the last dose of imetelstat (given on day 1 of cycle 1). However, it is noted that the patient appears to have had an unresolved bacterial pneumonia at the time of death which may have increased his vulnerability to COVID-19, despite an apparently normal neutrophil count at the time of infection. In addition, the patient's leukopenia had not entirely resolved at last documented evaluation and his neutrophil count had not increased appropriately for a serious infection, suggesting some level of immune dysfunction. In summary, the event is considered unlikely related due to timing, prior pneumonia that occurred during a neutropenic episode, and comorbid cardiac failure that occurred on treatment.*
L1644: (b) (6) 83 y/o F with intermediate-1 MDS and a baseline transfusion burden of 8 units/8 weeks, previously treated with ESA, randomized to imetelstat. The patient had a history of myelofibrosis and splenectomy. She received 4 cycles of imetelstat total and withdrew due to
L1645: TRADENAME, imetelstat
L1646: worsening of myelofibrosis and development of peripheral blood blasts on study day 125. Other AE noted included asthenia, malnutrition, abdominal pain, and stomatitis. She died on study day 131, with death attributed to AML.
L1647: *Clinical Reviewer comments: No bone marrow biopsy results after the initial screening biopsy are provided and therefore cannot confirm the diagnosis of AML. However, peripheral blood blasts were noted on the last laboratory evaluation on study. Of note, last neutrophil and platelet counts were normal or decreased to grade 1, which may call the attribution of death to AML into question. The patient's general physical condition (malnutrition, asthenia) may have contributed to the death.*
L1648: (b) (6) 75 y/o M with low risk MDS with baseline transfusion burden of &lt;6 units/8 weeks who was randomized to imetelstat arm of study. The patient discontinued treatment on study day 555 due to lack of efficacy (last dose of study medication day 527). Grade 2 neutropenia was noted at the time of last dose of imetelstat and was persistent through the last laboratory value on study on day 555. He developed gram negative bacteremia and cardiorenal decompensation on about day 562 of the study and died on day 583, with death attributed to cardiorenal syndrome. Adverse events of thrombocytopenia and neutropenia were noted during the study, with the last ANC on study showing grade 2 neutropenia.
L1649: *Clinical Reviewer comments: Although the neutropenia noted at about the time the patient developed fatal bacteremia and cardiorenal syndrome was not severe, it may have contributed to the event. In addition, the patient had a normal baseline neutrophil count, suggesting that the neutropenia could be attributed to use of imetelstat. Thus, this event is considered possibly related.*
L1650: (b) (6) 83 y/o M with low risk MDS and baseline transfusion burden of 6 units/8 weeks who was randomized to imetelstat. The patient had a past medical history of myocardial ischemia and myocardial infarction, but no documented history of cardiac insufficiency. The patient had a normal platelet count at baseline. On study day 25, he developed grade 4 GI hemorrhage, and a bleeding duodenal ulcer was found. Platelet count on study day 22 (last count prior to event) was 207 and platelet count documented during the event was 164. Coagulation profile was not documented, but INR was noted to be increased (grade 1) at end of study labs on day 59 with a normal baseline. He was successfully treated for the ulcer but fell during hospitalization on study day 29 and grade 4 deconditioning and cardiac failure were reported on days 36 and 47, respectively. Treatment was withdrawn on day 59 (second planned dose was not given). The patient died on day 95 due to multiple medical issues including cardiac insufficiency, deconditioning, and GI hemorrhage.
L1651: *Clinical Reviewer comments: This patient had multiple medical issues. The patient had platelet counts in the normal or grade 1 thrombocytopenic range except for a single value of 68 (grade*
L1652: TRADENAME, imetelstat
L1653: 2) on day 80 of the study. Grade 3-4 cardiac insufficiency was seen more frequently on the imetelstat arm of the study and may have contributed to death. However, death occurred &gt;90 days after the last (and only) dose of imetelstat and the GI hemorrhage was not known to be associated with thrombocytopenia, leading to doubt about the connection. Thus, this death is considered unlikely related to treatment.
L1654: (b) (6) 65 y/o M with low risk MDS and baseline transfusion burden of 19 units/8 weeks randomized to the imetelstat arm. The patient had a history of left ventricular failure and cardiac failure as well as iron overload. The patient received 4 cycles of imetelstat. On study day 80, an SAE of pneumonia was reported, and he was treated with antibiotics, steroids, and furosemide. Cycle 4 was delayed due to the pneumonia, but started on study day 113. On study day 131, an SAE of cardiac failure was reported. On study day 190, treatment was withdrawn due to cardiac failure and the subject died of this AE on study day 208.
L1655: Clinical Reviewer comments: The patient has a history of pre-existing cardiac issues which complicate the assessment of the reported fatal cardiac failure. Use of imetelstat may have worsened the cardiac failure, leading to death given that the AE was reported only on study day 131. Overall, this event is considered unlikely related due to multiple confounders and the time between last dose and death.
L1656: (b) (6) 71 y/o M with intermediate-1 MDS and baseline transfusion burden of &lt;6 units/8 weeks experienced GI hemorrhage on day 621 and died on study day 647, 105 days after the last dose of imetelstat was given. The patient had a history of vocal cord and prostate cancer, and the bleeding was suspected to be related to metastatic disease. The patient had experienced thrombocytopenia during treatment with a platelet count of 89 (grade 1) on the day of the GI hemorrhage and 48 (grade 2) on day 603 (last evaluation prior to event).
L1657: Clinical Reviewer comments: This patient had a history of metastatic solid malignancy which is likely to have been the major factor leading to the event of fatal GI bleeding. However, some contribution of drug related thrombocytopenia cannot be ruled out.
L1658: (b) (6) 66 y/o M with low risk MDS and baseline transfusion burden of 6 units/8 weeks with prior therapy with ESA was randomized to the imetelstat arm and received a total of 8 cycles of therapy. The patient withdrew from treatment on study day 253 due to lack of efficacy. Neutrophil count was reported as within normal limits at that time. The patient died of septic shock on study day 622.
L1659: (b) (6) 69 y/o M with low risk MDS and baseline transfusion burden of 8 units/8 weeks previously treated with ESA was randomized to imetelstat and received a total of 6 cycles of therapy. The patient had baseline grade 4 neutropenia, grade 2 anemia, and grade 1 thrombocytopenia. Multiple cycles were delayed due to neutropenia. Treatment was
L1660: withdrawn due to neutropenia on study day 260. The patient died on study day 548 with death being attributed to clinical degradation.
L1661: (b)(6) 87 y/o M with intermediate-1 MDS and baseline transfusion burden of &gt;6 units/8 weeks who was randomized to imetelstat. The patient died of respiratory insufficiency on study day 451, 245 days after last dose of imetelstat.
L1662: (b)(6) 64 y/o F with intermediate-1 MDS and a baseline transfusion burden of &gt;6 units/8 weeks randomized to the imetelstat arm. The patient died of pneumonia on study day 892, 646 days after the last dose of study medication.
L1663: *Clinical Reviewer comments: Given the long time period between last treatment and death in these patients, the fatal events are considered not related to imetelstat.*
L1664: (b)(6) 73 y/o F with low risk MDS and a baseline transfusion burden of 7 units/8 weeks, previously treated with ESA who was randomized to imetelstat. The patient died of multiple organ failure on study day 313, 93 days after last dose of imetelstat. The patient had multiple AEs of diarrhea during treatment as well as diverticulosis, neutropenia, and thrombocytopenia. AEs that were not documented to have resolved prior to death included diarrhea, abdominal pain, diverticulosis, and hyperferritinemia. The last neutrophil count documented on study was 780 (grade 3).
L1665: *Clinical Reviewer comments: Given the time between the last dose of medication and the fatal event of multiple organ failure, a relationship between imetelstat and the fatal event is unlikely. However, given that events leading up to multiple organ failure could be prolonged, a relationship cannot be ruled out entirely and the event is therefore considered unlikely related.*
L1666: (b)(6) 61 y/o M with low risk MDS and transfusion burden of ≤6 units/8 weeks treated with imetelstat died of CMV infection and graft versus host disease on study day 771, 525 days after last dose. The patient withdrew from treatment on day 274 due to progressive disease and underwent HSCT on study day 470.
L1667: (b)(6) 83 y/o M with intermediate risk disease and baseline transfusion burden of &gt;6 units/8 weeks randomized to the imetelstat arm. The patient is described as having had “sudden death on the bus” on study day 542, 304 days after last treatment with imetelstat. The patient was noted to have experienced thrombocytopenia while on study, but this AE resolved by study day 175. Of note, he had an AE of dizziness reported as well as two falls on study days 413 and 505.
L1668: *Clinical Reviewer comments: The sudden death of a patient on clinical trial examining use of a novel therapy is always of concern. Possible reasons for sudden death might include QT prolongation, intracranial hemorrhage, and cardiac events. However, it is unlikely that the*
L1669: *effects of imetelstat would last nearly a year after the last dose was given. The underlying condition and potentially other medications are more likely causes of the event of sudden death.*
L1670: (b)(6) 83 y/o F with low-risk disease and a baseline transfusion burden of &gt;6 units/8 weeks who was randomized to imetelstat died of unknown cause on study day 712, 248 days after last dose of imetelstat. The patient discontinued treatment due to relapse. No further details were provided regarding events leading to the patient's death. AEs reported during the patient's treatment included pyrexia, fatigue, and thrombocytopenia, all of which had resolved by day 464.
L1671: (b)(6) 86 y/o M with intermediate-1 MDS with a transfusion burden of &gt;6 units/8 weeks randomized to imetelstat died of unknown cause on study day 278, 109 days after the last treatment dose. The patient is described as having “died at home in his sleep”. It was noted that no autopsy was performed. Treatment was discontinued due to lack of efficacy. The only AEs noted during treatment were neutropenia and thrombocytopenia.
L1672: *Clinical Reviewer comments: The long interval between last dose of imetelstat and death and lack of unresolved AEs or AEs likely to result in long term complications during treatment make it highly unlikely that these patients' deaths were related to treatment.*
L1673: (b)(6) 72 y/o M with low risk MDS with baseline transfusion burden of 8 units/8 weeks who was randomized to imetelstat and received 5 cycles of treatment. The patient died on study day 283, 90 days after last treatment, due to complications of embolic stroke. He withdrew from treatment on study day 180 due to a new diagnosis of a lung neoplasm. He was noted to have developed atrial fibrillation while on study (on study day 3), as well as neutropenia and thrombocytopenia. The atrial fibrillation is noted to have resolved. No prior history of atrial fibrillation or other cardiac issues was noted.
L1674: *Clinical Reviewer comments: This patient had at least two risk factors for embolic stroke: atrial fibrillation and lung neoplasm. Although the event of atrial fibrillation documented resolved, atrial fibrillation may be intermittent and may have returned at a later time. The timing of the initial diagnosis of atrial fibrillation is suggestive of a possible relationship to imetelstat. Conversely, solid tumors are frequently associated with thrombosis and the diagnosis of lung neoplasm may have contributed. Overall, it is not possible to definitively rule in or out a contribution from imetelstat and the event is considered unlikely related.*
L1675: (b)(6) 85 y/o F with low risk MDS and baseline transfusion burden of &lt;=6 units/8 weeks randomized to imetelstat and treated with 5 cycles before discontinuing due to lack of efficacy died of progressive disease on study day 368, 241 days after last dose of imetelstat. AEs while on study were notable for cytopenias, UTIs, and an episode of QTc prolongation.
L1676: TRADENAME, imetelstat
L1677: *Clinical Reviewer comments: Given the long time between last dose and death as well as the diagnosis of progressive disease, this event is considered not related to imetelstat.*
L1678: (b)(6) 83 y/o M with low risk MDS with a baseline transfusion burden of ≤6 units/8 weeks randomized to imetelstat died of respiratory insufficiency on study day 286, 228 days after the last dose of imetelstat. The patient discontinued treatment after 3 doses due to thrombocytopenia and lymphopenia. Past medical history included chronic kidney disease, MI, hypertension, atrial fibrillation, and Parkinson's disease. The patient was noted to have had CHF and respiratory insufficiency during treatment. However, both were noted as resolved.
L1679: *Clinical Reviewer comments: Given the amount of time between last treatment and death as well as the multiple comorbid conditions, this death is considered unrelated to imetelstat.*

L1717: ## Deaths on phase 2 portion of study
L1718: Twenty-seven deaths (47%) were reported among patients in part 1 (phase 2) portion of the study, including 5 deaths on treatment. L1719: Among the 22 patients who died after the end of treatment, time from last dose of study treatment to death ranged from 60 to 1937 days. Death was attributed to progressive disease in 8 patients, in whom time from last dose to death ranged from 387 to 1446 days. L1720: progressive disease or transplantation complications will not be included here unless review of AEs suggests that an ongoing AE may have contributed to the death.

L1724: Relevant narratives are below:
L1725: TRADENAME, imetelstat
L1726: (b)(6) 82 y/o F with MDS treated with imetelstat for &gt;1 year was hospitalized on study day 1186 for a grade 4 cerebrovascular accident. CT showed vascular thrombus and no hemorrhage, and the patient underwent thrombectomy. The patient had multiple AEs while on study, including neutropenia, thrombocytopenia, leukopenia, transaminases increased, and multiple infectious and bleeding events. Platelet count on study day 1176 (last documented prior to event) was 139. On day 1187, the platelet count was 257. No platelet transfusions were documented. The patient died on study day 1192 after being transitioned to palliative care only.
L1727: (b)(6) 62 y/o F with IPSS low risk MDS with a transfusion burden of 5 units/8 weeks died of thrombosis of mesenteric vessel on study day 953, 3 days after last dose of imetelstat. The patient did not have thrombocytopenia documented as an AE prior to the event and last documented platelet count on study was 400 on study day 951.
L1728: *Clinical Reviewer comments: Thrombosis was not increased overall in patients receiving imetelstat. However, the timing of the events (within a few days of last dose of study medication) makes a relationship hard to definitively rule out, especially in light of the relatively young age of one of the patients.*
L1729: (b)(6) 67 y/o F with low risk MDS and a past medical history notable for hypertension, chronic gastritis, and cholecystitis, with a transfusion burden of 6 units/8 weeks died of neurodegenerative disorder on study day 391, 26 days after her last dose of study medication. The patient was stated to have been asymptomatic on study day 390 but was found dead on study day 391. The death certificate listed “Senile degeneration of the brain” as the cause of death. No autopsy was performed. Last platelet count documented on study was 56 on study day 367. Of note, the patient had a bleeding episode requiring RBC transfusion (hemorrhoidal hemorrhage) on study day 314, with the last platelet count prior to this event being 117 on study day 309.
L1730: *Clinical Reviewer comments: The diagnosis of neurodegeneration is unexpected in a patient with no apparent history of neurologic disorder of any sort and who was asymptomatic one day prior to the fatal event. Given that no autopsy was performed and in light of the patient's history of severe bleeding at a platelet count of &gt;100, an acute intracranial hemorrhage cannot be ruled out. Thus, the death is considered possibly related to imetelstat.*
L1731: (b)(6) 81 y/o F with intermediate-1 MDS with a transfusion burden of 8 units per 8 weeks died of complications of femur fracture on day 1042 of the study, 4 days after her last dose of study medication. The patient's course of therapy was complicated by multiple infectious issues and cardiac failure. On study day 1038, cycle 33 of imetelstat was administered. On study day 1039, the patient experienced a femoral fracture. The details of how the fracture occurred were not provided. The patient withdrew from treatment and died on study day 1042.
L1732: *Clinical Reviewer comments: Patients receiving imetelstat had a higher rate of fractures and severe fractures compared to those receiving placebo. Thus, this death is considered probably related.*
L1733: (b)(6) 58 y/o M with low risk MDS and baseline transfusion burden of 9 units/8 weeks as well as past medical history notable for hypertension, cardiomyopathy, Gilbert's syndrome, and splenomegaly, died on study day 1278 from COVID-19 pneumonia. The patient was found to have progressive disease on study day 1259. At that time, his hemoglobin was 4.7 g/dl, platelets were 80,000 and ANC 2870. However, his marrow showed 42% blasts. He discontinued treatment at that time, with last dose of imetelstat being given on that day. On study day 1269, he was diagnosed with COVID-19 pneumonia. His white blood count was 0.8 \times 10^9/L at that time. He was treated aggressively for COVID pneumonia, but died on study day 1278, 19 days after the last dose of imetelstat.
L1734: *Clinical Reviewer comments: Given the state of the underlying disease at the time of the AE as well as the potential for COVID-19 to cause severe disease even in non-immunosuppressed individuals, the death due to COVID-19 pneumonia is unlikely related to treatment. However, we cannot rule out the possibility that imetelstat treatment contributed to the death.*
L1735: (b)(6) 58 y/o M with MDS, IPSS and baseline transfusion requirement not specified, died on study day 428 of distributive shock at the time of acute ischemic abdomen, 313 days after last treatment with imetelstat. No narrative was provided for this patient. The patient was noted to have received subsequent therapy for MDS, but the details were not provided.
L1736: *Clinical Reviewer comments: Given the amount of time between last dose and the report of subsequent treatment for MDS, the death is considered not related to imetelstat.*
L1737: (b)(6) 80 y/o F with low risk MDS, RBC transfusion requirement not provided, treated on the phase 2 portion of the study (part 1), receiving a total of 4 cycles of imetelstat. On study day 98, she developed grade 3 cardiac failure. Evaluation for coronary artery disease showed mild CAD which did not explain the decrease in ejection fraction. On study day 195, cardiac MRI showed an ejection fraction of 50% and evidence of iron overload. Deferasirox treatment was started. The event of cardiac failure was stated to have ended on study day 238. On study day 1433, the patient died of cardiac decompensation stated to be related to anemia and MDS.
L1738: *Clinical Reviewer comments: Although the time between last treatment and the fatal event is long and there were confounding factors, notably anemia and MDS, a contribution of imetelstat cannot be ruled out as the patient developed cardiac failure on study and residual cardiac insufficiency may have contributed to the fatal event.*
L1739: TRADENAME, imetelstat
L1740: (b)(6) 60 y/o F with low risk MDS, transfusion burden not stated, and history of iron overload was treated with imetelstat on part 1 of the study. On study day 6, grade 3 anemia was reported (baseline grade 2) and transfusions were given with improvement in anemia. The patient experienced repeated episodes of cytopenia during treatment and was dose reduced several times due to this. On study day 814, the patient was withdrawn due to thrombocytopenia. No cardiac issues were noted during treatment, although non-cardiac chest pain was noted on days 649 and 651. On study day 1053, the patient died of congestive heart failure. No details of events between end of treatment and death were provided.
L1741: *Clinical Reviewer comments: The long time period between end of treatment and death, confounding issues of iron overload and chronic anemia, and lack of evidence of worsening of cardiac function on study make it less likely that imetelstat was the cause of the fatal event. However, the patient had no past medical history of cardiac disease and a decrease in ejection fraction that was not noted in the AE dataset during treatment cannot be ruled out. Overall, the event is adjudicated as unlikely related.*
L1742: (b)(6) 79 y/o M with intermediate risk MDS and baseline transfusion burden of 10 units/8 weeks treated with imetelstat on part 1 of the study. The patient experienced thrombocytopenia requiring dose decrease to 4.7 mg/kg in cycle 6 but received treatment at this dose through cycle 23. On study day 590, a UTI was reported and treated with ciprofloxacin. On day 599, hematuria was reported and workup for possible transitional cell carcinoma was started. On study day 630, a grade 3 SAE of transitional cell carcinoma was reported. The patient underwent nephroureterectomy and treatment with imetelstat resumed. On study day 748, biopsy of a supraclavicular LN showed metastatic transitional cell carcinoma and the patient was removed from treatment on study day 765. He was treated palliatively for the transitional cell carcinoma with radiation. He died on study day 896 of transitional cell carcinoma.
L1743: *Clinical Reviewer comments: The patient appears to have developed transitional cell carcinoma while on treatment with imetelstat and an increased risk of malignancy in patients receiving imetelstat cannot be ruled out (see section 8.2.9). Therefore, this event is considered possibly related to imetelstat.*
L1744: (b)(6) 80 y/o M with low risk MDS with a baseline transfusion burden of 14 units/8 weeks and a PMH that includes angina and hypertension, treated on part 1 of the study died of congestive heart failure and hepatic encephalopathy on study day 349, 299 days after last dose of imetelstat. The patient was admitted to the hospital for grade 3 CHF on study day 80. Grade 1-2 AST and ALT elevations were reported on days 80 and 81 as well. On study day 82, febrile neutropenia was diagnosed and on study day 106, bronchoalveolar lavage demonstrated disseminated tuberculosis. The patient was treated with quadruple therapy for TB and the
L1745: TRADENAME, imetelstat
L1746: event was reported to have resolved on study day 294. On study day 255, the subject withdrew from treatment due to CHF. He died on study day 349 of CHF and hepatic encephalopathy.
L1747: *Clinical Reviewer comments: The patient has risk factors for CHF, including history of stable angina and hypertension. However, no CHF was reported prior to start of imetelstat. The issue of hepatic impairment is not discussed in the narrative. However, it is notable that the patient experienced transaminase elevations at the same time as the CHF was noted. Overall, the events of CHF and hepatic impairment are possibly related to imetelstat, although attribution is confounded by the underlying disease, possible iron overload, concurrent infections, and concomitant medications.*
L1748: (b) (6) 78 y/o M with low risk MDS and baseline transfusion burden of 9 units/8 weeks treated with imetelstat on phase 2 component of study. Past medical history included hemosiderosis. The patient experienced neutropenia and thrombocytopenia during treatment, including events that required dose modification. On study day 744 (cycle 23 day 28), he had grade 3 thrombocytopenia and treatment was withdrawn due to the AE. Last neutrophil count on study (day 772) was 3400. Last neutrophil count on treatment (day 744) was 2300. The patient died of probable sepsis on day 777.
L1749: *Clinical Reviewer comment: The patient died approximately 60 days after the last treatment with imetelstat. Neutrophil count was normal at the time of last evaluation prior to end of treatment and prior to death. The event of probable fulminant sepsis is therefore considered unlikely related to treatment, although a subtle continued immune defect cannot be ruled out.*
L1750: (b) (6) 63 y/o M with low risk MDS and baseline transfusion burden of 10 units/8 weeks treated with imetelstat on part 1 of the study, died of cardiogenic shock 246 days after last dose of study medication. The patient received a total of 6 cycles of imetelstat and was discontinued on study day 246 due to grade 2 thrombocytopenia. On study day 330, the patient had a bone marrow biopsy that showed progressive disease (7% blasts). The patient died on day 457, with death attributed to cardiogenic shock. No information on events between day 330 and day 457 was provided. No AE related to cardiac function were noted during treatment.
L1751: *Clinical Reviewer comments: The long time between last dose and death as well as lack of cardiac related AEs make it unlikely that the event of cardiogenic shock was related to treatment with imetelstat, but the possibility cannot be entirely ruled out.*
L1752: *One additional death (patient (b) (6) was reported in the survival update, bringing the number of deaths among patients on the phase 2 portion of the study to 28 (49%). The final death reported was attributed to progressive disease and occurred &gt;1 year after the end of treatment. The patient was noted to have received subsequent treatment for MDS.*
L1753: *In summary, more deaths occurred in the imetelstat arm of the phase 3 portion of the study compared to the placebo portion. Few deaths occurred on study, although at least one fatal adverse drug reaction due to sepsis was documented in the imetelstat arm. However, several deaths related to infection occurred in patients who had documented leukopenia or neutropenia less than 30 days after the end of treatment, although the actual death did not occur for more than 30 days. In addition, the time needed for full recovery of cytopenias after cessation of imetelstat is uncertain and deaths due to infection or bleeding may be related to imetelstat use even if they occur &gt;30 days after the end of treatment. Finally, a subtle change in the disease or residual normal bone marrow leading to an increased risk of issues related to cytopenias (infection, bleeding) cannot be ruled out.*
L1754: *Deaths on the phase 2 portion of the study cannot be directly compared to those on the phase 3 portion. However, it is notable that several deaths on treatment or within 30 days of treatment occurred in the phase 2 portion of the study, including at least one death which appears likely to have been related to intracranial hemorrhage, one due to infection (COVID-19), and one due to complications of fracture, each of which is related to adverse events that appear to be linked to use of imetelstat (see below for further details).*
L1755: *Finally, although the majority of fatal events do not have an apparent relationship to imetelstat use, there is a consistent trend towards more events in the imetelstat arm that is concerning, but no definite risk has been identified at this time.*
L1756: ## Serious Adverse Events
L1757: ## Data:
L1758: In the pivotal MDS3001 Phase 3 study, serious TEAEs were reported in 32.2% of subjects in the imetelstat group (N = 188) and 22.0% of subjects in the placebo group (N = 59) (Applicant Table 53). In both groups, the frequency of SAEs was highest in the Infections and infestations SOC (11.9% and 13.6%, respectively).
L1759: In the MDS3001 Phase 3 imetelstat group (N = 118), the most commonly reported SAEs were anaemia, cardiac failure, and pneumonia (2.5% each). Treatment-related SAEs were reported in 5.1% of subjects in the imetelstat group (Table tae02m). No treatment-related SAE was reported in more than 1 subject. One subject discontinued treatment due to treatment-related SAEs of Grade 4 sepsis and Grade 3 atrial fibrillation (along with concurrent non-serious Grade 3 renal abscess) (Listing lae02e).
L1760: In the MDS3001 Phase 3 placebo group (N = 59), the most commonly reported SAEs were COVID-19 pneumonia (5.1%) and abscess limb (3.4%). No subjects in the placebo group had a treatment-related SAE and no SAEs led to discontinuation of study treatment.
L1761: In the MDS3001 Phase 2 imetelstat group (N = 57), treatment-emergent SAEs were reported in 47.4% of subjects (Applicant Table 53). The most commonly reported SAE was femur fracture (5.3% of subjects), followed by cardiac failure, pneumonia, and thrombocytopenia in 3.5% of subjects each. All other SAEs were reported in 1 subject each. Treatment-related SAEs were reported in 15.8% of subjects (Table tae02n). Thrombocytopenia (2 [3.5%] subjects) was the only treatment-related SAE reported in more than 1 subject. Two treatment-related SAEs led to study drug withdrawal (Grade 3 cardiac failure and Grade 3 diarrhea), and all events resolved (Listing lae02c).
L1762: In subjects across all monotherapy studies in hematologic malignancies (N = 391), treatment-emergent SAEs were reported in 47.8% of subjects (Table tae03f). The most commonly reported SAEs in subjects across monotherapy studies were anaemia (13.6%; driven by MF studies), pneumonia (4.3%), and dyspnoea (1.8%). Treatment-related SAEs were reported in 15.1% of subjects (Table tae02o). Treatment-related SAEs reported in more than 1 subject included anaemia (5.1%), febrile neutropenia (1.8%), thrombocytopenia (1.0%), dyspnoea (0.8%), and chills, fatigue, pneumonia, IRR, and headache (0.5% each).
L1763: Across all hematologic monotherapy studies (N = 391), 6 subjects had treatment-related SAEs that led to study drug withdrawal (Listing lae02c). In addition to the 3 subjects with treatment-related SAEs that led to study drug withdrawal in Study MDS3001, there were 3 subjects in the MYF2001 8.9 mg/kg group with 1 treatment-related SAE each (Grade 5 haemorrhage), Grade 3 pancytopenia, and Grade 3 aneurysm ruptured).

L1772: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1773: The most frequently reported SAEs in the Phase 3 imetelstat group occurred in ≤ 2.5% of subjects each and included anaemia, cardiac failure, and pneumonia. The most commonly reported SAEs in the placebo group were COVID-19 pneumonia and abscess limb. There were no major differences in the types and frequencies of SAEs reported in the MDS3001 Phase 2 imetelstat group compared to MDS3001 Phase 3 imetelstat group, and no major differences from the overall monotherapy pool, except for lower incidence of COVID-19 SAEs as most of the corresponding studies were completed prior to the start of the pandemic.
L1774: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1775: A total of 156 SAEs were observed in 78 patients, including 27 (47%) receiving imetelstat in the phase 2 portion of the study, 38 (32%) receiving imetelstat in the phase 3 portion of the study, and 13 (22%) receiving placebo (Table 54).
L1776: In general, the risk of SAEs appeared to be higher in the phase 2 portion of the study, possibly related to differences in patient characteristics at baseline. However, overall, SAE were higher in patients receiving imetelstat in either part of the study compared to placebo.
L1777: The risk of SAEs of cytopenias (anemia, thrombocytopenia, neutropenia), infections including sepsis, cardiac failure, hypertension, and fractures were more commonly seen in patients treated with imetelstat on either part of the study compared to placebo. Neutropenia and complications of neutropenia (infections) were more common in patients receiving imetelstat in either study cohort compared to placebo. Thrombocytopenia as an SAE was seen in the phase 2 portion of the study, but not in either arm of the phase 3 portion. However, SAE of hemorrhage was seen more often in the imetelstat cohorts.

L1786: No additional SAE were reported in the 120-day safety update.
L1787: *Clinical Reviewer comments: Cytopenias and events associated with cytopenias (bleeding, cardiac failure, infections) were more common SAE in the imetelstat arm. This is expected given the known risk profile of imetelstat but does highlight that the cytopenias may have serious consequences for patients receiving imetelstat. SAE of hemorrhage and sepsis were less common in patients receiving imetelstat on the phase 3 portion of the study versus the phase 2. This may reflect differences in the patient populations or differences in reporting and the decision to label an event as serious or not.*
L1788: *Serious fractures were also more common among patients receiving imetelstat compared to placebo. The reason for this finding is unclear and should be correlated with results of overall risk profile.*
L1789: *Hypertension and cardiac failure as SAEs were rare in the imetelstat groups but were consistently observed with imetelstat and not seen or more rarely seen in the placebo arm of the phase 3 portion of the study. These findings may suggest a cardiac or vascular risk related to imetelstat. However, correlation with overall findings is needed.*
L1790: *Also see overall AE, grade 3+ AE, and treatment modification for AEs for further characterization of events.*
L1791: ## Dropouts and/or Discontinuations Due to Adverse Effects
L1792: ## Data:
L1793: In the MDS3001 Phase 3 imetelstat group (N = 118), 14.4% of subjects discontinued imetelstat treatment due to a TEAE (Applicant Table 55). The median time to study treatment discontinuation due to a TEAE was 21.1 weeks (range: 2.3 to 44.0) in the imetelstat group (MDS3001 CSR [Phase 3]/Table tsiex18_p2). The most commonly reported TEAE leading to study treatment withdrawal was neutropenia in 5.1% of subjects, followed by thrombocytopenia in 3.4% of subjects. All other TEAEs leading to study treatment withdrawal in the imetelstat group were reported in 1 subject each. Eleven (9.3%) subjects had TEAEs leading to imetelstat discontinuation that were considered related to imetelstat, including 6 subjects with Grade 3/4 neutropenia, 4 subjects with Grade 2/3 thrombocytopenia, and 1 subject each with Grade 4 sepsis, Grade 3 renal abscess, Grade 3 atrial fibrillation, and Grade 2 pruritus (MDS3001 CSR [Phase 3]/Listing lsfae01_p2).
L1794: No subjects in the MDS3001 Phase 3 placebo group (N = 59) discontinued study treatment due to a TEAE.
L1795: In the MDS3001-Phase 2 imetelstat group (N = 57), 28.1% subjects discontinued study treatment due to a TEAE (Applicant Table 55). The most commonly reported TEAE leading to study treatment withdrawal was thrombocytopenia in 12.3% of subjects, followed by neutropenia in 8.8% of subjects. All other TEAEs leading to study treatment withdrawal were reported in 1 subject each. The median time to the first TEAE that led to study treatment withdrawal was 29.9 weeks (range: 1.6 to 112.7 weeks) (MDS3001 CSR [Phase 2]/Table tsiex18_p1). Nine subjects had TEAEs leading to study treatment withdrawal that were considered related to treatment, including 5 subjects with Grade 3/4 thrombocytopenia, 3 subjects with Grade 3/4 neutropenia, 1 subject with Grade 3 cardiac failure, and 1 subject with Grade 3 diarrhoea (Listing lae02d).
L1796: In the pool of subjects from all hematologic malignancy monotherapy studies (N = 391), 17.1% of subjects discontinued study treatment due to a TEAE (Table tae02r). Similar to LR MDS subjects, the most commonly reported TEAEs leading to study treatment withdrawal were thrombocytopenia in 4.6% of subjects and neutropenia in 4.3% of subjects. Other TEAEs leading
L1797: to study treatment withdrawal that were reported in ≥ 2 subjects included leukocytosis, cardiac failure, and dyspnoea (0.8% each); and leukopenia, asthenia, and sepsis (0.5% each).

L1805: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1806: Overall, reasons for discontinuation of imetelstat were similar across studies with cytopenias being the most common TEAEs leading to drug withdrawal. In the MDS3001 Phase 3 pivotal study, the proportion of subjects who withdrew from treatment due to AEs was relatively low (&lt; 20%) due to added measures for managing cytopenias implemented in the study. The remaining TEAEs leading to treatment discontinuation occurred in 1 subject each without any prevalent SOC.
L1807: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1808: Treatment discontinuation due to adverse events was required in 36 patients on study, all of whom received imetelstat. In addition, treatment discontinuation or withholding was required for non-treatment emergent AEs in 9 patients. The Sponsor clarified that in these cases, treatment was being held due to a TEAE and further events led to treatment withdrawal or further dose delay.
L1809: Although we agree that the Sponsor's approach seems reasonable in general, further evaluation of non-TEAE leading to treatment modification, including discontinuation and continued withholding, was undertaken to determine whether TEAE may have contributed to the later non-TEAE (e.g., neutropenia contributing to later infection). Fourteen non-TEAE that led to dose modification in 9 patients and any relevant related events are described below:
L1810: - Patient (b)(6) was treated on the phase 2 portion of the study. The patient experienced congestive heart failure leading to treatment discontinuation on study day 234. Treatment was interrupted for this patient at day 106 due to disseminated tuberculosis. Neutropenia of up to grade 3 was noted in this patient.
- Patient (b)(6) was treated on the imetelstat arm of the phase 3 portion of the study. The patient experienced study drug interruption due to thrombocytopenia on days 139-160 of the study. It is noted that treatment was initially interrupted due to thrombocytopenia occurring on treatment at day 133.
- Patient (b)(6) was treated on the placebo arm of the phase 3 portion of the study had treatment was interrupted on study day 126 in this patient due multiorgan failure that
L1811: was ultimately fatal. Treatment had been previously interrupted due to COVID-19 pneumonia. No neutropenia was reported.
L1812: - Patient (b) (6) was treated on the imetelstat arm of the phase 3 portion of the study and had treatment discontinued due to development of B-cell lymphoma on study day 267. Treatment had previously been interrupted due to COVID-19 infection.
- Patient (b) (6) was treated on the imetelstat arm of the phase 3 portion of the study and had treatment discontinued on study day 42 due to COVID-19 pneumonia that was ultimately fatal. It is unclear when or whether treatment was stopped since no other events that led to dose modification were documented. The last event documented as treatment emergent was bacterial pneumonia occurring on day 27.
- Patient (b) (6) was treated with imetelstat on the phase 3 component of the study and had treatment withdrawn due to cardiac failure congestive and physical deconditioning on study day 94. The patient had previously experienced GI bleeding and left femur dislocation on study days 25 and 29. The action taken with study drug for these events was also listed as treatment discontinued. The events of congestive cardiac failure and physical deconditioning were listed as starting on days 47 and 36, respectively.
- Patient (b) (6) was treated on the imetelstat arm of the phase 3 component of the study and had treatment interrupted due to febrile neutropenia on day 216. Treatment was interrupted on study day 210-211 for the treatment emergent events of thrombocytopenia and neutropenia.
- Patient (b) (6) was treated on the placebo arm of the phase 3 component and had study treatment interrupted due to thrombocytopenia that started on days 345 and 356. The patient had previously had treatment interrupted on day 303 due to thrombocytopenia.
- Patient (b) (6) was treated on the imetelstat arm of the phase 3 component and had treatment discontinued due to hepatic cirrhosis that was first documented on study day 413. Treatment had been previously interrupted due to cirrhosis and ascites noted on study day 361.
L1813: *Clinical Reviewer comments: One patient on the imetelstat phase 2 portion of the study discontinued due to a non-TEAE. Of note, this patient had previously interrupted treatment due to disseminated tuberculosis and discontinuation was due to cardiac failure, which could be related to disseminated TB. However, this is speculative.*
L1814: *Among patients on the imetelstat phase 3 portion of the study, four discontinued treatment due to non-TEAE, including one patient each with congestive heart failure and physical deconditioning, hepatic cirrhosis, COVID-19 pneumonia, and B-cell lymphoma. All events that were considered non-treatment emergent were documented as starting within 2 months of the last dose of study medication.*
L1815: *Treatment was held in one patient on the placebo arm due to thrombocytopenia and treatment continued to be withheld after &gt;30 days due to continued thrombocytopenia. This patient later discontinued participation due to progressive disease.*

L1827: No treatment discontinuations were required for TEAE in the placebo arm. Among patients who discontinued due to AEs that occurred or worsened while not receiving treatment, discontinuation was frequently due to events related to TEAE, including infectious complications, thrombocytopenia, and cirrhosis.*

L1830: ## Dose Interruption/Reduction Due to Adverse Effects
L1831: ## Data:
L1832: In the MDS3001 Phase 3 imetelstat group (N = 118), 68.6% of subjects had TEAEs leading to cycle delay, and neutropenia (50.8%) and thrombocytopenia (33.1%) were the most frequently reported (Applicant Table 57). The median time to cycle delay due to a TEAE in the imetelstat group was 4.0 weeks (range: 2.0 to 84.0 weeks). TEAEs leading to dose reduction were reported for 49.2% of subjects, with neutropenia (33.1%) and thrombocytopenia (22.9%) the most commonly reported (Applicant Table 58). The median time to imetelstat dose reduction due to a TEAE was 10.5 weeks (range: 2.0 to 78.9 weeks).
L1833: In the MDS3001 Phase 3 placebo group (N = 59), 23.7% of subjects had a TEAE that led to cycle delay, including COVID-19, ALT increased, COVID-19 pneumonia, diarrhoea, and gastroenteritis in 2 (3.4%) subjects each. Four (6.8%) subjects had a TEAE that led to dose reduction. The median time to cycle delay due to an AE was 6.4 weeks (range: 3.0 to 48.0 weeks) and median time to dose reduction was 14.6 weeks (range: 6.4 to 47.0 weeks) (MDS3001 CSR [Phase 3]/Table tsiex18_p2).
L1834: In the MDS3001-Phase 2 study (N = 57), 66.7% subjects had TEAEs that led to cycle delay, and neutropenia (49.1%) and thrombocytopenia (40.4%) were the most frequently reported (Applicant Table 57). The median time to the first TEAE that led to cycle delay was 4.0 weeks (range: 2.0 to 88.0 weeks) (MDS3001 CSR [Phase 2]/Table tsiex18_p1). A total of 52.6% subjects had TEAEs that led to dose reduction (Applicant Table 58). The majority of dose reductions were due to neutropenia (43.9%) and thrombocytopenia (28.1%). Overall, the type and frequency of TEAEs leading to cycle delay or dose reduction were consistent between the Phase 2 and Phase 3 imetelstat groups.
L1835: For subjects in the total hematologic malignancy monotherapy group (N = 391), TEAEs leading to dose reduction were reported in 31.5% of subjects. The majority of dose reductions in Group C were due to neutropenia (19.4%) and thrombocytopenia (15.6%) subjects (Table tae02u).

L1848: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1849: Most dose delays and dose reductions with imetelstat treatment were due to neutropenia or thrombocytopenia. These dose modifications enabled subjects to stay on study drug as demonstrated by the overall low number of discontinuations in comparison to dose modifications. In the Phase 3 study, dose reductions of imetelstat occurred later during treatment (median 10.5 weeks [range: 2.0 to 78.9 weeks]), suggesting that subjects were able to receive the full planned dose of 7.1 mg/kg imetelstat long enough to achieve a response (see Dose/Dose Response in Section 8.1.2).

L1852: reductions in one patient each in the imetelstat arm of the phase 3 portion included transaminase elevation and neutropenic sepsis.

L1866: There were more TEAE leading to dose decrease among patients treated with imetelstat on the phase 2 portion of the study as well as in the imetelstat arm of the phase 3 portion of the study compared to placebo, with 49% of patients on the imetelstat arm experiencing at least one dose reduction versus 7% on the placebo arm. Common TEAE leading to dose reduction included neutropenia, thrombocytopenia, and leukopenia. The number of events per patient and duration of events were similar in the imetelstat arm and placebo arm of the phase 3 portion of the study. However, the number of events requiring cycle delay as well as dose
L1867: reduction was higher in the imetelstat arm, with 89% of events leading to dose reduction in the imetelstat arm also requiring treatment delay, compared to 63% in the placebo arm.
L1868: Of note, the majority of events leading to dose reduction in the imetelstat arm (66%) occurred in cycles 1-3, whereas only one event in the placebo arm occurred in cycles 1-3.
L1869: *Clinical Reviewer comment: The requirement for dose reduction generally occurred early in the course of treatment with imetelstat, although the time to the event was similar in the imetelstat and placebo arms.*

L1878: TEAE leading to treatment interruption or delay occurred in the majority of patients receiving imetelstat on either arm. On the phase 3 portion of the study, the risk difference between imetelstat and placebo was +53. Infusion interruptions or abortions were relatively uncommon, occurring in 6% and &lt;1%, respectively, of patients on the imetelstat arm of the phase 3 portion and no patients on the placebo arm. Similar to dose reduction, a large number of the events leading to treatment interruption occurred in cycles 1-3.
L1879: The most common reasons for treatment delay in patients receiving imetelstat was neutropenia or thrombocytopenia. No patient receiving placebo required a treatment delay for neutropenia or thrombocytopenia. Other common reasons for treatment delay included
L1880: leukopenia, infections, and hepatotoxicity. With the exception of hepatotoxicity, each of these events were more common in the imetelstat arm.
L1881: *Clinical Reviewer comments: Patients receiving imetelstat had a high probability of requiring dose reduction, treatment delay, or both during the study, with nearly half and over ¾ of patients on the imetelstat arm of the phase 3 component requiring dose reduction and treatment delay, respectively. Neutropenia, thrombocytopenia, and infections were the cause of treatment delay or dose reduction in the majority of cases in the imetelstat arm, whereas neutropenia or thrombocytopenia leading to dose modification was rare in the placebo arm.*
L1882: *Treatment modifications, including dose decrease, were seen most commonly in cycles 1-3. However, some patients required treatment modification as late as cycle 13 or greater, suggesting that there are ongoing tolerability issues with imetelstat.*
L1883: *Overall, the dose modification data suggest that the dose of imetelstat used in this study is not tolerable and that, at minimum, close monitoring would be needed to ensure that patients do not develop life threatening complications from treatment.*
L1884: ## Significant Adverse Events
L1885: ## Data:
L1886: Grade 3/4 TEAEs were reported in 90.7% subjects in the MDS3001 Phase 3 imetelstat group (N = 118) and 47.5% subjects in the MDS3001 Phase 3 placebo group (N = 59) (Applicant Table 62). The most commonly reported Grade 3/4 TEAE was neutropenia, reported in 67.8% and 3.4% of subjects in the imetelstat and placebo groups, respectively, followed by thrombocytopenia (61.9% and 8.5%, respectively), and anaemia (19.5% and 6.8%, respectively). Most of these events were considered related to study treatment (Applicant Table 55). See Section 8.2.5 for further discussion of neutropenia and thrombocytopenia. Grade 3/4 TEAEs were most commonly reported in the Blood and lymphatic system disorders SOC in both the imetelstat (83.9%) and placebo (16.9%) groups.
L1887: In the MDS3001 Phase 2 imetelstat group (N = 57), Grade 3/4 TEAEs were reported in 87.7% of subjects (Applicant Table 62). The most commonly reported Grade 3/4 TEAE was neutropenia, reported in 59.6% of subjects, followed by thrombocytopenia in 54.4% of subjects, and anaemia and leukopenia in 19.3% of subjects each. Most of these events were considered related to study treatment (Applicant Table 58). Grade 3/4 TEAEs were most commonly reported in the Blood and lymphatic system disorders SOC (70.2%).
L1888: In the overall monotherapy grouping (N = 391), Grade 3/4 TEAEs were reported in 88.2% subjects (Table tae04c). As in the LR MDS subjects, the most commonly reported Grade 3/4 TEAEs were thrombocytopenia (46.8%), neutropenia (45.3%), anaemia (33.2%), leukopenia (12.5%), and fatigue (5.9%). All other Grade 3/4 TEAEs were reported in &lt; 5% of subjects. Most of these events were considered related to study treatment (Table tae04i).

L1894: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1895: The most commonly reported Grade 3/4 TEAEs in MDS3001 Phase 3 imetelstat-treated subjects were neutropenia and thrombocytopenia, reported in approximately 2/3 of subjects, and approximately half of all subjects had Grade 3/4 TEAEs considered related to imetelstat. These events are manageable with cycle delays and dose reductions and are generally reversible (see Section 8.2.5). The most commonly reported Grade 3/4 TEAEs in the placebo group were thrombocytopenia and anaemia, reported in &lt; 10% each.
L1896: The type and frequency of Grade 3/4 events reported in Phase 2 and in the overall hematological malignancy grouping were similar to those in the Phase 3 imetelstat group.
L1897: No new safety signals were observed following review of the overall hematologic malignancy monotherapy grouping.
L1898: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1899: Grade 3+ AEs were observed more frequently in patients treated with imetelstat in the phase 2 and phase 3 portions of the study compared to placebo (Table 63).
L1900: Differences of greater than 50 percent units were observed in grade 3-4 AE of neutropenia and thrombocytopenia in the imetelstat arm of the phase 3 portion compared to the placebo arm, with a similar frequency of events seen in the phase 2 study. Anemia and leukopenia were less common in imetelstat treated patients, but a large risk difference was seen when compared with patients treated with placebo.
L1901: Sepsis, hypertension, fractures, and cardiac failure were seen more frequently in the imetelstat treated cohorts, although the differences compared to placebo were smaller than the differences in grade 3 or higher cytopenias. As noted earlier, SAE of cardiac failure, sepsis, hypertension, and fractures were seen more frequently in patients treated with imetelstat compared to placebo.
L1902: Four patients in the phase 2 cohort and three in the imetelstat arm of the phase 3 component had grade 3 or higher events of neoplasm. All events consisted of a secondary malignancy or premalignant condition, rather than a complication or progression of the underlying MDS. Specific events included cholangiocarcinoma, hepatocellular carcinoma, transitional cell carcinoma of the bladder, renal cell carcinoma, bladder papilloma, and lung neoplasm. Of note, lung carcinoma and transitional cell carcinoma each occurred in two patients.
