L1910: *Clinical Reviewer comments: Grade 3-4 AE were higher in patients receiving imetelstat versus placebo in the phase 3 portion of the study. Since the majority of events in the imetelstat arm were neutropenia and thrombocytopenia, an analysis of events when these terms were excluded was performed and showed a continued, if smaller, difference between study arms. Similar rates of grade 3-4 AE in the phase 2 portion of the study support the possibility of causality.*
L1911: *We note that the laboratory data (see below) do not support a higher incidence of grade 3-4 hemoglobin decreased in the imetelstat arm. The reason for the higher rate of the adverse event of anemia being reported is unclear. In addition, cardiac failure was seen more frequently as a SAE and as a grade 3-4 event but was not higher overall. Thus, the relationship between imetelstat and cardiac failure is uncertain.*
L1912: ## Treatment Emergent Adverse Events and Adverse Reactions
L1913: Data:

L1939: ## Common Adverse Events
L1940: In the MDS3001 Phase 3 imetelstat group (N = 118), the most commonly reported TEAEs by PT were thrombocytopenia (75.4%) and neutropenia (73.7%), followed by anaemia (20.3%), asthenia (18.6%), and COVID-19 (15.3%) (Applicant Table 66). In the placebo group (N = 59), the most commonly reported TEAEs by PT were asthenia and oedema peripheral in (13.6% each), and diarrhoea, constipation, and pyrexia (11.9% each).
L1941: The SOC with the highest incidence of TEAEs in the MDS3001 Phase 3 imetelstat group was Blood and lymphatic system disorders (89.0%), followed by Infections and infestations (42.4%), General disorders and administration site conditions (41.5%), and Gastrointestinal disorders (34.7%). In the Phase 3 placebo group, the highest incidence of TEAEs were in the SOC of General disorders and administration site conditions (47.5%), Gastrointestinal disorders (40.7%), Infections and infestations (33.9%), and Metabolism and nutrition disorders (25.4%). The most commonly reported TEAEs by PT in the Phase 2 imetelstat group were neutropenia (66.7%) and thrombocytopenia (61.4%), followed by anaemia (24.6%), headache (22.8%), and leukopenia and pyrexia (19.3% each) (Applicant Table 66). In the Phase 2 imetelstat group, the highest incidence of TEAEs were in the SOC of Blood and lymphatic system disorders (77.2%), Infections and infestations (57.9%), General disorders and administration site conditions (54.4%), and Gastrointestinal disorders and Musculoskeletal and connective tissue disorders (49.1%) each.
L1942: In the overall hematologic malignancy monotherapy grouping (N = 391), the types and frequencies of any-grade TEAEs differed based on the subjects' underlying disease. Compared to MDS3001, higher incidences of fatigue and gastrointestinal events (diarrhoea, nausea) were reported, as well as lower rates of cytopenias, due to lower rates reported in MF and ET/PV subjects in Studies MYF2001, CB14B019, and CB14B015.

L1953: ## Adverse Drug Reactions
L1954: All TEAEs were reviewed in the initial selection of adverse drug reactions (ADRs) for imetelstat primarily based on the MDS3001 Phase 3 study due to the double-blind placebo-controlled design and the inclusion of the population for which imetelstat is intended. The initial focus was TEAEs (all grades) that occurred with a frequency of \ge 5\% in the imetelstat group, occurring \ge 2\% higher in the imetelstat group compared to placebo (Applicant Table 67). These cut offs were determined based on the size of the population being treated in the MDS3001 Phase 3 study. In addition, the TEAEs occurring &lt; 5\% but &gt; 2\% were reviewed and assessed for inclusion based on severity and relevance. All Grade 3 or higher TEAEs and serious TEAEs occurring \ge 2\% of subjects in the imetelstat group were also reviewed. To ensure the identification of any potential signals for less common events, all TEAEs not meeting these criteria were also reviewed. PTs were grouped where appropriate. Selection was based on the magnitude of difference in incidence between imetelstat and placebo, biological plausibility, consistency in time to onset, dechallenge/rechallenge/ information, the presence of alternative explanations, nonclinical data, and frequency expected in the treated population along with applied medical judgement.
L1955: Adverse events reflective of laboratory measurements were considered based on worsening of laboratory values, given the high prevalence of laboratory abnormalities in this subject population and higher degree of accuracy in the laboratory values compared to AE reporting, in order to provide the most conservative frequencies (Applicant Table 68).

L1965: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1966: The most commonly reported TEAEs in the MDS3001 Phase 3 imetelstat group were thrombocytopenia and neutropenia, reported in approximately 3/4 of subjects, and approximately 2/3 of subjects had thrombocytopenia and neutropenia considered related to imetelstat. These were also the most commonly reported Grade 3/4 TEAEs. Thrombocytopenia and neutropenia occurred more frequently during the early months of treatment and decreased over time, suggesting a lack of cumulative toxicity. The most commonly reported TEAEs in the placebo group were asthenia and oedema peripheral, reported in &lt; 15% of subjects. The most commonly reported Grade 3/4 TEAEs in the placebo group were thrombocytopenia and anaemia, reported in &lt; 10% each.
L1967: Grade 3/4 TEAEs are manageable with cycle delays and dose reductions and are generally reversible (discussed Section 8.2.5).
L1968: The profile of any-grade TEAEs reported in Phase 2, including the most common TEAEs, the timing of TEAEs, and the profile of treatment-related TEAEs, was similar to and supportive of the Phase 3 results.
L1969: No new safety signals were observed after review of the broader hematologic malignancy monotherapy population of 391 subjects that would alter the safety profile and management of imetelstat treatment.
L1970: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1971: We do not agree with the Applicant's overall assessment of adverse events as described above. In particular, the Sponsor's analysis minimizes or fails to document the extent and severity of a number of adverse events, including cytopenias, infection, and bleeding, and does not comment on several potential signals as discussed below.

L1975: As the table above makes clear, the rate of SAE, grade 3-4 TEAE, and AE leading to dose modifications of any type (discontinuation, decrease, or interruption) are higher in the imetelstat arm. Because cytopenias are a significant risk with imetelstat and might obscure other issues, the rate of grade 3-4 AEs excluding neutropenia and thrombocytopenia was examined and a higher rate of grade 3-4 AEs was still observed in the imetelstat arm. It is important to note that none of the grade 3-4 events of neutropenia or thrombocytopenia were considered by the Applicant to be SAE. L2001: Cytopenias were more common overall in patients treated with imetelstat in both parts 1 and 2 of the study compared to those treated with placebo. Of note, anemia was at least 10 percent units higher in both imetelstat groups. However, we note that this finding was not confirmed in the laboratory data (below). Cytopenias will be discussed in further detail in the laboratory findings section of the review.
L2002: Issues that might be expected to co-occur with cytopenias, including sepsis, hemorrhage, and fatigue, were seen more frequently in patients treated with imetelstat as well. COVID-19 infections were more common in patients treated with imetelstat in part 2, but not in part 1, probably reflecting the timing of enrollment and treatment in each part of the study. A more detailed comparison of infectious complications is described below in Table 104.
L2003: In addition, patients receiving imetelstat were more likely to experience hepatic disorders compared to patients receiving placebo. This finding was consistent across both cohorts of patients receiving imetelstat. Also see laboratory results for further discussion of hepatotoxicity.
L2004: Musculoskeletal issues, including fractures and arthralgia/myalgia, were seen more commonly in patients treated with imetelstat compared to placebo, across both cohorts of patients receiving imetelstat, suggesting a potential causative connection, although the mechanism of action is not clear.
L2005: Although SAE of cardiac failure was more common in patients treated with imetelstat, there was little difference in the overall rate of cardiac failure among patients treated with imetelstat compared to placebo, with a risk difference of +1.7 units for patients treated in part 1 and +1.8 units in patients treated on part 2. In addition, hypertension and atrial fibrillation were observed more commonly among patients receiving imetelstat than those receiving placebo.
L2006: The neurologic events of headache and syncope were more common in patients receiving imetelstat compared to placebo. In addition, pruritus and renal dysfunction were observed more frequently in patients treated with imetelstat.
L2007: *Clinical Reviewer comments: Review of the adverse events occurring in trial MDS3001 reveal potential risks of cytopenias, hepatic toxicity, cardiac toxicity, fractures, and arthralgia/myalgia. Cytopenias and related AEs, musculoskeletal toxicities, and hepatic toxicity were consistently seen more frequently in patients treated with imetelstat and the risk differences were generally*
L2008: *high. Cardiac toxicity, neurologic events, renal dysfunction, and pruritus were seen more frequently in patients treated with imetelstat, but with smaller risk differences and, for some events, with inconsistent results (i.e., more frequent events in one of the imetelstat cohorts.) See below for correlation with events in the ISS overall and in other subpopulations.*
L2009: In order to further characterize the risk of TEAE in patients receiving imetelstat, an analysis of patients in the integrated safety database was performed. As noted above, patients in the ISS were treated for a variety of malignancies, including solid tumors, MDS, myeloproliferative neoplasms, and other hematologic malignancies. In addition, multiple doses and dosing schedules were examined in various tumor types and imetelstat was given with other chemotherapeutic agents in some studies. Of note, of studies which currently have data available, only MDS3001 included a placebo arm. Thus, all patients who received placebo were being treated for lower risk MDS. (Also see section 8.2.3 above for details of the grouping of imetelstat doses.) Combination regimens were excluded as there is significant confounding related to other chemotherapeutics used.

L2018: Data cut-off date 13 October 2022
L2019: Fatal TEAEs in the lower dose group included head injury, splenic infarct, sepsis, and disease progression. Deaths in the recommended dose group were due to sepsis, COVID-19 pneumonia, femur fracture, neurodegenerative disorder, cerebrovascular accident, and thrombosis mesenteric. All occurred on trial MDS3001 and are discussed above. Among the 27 events leading to death in 25 patients treated at doses higher than 7.1 mg/kg/4 weeks, 7 were due to disease progression, five to hemorrhage, four to infection (sepsis in three, pneumonia in one), and three to cardiac arrest.
L2020: *Clinical Reviewer comments: AE described in the ISS generally supported the findings from MDS3001. Of note, the rate of grade 3-4 AE, hemorrhage, infections, neutropenia, and thrombocytopenia were higher even in the lowest dose group compared to placebo. The rate of neutropenia and thrombocytopenia increased dramatically between the low dose and the standard/high doses, and an apparent dose/response was seen for all of these issues.*
L2021: *In addition, fatigue, arthralgia/myalgia, pruritus, dizziness, and anemia were higher at all doses of imetelstat compared to placebo. Hepatic disorders and fractures were higher in the two higher doses compared to either low dose or placebo.*
L2022: *These results should be interpreted with caution as differences in underlying condition, supportive care, and other factors may have contributed to the observed differences. However, in general, the findings from the ISS support those from MDS3001 and in particular the comparison of imetelstat phase 3 dose with placebo.*
L2023: Specific issues identified in the review of the safety data are described below. Note that issues previously identified by the Applicant (thrombocytopenia, neutropenia, infusion related reaction, and hepatic toxicity) are discussed below in section 8.2.5.
L2024: ## Fractures
L2025: Fractures were observed more frequently in patients receiving imetelstat compared to those receiving placebo, as noted above.
L2026: In patients receiving imetelstat on the phase 2 portion of the study, six patients experienced fractures, including femur fracture, hip fracture, thoracic vertebral fracture, radius fracture, rib fracture, skull fracture, spinal compression fracture, and wrist fracture. Of these, only femur fracture, which was documented in three patients, occurred in more than one patient. Grade 3-4 events were documented in four patients and one fatal event of femur fracture was noted.
L2027: In the phase 3 portion of the study, fractures were documented in 6 (5%) of patients on the imetelstat arm, including two femur fractures and one each hand fracture, hip fracture, humerus fracture, and lumbar vertebrae fracture. One humerus fracture was documented in the placebo arm. Grade 3-4 events were documented in four patients on the imetelstat arm. No fatal events were documented. The one patient in the placebo arm who experienced a fracture had two grade 3 fractures. None of the fractures in either arm were associated with documented falls or other specific trauma.
L2028: In the ISS dataset, fractures were documented in 21 (3.9%) patients receiving imetelstat at any dose, including 12 (2.2%) patients in whom grade 3-4 events occurred and one death as noted above. All events occurred in patients receiving 7.1 mg/kg/4 weeks or higher and all occurred in patients receiving monotherapy.
L2029: *Clinical Reviewer comments: The reason for the increased rate of fractures in patients who received imetelstat is unclear. It is noted that only one event was associated with a documented fall. However, fractures were observed in patients receiving imetelstat in both parts of MDS3001 as well as in patients who received imetelstat in other studies.*
L2030: ## Fatigue
L2031: Fatigue was documented in 29% of patients in the imetelstat of the phase 3 portion of MDS3001 and 20% of patients on the placebo arm. A relatively high rate of fatigue (25%) was noted in the phase 2 portion as well. Fatigue was observed frequently in the ISS, with an apparent increase in incidence at higher doses, including a rate of 64% in patients who received more than 7.1 mg/kg/4 weeks and 33% in the overall population of patients who received imetelstat at 7.1 mg/kg/4 weeks. No grade 3-4 events of fatigue were observed in the imetelstat arm of the phase 3 portion of MDS3001. However, 30 (10%) of patients who received &gt;7.1 mg/kg/4 weeks experienced grade 3 fatigue.
L2032: *Clinical Reviewer comment: The higher incidence of fatigue in the imetelstat arm along with the apparent dose/effect in patients on the overall development program suggest a potential causal connection between use of imetelstat and fatigue. This effect may be somewhat ameliorated by the improved hemoglobin observed in patients with MDS.*
L2033: ## Arthralgia/myalgia
L2034: Arthralgia and myalgia were seen more frequently in the imetelstat phase 3 arm compared to the placebo arm (25% and 19%, respectively). Arthralgia and myalgia were high in the phase 2 portion of MDS3001 as well (32%) and was elevated in patients in the total development program.
L2035: *Clinical Reviewer comments: Infusion related reactions (IRR) may explain some of the increased incidence of arthralgia/myalgia in patients receiving imetelstat. However, not all events were associated with IRR and there may be a risk of arthralgia/myalgia with imetelstat use.*
L2036: ## Cardiac Issues
L2037: The risk of cardiac TEAE with imetelstat is ambiguous. A marginally higher rate of events in the SOC cardiac disorders was observed (15% in imetelstat, 14% in placebo). A higher incidence of some events, including atrial fibrillation and hypertension, was observed with use of imetelstat. Conversely, aortic occlusive events were less common in patients receiving imetelstat (3.4% for imetelstat versus 7% for placebo). In addition, the rate of cardiac failure overall was not higher with treatment with imetelstat, but the risk of grade 3-4 cardiac failure was elevated. Syncope, which could be due to neurologic or cardiac issues, was higher in the imetelstat arm.
L2038: *Clinical Reviewer comments: No conclusions can be drawn regarding risk of cardiac events and use of imetelstat. Further observations in the post-marketing setting, including longer term follow up of patients on MDS3001, other clinical trials, and in the non-clinical trial setting may identify or definitively refute a signal.*
L2039: ## Iron Overload
L2040: The definitive test for iron overload, cardiac and hepatic MRI, was not performed in study MDS3001. Therefore, any data on changes in iron overload are tentative and may not reflect changes in total body iron.
L2041: Iron overload based on TEAE was evaluated using a custom term including preferred terms hyperferritinaemia, iron overload, serum ferritin increased, blood iron increased, hemochromatosis, and hemosiderosis. Using these terms, iron overload was documented in 7 (6%) of patients on the imetelstat arm and 8 (14%) in the placebo arm.
L2042: Ferritin was collected during the trial, although day and cycle of collection varies. Overall patterns of change in ferritin are shown below. For patients on the phase 2 portion of the study, ferritin was stable to slightly decreased compared to baseline until about cycle 39, when increased values were seen (Figure 40, part A). It should be noted that few patients continued treatment to later cycles and that this may confound the results. Ferritin levels were essentially overlapping for patients on the phase 3 part of the study (Figure 33, part B).
L2043: **Figure 33:** Ferritin levels during treatment for patients on a) phase 2 portion of MDS3001 and b) phase 3 portion of MDS3001. Imetelstat is represented in blue, placebo in red.
L2044: Source: FDA analysis
L2045: Based on laboratory values for ferritin from the initial submission data cut-off date 10 May 2023
L2046: *Clinical Reviewer comment: No definitive conclusions can be drawn regarding the rate of iron overload observed with use of imetelstat. Based on the limited data available, there does not appear to be a major decrease in iron overload in patients receiving imetelstat. Longer term*
L2047: *follow up, more accurate diagnostic measures, and focus on iron status in patients who achieve long term transfusion independence may help clarify the issue.*
L2048: ## Laboratory Findings
L2049: Laboratory findings are presented for the LR MDS population (Safety Groups A and B).
L2050: ## Data:
L2051: ## Hematology
L2052: ## Neutropenia
L2053: Based on laboratory assessment, most subjects (108/118 [91.5%]) in the MDS3001 Phase 3 imetelstat group (N = 118) had worsening neutropenia from baseline while on treatment (Applicant Table 74). In the placebo group (N = 59), 28/59 (47.5%) subjects had worsening neutropenia from baseline while on treatment. In the imetelstat and placebo groups, 46.6% and 5.1% of subjects, respectively, had decreased neutrophils at a maximum CTCAE Grade 3, and 24.6% and 1.7% of subjects, respectively, at a maximum CTCAE Grade 4. Median time to onset of Grade 3 or Grade 4 decreased neutrophils was 4.43 weeks (range: 1.0 to 81.0 weeks) in the imetelstat group and 13.00 weeks (range: 3.0 to 23.0 weeks) in the placebo group (Table tlab06a). Median duration of Grade 3/4 neutropenia was 1.86 weeks (range: 0.0, 15.9) in the imetelstat group and 3.36 weeks (range: 1.0, 13.9) in the placebo group.
L2054: In the MDS3001 Phase 3 imetelstat group (N = 118), 226 (81.0%) of 279 Grade 3/4 neutropenia events resolved to Grade \le 2 and 49 (90.7%) of Grade 4 neutropenia events resolved to Grade \le 3 in under 4 weeks (Applicant Table 75). In the placebo group (N = 59), 3 (50.0%) of Grade 3/4 neutropenia events resolved to Grade \le 2 and 1 (100.0%) Grade 4 neutropenia event resolved to Grade \le 3 in under 4 weeks.
L2055: Most subjects in the MDS3001 Phase 2 imetelstat group (N = 57) (93.0% of subjects) had worsening neutropenia from baseline while on treatment. Overall, 84.6% of Grade 3/4 neutropenia events resolved to Grade \le 2 in under 4 weeks, which included 91.4% of Grade 4 neutropenia events (Table tlab06b). Median duration of Grade 3/4 neutropenia events was 2.00 weeks (range: .01, 16.7) (Table tsflb01_p1).
L2056: ## Thrombocytopenia
L2057: Based on hematology laboratory measures, 95.8% of subjects in the MDS3001 Phase 3 imetelstat group (N = 118) had worsening thrombocytopenia from baseline while on treatment (Applicant Table 74). In the placebo group (N = 59), 33.9% of subjects had worsening thrombocytopenia from baseline while on treatment. Median time to onset of Grade 3 or Grade 4 decreased platelets was 6.00 weeks (range: 1.7 to 88.3 weeks) in the imetelstat group and 15.14 weeks (range: 6.4 to 40.6 weeks) in the placebo group (Table tlab06a). Median duration of Grade 3/4 thrombocytopenia was 1.43 weeks (range: 0.1, 12.6) in the imetelstat groups and 2.00 weeks (range: 0.3, 22.3) in the placebo group (Table tsflb01_p2).
L2058: TRADENAME, imetelstat
L2059: In the imetelstat group (N = 118), 183 (86.3%) of 212 Grade 3/4 thrombocytopenia events resolved to Grade \le 2 and 40 (85.1%) of 47 Grade 4 thrombocytopenia events resolved to Grade \le 3 in under 4 weeks (Applicant Table 75). In the placebo group (N = 59), 4 (44.4%) of 9 Grade 3/4 thrombocytopenia events resolved to Grade \le 2. Among 3 Grade 4 thrombocytopenia events, none resolved to Grade \le 3 in under 4 weeks and 2 were ongoing as of the data cutoff date.
L2060: In the MDS3001 Phase 2 imetelstat group (N = 57), most subjects (91.2%) had worsening thrombocytopenia while on treatment (Applicant Table 74). Median time to onset of Grade 3 or Grade 4 decreased platelets was 4.0 weeks (range: 2.0 to 89.7 weeks) (Table tlab06b). Overall, 90.8% of Grade 3/4 thrombocytopenia events resolved to Grade \le 2 in under 4 weeks, which included 96.8% of Grade 4 thrombocytopenia events (Applicant Table 75). Median duration of Grade 3/4 thrombocytopenia events was 1.29 weeks (range: 0.1, 15.0) (Table tsflb01_p1).

L2073: ## Coagulation
L2074: In the MDS3001 Phase 3 imetelstat group (N = 118), most subjects were within normal range at baseline for aPTT and prothrombin INR (92.8% and 76.6%, respectively) and remained within normal range during treatment (75.3% and 84.0%, respectively) (Table tlab06g, Table tlab02g). In the Phase 2 imetelstat group (N = 57), most subjects were within normal range at baseline for aPTT and prothrombin INR (88.7% and 74.5%, respectively) and remained within normal range during treatment (67.9% and 58.8%, respectively (Table tlab06h, Table tlab02h)).
L2075: ## Liver Function Tests
L2076: In the MDS3001 Phase 3 imetelstat group (N = 118), worsening toxicity grades during treatment were reported for ALT (46/117 [39.3%] subjects), AST (57/118 [48.3%] subjects), ALP (53/118 [44.9%] subjects), and bilirubin (46/118 [39.0%] subjects) (Applicant Table 76). In the placebo group (N = 59), worsening toxicity grades during treatment were reported for ALT (22/59 [37.3%] subjects), AST (13/59 [22.0%] subjects), ALP (7/59 [11.9%] subjects), and bilirubin (23/59 [39.0%] subjects). There were no subjects with Grade 4 toxicities in liver function tests. All liver function test abnormalities resolved during the study.
L2077: In the MDS3001 Phase 2 imetelstat group (N = 57), worsening toxicity grades during treatment were reported for ALT (27 [47.4%] subjects), AST (27 [47.4%] subjects), ALP (19 [33.3%] subjects), and bilirubin (27 [47.4%] subjects) (Applicant Table 76).

L2085: ## The Applicant's Position:
L2086: ## **Hematology**
L2087: ## *Neutropenia*
L2088: Grade 3/4 neutropenia is very common with imetelstat and occurred more frequently during the first few cycles. These events are manageable with dose modifications and growth factor support and generally reversible. Few subjects discontinued imetelstat due to neutropenia. Moreover, the number of events of febrile neutropenia or significant infections was low across all 3 safety groups (Applicant Table 62 and Table tae04c), severe infections were generally not associated with severe neutropenia, and most were not related to imetelstat treatment (Applicant Table 102). Management of neutropenia will be included in the proposed product labeling and these events will be monitored with routine post-marketing pharmacovigilance. Neutropenia as an AESI is further discussed in Section 8.2.4.
L2089: ## *Thrombocytopenia*
L2090: Grade 3/4 thrombocytopenia is very common with imetelstat treatment and occurred more frequently during the first few cycles. These events are manageable with cycle delays and dose
L2091: reductions and are generally reversible. Few subjects discontinued imetelstat due to thrombocytopenia. Moreover, the number of significant bleeding events was low, the events were assessed by the investigator as not related to imetelstat treatment, and most were not associated with severe thrombocytopenia (Applicant Table 95). Monitoring for thrombocytopenia will be included in the proposed product labeling and these events will be monitored with routine post-marketing pharmacovigilance. Thrombocytopenia as an AESI is further discussed in Section 8.2.4.
L2092: ## Coagulation
L2093: Not applicable.
L2094: ## Liver Function Tests
L2095: Hepatic events were primarily low grade and reversible elevations of ALT, AST, and bilirubin, have been observed in both imetelstat and placebo-treated subjects, with no apparent dose effects. No cases meeting Hy's law criteria were observed.
L2096: Hepatic events as AESIs are further discussed in Section 8.2.4.
L2097: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L2098: We do not agree with the assessment above, which underestimates the significance and extent of cytopenias, coagulation abnormalities, and hepatic function abnormalities as well as not documenting any evaluation of electrolytes or creatinine. Details of the laboratory abnormalities are below.
L2099: ## Overview
L2100: Changes in hematologic, coagulation, and chemistry labs were seen in MDS3001 with differences observed between the imetelstat and placebo arms of the phase 3 portion of the trial in multiple areas. The major changes are summarized in Table 77 below.
L2101: There was a major increase in neutrophil count decreased and platelet decreased, both overall and when only grade 3-4 abnormalities were included. In addition, leukocyte count decreased was observed more commonly in patients receiving imetelstat. Of note, no difference was observed between rates of hemoglobin decreased overall or grade 3-4 only. Prolonged activated partial thromboplastin time (PTT) was seen in 22% of patients in the imetelstat arm, including two who had grade 3 prolongations.
L2102: High rates of creatinine increased, alkaline phosphatase increased, aspartate aminotransferase (AST), alanine aminotransferase (ALT), alkaline phosphatase (AP), bilirubin increased, hyperkalemia, and hyponatremia were observed in patients on the phase 3 portion of the study. Of these, AST, ALT, and AP increased were more common in the imetelstat arm.

L2109: ## Cytopenias
L2110: Based on reporting of TEAE, cytopenias, including neutropenia, thrombocytopenia, anemia, and leukopenia were seen more frequently in patients receiving imetelstat compared to those receiving placebo.
L2111: Individual cytopenias will be discussed below. This section gives a brief overview of the effects of imetelstat on hematopoiesis overall. Patients treated with imetelstat experienced more TEAEs of neutropenia, thrombocytopenia, and leukopenia, as well as more grade 3 or higher events compared to patients treated with placebo. More adverse events of anemia were reported in patients on the imetelstat arm compared to placebo as well, but the rate of hemoglobin decreased as per the laboratory datasets were similar (see below for further discussion). The rate of post-baseline laboratory abnormalities of neutropenia, thrombocytopenia, and leukopenia, however, were all higher in the imetelstat arm compared to the placebo arm (Figure 34). Laboratory values will be used to discuss the rate of cytopenias unless otherwise specified.
L2112: **Figure 34:** Percent of patients with grade 3-4 cytopenias and at least one grade decrease in relevant laboratory value on phase 3 portion of MDS3001
L2113: Includes only those values that represent a worsening of at least one grade compared to baseline.
Source: FDA analysis using ADBL dataset
Data cutoff 13 October 2022
L2114: Per Applicant's analysis shown in Table 79, the median duration of individual events of Grade 3-4 neutropenia and thrombocytopenia was just under two weeks in the imetelstat arm (Table 79). However, some patients experienced multiple events. For example, 77 patients with Grade 3-4 thrombocytopenia in the imetelstat arm experienced 212 events in total. The median total time in cytopenia among those who experienced at least one event in the imetelstat arm, defined as the sum of durations of all cytopenia events for each patient, was 4.9 weeks for Grade 3-4 neutropenia and 3.0 weeks for Grade 3-4 thrombocytopenia.
L2115: In order to confirm that the differences observed were not related to the underlying disease rather than to imetelstat, an analysis of the rate of cytopenias in the ISS laboratory database was undertaken.

L2126: ## Neutropenia
L2127: Neutropenia was observed more commonly in the imetelstat arm compared to the placebo arm. Changes in mean neutrophil rate over time is shown in Figure 35. Neutrophil count is similar between the arms at baseline but decreases in the imetelstat arm by weeks 1-2 and does not return to baseline at any time during treatment. The mean value for neutrophils is unchanged or increased during the study for patients in the placebo arm.
L2128: **Figure 35:** MDS3001 Phase 3: Neutrophil count by study arm at baseline and during treatment
L2129: Source: FDA analysis using ADBL dataset
L2130: Data cutoff 13 October 2022
L2131: In addition, more patients experienced shifts of 3-4 grades in their neutrophil count on the imetelstat arm as compared to the placebo arm. As noted in Table 80, any grade worsening was seen in almost all (92%) of patients in the imetelstat arm and in about half (53%) of patients on the placebo arm. Most events in the placebo arm were grade 1-2 worsening, whereas 60% of patients in the imetelstat arm had a three-grade worsening in neutrophil count compared to baseline at least once during the study and 17% had a four-grade worsening.

L2150: ## Thrombocytopenia
L2151: Thrombocytopenia was frequently observed in patients treated with imetelstat (Figure 36). As with neutropenia, mean platelet count was similar between arms initially but decreased in the imetelstat arm while staying constant in the placebo arm during treatment. Of note, the mean platelet count in the imetelstat arm decreased to grade 1 thrombocytopenia and remained in this range throughout the study.

L2176: ## Coagulation Parameters
L2177: Activated partial thromboplastin time (PTT) was documented as prolonged in 26% (25/98) of patients on the imetelstat arm who had a PTT documented with a normal range provided. In addition, 7 patients had at least one PTT documented with no normal range provided. Of these, three patients had values that were &gt;38 and were increased by at least 1 from baseline and one patient had a value of 114. Among patients on the placebo arm, 9 out of 52 (17%) experienced PTT Prolongation. Most events for both arms were grade 1-2.
L2178: Among patients receiving imetelstat, 96 had PT/INR values recorded with normal ranges provided. Of these, 33/96 (34%) had PT prolongation. Among patients receiving placebo, 14/46 with documented normal ranges had PT prolongation. One patient in each arm had a PT prolongation of grade 3.
L2179: **Table 86:** MDS3001 Phase 3: PTT and PT by study arm
L2180: Imetelstat	Placebo

PTT prolonged	25/98 (26%)	1 (1%)	9/52 (17%)	2 (3.8%)

L2181: Source: FDA analysis
L2182: Twenty-nine patients (25%) on the imetelstat arm and 18 (31%) on the placebo arm received anti-thrombotic agents at some point during treatment. Among patients on the imetelstat arm, this included two patients receiving vitamin K antagonists, thirteen direct factor Xa inhibitors, and nine heparin group medications, most commonly enoxaparin. Among patients on placebo, 18 (31%) received any anti-thrombotic agent, including one who received a vitamin K antagonist, 7 who received a direct factor Xa inhibitor, one who received a direct thrombin
L2183: TRADENAME, imetelstat
L2184: inhibitor, and 5 who received a heparin group medication, most commonly enoxaparin. On the imetelstat arm, 12 patients both received at least one anti-thrombotic medication (heparin group, vitamin K antagonist, or factor Xa inhibitor) and had at least one elevation of PT or PTT. Of note, the patient who had a grade 3 prolongation of PTT was not prescribed the medication at the time the grade 3 PTT Prolongation was documented. Seven patients on the placebo arm both received anti-thrombotics and had at least one prolongation of PT or PTT.
L2185: *Clinical Reviewer comments: Although some patients who experienced PT or PTT Prolongation were treated with various anti-thrombotics, the timing and extent of PT and PTT prolongation were not explained by anti-thrombotic use.*
L2186: ## Vital Signs
L2187: ## Data:
L2188: There did not appear to be any clinically significant mean or median changes in weight, pulse, systolic blood pressure, diastolic blood pressure, or temperature in the MDS3001 Phase 3, Phase 2, or pooled imetelstat groups.
L2189: ## The Applicant's Position:
L2190: No clinically relevant changes in vital signs were observed.
L2191: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L2192: The Agency does not agree with the Applicant's assessment of the vital signs data. Details are below.
L2193: ## Blood pressure
L2194: ## *Systolic blood pressure:*
L2195: Systolic blood pressure elevations were observed in the majority of patients at baseline and during the study. At baseline, any elevation in systolic blood pressure was seen in 67% of patients receiving imetelstat in either phase of the study and 57% of patients receiving placebo, with the majority of patients in each cohort having grade 1 SBP (SBP of 120-&lt;140) (Table). Blood pressure measurements on cycle 2 day 1 and cycle 4 day 1 were compared to evaluate changes in SBP over time. At cycle 2 day 1, any blood pressure elevation was seen in fewer patients in the imetelstat phase 2 portion and more patients in both arms of the phase 3 component. By cycle 4 day 1, elevations in SBP were seen in 75% of patients in the imetelstat arm of the phase 3 portion and 53% of patients in the placebo arm. No clear pattern of change in grade was observed.

L2215: *Clinical Reviewer comments: A trend towards more hypertension and more shifts towards higher grades of hypertension was seen in the imetelstat arm of the phase 3 portion of the study compared to placebo. This correlates with the higher rates of hypertension seen in the ADAE dataset. Overall, the data suggest a possible risk of hypertension with use of imetelstat, although few patients appear to have had severe elevations in their blood pressure.*
L2216: ## Temperature
L2217: At least one post-baseline temperature of &lt;35° C was reported in 8 patients, four of whom had baseline temperatures of &lt;35° C. Among patients who had a temperature of &lt;35° C and a baseline temperature of ≥35° C, three were on the imetelstat phase 2 portion of the study, one on the imetelstat arm of the phase 3 portion of the study.
L2218: Twenty-four patients had at least one post-baseline temperature of \ge 37.5^\circ \text{C}, including 9 on the phase 2 portion of the study, eight on the imetelstat arm of the phase 3 portion of the study, and 7 on the placebo arm.
L2219: Overall, no consistent increase or decrease in temperature was observed in patients treated with imetelstat.
L2220: *Clinical Reviewer comments: While fever and hypothermia were observed in a few patients, this is most likely consistent with infection or other systemic condition. No clear effect of imetelstat on temperature was observed.*
L2221: ## Heart Rate
L2222: Median heart rate (measured as peripheral pulse) on treatment for patients on the imetelstat phase 3 arm was 73 beats per minute (BPM) (range 42-151 BPM), with a baseline of 74 BPM (60-124 BPM). Twenty-one patients had at least one value of &lt;60 BPM, with no baseline values below 60 BPM. The timing of the incidents of bradycardia was variable, ranging from an unscheduled vital signs check on cycle 1 day 1 to cycle 28 day 1. No TEAE of bradycardia were reported, although bundle branch block was reported in one patient. Heart rates of &gt;110 BPM were reported in four patients, one of whom had an elevated baseline heart rate.
L2223: Median heart rate on treatment for patients on the placebo arm of the phase 3 portion of the study was 76 (range 18-117), with a baseline of 75 (53-106). Sixteen (27%) of patients had a heart rate of &lt;60 BPM at some time during the study. Of these, 11 had baseline of &gt;60 bpm. Note that this includes one patient who had two values of 18 BPM recorded. Five patients had at least one heart rate of &gt;110 BPM noted, none of whom had baseline heart rates of &gt;100 BPM.
L2224: *Clinical Reviewer comments: No major effects of imetelstat on heart rate/pulse are apparent from the data.*
L2225: ## Electrocardiograms (ECGs)
L2226: ## Data:
L2227: Based on subjects in hematological monotherapy studies, key findings for the clinical assessment of the proarrhythmic potential of imetelstat based on a review of the events included in the SMQ Torsade de pointes/QT prolongation with the additional PT of Seizure (Group C; N = 391) are as follows:
L2228: - Four (1.0%) subjects experienced events of ECG QT prolonged. Two subjects experienced non-serious Grade 3 events (1 of which also experienced a Grade 1 event) and 2 subjects experienced a Grade 1 event (Listing lae03c). All 4 ECG QT prolonged
L2229: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 217779 imetelstat
## TRADENAME, imetelstat
L2230: events were considered not related to imetelstat by the investigator. One of the subjects was from the MDS3001 Phase 3 imetelstat group (N = 118).
L2231: - One subject in the MDS3001 Phase 3 imetelstat group had a Grade 3 TEAE of ECG QT prolonged on Study Day 49 resulting in study drug interruption after receiving nitrofurantoin and furosemide on Study Days 48 and 49, respectively, for a Grade 2 urinary tract infection. Both events were considered unrelated to study treatment by the investigator, the ECG resolved without treatment on Study Day 51, and imetelstat treatment continued.
- In addition, a review of the associated events of ECG QTc Prolongation showed 10 (2.6%) subjects with events of syncope (6 [1.5%] were Grade 3), 3 subjects (0.8%) with events of cardiac arrest (2 [0.5%] were Grade 5 and 1 (0.3%) was Grade 4), 1 (0.3%) subject with an event of multiple organ dysfunction syndrome (Grade 4), 1 (0.3%) subject with an event of loss of consciousness (Grade 1), 1 (0.3%) subject with an event of seizure (Grade 3), and 1 (0.3%) subject with an event of ventricular tachycardia (Grade 1) (Table tcardae01c, Listing lcardae01c).The majority of these events were considered not related to imetelstat including the 2 fatal (Grade 5) cardiac arrest events in MF subjects (discussed below).One MDS/MPN subject from Study CP14B019 had an unrelated Grade 5 cardiac arrest following an esophagogastroduodenoscopy for Grade 3 upper gastrointestinal hemorrhage 26 days after the last dose of imetelstat.
- One post-ET MF subject from Study CP14B019 had an unrelated Grade 5 cardiac arrest in the setting of disease progression and Grade 4 sepsis 31 days after the first and last dose.
- Additionally, sponsor review of these associated events identified confounding by the underlying disease, underlying cardiac history including prior syncopal episodes, cardiac valve abnormalities, hypertension, and atrial fibrillation, concurrent anemia and other co-morbid conditions such as diabetes mellitus, advanced age and procedural complication. Therefore, an association of these events with QTC prolongation due to imetelstat is unlikely.

L2244: ## The Applicant's Position:
L2245: Overall, based on the current knowledge of imetelstat from nonclinical safety pharmacology assessments and the relevant clinical cardiac safety data, and supported by the limited proarrhythmic potential for other ODNs, imetelstat is not likely to prolong the QT interval.
L2246: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L2247: At least one ECG was performed, and some data was provided for all patients on study MDS3001. However, the data are extremely sparse. ECG were collected routinely only at baseline and cycle 2, day 1, severely limiting any conclusions that can be drawn regarding the long term or cumulative effects of imetelstat on cardiac function.
L2248: Among patients treated with imetelstat on the phase 2 portion, all patients had a documented baseline ECG, 52 had a cycle 2 day 1 ECG, and ECG at all other timepoints were collected for 1-2 patients. The only information provided was a description of the ECG as “normal”, “abnormal clinically insignificant”, and “abnormal clinically significant”. At baseline, 36 ECG were described as normal, 21 abnormal, clinically insignificant. At cycle 2 day 1, 19 were considered abnormal,
L2249: clinically insignificant. Of these, 7 had normal baseline ECGs. No further information regarding the specific change or how clinical significance was determined was provided.
L2250: Among patients receiving imetelstat on the phase 3 portion, all (118) patients received at least 1 ECG. Overall interpretation was available for 117 patients at baseline, 93 at cycle 2 day 1, and 11 at cycle 1 day 22. At baseline, 76 ECG were interpreted as normal and 41 as abnormal, clinically insignificant. At cycle 2 day 1, 68 ECG were interpreted as normal, 24 as abnormal, clinically insignificant, and one as abnormal, clinically significant. The baseline ECG was normal for 7 of the patients whose cycle 2 day 2 ECG were abnormal clinically insignificant and abnormal clinically insignificant for the patient whose cycle 2 day 1 ECG was abnormal clinically significant. Overall, two patients had ECG at any time documented as abnormal clinically significant.
L2251: Forty-nine patients who received placebo had cycle 2 day 1 ECG with results of the interpretation available. Of these, 28 were considered normal and 21 abnormal, clinically insignificant. Two patients with abnormal ECG had normal baseline ECG and one had an ECG read as abnormal, clinically significant.
L2252: *Clinical Reviewer comments: A small number of patients receiving imetelstat experienced a change in their ECG from normal to abnormal clinically insignificant and one changed from abnormal clinically insignificant to abnormal clinically significant. The interpretation of this finding is limited by lack of information about specific changes and how clinical significance was determined. No definitive conclusions can be drawn regarding the effect of imetelstat on cardiac events as diagnosed by ECG. Also see AE evaluation.*
L2253: ## QT
L2254: ## Data:
L2255: See above under ECGs. A thorough QT study has not been conducted.
L2256: ## The Applicant's Position:
L2257: A randomized double-blinded placebo-controlled clinical ventricular repolarization/QTc substudy is ongoing in subjects with MDS under Study MDS3001. Analyses of ECG parameters and the relationship between imetelstat exposure and the QT/QTc interval will be conducted on approximately 45 LR MDS subjects. Results will be provided upon completion of the study with an integrated risk assessment of nonclinical and clinical data to fully characterize the proarrhythmic potential of imetelstat.
L2258: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L2259: QT information was available on only a single patient in study MDS3001. This patient was treated with imetelstat on the phase 3 portion of the study. Their baseline QTcF was 423 ms.

L2266: ## Immunogenicity
L2267: ## Data:
L2268: There was no discernable effect of ADA on imetelstat PK (Applicant Figure 47), safety, or efficacy (See Section 6.3).
L2269: ## The Applicant's Position:
L2270: ADA do not impact the overall benefit/risk profile of imetelstat.
L2271: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L2272: Baseline screening for ADA (screening or cycle 1 day 1) was available for 226 patients on study MDS3001. The screen was positive for 10 patients. However, confirmatory testing was negative for all patients involved. At least one post-baseline value was available for 217 patients. Of these, 40 patients had at least one positive value at any time post-start of treatment and 28 had positive confirmatory evaluations, including 12 who were treated on the phase 2 portion of the study and 16 who were treated on the imetelstat arm of the phase 3 portion of the study.
L2273: Peak titers ranged from 10-80, with a median of 10. The dose of imetelstat was escalated in two patients ( (b) (6) and (b) (6) ). All other patients had dose maintained or decreased.
L2274: *Clinical Reviewer comments: Imetelstat appears to be immunogenic with positive ADA being observed as soon as study day 85.*
L2275: ## 8.2.5. Analysis of Submission-Specific Safety Issues
L2276: Specific safety issues are presented in the LR MDS population.
L2277: ## 8.2.5.1 Hepatic Adverse Events and LFT Elevations
L2278: ## Data:
L2279: ## Hepatic Events
L2280: Approximately 43% and 35% of subjects in the MDS3001 Phase 3 imetelstat and placebo groups, respectively, had a medical history of conditions associated with iron overload (hyperferritenaemia, haemosiderosis, haemachromotosis, and iron overload), and approximately 14% and 10% of subjects in the 2 groups, respectively, had a history of hepatobiliary disorders. In addition, 64% and 63% of subjects, respectively, were on iron chelating agents at the time of entering the study or while on treatment.

In the MDS3001 Phase 3 imetelstat group (N = 118), hepatic TEAEs (any grade) were reported in 28.8% of subjects (Applicant Table 92). The most commonly reported hepatic TEAE in subjects in the imetelstat group was ALT increased in 11.9% of subjects, followed by AST increased and hyperbilirubinaemia (9.3% each), blood ALP increased (4.2%), and gamma-glutamyl transferase (GGT) increased (3.4%). Seventeen (14.4%) subjects had hepatic TEAEs considered related to imetelstat (Table taesi01a). Eight (6.8%) subjects had a Grade 3/4 hepatic event, including 3 (2.5%) subjects with Grade 3 hepatic events considered related to imetelstat. One subject had a serious Grade 4 oesophageal varices haemorrhage (Listing lae02b). Median time to onset of the Grade 3/4 hepatic events was 198 days (range: 1 to 633 days) in the imetelstat group and 113 days (range: 85 to 330) in the placebo group (Table taesi03a). There were no other serious events and no Grade 5 events. One subject had a dose reduction due to Grade 2 events of ALT increased and AST increased (not related to imetelstat). No subjects discontinued treatment due to hepatic events.
L2281: In the MDS3001 Phase 3 placebo group (N = 59), hepatic TEAEs (any grade) were reported in 16.9% of subjects and Grade 3/4 TEAEs were reported in 5.1% of subjects with median time to onset of 113 days (range: 85 to 330 days) (Table taesi03a) (Applicant Table 92). The most commonly reported hepatic TEAE in subjects in the placebo group was hyperbilirubinaemia in 10.2% of subjects, followed by ALT and AST increased (6.8% each), and GGT increased (3.4%). Four (6.8%) subjects had events considered related to study treatment (Table tasei01a). There
L2282: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 217779 imetelstat
## TRADENAME, imetelstat
L2283: were no Grade 4 or Grade 5 hepatic TEAEs and no hepatic SAEs in the placebo group (Listing lae02b). Two subjects had dose reductions due to hepatic TEAEs (Grade 2/3 events of ALT increased, AST increased and GGT increased) all of which were considered related to study treatment. No subjects discontinued study treatment due to a hepatic TEAE.
L2284: In the Phase 2 imetelstat group (N = 57), hepatic TEAEs (any grade) were reported in 31.6% of subjects, and Grade 3 events were reported in 12.3% of subjects (Applicant Table 92). The most commonly reported event in subjects was ALT increased in 17.5% of subjects, followed by AST increased (14.0%), hyperbilirubinaemia (8.8%), hepatomegaly (5.3%), and blood ALP increased (3.5%). Nine subjects had hepatic events considered related to imetelstat (Table taesi01b). Median time to onset of the Grade 3/4 hepatic events was 439 days (range: 8 to 769 days) (Table taesi03b). Two subjects had SAEs: 1 subject with Grade 3 events of cholecystitis, biliary colic, and oesophageal varices haemorrhage; 1 subject with Grade 3 events of hepatocellular carcinoma and hepatic cancer (Table taesi04b, Listing lae02b). All of the events were unrelated to imetelstat. There were no Grade 5 hepatic events. No subjects discontinued treatment due to hepatic events.

L2292: ## Liver Function Test Abnormalities
L2293: No subjects in Study MDS3001 met Hy's law criteria.
L2294: In the Phase 3 of the MDS3001 study, 2 subjects, both in the imetelstat group (N = 188), met the laboratory criteria for Hy's law, ie, ALT or AST Grade \ge 2 (&gt; 3.0 \times \text{ULN}) with concurrent (within 7 days) bilirubin Grade \ge 2 (&gt; 2.0 \times \text{ULN}); however, an alternative etiology for these LFT elevations was established: deferasirox hepatotoxicity in 1 subject, and in the other subject, multiple risk factors for LFT alterations and possibly deferasirox-induced liver injury. These cases were reviewed by the HEC and determined not to be Hy's law cases.
L2295: In the MDS3001 Phase 2 imetelstat group (N = 59), 1 subject met the laboratory criteria for Hy's law, ie, ALT or AST Grade \ge 2 (&gt; 3.0 \times \text{ULN}) with concurrent (within 7 days) bilirubin Grade \ge 2 (&gt; 2.0 \times \text{ULN}); however, an alternative etiology for the LFT elevations was established (sepsis). The case was reviewed by the HEC and determined not to be a Hy's law case.
L2296: Additional findings based on laboratory are provided in Section 8.2.4 under the “Laboratory Findings” heading.

L2302: ## The Applicant's Position:
L2303: Hepatic events were primarily low grade and reversible elevations of ALT, AST, and bilirubin, have been observed in both imetelstat and placebo-treated subjects, with no apparent dose effects. No cases meeting Hy's law criteria were observed. The hepatic events and laboratory abnormalities in the MDS population are confounded by the high background rate of hepatic dysfunction due to iron overload from chronic transfusions in this transfusion-dependent patient population as well as the concomitant use of iron chelating agents, alcohol, antibiotics, and concurrent infections associated with hepatic abnormalities. Hepatic events were manageable and generally reversible, with few dose reductions and treatment discontinuations due to hepatic events observed across all imetelstat treated subjects.
L2304: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L2305: ## Hepatic Disorders
L2306: As noted above, a higher rate of hepatic disorders was observed in the imetelstat arm compared to the placebo arm of the phase 3 portion. The relative incidence of transaminase elevation and increased bilirubin were discussed in further detail in the laboratory data section, above. In summary, higher rates of transaminase (AST and ALT) elevation and alkaline phosphatase elevation were observed in the imetelstat arm of the study, although the rate of bilirubin increased was similar between the imetelstat and placebo arms.
L2307: Overall, TEAE in the general category of hepatic disorders were observed in 31 (26%) patients in the imetelstat arm of the phase 3 portion (based on the 120-day update), compared to 10 (17%) in patients on the placebo arm. The most common TEAE noted were transaminase elevation, hyperbilirubinemia, and GGT increased. A variety of other events of hepatic disorders were observed in the imetelstat arm, including hepatic cirrhosis, hepatic steatosis, and portal hypertension in two patients each and hepatitis, hepatomegaly, hepatotoxicity, jaundice hepatocellular, and varices esophageal in one patient each in the imetelstat arm. No abnormalities apart from laboratory abnormalities (including hyperbilirubinemia) were noted in the placebo arm. In the phase 2 portion of the study, 16 (28%) patients experienced at least one hepatic disorder. Again, investigations were the most common abnormalities. In addition,
L2308: hepatomegaly was seen in 3 (5%) patients and hepatic cirrhosis and chronic hepatitis in one patient each.
L2309: Two events meeting the laboratory definition of Hy's law cases were observed in the imetelstat arm of the phase 3 portion of the study. Of these, one was in a patient who had abnormalities in bilirubin at baseline and had no clear pattern of worsening during treatment. The other patient developed transaminase and bilirubin elevation while being treated with imetelstat and deferasirox simultaneously. The abnormalities resolved when both medications were stopped. Imetelstat was not restarted, although a milder increase in transaminases was seen after restarting deferasirox.
L2310: *Clinical Reviewer comments: Of the two patients who met laboratory criteria for Hy's law in the imetelstat portion of the phase 3 part of the study, one had baseline abnormalities. The second patient had abnormalities that occurred on treatment and were consistent with Hy's law. The finding was confounded by the coadministration of deferasirox. Given the positive de-challenge and the finding of more hepatic disorders in the imetelstat arm, the finding is suspicious for a possible Hy's law case.*
L2311: One additional patient on the phase 2 portion of the study experienced laboratory abnormalities consistent with Hy's law. Per the Applicant, these laboratory abnormalities occurred in the setting of sepsis (noting again that sepsis was not documented for this patient in the ADAE dataset, although a UTI that could be a precursor to sepsis was documented).
L2312: *Clinical Reviewer comments: Overall, the data suggest that imetelstat has at least minor hepatotoxicity. Liver function should be monitored in patients receiving imetelstat.*
L2313: ## 8.2.5.2 Thrombocytopenia and Bleeding Events
L2314: ## Data:
L2315: ## Thrombocytopenia
L2316: Thrombocytopenia findings based on laboratory data are summarized in Section 8.2.4 under the “Laboratory Findings” heading.
L2317: In the MDS3001 Phase 3 imetelstat group (N = 118), thrombocytopenia (any grade) was reported in 75.4% of subjects, including 61.9% of subjects with Grade 3/4 thrombocytopenia (Applicant Table 94). Most events occurred in the earlier months of treatment and rates decreased over time for any grade and Grade 3/4 thrombocytopenia, suggesting a lack of cumulative toxicity. Grade 4 thrombocytopenia was reported in 24 subjects (Listing lae02e), and 67.8% of subjects had events that were considered related to imetelstat treatment. In the imetelstat group, thrombocytopenia led to cycle delay in 46.6% of subjects, dose reduction in 22.9% of subjects, and discontinuation of imetelstat in 3.4% of subjects. Median time to cycle delay due to thrombocytopenia was 6 weeks (range: 2.9 to 71.7) and median time to dose
L2318: reduction was 16 weeks (range: 2.9 to 64) (MDS3001 CSR [Phase 3]/Table tsiex18a_p2). Subjects who discontinued treatment due to thrombocytopenia occurred later in treatment (\ge 150 days) (MDS3001 CSR [Phase 3]/Table 61).
L2319: In the placebo group (N = 59), thrombocytopenia (any grade) was reported in 10.2% of subjects, including 8.5% of subjects with Grade 3/4 thrombocytopenia (Applicant Table 94). Grade 4 thrombocytopenia was reported in 3.4% of subjects, and 5.1% of subjects had events that were considered related to study treatment (Listing lae02e). In the placebo group, thrombocytopenia led to cycle delay in 1 (1.7%) subject and dose reduction in 1 (1.7%) subject; no subjects in the placebo group discontinued treatment due to thrombocytopenia.
L2320: There were no Grade 5 events of thrombocytopenia and no SAEs of thrombocytopenia reported in either of the MDS3001 Phase 3 treatment groups.
L2321: In the MDS3001 Phase 2 imetelstat group (N = 57), thrombocytopenia (any grade) was reported in 61.4% of subjects, including 54.4% of subjects with Grade 3/4 thrombocytopenia (Applicant Table 94). Grade 4 thrombocytopenia was reported in 12 subjects, and most events were considered related to imetelstat treatment (Listing lae02e). There were no Grade 5 events of thrombocytopenia.

L2327: ## Bleeding Events
L2328: To investigate the temporal association between decreased platelet counts based upon laboratory results and concurrent bleeding AEs, subjects with decreased platelet counts by laboratory value were identified and assessed if a bleeding event occurred within \pm 7 days of the laboratory result. In the MDS3001 Phase 3 imetelstat group (N = 118), 65.3% of subjects had Grade 3 or Grade 4 decreased platelets while on study treatment, and 7.6% of subjects had a Grade 1 or Grade 2 bleeding event within \pm 7 days of the laboratory result (Applicant Table 95). These included Grade 1 events of haematoma (in 2 subjects), epistaxis (in 2 subjects), and gingival bleeding, haematuria, ecchymosis, and contusion (in 1 subject each), and Grade 2 gastrointestinal haemorrhage (in 1 subject) (MDS3001 CSR [Phase 3]/Listing lsflb14a_p2). None of the subjects in the MDS3001 Phase 3 imetelstat group had Grade 3 or Grade 4 or serious bleeding events associated with Grade 3/4 decreased platelets.
L2329: In the MDS3001 Phase 3 placebo group (N = 59), 8.5% of subjects had Grade 3 or Grade 4 decreased platelets while on study treatment, and none of them had a bleeding event within \pm 7 days of the laboratory result (Applicant Table 95).
L2330: In the MDS3001 Phase 2 imetelstat group (N = 57), 57.9% of subjects had Grade 3 or Grade 4 decreased platelets while on imetelstat treatment, and 14.0% of subjects had a bleeding event within \pm 7 days of the laboratory result (Applicant Table 95). Two subjects had Grade 3 bleeding events associated with Grade 3/4 decreased platelets (serious haematoma and non-serious post procedural haemorrhage; both not related to imetelstat). Two subjects had serious bleeding events associated with Grade 3/4 decreased platelets (Grade 3 haematoma and Grade 2 rectal haemorrhage; both not related to imetelstat) (MDS3001 CSR [Phase 2]/Listing lsflb14_p1). Among subjects with any grade of decreased platelets, 9 subjects had associated Grade 3/4 or serious bleeding events.

L2335: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L2336: Grade 3/4 thrombocytopenia is very common with imetelstat treatment and occurred more frequently during the first few cycles. These events are manageable with cycle delays and dose reductions and are generally reversible. Few subjects discontinued imetelstat due to thrombocytopenia. Moreover, the number of significant bleeding events was low, the events were assessed by the investigator as not related to imetelstat treatment, and most were not associated with severe thrombocytopenia. Monitoring for thrombocytopenia will be included in the proposed product labeling and these events will be monitored with routine post-marketing pharmacovigilance.
L2337: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L2338: Figure 40 shows mean number of Grade 3+ thrombocytopenia events over time estimated with a mean cumulative function (nonparametric). Mean number of Grade 3+ thrombocytopenia events by 12 months is 1.95 (95% CI: 1.50, 2.41) in the imetelstat group and 0.16 (95% CI: 0.02, 0.30) in the placebo group with a difference of 1.79 (95% CI: 1.32, 2.27) between the groups. Mean number of Grade 3+ thrombocytopenia events by 24 months is 2.84 (95% CI: 2.00, 3.67) in the imetelstat group and 0.16 (95% CI: 0.02, 0.30) in the placebo group with a difference of 2.68 (95% CI: 1.83, 3.52) between the groups. These results show a higher frequency of recurrent Grade 3+ thrombocytopenia events in the imetelstat group compared to the placebo group.
L2339: **Statistical Reviewer Comment:** The mean cumulative function results based on FDA analysis match the results based on the code provided by the Applicant in response to the information request (sent on 22 January 2024). The results reported by the Applicant in the response to the information request have some minor discrepancies. These discrepancies have no impact on overall conclusions.

L2355: Patients on the imetelstat arm experienced more adverse events of hemorrhage compared to those on the placebo arm, with 22% of patients in the imetelstat arm and 12% in the placebo arm experiencing at least one hemorrhage (Table 98). No specific site of bleeding dominated; however, epistaxis, hematoma, contusion, and GI bleeding were common events. All of these events were more common in the imetelstat arm except for contusion. Grade 3-4 events were rare but did occur in three patients on the imetelstat arm and one on the placebo arm. All grade 3-4 events were related to GI bleeding, except for one grade 3 hematuria in the imetelstat arm.

L2359: Thrombocytopenia and hemorrhagic events were seen frequently in patients receiving imetelstat in the ISS, with an increase in incidence as the dose of imetelstat increased. The finding is most notable in patients who received a higher dose of imetelstat.

L2363: Data cutoff date 13 October 2022
L2364: A total of 138 patients receiving &gt;7.1 mg/kg/4 weeks of imetelstat experienced at least one event in the broad SMQ of hemorrhage. When laboratory terms were removed, this decreased to 98 (33%). However, grade 3-4 hemorrhagic events were seen in 62 (21%) patients and fatal events were seen in 5 (1.7%) of patients who received &gt;7.1 mg/kg/4 weeks of imetelstat. Fatal events included hemorrhage, hemorrhage intracranial (2), esophageal varices hemorrhage, and upper GI hemorrhage.
L2365: In summary, platelet decreases were observed in over 90% of patients receiving imetelstat (by laboratory values, Table 83). The mean platelet count on the imetelstat arm decreased and did not improve to baseline in subsequent cycles, despite a higher rate of platelet transfusion in the imetelstat arm. Finally, hemorrhage was observed more frequently in patients receiving imetelstat. Overall, the risk of thrombocytopenia is high in patients receiving imetelstat.
L2366: *Clinical Reviewer comments:* The incidence of thrombocytopenia was much higher in the imetelstat arm of the phase 3 portion of MDS3001 compared to the placebo arm. This finding is confirmed by a high incidence of thrombocytopenia in patients who received imetelstat in the phase 2 portion of the study and in various studies in the ISS. There appears to be a dose-response relationship based on the incidence of thrombocytopenia compared to dose in the ISS; however, the relationship is confounded by the wide variety of underlying conditions being treated. Bleeding events and platelet transfusion were more common with imetelstat compared to placebo in MDS3001 and the ISS confirmed that bleeding events were observed throughout the developmental program, including fatal hemorrhagic events. Although no fatal hemorrhagic events were identified in MDS3001, the reviewer feels that the death attributed to "neurological degeneration" in the phase 2 portion of the study was consistent with death due to an intracranial hemorrhage, although no testing that could have confirmed this diagnosis (i.e., imaging, autopsy) was reported.
L2367: *We disagree with the Applicant's statement that events of thrombocytopenia occurred primarily in the early cycles. Although some decrease in incidence was observed after the first 3 cycles,*
L2368: *the rates of thrombocytopenia remained high. In addition, the less frequent CBC in later cycles may confound the comparison. Also see laboratory section above for further details.*
L2369: *Overall, thrombocytopenia is a common and severe risk of imetelstat use and careful monitoring and treatment is needed to prevent severe or fatal hemorrhage.*
L2370: ## 8.2.5.3 Neutropenia and Infections
L2371: ## Data:
L2372: ## Neutropenia
L2373: Neutropenia findings based on laboratory data are summarized in Section 8.2.4 under the “Laboratory Findings” heading.
L2374: In the MDS3001 Phase 3 imetelstat group (N = 118), neutropenia (any grade) was reported in 73.7% of subjects, including 67.8% of subjects with Grade 3/4 neutropenia (Applicant Table 100). Grade 4 neutropenia was reported in 24 subjects (Listing lae02e). Treatment-related neutropenia was reported in 64.4% of subjects. In the imetelstat group, neutropenia led to dose reduction in 33.1% of subjects, cycle delay in 50.8% of subjects, and discontinuation of study treatment in 5.1% of subjects. Median time to cycle delay due to neutropenia was 4.21 weeks (range: 2.0 to 119.0) and median time to dose reduction was 8.14 weeks (range: 2.0 to 78.9) (MDS3001 CSR [Phase 3]/Table tsiex18a_p2). Treatment discontinuation due to neutropenia occurred later in treatment (\ge 100 days) in all but 1 subject (MDS3001 CSR [Phase 3]/Table 65). TEAEs of neutropenia occurred at highest incidence during the first 3 months of treatment and decreased in frequency over time, suggesting a lack of cumulative toxicity.
L2375: In the placebo group (N = 59), neutropenia (any grade) was reported in 6.8% of subjects, including 3.4% of subjects with Grade 3/4 neutropenia (Applicant Table 100). One subject had Grade 4 neutropenia (Listing lae02e). Treatment-related neutropenia was reported in 5.1% of subjects. One (1.7%) subject had a dose reduction due to neutropenia, and 1 (1.7%) subject had a cycle delay due to neutropenia. No subjects in the placebo group discontinued study treatment due to neutropenia.
L2376: There were no Grade 5 events of neutropenia and no SAEs of neutropenia reported in either MDS3001 Phase 3 treatment group.
L2377: In the MDS3001 Phase 2 imetelstat group (N = 57), neutropenia (any grade) was reported in 66.7% of subjects, including 59.6% of subjects with Grade 3/4 neutropenia. TEAEs of neutropenia occurred at the highest incidence during the first 3 months of treatment and decreased in frequency over time, suggesting a lack of cumulative toxicity. Most events of neutropenia were considered related to imetelstat treatment. One subject had an SAE of Grade 4 neutropenia considered related to imetelstat that led to dose reduction. There were no
L2378: Grade 5 events of neutropenia. Neutropenia led to cycle delay in 49.1% of subjects, dose reduction in 43.9% of subjects, and discontinuation of imetelstat in 8.8% of subjects. All discontinuations due to neutropenia occurred later in treatment (&gt; 100 days on study) (Listing lsfac09_p1).

L2383: ## Infections
L2384: In the MDS3001 Phase 3 imetelstat group (N = 118), treatment-emergent infections (any grade) were reported in 42.4% of subjects, and 10.2% of subjects had Grade 3/4 infections (Applicant Table 101). The most commonly reported infection was COVID-19 in 15.3% of subjects), followed by urinary tract infection in 5.9% of subjects and pneumonia in 3.4% subjects. Four (3.4%) subjects had Grade 4 infections (1 subject each with COVID-19 pneumonia and Enterococcal sepsis, and 2 subjects with sepsis, 1 of which was considered related to study treatment) (Listing lae2e). Median time to onset of first Grade 3/4 infection 142.0 days (range: 20 to 644 days) (Table taesi03j). Six subjects had infections considered related to study treatment, including sinusitis, pneumonia bacterial, pneumonia, renal abscess, sepsis, urinary
L2385: TRADENAME, imetelstat
L2386: tract infection, and neutropenic sepsis (Table tae02j). Serious infections were reported in 11.9% of subjects, the most common of which was pneumonia in 3 (2.5%) subjects (Table tae02g).
L2387: In the MDS3001 Phase 3 placebo group (N = 59), 33.9% of subjects had infections, including 13.6% of subjects with Grade 3/4 infections (Applicant Table 101). Median time to first Grade 3/4 infection was 142.0 days (range: 20 to 644 days) (Table taesi03j). The most commonly reported infection was COVID-19 in 4 (6.8%) subjects. One subject had a Grade 4 infection of listeriosis, considered not related to study treatment (Listing lae02e). There were no infections considered related to study treatment in the placebo group, and no Grade 5 infections. Serious infections were reported in 13.6% of subjects, the most common of which was COVID-19 pneumonia in 5.1% of subjects. Median time to onset of first Grade 3/4 infection in the placebo group was 61.5 days (range: 15 to 638 days) (Table tae02g).
L2388: In the MDS3001 Phase 2 imetelstat group (N = 57), treatment-emergent infections (any grade) were reported in 57.9% of subjects in the overall subject population, and 21.1% of subjects had Grade 3/4 infections (Table taesi02t). The most commonly reported infection was urinary tract infection (17.5%), followed by bronchitis and nasopharyngitis (12.3% each). Ten subjects had Grade 3 infections, and 6 subjects had Grade 4 infections, one of which was considered related to study treatment (urinary tract infection) (MDS3001 CSR [Phase 2]/Section 12.4.4.2.2.2). One subject had a Grade 5 infection of COVID-19 pneumonia considered not related to study treatment.
L2389: To investigate the temporal association between decreased neutrophil counts based upon laboratory results and concurrent infection AEs, subjects with decreased neutrophil counts by laboratory value were identified and assessed if an infection event occurred within \pm 7 days of the laboratory result. In the MDS3001 Phase 3 imetelstat group, 72.0% of subjects had Grade 3 or Grade 4 decreased neutrophils while on study treatment, and 7.6% of subjects had an infection within \pm 7 days of the laboratory result (Applicant Table 102). Three subjects with sepsis events had Grade 3/4 infections associated with Grade 3/4 neutropenia, all of which were reported as SAEs (Listing llab05a). In the placebo group (N = 59), among the 6.8% of subjects with Grade 3 or Grade 4 decreased neutrophils while on study treatment, 1 (1.7%) subject had a Grade 4 infection within \pm 7 days of the laboratory result (Applicant Table 102 and Listing llab05a).

L2401: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L2402: Grade 3/4 neutropenia is very common with imetelstat and occurred more frequently during the first few cycles. These events are manageable with dose modifications and growth factor support and generally reversible. Few subjects discontinued imetelstat due to neutropenia. Moreover, the number of events of febrile neutropenia or significant infections was low, severe infections were generally not associated with severe neutropenia, and most were not related to imetelstat treatment. Management of neutropenia will be included in the proposed product labeling and these events will be monitored with routine post-marketing pharmacovigilance.
L2403: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L2404: Figure 39 shows mean number of Grade 3+ neutropenia events over time estimated with a mean cumulative function (nonparametric). Mean number of Grade 3+ thrombocytopenia events by 24 months is 3.56 (95% CI: 2.55, 4.57) in the imetelstat group and 0.07 (95% CI: 0.00, 0.15) in the placebo group with a difference of 3.49 (95% CI: 2.48, 4.50) between the groups. These results show a higher frequency of recurrent Grade 3+ neutropenia events in the imetelstat group compared to the placebo group.

L2436: Use of any anti-infective (including vaccines) was documented in 75 (64%) of patients receiving imetelstat and 35 (59%) of those receiving placebo (Table 106). If vaccines were excluded, systemic anti-infective treatment was used in 50 (42%) of patients on the imetelstat arm and 20 (34%) of patients on the placebo arm. Antibiotic use was documented in 43 (36%) of patients receiving imetelstat and 19 (32%) of patients receiving placebo. Antiviral medication use was documented in 12 (10%) patients receiving imetelstat and 3 (5%) of patients receiving placebo.

L2442: ## 8.2.5.4 Infusion-Related Reactions
L2443: ## Data:
L2444: In the MDS3001 Phase 3 imetelstat group (N = 118), IRRs (any grade) were reported in 7.6% of subjects, and Grade 3 events were reported in 1.7% of subjects (Applicant Table 107). Median time to onset of the Grade 3 events was 106.5 days (range: 85 to 128 days) in the 2 subjects with Grade 3 IRRs (Table taesi03g). The most commonly reported IRR was headache in 4.2% of subjects. All events were considered related to study treatment. There were no Grade 4 or Grade 5 events. One subject had an IRR of serious Grade 3 hypertensive crisis described briefly below
L2445: - Subject (b) (6) had an SAE of Grade 3 hypertensive crisis on Study Day 85 considered related to imetelstat (Listing lae02c). The subject had an ongoing medical history of hypertension. The subject's blood pressure was 135/54 mmHg at the start of the infusion. Prior to the infusion, the subject received premedication with steroid, but not the antihistamine (reason not specified). Approximately 1 hour into the infusion, her blood pressure was 206/87 mmHg, and the infusion was interrupted. She was treated with nitroglycerin. Approximately 30 minutes later, her blood pressure was 169/69 mmHg, and the infusion was restarted. The infusion was interrupted a second time (blood pressure 203/84 mmHg) and then restarted again (blood pressure 184/71 mmHg). At the conclusion of the infusion, her blood pressure was 209/91 mmHg and she was treated with nifedipine. Three days later, the hypertension improved to Grade 1, and there were no further events of hypertension in subsequent cycles. The subject continued treatment at a reduced dose of 4.4 mg/kg (active moiety, equivalent to 4.7 mg/kg imetelstat-sodium) due to thrombocytopenia and neutropenia. The subject discontinued imetelstat on Study Day 322 due to lack of efficacy.
L2446: In the placebo group (N = 59), 3.4% of subjects had IRR events. All events were Grade 1 and considered related to study treatment. There were no serious events.
L2447: Five (4.2%) subjects in the imetelstat group had IRRs that led to study treatment interruption (Table taesi01o). One subject in the imetelstat group discontinued imetelstat due to an IRR event of Grade 2 pruritus. No subjects in the placebo group had a treatment delay or discontinuation due to an IRR. No subject in either treatment group had a dose reduction due to an IRR event. IRRs were managed by symptom relief (steroids, antihistamines, blood pressure medications and pain medication).
L2448: TRADENAME, imetelstat
L2449: In the MDS3001 Phase 2 imetelstat group (N = 57), IRR (any grade) were reported in 10.5% of subjects, and Grade 3/4 events were reported in 7.0% of subjects (Table taesi02n). Median time to onset of the Grade 3/4 events was 520.5 days (range: 379 to 1197 days) in the 4 subjects with Grade 3/4 IRRs (Table taesi03h). The most commonly reported IRR was back pain in 5.3% of subjects, followed by chest pain and hypotension (3.5% each). There were no Grade 4 or Grade 5 events and no serious events (Table taesi01n). Imetelstat was interrupted in 5 subjects due to an IRR. No subjects discontinued treatment due to an IRR. Infusion-related reactions were managed by symptom relief (steroids, antihistamines, blood pressure medications and pain medications). The occurrence of IRR was similar in the Phase 2 and Phase 3 imetelstat groups.
