L2453: Note: Medical Dictionary for Regulatory Activities (MedDRA) v25.0 was used for TEAE coding.
L2454: IRR = infusion-related reaction; SAE = serious adverse event; TEAE = treatment-emergent adverse event.
L2455: ## The Applicant's Position:
L2456: The incidence of IRRs in the imetelstat studies was low (&lt; 15%) across the program and most events were Grade 1/2. The introduction of protocol mandated premedication with hydrocortisone and diphenhydramine has reduced the incidence and severity of IRRs. IRRs were managed by dose interruptions and symptom relief. There were no Grade 4 or 5 events or dose reductions due to IRRs. One subject had an IRR of serious Grade 3 hypertensive crisis in setting of not receiving premedication with an antihistamine, that led to treatment discontinuation. Two other subjects discontinued treatment due IRR events: Grade 2 pruritis in 1 subject, and Grade 3 dyspnoea and Grade 2 arthritis in the other subject. The most commonly reported IRR in imetelstat-treated LR MDS subjects was headache. Recommendations for premedication and management of IRRs will be included in the proposed product labeling.
L2457: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L2458: No events under the PT of infusion related reaction (IRR) or cytokine release syndrome were reported in the ADAE dataset for patients on either part or arm of MDS3001. However, symptoms associated with IRR were observed frequently in patients receiving imetelstat on MDS3001. The Applicant reported IRR based on their grouped term occurred in 17 patients, including 6 (11%) in the phase 2 portion of the study, 9 (8%) receiving imetelstat in the phase 3 portion, and 2 (3.4%) on the placebo arm of the phase 3 portion. Preferred terms commonly associated with IRR included back pain, headache, bone pain, and chest pain. IRR resulted in drug discontinuation in one patient and treatment interruption in 10 patients.
L2459: As noted by the Applicant, one of these events was considered grade 3. Among the preferred terms noted by the Applicant as related to IRR, six events in 6 patients, all of whom received imetelstat, were grade 3. These events included hypertension, hypertensive crisis, hypotension, non-cardiac chest pain, headache, and back pain.
L2460: Events considered to be related to IRR by the Applicant occurred in 91 (12%) of patients in the ISS dataset, including 30 who had IRR as the PT provided for the event. Treatment was withdrawn in 10 patients due to IRR.
L2461: Some adverse events that may be associated with IRR were more common in patients receiving imetelstat including headache, pruritus, fatigue, and arthralgia/myalgia. However, it is difficult to be certain how many of these events were related to IRR and how many were independent risks of imetelstat.
L2462: *Clinical Reviewer comments: Overall, there appears to be a significant risk of IRR with use of imetelstat. A warning and precaution statement is planned for the label and recommendations for premedication will be provided.*
L2463: ## 8.2.6. **Clinical Outcome Assessment (COA) Analyses Informing Safety/Tolerability**
L2464: ## **Data:**
L2465: ## **FACIT-Fatigue Score**
L2466: Additional analysis of the FACIT-Fatigue Score described in Section 8.1.2 demonstrated that subjects who reported TEAE of Grade 3 or 4 neutropenia or thrombocytopenia had the same rates of sustained meaningful improvement in fatigue (42/80; 52.5% and 39/73; 53.4% respectively) as the total imetelstat population (50%) (SCE Section 3.2.4.1.2).
L2467: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L2468: Treatment-emergent neutropenia and thrombocytopenia had no impact on improvement in fatigue observed in subjects who achieved TI.
L2469: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L2470: See the “Efficacy Results – Secondary or Exploratory COA (PRO) endpoints” section above for FDA discussion regarding the FACIT-Fatigue score and other PRO endpoints related to disease symptoms.
L2471: FDA also examined patient-reported tolerability data from the FACT GP5 item “I am bothered by side effects of treatment” and found that patients treated with imetelstat did not report increased side effects compared to placebo. This is likely because the full side effect profile of imetelstat includes non-symptomatic AEs such as cytopenias and is further discussed in detail in Section 3.4.
L2472: ## 8.2.7. **Safety Analyses by Demographic Subgroups**
L2473: ## **Data:**
L2474: ## **Age**
L2475: In the imetelstat group (N =118), 77.1% subjects were ≥ 65 years old. Events reported more frequently in subjects ≥ 65 years old than in subjects &lt; 65 years old included thrombocytopenia (79.1% vs 63.0%, respectively), and infections (48.4% vs 22.2%, respectively). In the placebo group (N = 59), 84.7% subject were ≥ 65 years old. Events reported more frequently in subjects ≥ 65 years old than in subjects &lt; 65 years old included thrombocytopenia (12.0% vs 0%, respectively), and infections (40.0% vs 0%, respectively). There were no differences of ≥ 10% of subjects in SAEs, hepatic events, bleeding events, or IRRs.
L2476: In the imetelstat group (N =118), 29.7% subjects were ≥ 75 years old. Events reported more frequently in subjects ≥ 75 years old than in subjects &lt; 75 years old included thrombocytopenia (82.9% vs 72.3%, respectively) and SAEs (44.8% vs 28.1%, respectively), driven by serious
L2477: TRADENAME, imetelstat
L2478: infections (20.7% vs 9.0%, respectively). In the placebo group (N = 59), 39.0% subjects were ≥ 75 years old. Events reported more frequently in subjects ≥ 75 years old included SAEs (38.1% vs 13.2%, respectively) and serious infections (23.8% vs 7.9%, respectively). There were no differences of ≥ 10% of subjects in hepatic events, bleeding events, infections (any grade), or IRRs.
L2479: ## Sex
L2480: In the imetelstat group (N =118), 60.2% of subjects were males and 39.8% of subjects were females. There were no differences of ≥ 10% of subjects in the subgroup analysis by sex for thrombocytopenia, neutropenia, SAEs, hepatic events, bleeding events, infections, or IRRs.
L2481: ## Renal Function
L2482: In the imetelstat group (N =118), subjects at baseline were distributed across the renal function categories of normal (30.5%), mild impairment (35.6%), or moderate impairment (33.1%). There was no trend of worse toxicity in increasingly renally impaired subjects. There were no differences of ≥ 10% of subjects across ≥ 2 subgroups in thrombocytopenia, SAEs, bleeding events, infections (any grade), or IRRs.
L2483: ## Hepatic Function
L2484: In the imetelstat group (N =118), most subjects had normal (57.6%) or mild (26.3%) hepatic impairment at baseline. Fewer subjects had moderate hepatic impairment (14.4%), and thus interpretation of results in this subgroup should be made with caution. There was no trend of worse toxicity in increasingly hepatically impaired subjects.
L2485: ## Race, Ethnicity, ECOG Performance Status (PS)
L2486: Most subjects were White (80.5%), Not Hispanic (84.7%), and ECOG PS 0 or 1 (94.9%) at baseline.
L2487: ## The Applicant's Position:
L2488: No clinically significant differences in safety from the overall population were observed in the subgroup analysis, including rate and intensity of TEAEs, SAEs, and events of interest in imetelstat-treated subjects based on age, sex, race/ethnicity, ECOG PS, mild to moderate hepatic impairment, or mild to moderate renal impairment. The analysis of race, ethnicity, and ECOG PS subgroups were not meaningful due to the disproportionate number of subjects who were White, Not Hispanic, and ECOG PS 0 or 1 at baseline, consistent with the characteristics of patients in the proposed indication.
L2489: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L2490: FDA evaluated risk of TEAE and grade 3-4 AE by sex, race, ethnicity, and age (&lt;65 years, ≥65 years of age). Results are below.
L2491: ## Sex
L2492: A total of 92 patients treated on trial MDS3001 were female, including 25 (35%) in the phase 2 component, 47 (40%) in the phase 3 imetelstat arm, and 20 (34%) on the phase 3 placebo arm. Comparison of AE by sex and study arm for the phase 3 portion of the study is provided in Table 108. Results were generally similar for the phase 2 portion of the study.

L2496: We disagree with the Applicant's statement that no differences of &gt;10 percent units between the rate of AE between sexes. Overall, men were more likely to have grade 3+ TEAE in both arms of the phase 3 part of the study. Women had a larger risk difference for grade 3+ events (+49 percent units versus +39), but both sexes showed a major increase in risk in the imetelstat arm. Risk difference was &gt;10 percent units higher in female patients compared to male for neutropenia, arthralgia/myalgia, iron overload, and peripheral edema. Infections were more
L2497: common in female patients, with a particularly notable higher rate of UTIs, possibly related to the higher rate of neutropenia.
L2498: The risk difference was positive for female patients and negative for male patients for iron overload, peripheral edema, diarrhea, and arthralgia/myalgia. The significance of this finding is unclear given the small total number of patients who experienced these TEAE but could suggest areas for post-marketing evaluation.
L2499: Overall, the risk was similar between female and male patients, although some events, including neutropenia, arthralgia/myalgia, iron overload, hypertension, infections, and diarrhea appear to be more common in female patients whereas hepatic disorders appeared to be more common in male patients.

L2508: Overall, the rate of TEAE, grade 3+ TEAE, neutropenia, and thrombocytopenia were high in both age groups. Grade 3+ TEAE were seen more commonly in the older age group, but only one patient on the placebo arm in the younger age cohort experienced a grade 3+ TEAE, such that the risk difference was much higher for younger patients (+78) versus older patients (+45). Hepatic disorders were seen more commonly in younger patients and only slightly more commonly in imetelstat than in placebo in older patients. Additional TEAE observed more commonly compared to placebo in younger patients (risk difference &gt;5 percent units) included infections, arthralgia/myalgia, COVID-19, nausea, pruritus, leukopenia, and hemorrhage. Among older patients, the rate of TEAE was higher in imetelstat for infections, arthralgia/myalgia, leukopenia, hemorrhage, fatigue, syncope, sepsis, and atrial fibrillation.
L2509: Neutropenia and infections were more common in imetelstat than in placebo in both younger and older patients. Of note, infections were not observed in the placebo arm for younger patients and in 33% of patients in the imetelstat arm. Although the total number of infections was higher in older patients, the difference between study arms was less than in younger patients. The finding was supported by high rates of neutropenia and infection in patients on the phase 2 portion of the study, where 8/14 (57%) of patients had neutropenia or infections.
L2510: Similarly, thrombocytopenia was high in both younger and older patients, but the difference was +10 percent units in younger patients compared to older patients. Hemorrhage was more common (&gt;5 percent units) in both younger and older patients.
L2511: Hepatic disorders, including transaminase and other laboratory abnormalities, were more commonly seen in younger patients and a large difference between study arms was seen in younger, but not older, patients.
L2512: In general, the results of the phase 2 component of the study supported the results of the phase 3 portion, with younger patients experiencing high rates of cytopenias, infections, hemorrhage, and hepatic disorders.
L2513: *Clinical Reviewer comments: No major increase in risk was observed for older patients. However, major risks such as overall TEAE, grade 3+ TEAE, neutropenia, infections, thrombocytopenia, hemorrhage, and hepatic toxicity were seen commonly in both older and younger patients. Strikingly, no infections were reported in younger patients receiving placebo while 33% of younger patients experienced at least one documented infection, highlighting the risk of neutropenia. In addition, only one of the patients experienced grade 3+ TEAE whereas almost 90% receiving imetelstat experienced at least one grade 3+ TEAE.*
L2514: ## Race and Ethnicity
L2515: TEAE rates were examined by race and ethnicity.
L2516: A total of 12 patients were identified as of Hispanic or Latino ethnicity, including one on the phase 2 portion of the study, 6 on the imetelstat arm of phase 3, and 5 on the placebo arm. In general, no major differences were seen compared with the overall population or with the subpopulation identified as non-Hispanic, with the exception of fatigue, which was notably high in the Hispanic population (50%), but given the small number of patients, no conclusions can be drawn from this finding.
L2517: A total of 4 patients identified as African American or black (1 in each imetelstat arm, two in placebo). This was considered too small a population to be able to draw any conclusions so the results from this analysis will not be shown.
L2518: Twelve patients were identified as Asian, including two in the phase 2 portion of the study, 8 on the imetelstat arm of the phase 3 portion, and 2 on the placebo arm. Hepatic disorders were notably common in the imetelstat arms including 1 out of 2 (50%) of patients on the phase 2 portion, 5 out of 8 (63%) on the imetelstat arm of the phase 3 portion, and no patient on placebo. However, again, the number of patients is too small to draw definitive conclusions.
L2519: *Clinical Reviewer comments: The number of patients in traditionally underrepresented ethnic and racial groups are too low to draw any conclusions regarding the safety of imetelstat in these populations.*
L2520: ## 8.2.8. **Specific Safety Studies/Clinical Trials**
L2521: ## Data:
L2522: Not applicable
L2523: ## The Applicant's Position:
L2524: A randomized double-blinded placebo-controlled clinical ventricular repolarization/QTc substudy is ongoing in subjects with MDS under Study MDS3001. Analyses of ECG parameters and the relationship between imetelstat exposure and the QT/QTc interval will be conducted on approximately 45 LR MDS subjects. Results will be provided upon completion of the study with an integrated risk assessment of nonclinical and clinical data to fully characterize the proarrhythmic potential of imetelstat.
L2525: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L2526: No data are available at this time from the ventricular repolarization/QTc substudy.
L2527: ## 8.2.9. Additional Safety Explorations
L2528: ## Human Carcinogenicity or Tumor Development
L2529: ## **Data:**
L2530: Not applicable.
L2531: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L2532: Refer to Section 5.5.3 Carcinogenicity, in Nonclinical Pharmacology / Toxicology, for information on the potential carcinogenicity of imetelstat.
L2533: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L2534: A higher rate of events in the SOC “neoplasms, benign, malignant and unspecified” was observed in the imetelstat arm of the phase 3 portion of MDS3001 compared to the placebo arm. In addition, the rate of events in this SOC was higher in the phase 2 portion.

L2541: Events in the neoplasms SOC were more common in the imetelstat arm of MDS3001. Although not directly comparable, the ISS showed a higher number of events in the standard and high dose groups compared to placebo or to the low dose group. However, no single malignancy dominated. Neoplasms observed included myelofibrosis, acute myeloid leukemia, lymphoma, and a wide variety of solid tumors.
L2542: TRADENAME, imetelstat
L2543: **Clinical Reviewer comments:** Overall, there is not strong clinical evidence of carcinogenicity for imetelstat. However, there is a small increase in the overall incidence of malignancies in patients who received imetelstat in the phase 3 portion of MDS3001 and the number of events appears to be increasing over time (i.e., more events in the 120-day follow up in the imetelstat arm but not in the placebo arm.) Longer follow up of the outcomes of patients treated with imetelstat may help resolve this issue.
L2544: ## Human Reproduction and Pregnancy
L2545: ## Data:
L2546: There are no available clinical data on the use of imetelstat in pregnant women. In embryo-fetal development toxicity studies, imetelstat was not teratogenic. However, administration of imetelstat to pregnant mice and rabbits during the period of organogenesis resulted in embryo-lethal effects at maternal exposures that ranged from approximately 2.5-times and 19.2-times, respectively, the human exposure at the recommended clinical dose.
L2547: ## The Applicant's Position:
L2548: There are no data available for imetelstat in pregnant women. Females of reproductive potential should be advised of the potential risk of miscarriage and to use effective contraception for at least 1 week after the final dose of treatment with imetelstat.
L2549: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L2550: We agree that no pregnancies were reported in the data provided by the Applicant and that therefore no conclusions can be drawn based on clinical data. Also see review of non-clinical data.
L2551: ## Pediatrics and Assessment of Effects on Growth
L2552: ## Data:
L2553: Not applicable.
L2554: ## The Applicant's Position:
L2555: There are limited studies of imetelstat in pediatric patients. A request for deferral for the initiation of pediatric studies was included in the NDA submission. In the agreed amended iPSP (FDA letter dated 14 March 2023), the proposed pediatric indication is for treatment, in combination with fludarabine and cytarabine, of pediatric patients with relapsed or refractory AML or MDS, including juvenile myelomonocytic leukemia (JMML), from &gt;1 year to ≤18 years of age.
L2556: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L2557: Agree that there is no data on pediatric patients at this time. The age of the youngest patient in the ISS was reported as 21 years of age. However, we note that a phase 2 study of imetelstat in
L2558: children with recurrent or refractory nervous system malignancies has been published (Salloum 2016). Notable AE reported from this study include neutropenia, thrombocytopenia, transaminase elevations, and CNS hemorrhage, including two fatal events. No specific issues with growth or development were reported.
L2559: ## Overdose, Drug Abuse Potential, Withdrawal, and Rebound
L2560: ## **Data:**
L2561: Not applicable.
L2562: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L2563: There is no clinical experience with overdose with imetelstat.
L2564: Imetelstat has no pharmacological properties that would promote its use for abuse or misuse for illegal purposes and no drug abuse or dependence has been observed in the clinical studies.
L2565: There is no evidence of withdrawal or rebound associated with imetelstat discontinuation in the clinical studies.
L2566: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L2567: No overdoses were reported in study MDS3001. A trend towards more frequent AE appeared to be seen in the ISS, suggesting that overdose may pose a significant risk. There were no reports of withdrawal or rebound in MDS3001. It is unlikely based on mechanism of action, use, primary effects, and adverse events profile that imetelstat has a high risk of abuse. Drugs that increase hemoglobin have some risk of being abused as performance enhancing drugs.
L2568: However, since imetelstat appears to act on the malignant clone in MDS, it is unlikely that it would be misused in this manner.
L2569: ## 8.2.10. Safety in the Postmarket Setting
L2570: ## Safety Concerns Identified Through Postmarket Experience
L2571: ## **Data:**
L2572: Not applicable.
L2573: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L2574: There is no postmarketing experience with imetelstat.
L2575: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L2576: FDA agrees that there is no postmarketing experience with imetelstat.
L2577: ## Expectations on Safety in the Postmarket Setting
L2578: ## **Data:**
L2579: The most commonly reported TEAEs in the MDS3001 Phase 3 imetelstat group were thrombocytopenia (75.4%) and neutropenia (73.7%), and the most commonly reported TEAEs related to imetelstat were thrombocytopenia (67.8%) and neutropenia (64.4%).
L2580: The most commonly reported Grade 3/4 TEAEs in the imetelstat group were neutropenia (67.8%) and thrombocytopenia (61.9%), and the most commonly reported Grade 3/4 TEAEs related to imetelstat were neutropenia (58.5%) and thrombocytopenia (54.2%). The majority of Grade 3/4 cytopenia resolved in &lt; 4 weeks, and decreased in frequency over time.
L2581: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L2582: The most common adverse reactions were reversible cytopenias (neutropenia and thrombocytopenia), consistent with the mechanism of action and on-target effect of imetelstat, but with minimal clinical sequelae as shown by a low incidence of serious bleeding events and infections. Cytopenias were generally Grade 3/4.
L2583: Cytopenias occurred more frequently during the first few cycles and were of short duration. These events were manageable with cycle delays and dose reductions for thrombocytopenia, and with dose modifications and growth factor support for neutropenia.
L2584: These risks can be effectively managed by health care providers and with information provided in the Prescribing Information (USPI).
L2585: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L2586: FDA agrees that it is likely that severe and potentially life-threatening neutropenia and thrombocytopenia will be seen in the post-marketing setting. We are concerned that in the absence of intense monitoring and rapid response to these issues provided in the clinical trial setting the risk of hemorrhagic events and infection may be higher than expected based on the trial results. The need for careful monitoring of blood counts and prompt response, including dose modification, if severe neutropenia or thrombocytopenia are seen will ameliorate this risk.
L2587: In addition, infusion related reaction is expected to occur as frequently or more frequently in the post-marketing setting as in the clinical trial setting. Guidance for pre-medication will be included in the label to reduce this risk.
L2588: ## 8.2.11. **Integrated Assessment of Safety**
L2589: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L2590: The safety profile of imetelstat has been well characterized and is consistent across the safety data pooled from MDS and hematological malignancies. LR MDS is a serious and life-threatening disease, and imetelstat treatment in these subjects demonstrated manageable and acceptable adverse events.
L2591: The median duration of treatment with imetelstat was 33.93 weeks and ~50% of subjects in the imetelstat group completed 48 weeks of treatment, indicating that imetelstat at the proposed 7.5 mg/g dose was tolerable.
L2592: The adverse event profile of imetelstat is characterized by hematological toxicities specifically thrombocytopenia and neutropenia, which were also the most frequently occurring Grade 3/4 TEAEs. Neutropenia and thrombocytopenia with imetelstat treatment are, in part, an indicator of on-target activity and are also common events in MDS. Despite being common, few events of thrombocytopenia and/or neutropenia led to discontinuation of study therapy, and no fatal cytopenia events occurred in the pivotal study.
L2593: Grade 3/4 thrombocytopenia and neutropenia events were short lived (median duration &lt; 2 weeks) and &gt; 80% of cases resolved to Grade \le 2 in under 4 weeks. The number of severe (Grade 3/4) bleeding events and events of febrile neutropenia, or severe infections, was low and generally assessed by the investigator as not related to imetelstat treatment. No severe bleeding events were associated with severe thrombocytopenia and severe infections were generally not associated with severe neutropenia.
L2594: Management of thrombocytopenia and neutropenia using regular monitoring of blood cell counts and dose modifications as well as monitoring for the clinical consequences (bleeding and infection) are included in Section 5 Warning and Precautions and Section 2 Dose modifications of the US USPI. These events will also be monitored with routine post-marketing pharmacovigilance.
L2595: Infusion-related reactions are also included under Section 5 (Warning and Precautions) of the USPI. IRRs were generally mild and premedication with hydrocortisone and diphenhydramine is required under Section 2.2 of the USPI.
L2596: Identified ADRs for imetelstat include thrombocytopenia, neutropenia, AST increased, ALP increased, asthenia, headache, arthralgia, infusion related reactions, pruritus, epistaxis, and hematoma.
L2597: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L2598: There are several safety concerns with the use of imetelstat in the setting of lower risk MDS.
L2599: The most serious concerns are the increased number of deaths on study in the imetelstat arm and the risk of neutropenia and thrombocytopenia and accompanying risks of infection and hemorrhage.
L2600: The number of deaths was higher on the imetelstat arm at all timepoints evaluated. Although the differences were not statistically significant and the majority of the deaths were &gt;30 days
L2601: post-treatment and often years post-treatment, the consistent trend is concerning. At minimum, harm cannot be ruled out.
L2602: Grade 3-4 neutropenia was seen in most patients receiving imetelstat on either portion of the study. Although deaths on study were rare, one death due to sepsis was seen in the phase 3 portion and another due to COVID-19 in the phase 2 portion of the study. In addition, the possibility of ongoing neutropenia after the end of treatment cannot be ruled out and thus deaths occurring &gt;30 days after the end of treatment may be related, as noted in section 8.2.4. Use of neutrophil growth factor support and antibiotics was more common in the imetelstat arm. Despite this, infections were more common in the imetelstat arm of the phase 3 portion of the study compared to placebo. Although not directly comparable, there was a higher risk of infection in the phase 2 portion than was seen in either arm of the phase 3 portion. This could be related to different baseline populations or to increased experience with the risk of cytopenias and more aggressive prophylaxis and treatment in the phase 3 portion of the study.
L2603: Similarly, grade 3-4 thrombocytopenia was observed frequently in patients treated with imetelstat and hemorrhagic events were more common in patients treated with imetelstat compared to placebo. No confirmed fatal hemorrhagic events were reported, although the narrative of one death in the phase 2 component was suspicious for intracranial hemorrhage and grade 4 hemorrhagic events were reported. Patients on the imetelstat arm were more likely to receive platelet transfusions and aggressive management of thrombocytopenia, which may have reduced the risk of bleeding during the study. However, platelet transfusion holds risks, including the risk of transfusion reaction and antibody formation. The risks associated with more platelet transfusions must be balanced against the benefit of fewer red blood cell transfusions.
L2604: Other events that occurred more frequently in the imetelstat arm included hepatotoxicity, fatigue, arthralgia/myalgia, fractures, infusion related reaction, PTT prolongation, and headache. The effect of imetelstat on cardiac function was unclear: no increase in risk of arterial occlusive disease was observed with imetelstat, but rates of atrial fibrillation and hypertension were higher. In addition, more grade 3+ cardiac failure was seen in the imetelstat arm, although the total rate of cardiac failure was not elevated.
L2605: In summary, there are significant risks to the use of imetelstat in patients with lower risk MDS. These risks must be considered carefully in the context of any benefit observed in terms of red blood cell transfusion reduction.
L2606: ## SUMMARY AND CONCLUSIONS
L2607: ## 8.3. Statistical Issues
L2608: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L2609: ## **Overall Survival: Harm Cannot be Ruled Out**
L2610: The latest estimate (5 January 2024) of the overall survival hazard ratio was 0.98 (95% CI: 0.53, 1.82) based on the Cox proportional hazards model with stratification factors and 1.11 (95% CI: 0.61, 2.03) based on the model without stratification factors. Both point estimates are close to 1 and the upper bounds of the confidence intervals are close to 2, suggesting that potential harm cannot be ruled out.
L2611: Given the patients still remaining on study, at most 55 events could be observed in the imetelstat arm and at most 26 events could be observed in the placebo arm. Per protocol, patients “will be followed in the Extension Phase for at least 5 years from the first dose of imetelstat received in Part 2 of the main study (including treatment and follow-up), or 3 years of post-treatment follow-up from the last dose of study treatment, whichever occurs later, or until death, withdrawal of consent, study termination, or until a subject is lost to follow-up.” Due to a limited planned follow-up, not all patients remaining on study might reach an event. Monitoring the survival status and death narratives throughout the Extension Phase will be important for continued reassessment of the potential harm to patients.
L2612: ## **Frequency and Duration of Cytopenia**
L2613: There are several ways to estimate frequency and duration of cytopenia. These outcomes are inherently complex to describe with a single summary because not all patients will experience cytopenia, and those who will, may experience multiple instances of varying duration. FDA has conducted various time-to-event analyses, including mean cumulative function, among others (see Section 8.2.5 *Analysis of Submission-Specific Safety Issues*). Based on these additional analyses, our conclusions regarding safety remain consistent with the other results, indicating potential increased cytopenia risk for patients on the imetelstat treatment.
L2614: ## **Residual Uncertainty on the Applicability to the US Population**
L2615: In the US population of Study MDS3001 (Phase 3), one out of eight patients (12.5%, 95% CI: 0.3%, 52.7%), achieved 8-week RBC-TI in the imetelstat group and none out of five patients (0%, 95% CI: 0%, 52.2%), achieved 8-week RBC-TI in the placebo group. No definitive conclusions can be drawn due to the small sample size of the subgroups.
L2616: Potential impact of region-specific patient or treatment-related factors cannot be ruled out. Residual uncertainty regarding applicability of the efficacy results to the US population remains.
L2617: ## 8.4. Conclusions and Recommendations
L2618: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L2619: The Applicant is seeking approval of imetelstat for the treatment of transfusion-dependent anemia in adult patients with low- to intermediate-1 risk MDS who have not responded to, or have lost response to, or are ineligible for ESA. This application is based primarily on the results of Study MDS3001-Phase 3. Study MDS3001-Phase 3 met the statistical goals for the primary endpoint of 8-week RBC-TI (39.8% imetelstat versus 15.0% placebo; p &lt; 0.001) and key secondary endpoint 24-week RBC-TI (28.0% imetelstat versus 3.3% placebo; p &lt; 0.001). Although the median duration of RBC-TI was only 5.0 weeks (95% CI 4.0, 7.7) for imetelstat versus 3.9 weeks (95% CI 3.6, 4.0) for placebo in all subjects, the median duration of RBC-TI in 8-week RBC-TI responders was 51.6 weeks (95% CI 26.9, 83.9) for imetelstat versus 13.3 weeks (95% 8.0, 24.9) for placebo, indicating that a subset of patients has a clinically meaningful duration of benefit. The Phase 2 results for 8-week and 24-week RBC-TI support the Phase 3 results.
L2620: While MDS3001-Phase 3 met its primary and key secondary endpoints, questions remain regarding the nature of these effects. Imetelstat is purported to have a direct effect on the underlying MDS through telomerase inhibition resulting in cell-cycle arrest, apoptosis, or senescence of malignant cells. However, a statistically significant treatment effect was not observed on the key secondary endpoint of HI-E per IWG 2006 criteria (63.6% vs. 51.7%) or other secondary endpoints reflective of a disease-modifying effect such as CR or PR (0% vs. 0% at the time of primary analysis; 5.9% at best vs. 0% at the time of updated analysis) and OS (HR=0.98 [95% CI: 0.53, 1.82] at most recent data cut). In addition, the patient-reported outcomes collected in Study MDS3001-Phase 3 did not reflect an improvement in fatigue or other anemia-related symptoms. There are also residual uncertainties regarding the impact of imetelstat on overall medical resource utilization and the applicability of the trial results to the US population, given the limitations of the medical resource utilization analyses and lower RBC-TI rates in the US population compared to the non-US population. Additionally, very few patients in Study MDS3001 had prior treatment with luspatercept, which is now approved for frontline treatment of transfusion-dependent anemia in patients with lower-risk MDS.
L2621: A high rate of cytopenias was observed in the imetelstat arm of MDS3001-Phase 3. Such cytopenias may require further management and elevate a patient's risk for infection or bleeding. The incidence of at least one Grade 3-4 neutropenia event was 71% in the imetelstat arm and 7% in the placebo arm. Myeloid growth factors were required in 36% of patients in the imetelstat arm compared to 3% in the placebo arm. Similarly, the incidence of at least one Grade 3-4 thrombocytopenia event was 65% in the imetelstat arm and 8% in the placebo arm. Platelet transfusions were required in 18% of patients in the imetelstat arm compared to 2% in the placebo arm. It is worth noting that cytopenias occurred regardless of whether a patient responded to imetelstat or not. As most patients do not respond to imetelstat, there is a
L2622: substantial risk of exposing patients to toxicity with no durable RBC-TI. In contrast, worsening Grade \ge 3 neutropenia occurred in 7% of patients on the placebo arm, as did worsening Grade \ge 3 thrombocytopenia in 8% of patients.
L2623: Additional potential risks with imetelstat treatment were identified in MDS3001-Phase 3, particularly events related to cytopenia events. For instance, at least one occurrence of any all-grade infection was higher in the imetelstat arm than the placebo arm (47% vs. 34%). Grade 3-4 infections was similar between the two arms (11% vs. 14%). In addition, at least one occurrence of any all-grade hemorrhage was higher in the imetelstat arm than the placebo arm (22% vs. 12%). Grade 3-4 hemorrhage was similar between the two arms (2.5% vs. 1.7%). Other toxicity signals were identified in MDS3001-Phase 3, such as an increased incidence of at least one any grade fatigue event, although this signal is not reflected in patient-reported outcomes.
L2624: The potential benefits and risks should be weighed in the context of residual uncertainties. For instance, there is residual uncertainty regarding the optimal dose of imetelstat given the limited dose exploration in the target population and high rate of dose modifications observed in Study MDS3001. This is notable given that dose reductions due to AE occurred in 49% of patients in the imetelstat arm vs. 7% in the placebo arm. In addition, while the increased risk of cytopenias with imetelstat treatment seems apparent, other risks such as fractures may be increased with imetelstat treatment, though such risks are less well-understood. These uncertainties are due in part to the nature of the development program, which includes only one randomized trial in LR-MDS and limited dose-optimization for this indication.
L2625: Overall, treatment with imetelstat is associated with risks that might be considered substantial. Therefore, FDA held an Oncologic Drugs Advisory Committee (ODAC) meeting on 14 March 2024 for discussion of whether the benefits of imetelstat outweigh the risks for the proposed indication. The AC voted that the potential benefits of imetelstat outweigh the risks. Given this, in addition to the high unmet need for the proposed patient population, the Agency has concluded that the benefits of imetelstat outweigh the risks for the treatment of adult patients with IPSS low- to intermediate-1 risk MDS with transfusion-dependent anemia requiring 4 or more red blood cell units over 8 weeks who have not responded to or have lost response to or are ineligible for ESA. FDA recommends granting regular approval to imetelstat for this indication. PMR/PMC recommendations are noted in the “Postmarketing Requirements and Commitments” section below.

L2642: ## 9 Advisory Committee Meeting and Other External Consultations
L2643: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L2644: Summary provided by Division of Advisory Committee and Consultant Management (DACCM) with edits for clarity.
L2645: NDA 217779 was discussed by the Oncologic Drugs Advisory Committee (ODAC) on 3/14/2024. There was 1 discussion question and 1 voting question.
L2646: Collectively, the Committee members agreed that Study MDS3001 met the statistical goals for the primary endpoint of achievement of red blood cell transfusion independence (RBC-TI) for 8 weeks and the secondary endpoint of RBC-TI for 24 weeks. When it comes to overall safety, the Committee acknowledged the occurrences of cytopenia shown in the data but expressed that these toxicities are not foreign in the population studied and can be well controlled in a real-world setting.
L2647: Some members noted the lack of measurable outcomes in the study. They suggested for the applicant to establish a true surrogate, in further studies, so a measurable quality of life (QOL) benefit can be captured in the population studied. Other members noted that transfusion independence (TI) is a QOL metric.
L2648: The Committee highlighted the current challenges of MDS patients and the limited options available to manage the disease. The members pointed out that avoiding transfusion as long as possible reduces toxicity and cost, which are considered meaningful outcomes in the MDS community. Although the data did not show any markers of overall survival or imetelstat preventing the transitioning of MDS to acute myelogenous leukemia, the Committee agreed that the possibility of achieving TI up to 6 months is remarkable to the population affected.
L2649: The voting question was “Do the results of imetelstat outweigh its risks for the treatment of transfusion-dependent anemia in adult patients with IPSS low- to intermediate-risk MDS who have not responded to or have lost response to or are ineligible for erythropoiesis-stimulating agents?”
L2650: Vote Result: Yes: 12 No: 2 Abstain: 0
L2651: The majority of the Committee voted “Yes”, that imetelstat’s benefits outweigh its risks for the treatment of transfusion dependent anemia in adult patients with International Prognosis Scoring System low- to intermediate-1 risk MDS who have not responded to or have lost response to or are ineligible for erythropoiesis-stimulating agents.
L2652: The Committee members who voted “Yes”, agreed that the study met its primary and secondary endpoints. Although the Committee acknowledged the severity of the side effects observed from imetelstat in the study, the members discussed that these episodes can be well-controlled in a real world setting as the intended population is already closely monitored by specialists who are familiar with neutropenia and thrombocytopenia. One member stated that hematologists are no strangers to neutropenia, and although neutropenia cannot be mitigated, to have an option where patients can potentially benefit is promising in the management of MDS. Another member expressed that the long-term consequences of transfusion dependence are debilitating enough, and the demonstration shown of imetelstat’s ability to reduce the number of transfusions is “impressive.”
L2653: Two Committee members voted “No”, that the data shown did not convince them of imetelstat’s benefits (RBC-TI for eight weeks) outweighing it risks (i.e., cytopenia). Although there was an agreement that the primary and secondary endpoints were met, these members were not persuaded by the trial’s outcome relative to the safety profile observed. Please see the transcript for details of the Committee’s discussion.

L2656: Geron has an agreed amended iPS (FDA letter dated 14 March 2023). A request for waiver to exclude the youngest subset of pediatric patients, from birth to 1 year of age, based on the rare incidence of myeloid malignancies (AML, MDS, and JMML) in this subset was submitted with the NDA submission. The agreed iPS included 2 studies to provide an assessment of PK, safety, and preliminary efficacy in pediatric patients with relapsed/refractory acute myeloid leukemia, myelodysplastic syndrome, or juvenile myelomonocytic leukemia treated in combination with chemotherapy. L2689: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L2690: Draft labeling submitted as part of the NDA describes the indication, usage, contraindications, warnings and precautions, adverse reactions, use in specific populations, clinical pharmacology, and clinical efficacy. The data presented in the IMerge (MDS3001) Phase 3 study in patients with low- to intermediate-1 risk myelodysplastic syndromes (MDS) who have transfusion-dependent anemia and have failed to respond or have lost response to or are ineligible for
L2691: NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 217779 imetelstat
L2692: TRADENAME, imetelstat
L2693: erythropoiesis-stimulating agents (ESA) demonstrate the favorable benefit risk of imetelstat and support the proposed indication in this high unmet need population.
L2694: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L2695: FDA modified the USPI as described in the table above; additionally, the applicant proposed a Patient Package Insert (PPI) but the FDA determined that a Medication Guide would be required. The Medication Guide was reviewed by the patient labeling team within the Division of Medical Policy Programs; see separate review in DARRTS for details. In general, the information contained within the Medication Guide aligns with information in the USPI.

L2699: ## 13 Postmarketing Requirements and Commitment
L2700: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L2701: A total of 6 PMRs and one post-marketing commitment (PMC) are planned for this application.
L2702: PMR #1: Pediatric study(ies) to be conducted under the FDARA amendment to PREA. A clinical trial to examine the safety, tolerability, pharmacokinetics, pharmacodynamics, and preliminary efficacy of imetelstat in combination with chemotherapy in pediatric patients &gt; 1 year old with acute myeloid leukemia (AML) or myelodysplastic syndromes (MDS) will be required.
L2703: PMRs #2 and #3: Carcinogenicity studies in rats and mice, respectively. Because of the potential for prolonged survival in the proposed patient population, the risk of carcinogenicity must be characterized.
L2704: PMR #4: Long-term safety of imetelstat in Study MDS3001-Phase 3. Because the long-term safety of imetelstat is unknown, extended follow-up of Study MDS3001 to establish safety of long-term treatment with imetelstat in patients with low or intermediate risk MDS is required, including the risk of neutropenia, thrombocytopenia, infections, and bleeding.
L2705: PMR #5: Randomized trial of at least two dosages of imetelstat in patients with lower risk MDS. Dosages lower than the proposed recommended dosage, 7.1 mg/kg every 4 weeks as a 2-hour infusion, have not been evaluated in patients with low or intermediate-1 risk myelodysplastic syndromes (LR-MDS). A randomized trial comparing at least 2 dosages of imetelstat in patients with LR-MDS will be required to potentially minimize the serious risks of imetelstat treatment including neutropenia and thrombocytopenia and other serious adverse events and improve tolerability while maintaining clinical benefit to patients. Additionally, given that the duration of neutropenia and thrombocytopenia after treatment discontinuation with imetelstat is unknown, extended follow up of patients in this trial to evaluate the effects of imetelstat on myelosuppression after treatment discontinuation is warranted. The study should enroll sufficient patients in the United States to establish efficacy and safety in relevant ethnic and racial populations in the US.
L2706: PMR #6: Evaluation of the risk of QT prolongation through completion of the ventricular repolarization substudy of MDS3001 Phase 3.
L2707: PMC #1: Exploratory analyses to identify markers that may predict response to imetelstat to achieve transfusion independence. A small number of patients receiving imetelstat experienced long-term transfusion independence, however predictors of response are currently unknown.
L2708: TRADENAME, imetelstat

L2859: ## 19.4. OCP Appendices (Technical documents supporting OCP recommendations)
L2860: ## 19.4.1. Population PK Analysis
L2861: ## 19.4.1.1. Executive Summary
L2862: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L2863: In general, the applicant's population PK (popPK) analysis is considered acceptable for the purpose of characterizing the PK profile of imetelstat in adult patients with hematologic malignancies or solid tumors. The applicant's analyses were verified by the reviewer, with no significant discordance identified. More specifically, the model was used to support the current submission as outlined in Table 111.
L2864: **Table 111:** Specific Comments on Applicant's Final Population PK model
L2865: Utility of the final model	Reviewer's Comments
Support applicant's proposed labeling statements about intrinsic and extrinsic factors	Intrinsic factor	No clinically significant differences in the PK of imetelstat were observed based on age (21 to 87 years), sex, race, mild to moderate renal impairment (CrCL 30 to < 90 mL/min), mild hepatic impairment (total bilirubin ≤ ULN and AST > ULN, or total bilirubin > 1x to 1.5x ULN and any AST), or moderate hepatic impairment (total bilirubin > 1.5x to 3x ULN and any AST). The PK of imetelstat have not been studied in patients with severe renal impairment (CrCL 15 and < 30 mL/min), end-stage renal disease, or severe
The statement is acceptable. The applicant included 14 severe renal impairment patients (minimum CrCL: 25 mL/min; N=7 in MDS patient), and 4 severe hepatic impairment patient (N=3 in MDS patient) in the popPK dataset. No significant effect was observed for these patients with severe renal impairment or hepatic impairment based on PopPK analysis. A population size of N=4 for severe hepatic
L2866: TRADENAME, imetelstat
L2867: hepatic impairment (total bilirubin > 3x ULN and any AST).	impairment may not be enough subjects to draw reliable conclusion.

To derive individual exposure parameters to be used for the E-R analyses, including Cycle 1 C_{max}, Cycle 1 AUC, and C_{avg} up to the time of the event

Final Model	Summary	Acceptability [FDA's comments]
Software and Version	NONMEM® program version 7.4 (ICON Development Solutions, Ellicott City, MD); Pirana version 2.9.8 (Certara, Princeton, NJ); Visual predictive checks (VPCs) were conducted using R version 4.1.3 (http://www.r-project.org).	Acceptable
Model Structure	2-compartment TMDD model with saturable binding/distribution and dose- and time-dependent elimination from the central compartment (Applicant Figure 41).	Acceptable
L2875: TRADENAME, imetelstat
L2876: The model was parameterized in terms of CL, V_c, transfer rate from the peripheral to the central compartment (K_{back}), total concentration of target (B_{max}), binding rate constant (K_{on}), dissociation rate constant (K_{off}), and internalization rate constant (K_{int}).
Theory based allometric exponents of 0.75 for CL, 1 for V_c, and -0.25 from K_{back} were applied to body weight to account for the effect of body size.
Interindividual variability was included on CL, V_c, B_{max}, and residual variability.

Model Parameter Estimates	See Applicant Table 114	Acceptable
Uncertainty and Variability (RSE, IIV, Shrinkage, Bootstrap)	
All fixed effects were estimated with sufficient precision. Effectively, relative standard errors (RSE) were lower than 11% for system related parameters. Condition number of the final model was 23.9, which is substantially less than 1000, supporting the fact that the popPK model developed is unlikely to be overparametrized (Bonate, 2012).
Residual variability was quantified to be 22.2%. All random effects were also estimated with sufficient precision with RSE lower than 10%.
The Applicant updated their PopPK model based on Applicant IR response to the information request sent on Apr. 15, 2014.
The correct residual variability based on new model should be 21.8%.

BLQ for Parameter Accuracy	BLQs were retained in the dataset and were treated using the M3 method (Beal, 2001; Ahn, 2008).	Acceptable
GOF, VPC	Applicant Figure 42, Applicant Figure 43, Applicant Figure 44	Acceptable
Significant Covariates and Clinical Relevance

Special populations
No clinically significant differences in the PK of imetelstat were observed based on age (21 to 87 years), sex, race, mild to moderate renal impairment (CrCL 30 to < 90 mL/min), mild hepatic impairment (total bilirubin \le ULN and AST > ULN, or total bilirubin > 1x to 1.5x ULN and any AST), or moderate hepatic impairment (total bilirubin > 1.5x to 3x ULN and any AST). The PK of imetelstat have not been studied in patients with severe renal impairment (CrCL 15 and < 30 mL/min), end-stage renal disease, or severe hepatic impairment (total bilirubin > 3x ULN and any AST).
However, per applicant IR response to the information request sent on Apr. 15, 2014, the correct molecular weight should be imetelstat sodium salt (4896 g/mol) given that the PK analyte is imetelstat sodium. The Applicant is asked to update the final PopPK model and associated analysis plot using the correct DV2 and LDV2. The reviewer has reviewed the updated PopPK model and deemed it acceptable.

L2936: The PopPK analysis included pooled data from seven studies comprising 424 subjects with multiple myeloma (MM), chronic lymphoproliferative disease (CLD), solid tumor malignancies, essential thrombocythemia (ET), polycythemia vera (PV), myelofibrosis (MF), or myelodysplastic syndrome (MDS). Brief descriptions of the study included are presented in Table 116.

L2941: Source: Table adapted from Appendix Table 1 in Population Pharmacokinetic and Exposure-Response Analyses for Imetelstat in Patients with Hematologic Malignancies or Solid Tumors
L2942: NONMEM 7.4 and Pirana version 2.9.8 was used for PopPK modeling. R (version 4.1.3) were used for exploratory and post-modeling analyses. BLQ were retained in the dataset and were treated using the M3 method. Applicant Table 112 provides summary statistics of the baseline demographic covariates in the analysis dataset.
L2943: ## Base model
L2944: The PK of imetelstat was best characterized with a 2-compartment TMDD model with saturable binding. Models were estimated using a sequential process that implied ITS, SAEM, and IMP (with EONLY=1 option) algorithms. The structural model is illustrated in Applicant Figure 41
L2945: ## Covariate analysis
L2946: Covariates were evaluated using a forward additional following by backward elimination steps. Significant (p value \le 0.01 in likelihood ratio test and/or improvement in diagnostic plots) covariates identified in the forward addition steps are: the effect of spleen volume and myelofibrosis malignancy on Bmax (total target concentration); the effect of time-variant ADA, dose, myelofibrosis malignancy, sex, time, CrCL on CL; the effect of sex and multiple myeloma malignancy on Vc. The backward elimination step used a more stringent criteria (p value \le 0.001 in likelihood ratio test), the effect of time-variant ADA and CrCL on CL were dropped off from the model.
L2947: ## Final Model
L2948: The parameter estimates for the final PopPK models are listed in Applicant Table 114. The goodness-of-fit (GOF) plot for the final PopPK model are shown in Applicant Figure 42. The visual predictive check (VPC) plot for the final PopPK model is shown in Applicant Figure 43 and Applicant Figure 44.
L2949: ## PPK Review Issues
L2950: The Applicant's original PopPK model was developed based on DV2 and LDV2 calculated using the molecular weight of imetelstat free acid (4610 g/mol). However, per applicant IR response to the information request sent on Apr. 15, 2014, the correct molecular weight should be imetelstat sodium salt (4896 g/mol) given that the PK analyte is imetelstat sodium. The Applicant is asked to update the final PopPK model and associated analysis plot using the
L2951: correct DV2 and LDV2. The reviewer has reviewed the updated PopPK model and deemed it acceptable. The updated PopPK estimates are similar to original model. Overall, the incorrect calculation based on wrong molecular weight will not cause significant impact to the review outcome.
L2952: The reviewer deems the updated model provides a good description of imetelstat PK data. Based on the PopPK model, the effect of time on clearance is modeled with Emax equation (see below) and the T_{50} is estimated to be 6190 hours, or approximately 9 cycles of treatment.
L2953: \text{Time effect} = 1 - \frac{\text{Time}}{T_{50} + \text{Time}}
L2954: Because of large T_{50} estimates, the time dependency of CL is very small and not significant. The impact of covariates included in the final PPK model was evaluated using forest plots of relative changes in steady state exposure using a male subject weighing 70 kg with MDS malignancy type receiving 7.5 mg/kg imetelstat as reference (Applicant Figure 48). Based on the analysis, dose and MF malignancy type can impact imetelstat exposure greater than 80-125% range. However, the reviewer deems the estimated effect of dose on clearance mainly comes from data in other non-MDS patient population as there is only one dose (7.5 mg/kg) studied in MDS patient. In addition, based on the box plots of post-hoc ETAs versus categorical baseline covariates for final popPK model, sex, race (white vs other), hepatic and renal functions (normal, mild, moderate or severe), should not significantly impact imetelstat clearance and volume distribution (Figure 49).

L2971: ## 19.4.2. Exposure-Response Analysis
L2972: ## 19.4.2.1. ER (efficacy) Executive Summary
L2973: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L2974: The Applicant conducted ER analysis using the data from 156 patients in study #MDS3001 for ER efficacy analysis. The efficacy endpoints include: 1) Proportion of subjects with \ge 8 week red blood cell (RBC) transfusion independence (TI); and 2) Proportion of subjects with \ge24 week. The applicant's ER analysis is based on original PopPK model developed based on DV2 and LDV2 calculated using the molecular weight of imetelstat free acid (4610 g/mol). However, per
L2975: applicant IR response to the information request sent on Apr. 15, 20141, the correct molecular weight should be imetelstat sodium salt (4896 g/mol) given that the PK analyte is imetelstat sodium. The applicant is asked to update the final PopPK model and associated analysis plot using the correct DV2 and LDV2, and repeat ER analysis if significant discordancy was observed between the updated PK exposure predictions and original exposure predictions at the individual level. The applicant submitted the IR response on 4/25/2024. The reviewer has reviewed the updated PopPK model and deemed it acceptable. The updated PopPK estimates are similar to original model. In addition, there is high concordance (r^2=0.999-1.00) between exposure metrics for MDS patients derived from original and updated PopPK model. Thus, the incorrect calculation based on wrong molecular weight will not cause significant impact to the ER efficacy analysis outcome. The applicant did not repeat the ER analysis and the reviewer deems it is acceptable. Overall, the applicant's E-R analysis is considered acceptable for the purpose of characterizing the ER relationship of imetelstat and its efficacy endpoints (Table 119).
L2976: **Table 119: Specific Comments on Applicant's E-R Analysis**
L2977: Utility of the final model	Reviewer's Comments
Support applicant's proposed labeling statements about E-R Analysis	N/A	Based on ER analysis, higher imetelstat Cycle 1 Cmax is associated with higher incidence of Grade 3&4 thrombocytopenia in MDS patients. Higher imetelstat Cavg up to the event is associated with higher proportion of subjects with \ge8-week and 24-week RBC transfusion independence. However, the exposure response analyses are limited by the narrow dose/exposure range in Study MDS3001. The E-R analysis for efficacy has additional limitations due to potential confounding effect caused by dose modification and patient drop-out.

L3015: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L3016: The Applicant conducted E-R analysis for primary efficacy endpoint proportion of subjects with \ge8-week RBC TI and key secondary endpoint proportion of subjects with \ge24-week RBC TI. A total of 152 subjects with MDS were included in the analysis. E-R relationship was characterized using univariate and multivariate logistic regression models. For key primary efficacy endpoint, positive E-R relationship was identified for \log_{10} of Cavg up to the event and proportion of subjects \ge 8-Week RBC TI (Applicant Figure 50, Applicant Table 121). Similar relationship was identified using key secondary endpoint \log_{10} of Cavg up to the event and proportion of subjects \ge 24-Week RBC TI (Applicant Figure 51, Applicant Table 122). No significant E-R relationship was detected using other exposure metrics including Cmax and AUC on Cycle 1. The reviewer verified that the ER for efficacy analysis is acceptable.
L3017: ## 19.4.2.3. ER (safety) Executive Summary
L3018: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L3019: The Applicant conducted E-R analysis using the data from pooled dataset from all studies and 156 patients in study MDS3001 for ER safety analysis. The safety endpoints include: 1) Proportion of subjects with Grade 3/4 neutropenia; 2) Proportion of subjects with Grade 3/4 thrombocytopenia; 3) Proportion of subjects with resolution of first grade 3/4 neutropenia in less than 2 weeks and 4 weeks; and 4) Proportion of subjects with resolution of first grade 3/4 thrombocytopenia in less than 2 weeks and 4 weeks.
L3020: The Applicant's ER analysis is based on original PopPK model developed based on DV2 and LDV2 calculated using the molecular weight of imetelstat free acid (4610 g/mol). However, per applicant IR response to the information request sent on Apr. 15, 20142, the correct molecular weight should be imetelstat sodium salt (4896 g/mol) given that the PK analyte is imetelstat sodium. The applicant is asked to update the final PopPK model and associated analysis plot using the correct DV2 and LDV2, and repeat ER analysis if significant discordancy was observed between the updated PK exposure predictions and original exposure predictions at the individual level. The applicant submitted the IR response on 4/25/2024. The reviewer has reviewed the updated PopPK model and deemed it acceptable. The updated PopPK estimates are similar to original model. In addition, there is high concordance (r^2=0.999-1.00) between exposure metrics for MDS patients derived from original and updated PopPK model. Thus, the incorrect calculation based on wrong molecular weight will not cause significant impact to the ER safety analysis outcome. The applicant did not repeat the ER analysis and the reviewer deems it is acceptable.
L3021: Overall, the applicant's E-R analysis is considered acceptable for the purpose of characterizing the E-R relationship of imetelstat and its safety endpoints.

L3036: Overall Clinical Relevance for ER	Multivariate E-R analyses for safety using laboratory data collected for imetelstat 7.1 mg/kg q4w in MDS3001 Phase 2 and Phase 3 demonstrated no significant E-R relationship for Grade 3/4 neutropenia and a positive E-R relationship for Grade 3/4 thrombocytopenia (statistically significant for C_{max} p_{LRT}=0.0195, approaching significance for AUC p_{LRT}=0.0594). Investigations of toxicity in identified subpopulations in MDS3001 Phase 2 and Phase 3 with potentially higher imetelstat exposure showed no clear trends between incidence of Grade 3/4 thrombocytopenia and increasing body weight quartiles or between sex. These analyses support that these subpopulations are not expected to be at increased risk of Grade 3/4 thrombocytopenia, and along with low rates of high-grade bleeding events in MDS patients in Study MDS3001, support the limited clinical impact of the characterized exposure-safety relationships. Further, E-R analyses showed no significant E-R relationship between subjects recovering from Grade 3/4 neutropenia or thrombocytopenia and imetelstat exposure in MDS3001 Phase 2 and Phase 3 with more subjects recovering within 4 weeks compared to 2 weeks, supporting the 4-week schedule. L3072: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L3073: E-R relationship was characterized using univariate and multivariate logistic regression models for safety endpoints related to Grade 3/4 neutropenia and thrombocytopenia. Among all tested exposure metrics (cycle 1 AUC, cycle 1 Cmax, Cavg), positive E-R relationship was only identified between Cmax /\text{Log}_{10}\text{Cmax} and Grade 3/4 thrombocytopenia in MDS patients (Applicant Figure 54, Applicant Table 124). A positive trend for neutropenia was also observed for cycle 1 Cmax in pooled safety patient population. The reviewer verified that the ER for safety analysis is acceptable.
L3074: ## 19.4.2.5. Overall benefit-risk evaluation based on E-R analyses
L3075: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L3076: Exposure-Response (E-R) efficacy analyses support the imetelstat 7.1 mg/kg q4w dose and dosing regimen. Results from multivariate E-R efficacy analyses based on MDS3001 Phase 2 and Phase 3 demonstrated a statistically significant positive E-R relationship between imetelstat Cavg and the primary efficacy endpoint, 8-week TI rate (p_{\text{LRT}}=0.0211), and the key secondary endpoint, 24-week TI rate (p_{\text{LRT}}=0.0396). These outcomes support that imetelstat doses (and accordingly Cavg) lower than 7.1 mg/kg q4w may result in reduced clinical efficacy. Multivariate E-R analyses for safety using laboratory data collected for imetelstat 7.1 mg/kg q4w in MDS3001 Phase 2 and Phase 3 demonstrated no significant E-R relationship for Grade 3/4 neutropenia and a positive E-R relationship for Grade 3/4 thrombocytopenia (statistically significant for Cmax p_{\text{LRT}}=0.0195, approaching significance for AUC p_{\text{LRT}}=0.0594). Investigations of toxicity in identified subpopulations in MDS3001 Phase 2 and Phase 3 with potentially higher imetelstat exposure showed no clear trends between incidence of Grade 3/4 thrombocytopenia and increasing body weight quartiles or between sex. These analyses support that these subpopulations are not expected to be at increased risk of Grade 3/4 thrombocytopenia, and along with low rates of high-grade bleeding events in MDS patients in Study MDS3001, support the limited clinical impact of the characterized exposure-safety relationships. Further, E-R analyses showed no significant E-R relationship between subjects recovering from Grade 3/4 neutropenia or thrombocytopenia and imetelstat exposure in MDS3001 Phase 2 and Phase 3 with more subjects recovering within 4 weeks compared to 2 weeks, supporting the 4-week schedule. Taken together, the results from ER analyses support the favorable benefit-risk profile of the recommended dosing regimen of imetelstat 7.1 mg/kg q4w.
L3077: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L3078: Although the Applicant's ER analysis is deemed acceptable, we need to be cautious when use this ER analysis to guide benefit-risk assessment due to the following reasons: 1) only one dose level 7.5 mg/kg is tested in pivotal study in MDS patients thus the studied imetelstat exposure
L3079: range is limited; 2) frequent dose modification and subject drop off may confound the ER for efficacy analysis result. Only Cavg but not Cmax and AUC, was found associated with two efficacy endpoints in ER for efficacy analysis. The reviewer deems the ER for safety analysis can still reflect the trend despite in limited drug exposure range that the higher imetelstat Cmax is correlated with higher incidence of Grade 3/4 Thrombocytopenia.
L3080: ## 19.4.3. Mutational analyses in Study MDS3001
L3081: ## The FDA's Assessment
L3082: **Baseline mutation status and treatment response:** The Applicant conducted exploratory studies to investigate associations between mutational status in MDS-related genes at baseline and clinical response. Mutational status was examined in DNA from peripheral blood leukocytes with the LeukoVantage® MDS gene panel, which detects mutations by targeted sequencing in 36 genes that are frequently mutated in MDS. Overall, 98% of patients in the study had at least one somatic mutation at baseline, and mutation frequencies were similar in the imetelstat and placebo groups (Table 125). The observed mutation frequencies at baseline are in agreement with estimates from the literature (Ogawa 2019, Bersanelli 2021). The Applicant's exploratory analysis focused on six genes that are frequently mutated in low-risk MDS. Analysis by individual gene showed that in imetelstat-treated patients, the average 8-week RBC TI rate was 36.8% higher in patients with *SF3B1* mutations in comparison to patients without *SF3B1* mutations (p = 0.001). In patients who received placebo, the average 8-week RBC TI rate in patients with and without *SF3B1* mutations were similar. The presence of a mutation in *SF3B1* was associated with a 32.5% increase in 8-week RBC TI in comparison to the corresponding patients who received placebo. The patterns of *SF3B1* mutations in imetelstat-treated and placebo groups were similar and consistent with reports from the literature (PMID: 37007111), with the most common mutations in *SF3B1* being K700E (46%) followed by "hotspot" mutations H662 (13%), R625 (10%), and K666 (9%). *SF3B1* mutations lead to RNA splicing alterations, and there is no clear mechanistic relationship between mutant *SF3B1* and the target of imetelstat hTERT that would account for the observed difference in response rates. In both imetelstat-treated patients and placebo-treated patients, 8-week RBC TI rates were similar between patients with or without mutations in *TET2*, *ASXL1*, *DNMT3A*, *CUX1*, and *SRSF2*. In the subgroups of patients without baseline mutations in *TET2*, *ASXL1*, *DNMT3A*, *CUX1*, or *SRSF2*, 8-week RBC TI rates were higher in imetelstat-treated patients in comparison to placebo-treated patients (p&lt;0.05 for all comparisons); results were similar in the mutation positive subgroups but these comparisons were limited by the small number of patients in each of the mutation positive subgroups (Table 125). Together, this exploratory analysis suggests that baseline mutations in *SF3B1* are associated with increases in 8-week RBC TI rate in patients treated with imetelstat. However, given the exploratory nature of this analysis that included multiple
L3083: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 217779 imetelstat
## TRADENAME, imetelstat
L3084: comparisons, the lack of a mechanistic rationale, and the fact that patients without *SF3B1* mutations did respond, no clinical action is recommended based on this finding.

L3093: ## 19.4.4. Bioanalytical Methods
L3094: ## The FDA's Assessment
L3095: Imetelstat plasma concentrations were quantified using a hybridization enzyme-linked immunosorbent assay (ELISA). The method involved competition of imetelstat and a 3'-labelled digoxigenin analogue for a complementary oligonucleotide sequence containing biotin at the 3' end, with an ELISA used for detection. The resulting complex was captured onto the surface of a neutravidin-coated microtiter plate. The measurement of the digoxigenin-labelled probe was performed after reaction with anti-digoxigenin antibody conjugated to alkaline phosphatase, which catalyzed the fluorescent AttoPhos® substrate. Fluorescence intensity was measured using a Spectramax Gemini fluorescence plate reader.
L3096: The method was initially validated for the detection of imetelstat in human K3EDTA plasma (report #320026), then re-validated for the detection of imetelstat in human K2EDTA plasma (report #340713) with respect to specificity (interference check/selectivity), dilution linearity (including prozone assessment), working calibration range, lower and upper limit of quantitation (LLOQ and ULOQ), intra- and inter-assay precision and accuracy, and stability. Re-validations were performed for this method to assess long-term stability (report #345814 and #3450124), selectivity in hemolyzed human plasma (report #345814), antibody interference (report #3450124), selectivity in healthy, lipemic, and disease state plasma, intra- and inter-assay precision and accuracy using a compressed curve range of 100 to 400 nM imetelstat in neat plasma, stock solution stability in buffer, and imetelstat stability in human whole blood (report #3450278). The method was re-validated in 2022 (Validation report #3451131) in compliance with the 2018 FDA Guidance on Bioanalytical Method Validation. The validated parameters included selectivity at the LLOQ concentration, linearity assessments, extended long-term stability and specificity and stability in the presence of ruxolitinib. All clinical studies involved the measurement of imetelstat concentrations in human (K2EDTA) plasma.
L3097: The method validation summaries and the bioanalytical report for the pivotal study MDS3001 are provided in Table 126 and Table 127 below.
L3098: **Table 126:** Bioanalytical Method Validation Summary for Imetelstat Quantification

## TRADENAME, imetelstat
L3101: QCs performance during accuracy & precision	Cumulative precision (%CV)	0.540 to 7.150
Intra-assay accuracy (%bias)	-6.4 to 17
Intra-assay precision (%CV)	0.9 to 11.3
Percent total error (TE)	≤ 40% at LLOQ and ≤ 30% at the other levels
Selectivity & matrix effect	
Acceptance Criteria: Results were considered acceptable if 5 out of 6 unspiked plasma lots were below the LLOQ and if imetelstat-spiked plasma samples had a mean recovery within 75 to 125% of the theoretical concentration.
Normal Human Plasma Results: Six lots of blank normal human plasma were assayed unspiked and spiked with imetelstat to a final concentration of 120 nM. The mean recovery of the 6 plasma lots was 99.8%.
CLL Patient Plasma Results: Six lots of blank human plasma from CLL patients were assayed unspiked and spiked with imetelstat to a final concentration of 120 nM. The mean recovery of the 6 plasma lots was 106.3%.

Hemolysis effect	
Acceptance Criteria: Selectivity in 5% (v/v) hemolyzed human plasma (K2EDTA) was considered acceptable if the unspiked 5% (v/v) hemolyzed plasma lot tested was below the LLOQ and if the mean concentration had recoveries within 80 to 120% of their theoretical concentration at each concentration.
Results: The tested blank sample gave a mean concentration below the LLOQ (0.75 nM) and the imetelstat spiked samples gave mean concentrations within 80 to 120% of theoretical. Mean recovery at 1.20 nM and 2.50 nM were 103.0% and 87.1%, respectively.

Interference & specificity	
Interference: Based on results obtained from the dilution linearity assessments, no interference from the matrix was evident. No observed interference with PANK-1 antibody up to the maximum tested concentration of 1.000 µg/mL PANK-1 sample.
Observed %bias: -24.2 to 2.7% (excluding 2.500 nM imetelstat /1.000 µg/mL PANK-1 sample that had a %bias of -28.4%*)
* reviewer's comment: acceptable after evaluation on the impact of deviation

Selectivity & matrix effect	
Acceptance Criteria: At least 80% of blank lots < LLOQ and at least 80% of spiked samples within 80 to 120% of their theoretical concentration.
Normal Human Plasma Results: All blank samples (10 of 10 lots) gave concentration results below the LLOQ, and 100% of spiked lots (10 of 10 lots) gave recoveries within 80 to 120% of the theoretical concentration with CV(%) \le20% for each replicate.
Myelofibrosis Patient Plasma Results: All blank samples (10 out of 10 lots) gave concentration results below the LLOQ; 10 out of 10 lots spiked at 150 nM and 8 out of 10 lots spiked at 300 nM were within \pm20% of the theoretical concentration.
Normal Human Plasma Results: All tested blank samples gave concentration results below the LLOQ and 100% of the human plasma (K_2EDTA) spiked lots tested gave recoveries within \pm 25% relative error with CV (%) \le20% for each replicate at the LLOQ level and 100% of the spiked lots tested gave recoveries within \pm 20% relative error with CV (%) \le 20% for each replicate at the high QC level.

Hemolysis effect	In 5% (v/v) hemolyzed human plasma, the tested blank sample gave a concentration result below the LLOQ of the method and the Imetelstat spiked lot gave recoveries within \pm25% relative error and CV(%) between replicates \le20% when tested at 100 nM (LLOQ) and within \pm20% relative error and CV(%) between replicates \le20% when tested at 300 nM (HQC).
Lipemic effect	All blank samples (10 out of 10 lots) gave concentration results below the LLOQ; 10 out of 10 lots spiked at 150 nM and 8 of 10 lots spiked at 300 nM were within \pm20% of the theoretical concentration. L3144: Figure 14 is a line graph showing the Mean Values (units) (\pm SE) of the absolute change in RBC transfusion units over time (Weeks) for the Imetelstat and Placebo groups in the Phase 3, Intent-to-Treat Analysis Set. L3168: Figure 37: Study MDS3001: Mean Number of Grade 3+ Thrombocytopenia Events over Time. This is a step plot showing Mean Cumulative Frequency (MCF) Estimates versus Months (0 to 30). The Y-axis (MCF Estimates) ranges from 0 to 4. The Imetelstat arm (solid red line) shows a significantly higher mean number of Grade 3+ Thrombocytopenia events compared to the Placebo arm (dashed gray line). By Month 30, the Imetelstat arm reaches approximately 3.8 events, while the Placebo arm remains below 0.5 events. Dotted lines represent the 95% confidence intervals for both arms.
L3169: Histogram showing the percentage distribution of platelet transfusion requirements (Number of Platelet Transfusions) for patients on the Imetelstat arm (blue bars) and Placebo arm (red bars) in the MDS3001 phase 3 study. The X-axis ranges from 0 to 10 transfusions. The Y-axis is Percent (0% to 100%). For both arms, the majority of patients required 0 transfusions. The Imetelstat arm shows a higher percentage of patients requiring 1 transfusion (approximately 10%) compared to the Placebo arm (approximately 5%). The Placebo arm shows a higher percentage of patients requiring 0 transfusions (approximately 80%) compared to the Imetelstat arm (approximately 75%).
L3170: Figure 39: Study MDS3001: Mean Number of Grade 3+ Neutropenia Events over Time. This is a step plot showing the Mean Cumulative Function (MCF) Estimates (Y-axis, 0 to 5) versus Months (X-axis, 0 to 30). The Imetelstat arm (solid red line) shows a significantly higher mean number of Grade 3+ neutropenia events compared to the Placebo arm (dashed gray line). The Imetelstat MCF estimate rises sharply, reaching approximately 4.0 by 24 months and remaining stable thereafter. The Placebo MCF estimate remains near 0.0 throughout the study period. Dotted lines represent the 95% confidence intervals for both arms.
L3171: Histogram showing the percentage of patients requiring myeloid growth support (Number of Growth Factor Administrations) by study arm (Imetelstat and Placebo). The X-axis ranges from 0 to 20. Subsequent bars show very low percentages for 1 to 20 administrations, indicating minimal need for growth factor support in the majority of patients.
L3172: Diagram illustrating the Imetelstat 2-Compartment TMDD Model with Saturable Binding Schematic.
The model consists of three compartments: Central (1), Receptor pool (B_{max}), and Peripheral (2).
Dose input is shown entering the Central compartment.
The Central compartment has a volume labeled V_c and clearance labeled CL/V_c.
Transfer rates between compartments are labeled: K_{on} (Central to Receptor pool), K_{off} (Receptor pool to Central), K_{back} (Peripheral to Central), and K_{int} (Receptor pool to Peripheral).
